# REVIVAL NOTES

Living file. Continuity between sessions lives here (no other memory). Rules for a Claude session are in `CLAUDE.md`.
Branch: `revive`. Goal: play the SW5e simulator from a phone browser, hosted on VM1 (Oracle free tier).

Acceptance test: create a character, it saves and reloads; create a campaign with the local generator; open Play with that character and campaign; move between locations; run a combat encounter; HP changes persist after reload.

Done in this review (each checked in headless Chromium at phone size; throwaway scripts against `e2e/lib.mjs`):
- Character sheet (`components/CharacterManagement/CharacterSheet.tsx`): skills and their abilities come from the skills data (Piloting was shown as Dexterity); modifiers come from `rules.ts`; the Combat tab lists carried weapons with attack and damage numbers; the Equipment tab reads equipment saved by name; the Powers tab shows force and tech powers with cost, attack bonus and save DC; languages, tools, saving throws, Hit Dice, experience and conditions are shown; the backstory can be edited. It no longer imports the legacy store.
- Weapons: `carriedWeapons` (characterCalculation.ts) is the one list for the sheet and Play. `hasWeaponProficiency` (rules.ts) parses the class's own wording ("martial vibroweapons with the finesse property", "that lack the ... properties"). Blasters use Dexterity, other weapons Strength, finesse the better. Without proficiency the bonus is not added. Play picks the best melee and ranged weapon carried.
- `components/ui/ErrorBoundary.tsx` is mounted around the routes (App.tsx): a failing page shows the error with Home and Try Again, and clears on navigation.
- Personality: `PersonalityTraitForm` (legacy, never rendered, wrote to the dead store, hard-coded examples) now offers the chosen background's own trait, ideal, bond and flaw tables (all 61 backgrounds have them) and hands its result to the opener. It opens from the Background step in creation and from the sheet. It is not a form element, so it cannot submit the creation form.
- Class step: shows the book's Quick Build and "Creating a ..." text (`quickBuildText`, `creatingText` in the class data). This is what the legacy TemplateSystem and GuidedMode hard-coded.
- Character page header and tab bar fit a phone; species, class and background are shown by name.

- GM tab (Play, "GM Interface"), rebuilt on the game's own state; still copy and paste, no API:
  - `lib/llm/reportHandler.ts`: `generateGameMasterReport(state, action)` writes the report from Play's character, campaign, current area and exits (with the themes and hooks `characterLanguageProcessor` reads from the backstory and personality, and the last three story turns). `parseGameMasterResponse` splits the reply at `---SYSTEM_DATA_FOLLOWS---`. `applyGameMasterResponse` returns the fields to save: hit points, credits, experience, items, conditions; the area's description and new occupants; new quests and objectives the story completed (a finished quest pays its reward). A hostile named by the GM is looked up in the stat blocks (`/api/sw5e/monsters`, `/api/world/occupant`) so that it can be fought; without a match it is a neutral stranger.
  - The story is kept in the campaign's `sessionLogs` (added to the save schema) and shown on the Story tab. Each exchange is also recorded through `/api/debriefs` (`submitPlayerAction`, `processLLMResponse` in debriefCompiler.ts, repaired).
  - House rule (labelled where it applies): the story cannot take the last hit point; outside a fight hit points stop at 1. "Dead" cannot be set by a reply.
  - Play no longer uses the legacy `useCharacter` and `useCampaign` stores.
- A campaign without a world: Play offers "Generate a world" in place of the old location list (the user's decision, 2026-10-13). The campaign's old quests are kept beside the generated ones. Test Combat stays for such campaigns.
- Appearance: `characters.appearance` (json: gender, age, height, weight, description; migration 0002, the user's decision). Creation's Details step saves them, shows what the chosen species usually looks like (height, weight, skin, hair, eyes, distinctions from the species data), and the personality form has its appearance field back.

## Environment and ops
- Windows PC (2026-10-23, the project has moved here): `deploy/windows-start.ps1 -Mode prod|test` starts what a server needs (Tailscale, PostgreSQL, packages, migrations, build), stops whatever listens on ports 5000, 5001 and 5002 (a built server or a dev one, with `taskkill /T`; the user's word 2026-10-31: either shortcut stops any server on those three, so a stale `npm run dev` no longer blocks it), starts it hidden, sets Tailscale Serve and opens the browser. Prod: port 5000, database `sw5e`, tailnet HTTPS on 443. Test: port 5002, database `sw5e_test` (no showcase world), built with `VITE_TEST_MENU=1` from a snapshot in `%USERPROFILE%\sw5e-test` (node_modules is a junction), tailnet HTTPS on 10000. Desktop shortcuts "SW5e Production" and "SW5e Test" run it. Logs in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\sw5e\logs`. The servers outlive the window; stop one with `Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort <port>` and `Stop-Process`. Serve on this PC also proxies 3000 and 3001 (not ours; left alone). The production start is not yet tried on this PC; the test start was (2026-10-23).
- VM: Ubuntu 24.04, aarch64, 1 vCPU, ~8.9 GB RAM, 4 GB swap, ~30 GB free disk, Node v18.19.1, npm 9.2.0. No Docker.
- Postgres 16 on this VM, role and database `sw5e`, listening on 127.0.0.1 only. `DATABASE_URL` is in `.env` (gitignored, mode 600). Do not print it.
- The server listens on 127.0.0.1:5000 unless `HOST` is set (server/index.ts). The app has no login, so keep it that way: Tailscale is what stands in front of it.
- The app is a systemd service, `sw5e` (unit file in `deploy/`, installed in `/etc/systemd/system/`, enabled at boot, restarts on failure, NODE_ENV=production). It owns port 5000: after `npm run build` run `sudo systemctl restart sw5e`; do not start node by hand. Logs: `journalctl -u sw5e`.
- Backups: `sw5e-backup.timer` runs `deploy/backup.sh` at 03:30 (pg_dump to `~/sw5e-backups`, mode 700, newest 14 kept). Restore: `gunzip -c <file> | psql "$DATABASE_URL"`.
- Tailscale 1.104.1 (official apt repo). This machine is `sw5e` on the user's tailnet, and their phone is on it. The tailnet's name, addresses and approval links are not written here because this repository is public: `tailscale status` shows them. `tailscale serve --bg 5000` publishes the app over HTTPS at this machine's tailnet name, to tailnet devices only, but Serve must first be enabled for the tailnet by the user: the command prints the approval link and waits. A user unit, `sw5e-serve-enable` (started with systemd-run, log in `~/sw5e-logs/serve-enable.log`), is waiting on that; if it is gone, run the command again. Check with `tailscale serve status`. HTTPS matters because the clipboard GM loop needs it.
- Host firewall is OFF. `iptables -S` shows only ACCEPT policies and `netfilter-persistent` is masked, so the saved rules in `/etc/iptables/rules.v4` (Oracle default: allow 22, reject the rest, plus an old allow for port 8000 from 10.0.0.0/24) are not loaded. Only the Oracle VCN security list protects the VM. rpcbind also listens on port 111. Nothing was changed; decide with the user in Phase 3.
- Test data in the DB: character "Test Pilot" and campaign "Test Campaign" (both id 2, from the 2b script). Delete from Home when no longer wanted.
- Commits need a git identity: `git -c user.name=Tombdbad -c user.email=<account email> commit`. No identity is configured globally.
- Stopping a server by name: `pkill -f "node dist/[i]ndex.js"` or `pkill -f "tsx server/[i]ndex.ts"`. Without the brackets pkill also kills the shell that runs it.
- The base server on 127.0.0.1:5002 (a `node dist/index.js` started 2026-10-15 by hand, not a unit, against `sw5e_test`) was stopped on 2026-10-18 at the user's request. Its transient unit `sw5e-base.service` had pointed at a missing `~/sw5e-base`. Nothing listens on 5002 now.
- Old helper scripts: `~/sw5e-phase0/` (historical). Run logs and screenshots: `~/sw5e-logs/` (`e2e/`, `e2e_phone/`, `shots_desktop/`, `shots_phone/`).
- A Claude session on this VM through Telegram needs a one-time code for most writes; the CLI session does not.

## Rules data: one source (shared/data)
- `npm run import:sw5e` (shared/scripts/import-sw5e.ts) downloads every dataset from `https://sw5eapi.azurewebsites.net/api` and writes `shared/data/sw5e/<name>.json` plus `manifest.json` (data version 199, 36 datasets, 9.2 MB, about 15 seconds). It strips storage metadata and duplicate "...Json" / "...Enum" fields, repairs the API's broken dashes and apostrophes, gives every record an `id`, and stores class, archetype and species features with their owner. `SW5E_CACHE=<dir>` reads saved responses instead of the network.
- It also derives two small files from book text: `travel.json` (the nine galactic regions, the hyperspace travel-time table, realspace times, starship size table) and `lookups.json` (languages, the nine alignments, point-buy costs, wealth by level).
- Two more files are derived from book text (2026-10-15): `transport.json` (the Expanded Content's mounts, tack and vehicles tables and the Player's Handbook travel pace table: speeds, costs, carrying capacity, miles an hour) and `tools.json` (Wretched Hives' Tool Proficiencies chapter: for each tool the skills it helps, its special use and its sample activities with their DCs). `DERIVE_ONLY=1 npm run import:sw5e` rebuilds just those two from the files on disk, without the network.
- Do not edit `shared/data/sw5e/*.json` and do not define rules content anywhere else. Re-run the importer to refresh.
- `shared/data/index.ts`: `loadDataset(name)`, one lazy loader per dataset. `client/src/lib/sw5e/{species,classes,archetypes,backgrounds,feats,forcePowers,techPowers,equipment,items,monsters,npcs,droids,vehicles,starships,maneuvers,fightingStyles,weaponProperties,conditions,skills,exploits,constants}.ts` keep their old export names but only map that data. `client/src/lib/data/*.ts` re-export them.
- Server: `server/sw5eData.ts` loads datasets on demand; `GET /api/sw5e` (manifest) and `GET /api/sw5e/:dataset?search=&fields=&limit=`.
- Bundle: pages load lazily (App.tsx), so the home page script fell from 1.9 MB to 0.4 MB; character creation loads a 2.6 MB chunk (0.5 MB gzipped) with the data it needs.
- The setting's known systems and planets (`client/src/lib/sw5e/starSystems.ts`, `locations.ts`) are not rules data and are not from sw5e.com; the world generator uses them as seeds.

## World generator (shared/logic/index.ts)
- Model: `World { areas, paths, startId, antagonist, partyLevel, ... }`. An `Area` has a kind (galaxy, region, system, planet, settlement, building, room), a parent, a position, a danger level, `gateway` (the way in and out of its parent) and its contents: `occupants`, `ships`, `loot`. A `Path` joins two areas of the same kind: hyperlane, sublight, route, street or door, with a travel time or distance and optional `locked` DC and `hidden`.
- Every area is a place to stand: a room, the street in front of a building, the outskirts of a settlement, a planet's orbit, a system's jump point. `exitsOf` lists the moves from an area (along a path, in through a gateway, out to the parent); `findPath` gives the route between any two areas. Regions only group systems.
- How it builds: regions and hyperspace times from `travel.json`; known systems placed in their regions and generated ones added so no region is empty; hyperlanes as a minimum spanning tree per region plus one lane between neighbouring regions and a few shortcuts; planets on sublight lanes; then for a planet: a starport settlement, a second settlement and one or two wilderness sites suited to the biome, joined by routes; buildings by settlement type joined by streets; rooms by building type with fixed layouts, locked and hidden doors. Only the starting planet is built in full; `expandArea` builds the rest when someone goes there.
- Placement, all from the rules data: staff by what a room is for, with gear picked by name from the equipment data; faction sentries and hostile garrisons from the stat blocks a faction fields; beasts whose text matches the planet's biome; horrors in ruins; utility droids in ports and workshops; fights sized by the 5th edition XP budgets for the party's level and size and the area's danger; stat-block loadouts read from their attacks and armor; ships assembled from starship equipment by size; loot caches by room purpose, with credits sized against the Wealth by Level table and enhanced items of a rarity suited to the level.
- `validateWorld(world, catalog?)` lists what is wrong: wrong nesting, paths between different kinds or parents, a container without a gateway, areas unreachable from the start, and (with the catalog) any stat block or item that is not in the rules data.
- Edit functions keep the map sound and say why when they refuse: `updateArea`, `addArea` (joined to its nearest neighbour), `removeArea` (neighbours are re-joined), `connectAreas`, `disconnectAreas` (refused if it would cut the map), `setGateway`, `setStart`.
- Server (`server/sw5eRoutes.ts`): `POST /api/world/generate | expand | populate | validate | occupant`. The world is saved in the campaign's `galaxyMap` column; `currentLocation` is then an area id.
- UI: the campaign page (`components/Campaign/CampaignManager.tsx`) generates, browses and edits the world and now saves everything it edits. Play (`pages/GameBoard.tsx`) shows the current area, who and what is there, and its exits; enemies bar the way onward until beaten; fights use the stat block's AC, HP, attack and damage and the weapons the character carries; the fallen leave their gear; loot goes to the character; locks and hidden things take ability checks; `Explore` builds an unvisited place. The Play galaxy map draws the world's systems and hyperlanes.
- The same seed and options give the same world.

- Floors (life 4; "Levels" in shared/logic: `levelsOf`, `linkLevels`, `planBuilding`). Rooms have `layout.level`: 0 the ground, below 0 beneath it (cellar, basement, crypt, vault, detention block, hidden compartment, escape tunnel), above 0 over it (apartments, quarters, offices, command and operations rooms, control tower, rooftop, lookout, bridge, port office), and a gallery half a floor over a tall room (main bar, workfloor, storage floor, reading hall, ...). Anything that holds a ship (or is called a docking bay, hangar, berth, arm) is on the building's top floor (at least the first above ground; landing fields and pads are the ground) and has a `roof`: `open` to the sky, or `hangar` (doors that slide back, animated) where the air is hostile (volcanic, ice, barren, gas giant, ocean, stations). Rooms on different floors are not joined directly: a room of its own lies between them (`layout.form`: since 2026-10-20 a stair block in a built building, see the next point; a lift where a ship room is one end; a rising passage in the wild; `climb` says which way the floor rises), plus now and then corridors (large buildings), elevated walkways (upper floors of urban, ocean and station buildings), a ladder up the outside wall (a gateway room, a second way in to an upper room) and a gallery with a flight of `stairs` assets either side. Nothing is ever put in those (furnishing skips them; a gallery is furnished). Rooms on other floors may stand over each other (`clash` compares floors as well as squares). The 3D map draws each room at its floor, the party climbs the steps, what is over a room being looked into is taken away and the ground is taken away from a cellar. Worlds before life 4 get all of it once: every built building is planned again (the secret ways the party found stay found).
- Stair blocks (2026-10-20, the user's design; "Stair blocks" and `linkColumns` in shared/logic, `tests/world/access.test.ts`). Rooms over and under one another stand on one footprint, a column: a ground room (the host), the rooms stacked over it (`stack:<id>`, a tower has several) and a basement under it (`under:<id>`). `linkColumns` puts every room over the ground on a column top (the floor it names, else the nearest: a rooftop over the apartments) and every room under it under a ground room without one (the room it opens off first), never a way in from the street (`levelsOf` keeps a gateway on the ground), a tall room, a hangar or a round room; a column with nothing over or under its host gets an "Upper storeroom" or a "Storage cellar", so that every block is five lanes. Passages of a column's rooms to rooms outside it move down to their hosts (the block is the way between its floors); the lock or secret of a room's way in stays on the door into it at its stairs (`entry:` tags). The block (`stairBlock`) stands against a side of the host, five squares wide and `BLOCK_LENGTH` (4) long away from it, in lanes: upReturn, upStart, hall, downStart, downReturn (the other way about in some buildings). The hall is a corridor at the ground floor: the host's door at its near end, at its far end the next room the host opened onto (that passage moves to the hall) or a dead end. The two flights up are one room, a switchback (`layout.switchback`, form stairs, climbing one level): up the low lane from its foot (a doorway in the host's wall) to the landing (the far row, half a floor up), and back up the other lane to its head, a doorway into the room over the host; the two flights down are the same from the basement's doorway up to their head, a doorway in the host's wall. So the host's wall has three doorways side by side: the flight up, the hall, the flight down (at first the flights opened off the hall's first square, beside the host's door, but three doors on one square could not be told apart by a tap). A tower has a switchback over the first for each floor more, its foot a door from the room on the floor below. Every flight has a doorway at its foot and at its head (the landing could take a door to a half-level room; none is made yet). `planBuilding` sets a column on its host's footprint and its block against the host's wall as soon as the host has its place (`settle`, `placeBlock`, before anything else is put round the host; the host is at least 5 by 5); if no side fits, a lift beside the host instead (`liftInstead`; none in the test worlds). The map walks a switchback lane by lane: `canStep` (shared) never steps across the well but on the landing, used by the tap route, the stick and the walk check; heights from `climbAt`/`floorLevelAt`; `Switchback` draws the treads, landing and the wall between the flights. No more in-room stairwells (`stairwellSpot` is gone) or 3 by 7 stair rooms in built buildings; what is left between floors there (a hangar on the top floor, a room no column could take) is a lift. The mezzanine keeps its straight in-room flight (`toward` on the stairs asset), drawn with a doorway at its foot and at its top. Taking stairs: a tap walks to the foot of a flight nobody stands on (`stairsApproach`; it used to walk onto the stairs' own blocked squares, so the mezzanine could not be reached, fixed `f762739`), and arriving the party stands at the way back (`arrivalSquares`). Since the evening of 2026-10-20 the block stacks both ways (`stairBlock(host, side, at, mirror, ups, downs)`): a switchback up for each floor over the host (city towers of three to five floors, `MAX_HEIGHT` 5) and one down for each floor under it (sub-basements, `MAX_DEPTH` 2; vaults, crypts and cells go under a basement where there is one); doors keyed `foot-up<n>`/`head-up<n>`/`head-down<n>`/`foot-down<n>`; the block's side, offset and mirroring are kept on its hall (`geo:`). A roof on a column's top floor (planDistrict prefers one) gets a switchback more, from the top floor to the roof, with the roof door at its head (`roofStairs`): a way in from the roof's ladder. Windows have a `pane` (size, latch, lock DC, sensor) and lead to their building (`to`), so arriving through one stands the party at it; balconies (form `balcony`, open air, not a way in) hang off the great houses' upper rooms over the street, joined to their room by patio doors (a path that may be `alarmed`) and reached only that way: no climb from the street (grapple hooks will, once written). The camera follows the party up from the street at once (the street's follow point was cleared a frame late, which left the camera aimed at the street after a roof's ladder). The audit (`auditBuildings`) checks columns (footprint, a floor apart, a door to the block), blocks (against the host, five lanes, switchbacks of two lanes) and that the floors either side of every door are within a step (a sixth of a floor), and walks every building (`walkBuilding`): from every way in, over the squares the map lets a player walk, through every door and up and down every flight; every room reached and every way usable. A room counts as standing on the ground for the street (`streetGround`, the ladder's foot, the map's `inRoom`) if its floors reach the ground (`floorsOf`), so the stairs down are not open street.

## Play rules (Phase 2f, 2026-10-13)
Where the code is: `client/src/lib/sw5e/characterCalculation.ts` (features, their effects, levelling), `client/src/lib/sw5e/rules.ts` (casting), `client/src/lib/stores/useCombat.ts` and `components/Combat/PlayCombat.tsx` (the fight), `shared/logic/index.ts` (quests, ships, fuel, passage), `pages/GameBoard.tsx` (Play), `pages/CharacterManagement.tsx` (level-up panel, Features tab).
- No dice in the shooter mode, outside a fight either (2026-11-02, house rule; see "Shooter: no tabletop outside the fight"): a check is passive (10 + the bonus, +5 with advantage, -5 with disadvantage), no die is thrown on the table (each shows its middle face), a fall is not checked in the air and does the dice's average, and flight gear fits a carried power cell by itself. No turns either (2026-11-02, the user: "ZERO turn based mechanics in the shooter mode"): pit bouts are fought in real time on the arena floor, a ship fight from Engage is the dogfight against the ships in orbit, and the practice fight is not offered.
- Flight gear switched on slows the clock to real time, in both modes (2026-11-02, house rule): a minute of its charge is a minute on the clock, also in a real-time fight (the clock runs while the pack burns).
- Proficiency tiers (2026-11-02): Wretched Hives' six tiers are played (an item's "one step" or "grand mastery"); high and grand mastery's rerolls are taken as advantage only (house simplification). The Rocketpack's rocket bursts round its target, hit or miss, and only the burst is played (house reading).
- Gear in the shooter's fight, and foes' gear (2026-11-02, house rules; see "Gear in the shooter, and foes that use theirs"): every item of use in a fight is on the item belt with no roll or save (a moment in the hand); conditions last their turns at six seconds each, eight at most; binders and a restraining bolt only take a foe beaten down; soldierly foes now and then carry a grenade and a medpac and use their stat block's area actions; one with a comlink calls for backup (one or two more, 12 s later) unless the party's comm jammer is on; a nemesis comes back carrying what it learned to carry against the party.
- Ways in from outside (2026-10-20, house rules; shared/logic "Windows", "Balconies", `roofStairs`; the window card in `GameBoard.tsx`). Windows are slits, small, large or wide; large and wide ones on the ground floor are climbed through from the street (in unseen, as by a secret way) or out from inside. Shut: open, latched (from outside: jimmied, Dexterity (Sleight of Hand) DC 12; a failure costs a minute) or locked (picked like a door's lock with its DC, a security kit helps; or the glass broken, Strength (Athletics) DC 10: those in the room hear it, a sensor trips, and the law hears of it where it is about); from inside a window opens without a check. An alarmed window's sensor is seen with a passive Perception of 13 or more; it is disabled with Intelligence DC 13 (a security or slicer's kit helps; a failure trips it); going through an armed one trips the building's alarm and turns those in the room on the party. Looking in needs no check. Balconies of a palace, an estate or a manor house are reached only through their patio doors from inside (not climbed to from the street: the user's intent is that grapple hooks will reach them once grapple hooks have rules); the patio doors are locked (DC 15 in a palace, 13 elsewhere) and alarmed in a palace, half the time elsewhere; slipping past their sensor is Intelligence DC 13, a failure raises the alarm but the party goes through. A roof on a column's top floor has stairs down into the building (its roof door locked in a hostile building, DC 10 + 5 per danger level to 25, and two times in five elsewhere, DC 12). On a city planet a building's tallest column rises to three to five floors over the ground, and some go down a floor more to a sub-basement.
- Ship upgrades (2026-10-21, asked by the user; `shared/logic/shipyard.ts`, a leaf module because index.ts is 700 KB and the rules are testable alone; `shipSheet`, the ship fight, `partsPlaceAt`, `fitShipPart` in index.ts; `components/Play/ShipWorks.tsx`; tests `tests/items/shipyard.test.ts`, browser `e2e/works.mjs`). The book's system (Starships of the Galaxy chapters 3 to 5, its tables derived into `ships.json` `modifications`, `upgrade`, `equipment`): a modification costs the category's base cost x the size's modifier x its grade (from grade 1), takes (base x size) / (250 x workforce) days with at least the size's workforce, and an Intelligence (Mechanic's Kit) check against the grade-and-tier DC (+2 a modification over the slots for Tiny to Medium, +1 larger); equipment costs its price x the size's modifier (weapons and ammunition the same for any size), days = cost x size / (500 x workforce); a tier costs the tier's base x the size's upgrade modifier, days = cost / (500 x workforce). A party that brings the part and does the work pays the material cost only (half); a shipyard installs without a check (the book allows the GM to assume it). Removal: half the cost and time. Prerequisites, "only once" categories, suites (Small and up, the maximum by size and Constitution), exclusions and slots (an Equipment Room adds four) are kept. What a modification does is read from its text (137 of 257): ability scores (a makeshift system lowers another, chosen per ship and system: house rule for the GM's choice), AC, damage reduction, hull and shield resistances, flying and turning speed, saving throw proficiency (Resilient systems), no critical hits against it, fuel capacity, a hyperdrive slot, slots, weapon systems on the weapon they are fitted to (reload, range, all-round fire, no helm disadvantage, Gauss rerolls, S-foils +1, ionizing/shocking/stunning/blinding rounds on a critical), crew actions with a saving throw (30: launchers, dischargers, projectors, the tractor beam, slicers ...; uses the ship's tier a fight, house rule: a fight is between recharges; conditions last to the end of the target's next turn, house rule), free actions of tuned systems, flight computers and damage control (house rule: a bonus action is a free action once a turn in the ship fight), power backup and emergency generator, flares, a feedback shield, escape pods (the crew gets away whole), and what suites give a long rest aboard (luxury quarters: all Hit Dice and exhaustion -2; mess hall: 2 Hit Dice more; medbay: a level of exhaustion for each two hours; armory: advantage on the first attack; recreation, laboratory, investigation, workshop: advantage on the first check; meditation chamber and supercharger station: temporary points). Comms, scanners, cargo and the like are shown, not applied. Enhanced ship armor, shields and weapons (+1 to +3) are fitted the same way. Ship conditions (blinded, disabled, ionized, shocked, slowed, stunned, tractored) follow the book's appendix A.
  - Who sells parts (the user's rule, house rules for the stock): a scrapyard's dealer (junkyard, scrap dealer: trade `salvage`, life 13 for old worlds) sells salvaged parts of grade 0 and 1 and premium enhanced parts at best, at 60% of the material cost, only where the settlement has a port and it lies away from the Core worlds (Deep Core, Core, Colonies, Inner Rim) or the law hardly reaches (frontier or lawless), and buys parts at 40%; a shipyard's showroom outfitter sells new parts up to a grade set by the place (a city: 5 in the Core worlds, 4 in the Expansion Region and Mid Rim, 3 further out; a station one less; a town 2 or 3; an outpost 1) and enhanced parts by that grade, and sells them installed (the yard's crew is twice the least workforce: house rule; the ship is grounded until the work is done, `Ship.work`); its mechanic fits the parts the party brings for the installation half of the cost (an enhanced part: a tenth of its worth and a day, house rule) and upgrades the tier. A wreck left by a ship fight gives up one piece of its equipment one time in two, salvaged (house rule). Parts are carried in the hold (`playState.shipParts`). Aboard, the ship's systems panel lists all of it and lets the crew install (tick who works; the check dialog asks who leads; away from a port twice the time and DC +5, house rule from the book's "up to 100%"; a failed check costs half the time and half the part's worth, the part is kept; a mechanic's shop suite adds the proficiency bonus) and upgrade the tier at a port.
- Droid shops (2026-10-21, asked by the user: a shop, not a stall, with the droids and the parts on display). Every city has a droid dealer, a town now and then; its Showroom is a shop floor (`ROOM_PLANS` "droid showroom": a sales counter, four to six droid plinths, parts shelves, a display case, a charging station) and its Repair bay has a droid repair rig. The droids of the day's stock stand on the plinths (`droidDisplay`; Play sets `interiors/display.ts`, the map draws the figures) and the customizations it sells are on its shelves and in its case (the shelf's own pool otherwise); a tap on a plinth calls the dealer over. Prices: the Expanded Content's Droids table where it names the droid (an R2 astromech 2,800 cr, a 3PO 5,500, a B1-A 1,300 ...), the others on a curve fitted through that table by experience points (house rule; it was 6 cr an experience point), derived into `gear.json` `droids`. The dealer sells Droid Customization items (house rule: Wretched Hives has them found or made), six to nine a day up to a rarity set by the place (standard and premium in a town, prototype in a city, advanced too in a city of the Core worlds), at their value; a scrapyard has salvaged ones (standard, premium) at 60%. Bought, a customization is carried (to fit later, yourselves with astrotech's implements, the dialog asking who does it) or fitted at once by the technician for the fee (no check). Old worlds (life 13): droid showrooms and repair bays are fitted out again; a city or town without a droid dealer sells droids at its tech shop's repair counter, with plinths there.
- Gear in play (2026-10-21, asked by the user: every item serves its purpose by the rules; `shared/logic/gear.ts`, a leaf module read by `gearUse`/`gearRole` in index.ts; the book texts the data lacks are derived into `gear.json`; tests `tests/items/*.test.ts`, `npm run test:items`). Every equipment row has a use (`tests/items/classify.test.ts` fails on one without). House rules, each labelled where it shows:
  - Ammunition (fight: `useCombat` `ammo`, `reload`, `spray`): a shot spends a round from the load; reloading is an action, or a bonus action (the book), free with an ammo pouch, power belt, bandolier, quiver or feed belt once a turn; a weapon never fired comes loaded with its plain round; a power generator gives 10 shots (the book gives none); a flechette clip or projector canister is a shot each; burst and rapid fire spend their number; special rounds and enhanced rounds bring their rider (save, damage, condition, burst, line or cone, burning ground for a minute, disintegrate or carbonite at 0 hit points, a snare's restraint); out of a fight, loading takes a minute. Calibrators and collimators: an action, +1 damage and their property for the next fight (the top grades +3 to hit and +1d8).
  - Food and water (`passDays`, the daily clock): in a settlement the lifestyle paid feeds and lodges (the book's expenses); aboard the party's ship the stores give water; in the wild a ration a day and a gallon of water (two in heat), or forage (Wisdom (Survival) against 10/15/20 by the land, after the DMG; nothing grows on barren worlds). Hunger and thirst follow the PH (3 + Con days, then a level of exhaustion a day); levels so taken are not rested off until fed. Sleeping rough (no bedroll, tent or shelter) gives a quarter of the Hit Dice back; a tent all; a blanket or traveler's clothes give advantage on the night's save against cold; a raft carries four across water on an ocean world.
  - Hazards (`hazardsAt`, gear.ts `exposure`, `HAZARD_HOUR`): where they are is a house rule (cold of an ice world and desert heat by day or volcanic worlds out of doors, no air on barren worlds' wild sites, sewer gas and volcanic fumes, dark caves, sewers, ruins, crypts and the wilds at night, a reactor's radiation, a gas giant's gravity, a sunken wreck on an ocean world); cold and heat are the DMG's hourly saves; bad air DC 13 Con or 1d6 poison and poisoned, radiation 1d6 necrotic an hour, gravity disadvantage and half speed (house rules); vacuum and the deep are refused without the suit (the book's death in a few rounds); water: hold your breath (PH) or a suit or respirator's air, which runs down while worn there; the suits, respirators, oxygen tanks, belt generators and lights answer them as their texts say. Darkness blinds in a fight without a light or darkvision. Putting on a suit takes 5 minutes, other gear a minute; powered gear (glowrod 10 h, belt generators 10 min, jetpacks a minute a cell ...) recharges with a power cell or for free in an hour at a settlement or aboard (house rule).
  - Carrying (PH Lifting and Carrying; the Variant: Encumbrance is a toggle on the inventory): Strength x 15, size multipliers; encumbered and heavily encumbered as the variant; over capacity no travel. House rule: what lies beyond the containers carried and 20 lb loose is carried awkwardly, as if encumbered. A hovercart takes 2,000 lb, a beast of burden its capacity, a companion Strength x 15; a tracker vest carries ten light items free.
  - Clothing (house rules): fine clothes or a dress uniform give advantage on Persuasion with officials and the well-to-do; a costume advantage on Deception to pass as someone else; clandestine clothes hide 2 lb (Investigation DC 15 to find, the book); sunshades as the book.
  - Accessories by their text: flight (jetpack, rocket boots: flying in a fight ignores furniture), a repulsor pack's slow fall (no fall damage), friction grips (climb speed) and a grappling hook or fibercord line (DC 5 easier climb, house rule), shock gloves (unarmed +1d4 lightning), a stealth field (advantage on sight-based Stealth while it runs), bipod/tripod braced (Strength requirement ignored, speed 0). Medpacs, stims and spice by grade: an enhanced consumable reads its base item's rule with the grade's dice and DC (`enhancedConsumableUse`); a vitapac restores the Hit Dice its text gives.
  - Ship work: a party short of the workforce hires skilled hands at a port (PH Services, 20 cr a day each).
  - Field gear in a fight (2026-10-21, 74970bc and bd7676d; `useCombat.ts`, tests `tests/items/fieldgear.test.ts`). Drawing a weapon (PH Equipping and Unequipping Weapons; the first attack is made with the weapon brought in): changing to the other kind takes the turn's one free object interaction, a second change takes the action (`drawPlan`, "Switch weapon"), and a holster (its text) draws one weapon free of both until a weapon is stowed in it as an action ("Holster it"). Fire extinguisher (an action, from Items): a 15-foot cone toward the enemy picked out puts out unenhanced fires (ground set ablaze by an unenhanced round or mine) and blinds those in it who fail a DC 14 Constitution save until the start of their next turn; one who saves is immune to it for the fight. Mines (an action, from Items): set where the character stands, arm at the start of their next turn, go off for anyone but the party who comes within 15 feet of them (house rule: the party knows where they lie), or, paired with a remote detonator beforehand (a minute, from the detonator's card, two at most, within 10 feet: the book), only to it (an action, within 30 feet, `Detonate`); each kind's damage and conditions are read from its text, an incendiary mine leaves the ground burning for a minute, and mines not set off are picked up after the fight (house rule). Breaching charges (a minute to install) are only for doors. A propulsion pack running gives its swimming speed, otherwise water halves speed (PH Swimming: `waterSpeed`). A machinist's catalyzer taken for the fight gives tech powers its bonuses (damage and healing, from the second grade attack and save DC); a portable teleporter (an action) steps the character through to a free square in sight up to its range and five feet more, beside the enemy picked out or away from them; nothing provokes and nobody else passes the portals (house rules).
  - Small gear (house rules; the books give most of these a line of text): a mirror shows the next room through a doorway without a check; a chronometer lists the exact time, dawn or dusk, the heist's take-off and the search's end; a shovel serves as the kit for ore and plant finds of the first two tiers (`harvestKit`); a hydrospanner is a mechanic's kit and a mess kit a chef's kit, with that kit's proficiency (`kitAlias`); a datapad or wristpad keeps 50 notes and a stylus pen alone 5 (`comms.ts`); a holorecorder records the room in ten minutes (the last five places are kept, and each is a piece of lore of the place); a code cylinder opens a lock of DC 15 or less; a holotrace device lists every call to security in the district with where it comes from and when it is put through (without one only the calls in the room are known); a pocket scrambler keeps a call to a contact from being overheard while the law searches (a plain call tells the search where the party is); a personal translator (5 languages) or vocoder mask worn (15) set at a settlement, a ship or with a protocol droid covers a people that speaks only its own tongue (Wookiee, Jawa, Ewok, Tusken, Binary: `OWN_TONGUE_ONLY`), whose haggling is otherwise at disadvantage. The law searches the party at customs posts and checkpoints and on landing where it is policed: contraband carried openly is seized, what is hidden in a smugglepack's compartment or clandestine clothes' pockets is found only if the searcher's Investigation (d20 + 1, 3 or 5 by the law) meets the hiding place's DC (`lawSearch`).
  - Surgeons and technicians (2026-10-21, dd6e4cd; `surgeonStock`, `modStock`, `modFee`, `fitAtShop`, `modFitProblem`; tests `tests/items/vendors.test.ts`). A medic lists every prosthesis the place can afford and three to five cybernetic augmentations a day, none above the place's rarity (standard in a village or outpost, premium in a town, prototype in a city or station, advanced in a city of the Core worlds; never legendary or artifact), at the item's value moved by region and settlement; bought, a part is carried or fitted at once (the surgeon's check and fee as before). An armsmith sells the modifications of blasters, vibroweapons and armor, a tech shop (and a mechanic) of wristpads, focus generators and augments, an outfitter of clothing and armor, a scrapyard used ones at 60% (premium at best), the black market any at 1.6 times and a rarity more; five to eight a day; a technician fits one in an hour for 100 credits a rarity step with no check and no kit; the party's own fitting is as before (the shared `modFitProblem` decides what fits). Crafting covers modifications and chassis by blueprint. Medical cabinets may hold cybernetics, stashes instruments, and a cantina's dance floor an instrument case.
  - Armour and shield properties (2026-10-21, 32d27be; `armorTraits`, `wornTraits`; tests `tests/items/armor.test.ts`): a Strength requirement not met costs 10 feet of speed and Cumbersome 5 (in a fight; the overland pace does not read them); Bulky, Obtrusive and the stealth flag are disadvantage on Stealth (and on hiding), Silent and Concealing advantage, a mix cancels (the stealth field too); Absorptive (and an enhanced shield's "damage you take is reduced by N") takes weapon damage down but leaves at least 1; Regulated rerolls a die's maximum; Rigid halves a Dexterity bonus to Dexterity saves; Reactive adds to AC against the next attack of whoever hit the wearer until the wearer's turn; Avoidant narrows a keen weapon's critical range; Insulated lifts a low d20 against ion and lightning; Imbalanced leaves the shield open after a critical hit (no reaction, no shield bonus against the next attack). Not played: Charging, Obscured, Impermeable, Reinforced, Spiked, Magnetic, Anchor, Lambent, Versatile, Interlocking, Responsive.
  - Graded gear and blueprints: graded adventuring gear (binders, camtonos, clandestine clothes, smugglepacks, jetpacks, rocket boots, remote detonators, restraining bolts, credit chips, datacrons, holocrons) is its plain item at the grade's numbers (`enhancedConsumableUse`); a blueprint item is learned over an hour with an Intelligence check by its rarity's DC (a miss waits 24 hours), giving a blueprint of that rarity.
  - On the figure (2026-10-21, a0345c2 and 282b914; `anchors.ts`, `gear.ts`, `models.tsx`; tests `tests/interiors/`): a saber's blade has a haze halo (`withHalo`); the second hand of a rifle, bowcaster or instrument closes on its `off_hand` socket where an arm reaches it (`offHandAt`); what is worn on the head (goggles, masks, earpieces, helmets: only when put on), neck, chest and fingers is fitted to the skull and build (`headFit`, `torsoFit`), gloves, boots (rocket boots with thrusters) and a bandolier's sash are drawn by code; a space, diving or hazard suit put on is drawn as one with its helmet, pack and fittings (`suitStyleOf`); a prosthesis or augmentation fitted by a surgeon is drawn (it has left the pack but is on the body); a droid companion carries its installed customizations (`moduleAnchor`: a sentry's dome on top, lifts under, the rest in a ring).
- Rests: a short rest spends one Hit Die (class die + Constitution; `characters.hit_dice_used`) and restores tech points; a long rest needs 1 hit point, restores hit points and power points and returns half the Hit Dice. No resting with enemies in the area.
- Dying: at 0 hit points the character rolls death saves on their turn (10+ succeeds, a 1 is two failures, a 20 gets up with 1 hit point); damage that carries past the hit point maximum kills outright. Three successes: stable, and the character comes to with 1 hit point (back where they came from, when that is known). Three failures: `conditions: ["Dead"]`.
- Powers: cost is the power's level + 1, at-will powers are free. `powerEffect` reads the power's text: an attack roll, a saving throw (half on a save where the text says so) or healing, with the dice it names; at-will dice grow at 5th, 11th and 17th level. Force powers use Wisdom (light), Charisma (dark) or the better (universal); tech powers Intelligence. Save DC 8 + proficiency + modifier. Of the 465 powers about 120 resolve this way; the rest are cast, paid for and described. Healing powers can be cast between fights. Stat blocks carry their ability modifiers for saves. Creation and level-up require the power a power builds on.
- Features that change the numbers (read from their text, so only where the books use the same words each time): natural armor and Unarmored Defense ("your AC is 12 + your Dexterity modifier"), unarmed strike dice (41 species, and the Monk's Martial Arts die), skill and saving throw proficiencies, hit points per level (Toughness, Durable), speed (Mobile, the Monk's Unarmored Movement), and a feat's +1 to an ability. A species' unarmed strike is saved in the character's `weapons` (`natural: true`) so Play needs no species data. Everything else a feature says is shown (character page, Features tab) but not automated.
- Levelling: Play shows a link when the experience is there; the character page then offers the level. Hit points use the average of the Hit Die plus Constitution (a higher Constitution counts for earlier levels too); proficiency, power points, powers known and the highest power level come from the class table; an archetype is chosen at 3rd level (saved in `subclass` by id); Ability Score Improvement levels are the class's own (Fighter 4, 6, 8, 12, 14, 16, 19) and give two ability points or a feat. A character made at 3rd level or higher can take its archetype from the same panel.
- Creation: the background's language and tool choices and the species' extra language are picked in the Background step. Class and background starting equipment are not handed out because the app uses the Player's Handbook's starting wealth variant, which replaces them.
- Quests: `worldQuests` reads three off a new world (clear the most dangerous hostile site on the starting planet, recover a locked or hidden cache, reach the nearest other planet). Objectives name a target (`defeat`, `loot` or `reach` an area) and `advanceQuests` ticks them as the world changes; a finished quest pays credits sized against Wealth by Level. Objectives without a target (older campaigns) are ticked by hand.
- Ships: the party's ships are in the campaign's `starships` (`PartyShip`: a `Ship` with `fuel` and the berth `areaId`). A ship standing in an area can be bought at its value (size stock cost + fitted equipment) or, when it belonged to the campaign's enemy and nobody is left to guard it, taken. Trips that need a ship burn fuel as Starships of the Galaxy says (one unit for a jump and one per day in hyperspace, one for realspace or atmosphere); a hyperlane needs a hyperdrive; fuel capacity and price by ship size come from the size tables; refuelling is at a settlement with a port.
- The campaign generator shows and saves the generated world (its quests, places and people). Only the pick of the enemy faction still comes from `lib/sw5e/campaignGenerator.ts`.
- The 3D map (2026-10-13 evening; `components/Map/LocalMap.tsx`, `GalaxyMap.tsx`, wired in `pages/GameBoard.tsx`). The user's rule: no 2D map anywhere; places, people and things are 3D and are used by tapping them.
  - Views, one inside the other: galaxy (systems and hyperlanes) > system (star, orbits, planets) > planet (a globe with its settlements and wild sites, ships in orbit above it) > settlement (buildings along streets) > building. `viewOf(world, areaId)` is the view that shows where the party is, and Play shows it unless another was opened. A tap picks a place out (a card with what it is, the way there, Look inside, and Travel when an exit leads to it); a double tap opens it (`useTaps`: two taps within half a second, mouse or finger). The strip at the top is the way back up, with "Back to the party". Looking is free and moves nobody; the one exception is a building one street away, which a double tap walks to, so that its surroundings can be walked in.
  - A building (`BuildingScene`) is drawn to the scale of its floor plan with 6 squares of ground round it. From the street it is closed: walls, roofs, the ways in (green), windows, a secret way once Search has found it (violet grate), a lit pad on the ground before each way in, and a signpost for each street and for the way out of the settlement. Inside, the room the party is in is opened up (walls cut to waist height) with its furniture, people, caches and ships; the other rooms stay closed.
  - On foot: a tap on the ground walks there (`route`: 8 directions, round what blocks, no cutting corners). A tap on a door, way out, secret way, signpost, cache, person or ship walks up to it first and then does it: through the door (`travelTo`, with the watch and locks as before), take the cache, or a card for the person (hostile: the watch and Bluff, Persuade, Intimidate, Fight) or the ship (buy, take as a prize). Where the character stands is `usePartySpot` (the building's grid): going through a door they come out just beyond it, a fight starts where they stand and leaves them where it ended. Out of doors the camera follows the character.
  - Secret ways from the street: Search (HUD) finds them as before; also, when the character stops within two squares of one, their passive Wisdom (Perception) (10 + modifier, + proficiency if trained; Player's Handbook) is set against its DC, and if it is enough the way is noticed. Found, it is a violet grate in the outside wall with a lit pad before it: a tap walks there and slips in unnoticed.
  - A fight (`FightScene`) is on the same floor: everyone on their square with a hit point bar, the squares the character can reach lit green, a tap on one moves, a tap on an enemy picks it out; the panel keeps the buttons and the log. An arena that is open ground (`Arena.open`) has no walls.
  - Models (2026-10-18, asked by the user: better ships, droids, characters, vehicles, creatures and buildings, with curves). They are in `components/Map/models.tsx`, still built in code with nothing downloaded: a `Kit` of curved solids (turned profiles `lathe`, tubes bent along a curve and tapering `tube`, outlines pushed out with softened edges `extrude`, rounded boxes, noise-shaped lumps `blob` for rocks and bodies) baked by `useModel` into one mesh per finish (matte, sheen for metal and armor, glow, glass, haze for force fields and holograms, cloth seen from both sides), shared by key and let go a minute after the last user leaves. A person is two or three draw calls however many parts. `Figure` picks the model: people take their look from the species data, fetched once (`useSpecies`, `lookOf`: skin, hair and eye colours picked per person, height from the average height and roll, and the distinctions read for lekku, montrals and head-tails, horns, tusks, snouts, trunks, beaks, muzzles, fur, scales, carapace, big or side eyes, eye stalks, antennae, ears, head and face tentacles, wings, four arms, tails, cone or domed skulls, long necks, hunches, rebreathers, wrappings, bone masks; Jawas are hooded) and their clothes and gear from their role or stat block (`outfitOf`: stormtroopers, scouts, death troopers, navy pilots, officers, royal guards, inquisitors, Sith and Jedi, Tuskens, Wookiees, Jawas, bounty hunters, mercenaries, guards, bartenders, medics, mechanics, pilots, smugglers, merchants, nobles, captives...). The character's figure takes their species and, for a Force user, a lit blade (red on the dark side): Play sets `usePartyLook`. Droids by series (`droidKind`: R2, R5/C1, BB, protocol, B1, B2, BX, tactical, destroyer, probes and seekers, gonk, mouse, pit, medical, IG/HK assassins, KX security, spider, junk, service), war machines by name (`constructKind`: AT-ST, AT-RT, AT-AT, AT-TE, tanks, turrets, the 74-Z with its rider, brain walker, hailfire, vulture), creatures by body plan (`creaturePlan`: the setting's beasts by name, others by type and size). Furniture: every name in `ROOM_PLANS` and the ship decks, with curves (round tables and stools, a stadium bar counter, U-shaped booths, a bandstand's shell, turned pillars, canvas awnings, bucket seats facing the bow, consoles that curve into a slanted desk). Ships: X-wing, shuttle, YT freighter and corvette shapes, and for the Empire, the First Order and the Sith a TIE, a winged shuttle and a wedge (`ShipBody`; the flying games and space fights use them too). Speeders: a bike with vanes, an open landspeeder, an airspeeder. Buildings and settlements on the settlement and planet views: domes, a sunken round bay, canvas stalls, a bunker, a palace with towers, barrel vaults, spires. `/dev/models` (`pages/ModelGallery.tsx`, not linked from the game) lays every model out; `?section=people|roles|player|droids|beasts|furniture|ships|places&cam=x,y,z&look=x,y,z`.
  - Curves in buildings (2026-10-18; `styleOf`, `RoomBlock`, `CornerWall`, `RoundedFloor`, `DomeRoof`, `Opening` in models.tsx; `radiiOf` in `BuildingScene`). The rooms keep their squares; what is drawn follows the world's style: rounded adobe with arches and domes in the desert (corners rounded 1.4 squares), hulls with portholes on stations, ocean and gas giant platforms and starships (0.85), igloo-like on ice, softer corners in forests and jungles, hard corners in cities (0.3), bunkers on volcanic and barren worlds. Only an outside corner is rounded: one that another room touches at the same height stays square, and so does one with a door or window in the wall beside it (cut back to the nearest one). The closed room is an extruded rounded outline with a band under the roof, a roof slab and a parapet that follow the curve; the room the party is in curves at the same corners (at most 1.1 squares, so the corner square stays one to stand on) with a rounded floor; the way-in room has a turned dome; doors and windows are arched, rounded or round by style. Measured in headless Chromium: frame rates within noise of the build before (about 1 frame a second in the main bar and 2 on the street under software rendering, either build), fewer draw calls, more triangles.
  - The character's companions follow a step or two behind (`Follower`); speeders parked at a settlement stand in the street before a building and those kept at a base by its door (`SpeederModel`); a disabled droid lies where it fell and a captive stands in their bonds. Not done: walking between buildings on one continuous street map, climbing through windows, furniture that does something (beyond being moved by the Force), keyboard movement.
  - `/map` is the atlas of a campaign's world on the same 3D map (`?campaign=<id>`), with `reveal`: any room can be opened by tapping it. Without such a campaign `/map` shows the old stock viewers.

- Eras and the law (2026-10-14; `ERAS`, `eraOf`, `lawAt` in shared/logic). Setting, not rules (the books have no era table): Old Republic, Clone Wars, Galactic Civil War (the default, and what worlds made before eras are), New Republic, First Order. An era names the authority, the enemies a campaign can have (the generator picks one of them unless one is named; this replaces the old picker's choice when it does not fit) and a law level per region: 0 lawless, 1 frontier, 2 policed, 3 strict. Factions in the setting seeds are renamed for the era (the Empire is the Republic in the Clone Wars, and so on). A planet held by a crime syndicate or the Tusken is lawless; one held by the authority at least policed. Chosen on the campaign generator and in the world options of the campaign page; saved in `World.era` and the campaign's `setting.era`. The HUD shows the clock, the era and the law where the party is.
- Time (2026-10-14; `tripMinutes`, `clockText`, `daylight` in shared/logic; `useClock` in LocalMap.tsx; the clock in Play). One clock per campaign in `campaigns.time_elapsed` (`{ total }` minutes; play begins at 08:00 on day 1), saved on its own so that the world is not sent each time. On the map one game minute passes each real second, except in a fight, when the character is down, or with the tab hidden; it is saved every game hour and when the tab is hidden. A move takes the time its path gives (hyperspace hours times the hyperdrive class of the party's ship), a short rest an hour, a long rest eight. Wait 1 h, Until evening (18:00) and Until morning (07:00) are next to the rests, not with enemies about. The surface scenes (a building and its street, a settlement) are lit by the hour: blue sky by day, warm dawn and dusk, dim blue moonlight at night.
- Trade (2026-10-14; "Trade" in shared/logic: `WARES`, `waresOf`, `stockOf`, `priceOf`, `offerFor`, `attitudeOf`, `contrabandFrom`, `isOpen`; the panel in a trader's card in Play, `tradePanel`). Who sells: bartenders (drinks), merchants (a line of goods each, `Occupant.trade`: general goods, arms and armor, tools and kits, medical supplies, music and games), mechanics (tools and kits), medics (medical supplies), dockmasters (spacer's supplies), and the black market: a fence in every cantina's back room (code `X`), smugglers, and in lawless markets one stall in three. Only items of the equipment data, at their cost moved by: region (Core -8% to Unknown Regions +35%), settlement (city -5%, outpost +10%, wilds +20%), what the planet needs (desert and ice worlds pay more for life support, jungles for medicine, cities less for comms), goods named as from this planet (-40%), a daily drift of up to 10%, the last one in stock (+15%), each one the party bought there today (+5%), the black market's risk premium on contraband (+60% per law level), the trader's attitude (5% a step) and a haggle (10%). Stock is picked daily per trader (the same day gives the same stock), up to what the settlement can carry (outposts hold nothing over 1,500 cr); what the party bought is gone, what it sold is for sale again. Selling: half the cost (Player's Handbook "Selling Treasure"), moved the same way, each one sold there today fetching 10% less; a trader buys only what they sell, the black market anything (contraband at 80% of cost, the rest at 30%: Wretched Hives' shady buyer). Enhanced items are not bought over a counter (Wretched Hives: a downtime activity).
  - Reputation, by the trader (`attitudeOf`, -4 to +4, shown with the reasons): renown with the faction that holds the place (a third of it, at most 2 either way; the campaign's enemy starts at -3), the same species (+1), the planet's own people or the species' homeworld (+1), alignment against the faction's leaning (Empire, Sith, Hutts, Black Sun dark; Republic, Rebels, Resistance light: +1 or -1), for the black market chaotic +1 and lawful -1, and familiarity (3 dealings +1, 10 +2). Renown (Wretched Hives): a won fight costs 1 with the side that lost it and gains 1 with whoever holds the place; a finished quest gains 2 with them and costs 1 with the campaign's enemy. Kept in `campaigns.faction_influence` ({ renown, dealings }); shown under Standing on the Quests tab.
  - Haggling: Charisma (Persuasion), once a day per trader, with advantage and expertise read from the character's features (`hagglingEdge` in characterCalculation.ts: the Haggler feat, Business Savvy). Made: 10% better prices today; missed by 5 or more: 5% worse.
  - Today's dealings in `campaigns.available_resources` ({ day, traders: { id: { bought, sold, haggle } } }), cleared when the day changes.
- Routines (2026-10-14; "Routines" in shared/logic: `settleRoutines`, `whereAt`, `shipAt`, `withPresence`). Setting, not rules. When a settlement is built its people get a home (up to four in each apartment room; the rest live somewhere in the settlement, off the map) and a day: staff keep their building's hours (cantina 10:00 to 04:00, market 07:00 to 19:00, ...; where it never closes a 06:00 to 22:00 shift), patrons work somewhere in the settlement and drink in the evening (one in four from midday), shoppers do the market in the morning and afternoon, residents work and some go out, musicians play 17:00 to 03:00, the fence keeps the back room 18:00 to 04:00; about one in seven takes a day out to another town on the planet by speeder (out at 07:00, back by 21:00). Ships in friendly ports are flown by a pilot for hire, smuggler or bounty hunter of the settlement on a voyage between their berth and one to three ports (other settlements, planets of the system, systems a jump away), with a day or so in each and the legs' travel times; their pilot is about only while the ship is in. Where anyone is comes from the clock (nothing is moved in the saved world): `withPresence(world, minutes)` gives the world as it is then, with `moving` for those on the way (15 minutes between places in a settlement). Play shows that world on the map and in the HUD and cards; edits go to the saved world. A ship bought while visiting is taken from its home berth.
  - On the map: someone who arrives while the party is in a room comes in at a door and goes to a free seat or their counter; someone whose day takes them elsewhere walks out by a door. Outside a building, people walk between its door and the signpost for where they are going (or pass between two other places). The settlement view shows people walking between buildings; the planet view speeders between towns and ships to and from orbit; the system view ships between planets and to the jump point; the galaxy view ships in hyperspace, with their names.
  - Worlds made before (life below 3) get routines and voyages for every built settlement when Play opens them (`livenWorld`), and merchants their lines of goods and cantinas their fences (life 2).
  - Not done: people do not ride along in the party's view of a speeder or ship; nobody is drawn inside a ship; travellers do not arrive on the ships (hangars do show ships coming and going: see "Streets, hangars and encounters"); schedules do not change with the day of the week.
- People talking among themselves (2026-11-02; house rule, flavour: nothing rolls). Two people of a room or two passers-by stop and talk, a line at a time made by the local model and spoken in their own voices, by their natures (the nemesis personalities), what they are to each other (kin, friends, grudges by the nemesis tiers) and what they know of the party (regard, nemesis kin, notoriety and news). See "The model makes their talk ..." and "Their own voices, natures and bonds".
- The pit's calendar, tickets and the bubble under the sea (2026-11-02; house rules, see "The pit's weekend card, bets by ticket, staff at their posts, underwater bubbles"): days in weeks of seven, day 1 a Monday, the pit fights only on days 6 and 7 of a week, five bouts a day two hours apart from noon, bets as tickets collected after the bout; talk of the headline bouts fades over three days; a place under the sea is one bubble of air (no drowning inside it); a quest or job on the sea floor is offered to all but warns in red that sub-aquatic gear is required unless the species breathes water.
- Gear and tools (2026-10-15; `gearUse`, `toolFor`, `toolTrained`, `boonOf` and the gambling tables in shared/logic "Gear, tools, companions and ground transport"; the Inventory tab, the combat panel's Items, rests, in `pages/GameBoard.tsx`). What an item does is read from its description and category; each carried item that does something has a Use button (a Tool, Game or Play button for kits, gaming sets and instruments), with its text and the uses left.
  - Healing: Medpac (a hit die + Constitution for a person or beast, the most it can give after a Biochemist's kit has prepared it), Repair kit (3 uses, droids and constructs), Traumakit (5, stabilizes the fallen, 1 hit point), Emergency battery (10, a droid), Antitoxkit (5 charges, cures Poisoned or gives advantage against poison for an hour). Hit dice for creatures with no class are by size (5th edition: d4 Tiny to d20 Gargantuan). Charges are kept in the character's `playState.charges` for the kit that is open.
  - Explosives: grenades and thermal detonators are thrown in a fight (Items in the combat panel; within 30 ft. + Strength modifier x 5; everyone within the radius saves, friends too; damage and conditions parsed from the text: Blinded, Deafened, Stunned, Unconscious, Frightened, Shocked, Poisoned, Prone for 10 rounds with a repeat save). Any explosive can blow a locked door ("Blow it": the cheapest carried; it sets off the building's alarm). Mines and charges are not set as traps in a fight (not done).
  - Forcing a door (house rule): Strength against the lock's DC + 5, noisy; a Portable ram gives +4 and advantage with a helper; a Fusion cutter takes ten minutes per 5 of DC and no check. Security and Computer spikes reroll a failed lock check by themselves (the kit has to match). Locks and caches count the kit's proficiency only when the character is trained with it, not for merely carrying it.
  - Substances (Wretched Hives, "Substances": the 8 spices and 8 alcoholic beverages): a high of +d3 on checks, attacks and saves with one ability (Karrak: 2d3 temporary hit points each turn; Muon gold and Yaladai: points), then a Wisdom save against a low of 10 minutes (-d3, reversed boosts), then a Constitution save against addiction; addiction levels 1 to 6 (lows doubled, highs halved, lows quadrupled, ...); a day without one and a long rest allows a Wisdom save to lower it; taking another while high or low is an addiction save at once. House rule: out of a fight the high waits (up to ten minutes) for the next ability check or fight, and ends with it, since the map clock would otherwise run it out unused. State in `playState.substance`, `addiction`. A fight takes a minute for each ten rounds (house rule).
  - Poison: coats a vibroweapon or wrist launcher; the next hit adds its dice (Constitution save for half); dries at the next rest.
  - Tools (Wretched Hives, "Tool Proficiencies"): the Tool button lists the tool's skills, special use and sample activities with a Try button each (Intelligence, proficiency when trained with the tool, advantage when also trained in one of its skills: the book says "consider allowing", so house rule). A special use can be worked once at a rest (the select under the rest buttons; DC 15): Armormech (a die for AC against one attack), Armstech and Artificer (a die on one attack), Munitions, Archaeologist, Demolitions (a die on one damage roll), Scavenging (extra damage equal to the Intelligence modifier), Audiotech (a die on one Performance check), Biochemist (the next medpac gives the most it can), Chef (a short rest: +1 hit point per Hit Die spent for everyone; a long rest: 2 more Hit Dice; needs rations), Astrotech (droids regain a die more on a short rest), Bioanalysis (cures a poison or disease). The die is a d(proficiency bonus). What is prepared lasts until the next rest and shows under the character's credits; others of the book's special uses are only listed. Mechanic's kit mends a wrecked speeder (DC 15, an hour).
  - Gambling (Wretched Hives, "Gambling (Uptime)"): a gaming set and a stake of 20 to 2,000 credits; five rounds against someone at a table (a contest where bluffing beats the game, the game beats reading and reading beats bluffing, each by advantage; opponent +2, a gambler +4: house rule) or against the house (Intelligence DC 20 each); 0 to 5 wins pay by the book's table (a debt at 0 wins, kept in `playState.debt`); each win has a one in ten chance of one of the book's six complications (shown, not enforced).
  - Instruments (house rule; the book gives sample DCs and no tips): "Play for the room" is Charisma (Performance) against 12 in a cantina and 15 elsewhere, an hour; the audience (up to 12) tips d4 each, doubled 5 over and tripled 10 over; 10 over earns 1 renown with whoever holds the place. Untrained players are allowed.
  - Not done from this list: breaching charges set in a fight. (Environment gear, mines, binders, grappling hooks and translators are played now: see "Gear in play" above; what is left is in ITEM_TODO.md.)
- Enhanced items, stimpacs and augmentation (2026-10-15; "Enhanced items in play" at the end of `shared/logic/index.ts`: `parseItemEffects`, `parseTimedEffect`, `itemFactsOf`, `scoresWith`, `augmentPlan`; `GET /api/sw5e/itemFacts`; `withItemEffects`, `activeEffects` in `characterCalculation.ts`; the Inventory tab and the medic's card in `pages/GameBoard.tsx`; `e2e/items.mjs`). Each enhanced item's text is read once on the server into facts (386 of the 1,918 have something the game applies); Play wraps the character in `withItemEffects`, so ability scores, armor class, weapons and the combat kit all see the items, and the sheet keeps its own numbers (saves send only what they change).
  - Attunement (book): 133 items need it; slots = proficiency bonus; standard, premium, prototype 1 slot, advanced, legendary, artifact 2; without it the item gives only what the plain item does. House rule: attuning (and breaking) takes an hour of the clock and is not a rest.
  - Read from the text: +N attack and damage rolls of the weapon itself (and of unarmed strikes for handwraps), +N AC (armor, shield, robe), "AC becomes 13 + Dexterity modifier" unarmored, "score becomes N" (no effect when higher), "increases by N to a maximum of M" (packages raise the maximum too), and the "Side Effect: one ability score falls by 1" (house rule: the GM's choice is one of the other abilities at random, chosen when attuned or fitted and kept in `playState.sides`). Enhanced weapons, armor and shields are the plain kind named in their subtype with their bonus; ones that name no kind ("any blaster") are not applied. Not applied, only shown: everything else in the texts (flying speeds, powers cast from items, advantage riders, force and tech attack bonuses of focuses and wristpads, grenade launchers).
  - Consumables: stimpac (one at a time, an hour, ability score +N to a maximum), adrenal (speed, temporary hit points; a minute), barrier (its hit points as temporary hit points), collimator (+N attack and damage to a wielded weapon of its kind, a minute). `playState.buffs`. House rules: what lasts a minute, and a barrier, waits for the next fight and ends with it; used outside a fight only; a barrier is temporary hit points against any damage type (the book's types are not tracked). Not done: using them in a fight, on companions, enhanced grenades, medpacs, poisons and substances (they have no entry in the equipment data, so `gearUse` does not see them), cartridges, darts, flechettes.
  - Augmentation (book: Cybernetic Augmentations, Prosthetics): at a medic's card ("Surgery"); 8 hours for a Medium patient at 100 credits an hour (the clock moves), a check against the rarity's DC (10 to 30); a failed enhancement is a level of exhaustion, a failed replacement the loss of the part for as long as the work took (shown, not enforced); removal takes half the time and cost and a failure destroys the augmentation. A body supports as many augmentations as the proficiency bonus; the ten prostheses (bought from medics as Medical gear, treated as Standard) do not count against that but count towards the side-effect table: 2 augmentations, disadvantage on saves against ion (shown, not enforced); 4, vulnerable to ion (applied in a fight); 6, counts as a droid (shown). Body parts: two arms, two legs, two eyes, two ears, one face, torso and throat; a replacement needs its parts free. House rules: the surgeon rolls d20 + 5 (the books give no NPC modifier), and a replacement that says "using only this arm" (Brawny Arm, Celerity Legs) applies its score everywhere.
  - Modifiable items (2026-10-15; `modKindOf`, `RARITY_ORDER`, `augmentSlots`, `Fitted`, `crystalFor` in shared/logic; `installMod`, `upgradeChassis`, `modPanel` in GameBoard; `playState.fitted`). The setting is the book's "Variant: Common Modifications": every plain weapon (blaster, vibroweapon, lightweapon), armor, shield, clothing, wristpad and focus generator is a standard chassis with 4 slots; a modification must be of its kind (the item's `subtype`) and not above the chassis's rarity; augments need augment slots (prototype and advanced 1, legendary and artifact 2) and not two of one kind. A fitting takes an hour and an Intelligence check with the item's tool (the tools data's `worksOn`) against the rarity's DC; a failure waits a day (book). House rules: untrained with the tool, DC +5 (the book says only the proficient may); a chassis item (Premium to Artifact "Blaster Chassis" and the like) upgrades a plain item one rarity step at 8 hours and a check, standing for the rare material, a 1 on a failure destroys a modification; a modified item needs attunement (1 slot, 2 from advanced up). What is applied: +N attack and damage, +N AC, ability scores, and a property a modification "gains" is added to the weapon's properties (so reach and the like work in a fight); shown only: focus save DC and force/tech attack bonuses, extra damage dice, everything else in the texts. Color crystals (lightweapons, Force-sensitive characters): the book's check and d100 table, the color rolled or any below it; cosmetic.
  - Droid customizations (`customize`, `droidWith`, `droidPanel`; `Companion.custom`, `motor`, `stock`): a droid companion's card has Customize; its motor supports 2, upgraded to 6 for 2,000, 10,000, 50,000 and 200,000 credits (times the size modifier); fitting takes 8 hours (times the size modifier) and a check with astrotech's implements. Applied: plating's "base AC becomes N", "13 + Dexterity" formulas and speed (worked from the droid's stock numbers, so removal restores them); the powers, protocols and parts that need rules the companions do not have are shown only.
  - Information gear (all house rules; the books give these no numbers; `gearUse` kinds `data` and `call`, `readData`, `callContact`, the code cylinder in `unlock`): a credit chip is cracked with a slicer's kit (DC 15, +5 without one; the book's DC) for 2d6 x 25 x (level tier) credits; a datacard is read on a datapad, wristpad or holocomm (DC 12) and holds lore of a place (kept in the downtime state, spent there), a blueprint (rarity by level, from the enhanced items) or credits; a datacron is cracked like a chip (DC 15); a holocron answers only the Force-sensitive (Wisdom DC 15) with lore or a blueprint; each read takes ten minutes and the card is spent. A commlink or holocomm calls an allied contact (made in Downtime) for a tip about a place near them, once a day each. A code cylinder opens a lock of DC 15 or less at once. Found blueprints now feed "From a blueprint" in Downtime.
  - Not done: unarmed attack bonus of augmentations, the force/tech save DC and attack bonuses of focuses and modifications, adding augment slots with tinker's implements, personal translators and vocoders (languages are not played), a fitted modification surviving a trade of the item, droid powers and protocols that cast, a datapad as a store of notes.
- Companions (2026-10-15; `Companion`, `companionFrom`, `canHire`, `hireFee`, `wageFor`, `FREEING`, `gentleDC` in shared/logic; `companionData.party` on the character; at most 4 (house rule); cards and actions in `pages/GameBoard.tsx`). Each comes from a stat block, so fights with the numbers printed there, on the character's side (`Combatant.ally`, `onPartySide` in `useCombat`): they roll initiative, act by themselves (move in and attack), are hurt and healed, and a victory with the character down leaves them 1 hit point. After a fight each fallen person or beast makes death saving throws at once (stable at 0, or dead and gone); a droid is only powered down. A short rest tends each (a hit die); a long rest restores them.
  - Hiring (house rule, no wages in the books): mercenaries, bounty hunters, smugglers and pilots for hire have stat blocks (CR up to about half the level + 1: `TRADE_STATS`); a signing fee of five days' wages, 5% less for each step of goodwill (`attitudeOf`), then a wage each day (`wageFor`: a tenth of the stat block's experience, at least 10) taken when the day changes; a hireling not paid leaves.
  - Freeing captives: people held in shackles (Security kit DC 15 or Strength DC 20: Binders), a cell (Security kit DC 15) or a slave collar (Slicer's kit DC 20), found in detention blocks, hideouts' holding cells and camps' slave pens (room code `P`); each try takes ten minutes and is done under the guards' noses (a Stealth check where hostiles are in the room). Freed, they can come along (a CR 1/8 to 1/2 stat block) or be sent home for 25 XP x level (house rule; a point of renown lost with whoever held the place).
  - Droids: a droid that falls in a fight is powered down where it fell (`Occupant.disabled`, wounds 0), not destroyed, and keeps its gear dropped as before. Stabilize it (Emergency battery, or Intelligence (Technology) DC 10), mend it (Repair kit), then fit a Restraining bolt (1 minute; it saves against the item's DC 14 with advantage, the book says; a save overloads the bolt and the droid wakes hostile) or rewrite its routines (Slicer's kit, DC 20 + 2 from CR 5; failure by 5 wakes it hostile). Bolted or reprogrammed, it is a companion.
  - Beasts: Wisdom (Animal Handling) against 10 + CR (house rule; 2 less when hurt), CR up to level + 2, an hour; rations in hand give advantage; a miss by 5 or more and it attacks. A beast of size Large or more, or one of the book's mounts (Tauntaun, Kaadu, Rancor, Sleen, Varactyl, Bantha, Dewback: "other mounts are rare ... acquiring such a mount is often an adventure"), can be ridden. Mounts are also sold by one stall in two of the markets of towns and outposts on worlds that are not paved over (`trade: "mounts"`: Tauntaun, Kaadu, Dewback, Varactyl, Bantha, Rancor; not Sleen, which has no stat block).
- Ground transport (2026-10-15; `groundRides`, `kmOf`, `journeyMinutes`, `PartyVehicle`, `Vehicle` in shared/logic; the campaign's `vehicles`; `ridesFor` in GameBoard). Routes between settlements give their length in km. The rides: on foot at the Player's Handbook normal pace (3 mph; 8 hours of travel a day, the other 16 resting: `journeyMinutes`), riding a mount at its speed in feet / 10 mph (the same scale), a speeder of the party's parked at the settlement at the Vehicles table's mph (Airspeeder 750, Landspeeder 220, Speederbike 300; 74-Z Speeder Bike is a speederbike), or a hired ride in a city, town or station (house rule, the books price no fares: 5 credits + 1 per 4 km, at landspeeder speed). Seats are a house rule (bike 2, airspeeder 4, landspeeder 6); the party is the character and every companion that is not a beast. A tap on the map takes the quickest free ride; the exits list shows each ride with its time. The speeder goes along and is parked at the other end. Routes were "N h by speeder" for nothing before, at 80 km/h.
  - Getting one: bought from the shopfront of a city's or town's workshop or of a station's (`trade: "vehicles"`, in the markets' price rules with the caps by settlement: cities and stations stock them, towns, with a cap of 4,000 credits, do not), taken as a prize from an enemy base (camps, hideouts, garrisons and command posts keep a speeder bike one time in two, kept on the building: `Area.vehicles`), or mended from a wreck (crash sites; Mechanic's kit, DC 15). A tap on one on the street gives its card.
- Beasts (2026-10-26, asked by the user: "beasts should NOT be party members, they should follow and there should be a mechanic to be able to ride/mount/dismount & load items"; `companionData.beasts` and `companionData.riding` on the character, `BEAST_LIMIT`, `carryingCapacity` in shared/logic; the Beasts list of the Party tab, the beast's card, `mountBeast`/`dismount`/`loadOnto`/`unloadFrom` in GameBoard). A gentled, bought or test-menu beast goes to the beasts, not the companions; a beast saved among the companions before is moved over on load. House rules: four beasts at most; they follow on the map but keep out of fights (a rider fights on foot and is back in the saddle afterwards); one big enough to ride is mounted and dismounted from its card or the list, the rider is drawn on its back and a journey on the roads takes its ride by default.
