Any companion or beast carries what is loaded onto it from the Pack tab ("Load onto"), up to the mount table's capacity where the book gives one, else Strength (10 + twice its Strength save) x 15 doubled for each size above Medium, a mount as one size larger; what it carries is not to hand until taken back, and comes back to the pack when it is dismissed or let go. Later the same day (the user's screenshot: the rider hovered over the bantha and Dismount did nothing): `dismount` passed `undefined` to `saveBeasts`, whose parameter default (the rider) then kept the character in the saddle, now `null`; the rider is placed by the hips (`RIDER_HIPS`), not the feet, on the beast's back and has no ring of their own; the bantha's coat is lighter (`#7a5c40`).
- Carrying, the weight fix (2026-10-26, the user: "the encumbrance is not wired properly - it currently ignores the weight of the player inventory"). Two faults: Play fetched the equipment data without its `weight` field, so every item weighed 0 to the load, the hiding places and storage; and `otherCarriers` let every companion take 150 lb and every mount 300 lb (or its capacity) off the top without being asked. Now the pack weighs what the book says, only a hovercart pushed along takes its 2,000 lb, and the rest is carried by companions and beasts only when loaded onto them.
- Bases (2026-10-26, asked by the user: "start with deploying tents at a location/claiming the hideout of defeated enemies, set down chests and other storage, add items and have it persist; ensure the implementations are easily extendible"; `shared/logic/bases.ts`, a leaf module re-exported by index.ts; `claimableSite` in index.ts; the Base panel of the Party tab and the piece card in GameBoard; placement in LocalMap (`usePlacing`, MapAction "place" and "piece"); the models are `base_*` in `siteAssets.tsx` (`BasePieceModel`); tests `tests/items/bases.test.ts`, browser `e2e/bases.mjs`). A base lives in the world (`world.bases`), one to a building: a camp is made by setting something down in front of or inside a building (a tent, an emergency shelter, a chest, a crate or a locker from the pack: `pieceTypeOf`), a site by claiming a building of a hostile kind (hideout, camp, cave, ruin, wreck, sewer) once none of its holders stands. Pieces take squares of their own (they stand in the way like furniture; nobody may be on them), a tap opens one. House rules: a tent goes up outdoors only (10 min), an emergency shelter anywhere (1 min), storage anywhere; a chest holds the book's 300 lb, a crate 200 lb (the book gives it in cubic feet); a pitched shelter makes a long rest there a sheltered one (camping); taking a piece down gives back the item and what was kept in it; an empty camp is struck, a claimed site stays the party's. To extend it: add a role to `PieceRole` and a type to `ITEM_PIECES` (or a type not made from an item), its look to the library, and read the role where it matters (`piecesWith`). A shelter's card (a tent, an emergency shelter) offers the rests and waits of the Here tab (the user: "clicking the tent should offer rest/wait options"); the tent's model was rebuilt so that its sheets reach from the ridge pole to the ground (they were 0.9 long on a 1.47 slope and hung in the air).
- Carts (2026-10-26, the user: "The hover cart needs a physical entity that players or party members can push/pull"; `Cart`, `cartCapacity`, `cartHandler`, `isCartItem` in shared/logic; `companionData.carts`, the Carts panel of the Party tab, the cart card, `loadCart`/`handleCart`/`parkCart`/`stowCart` in GameBoard; `HandledCarts`, parked carts and `CartModel`/`cart_hover` in LocalMap and siteAssets). A hovercart that comes into the pack is taken out and set on the map, pulled behind the character. House rules: someone of the party has to push it (it floats ahead of them, the push bar toward them) or pull it (behind them); a beast only pulls, in harness; it holds what is loaded into it from the Pack tab ("Load onto"), up to the book's 2,000 lb, and that does not count toward anyone's load; it goes along with whoever handles it, on any ride; parked, it stays on its square with what is in it until someone takes it again (from its card or the panel); a companion who leaves hands it back to the character. "Pack it away" puts it back in the pack folded (150 lb, the book's weight; `playState.foldedCarts` keeps it from being set out again) with what it held, so that it can be sold; the panel sets a folded one out again. Before this, a hovercart in the pack silently added 2,000 lb to the character's capacity (`otherCarriers`, now 0).
- Speeder makes (2026-10-26, the user: "extend the vehicles to include other types of star wars machines", from the films and series; `VEHICLE_MAKES`, `makeOf`, `makesSold`, `makeFor` in shared/logic; `Vehicle.look`; `buildMake` in models.tsx; gallery `/dev/models?section=ships`). House rule: the Vehicles table prices three classes; each make is a name and a look of a class at the class's price and speed, with seats of its own: 74-Z speeder bike, swoop bike, BARC speeder, 614-AvA speeder bike (bikes); X-34, V-35 Courier, flash speeder, Bantha-II cargo skiff, Imperial troop transport (landspeeders); T-47 snowspeeder, Storm IV cloud car, Koro-2, XJ-6 (airspeeders). Some are found only on some worlds (a snowspeeder on ice, temperate and barren worlds; a cloud car over gas giants, oceans and cities; a skiff in deserts, barren and volcanic lands; the XJ-6 in cities); the military ones (74-Z, BARC, 614-AvA, troop transport) are never sold, only taken as prizes from garrisons and command posts. Shops sell the makes of their world; parked civilian rides, dealers' stock and wrecks get a make picked from a hash of the vehicle's id, so a seed's world draws the same dice as before. Walkers and tanks (AT-ST, AT-AT, AAT ...) stay enemies with their stat blocks, not rides.
- Spacing (2026-10-26, the user: "followers and party members should be at least one square away from the party leader when stationary; npcs, droids, party members should NEVER share the same square when stationary"; `claims`, `followSpot` in LocalMap). The leader, the companions and beasts following, the people of a room or street and the folk of an encounter claim the square they stand on or are walking to. A follower stands at least two squares from the leader (one clear square between) where the room allows, else next to them, never on a claimed square, and chooses again when the leader has moved on two squares or comes up to it; someone of the room standing where the party stops steps aside within half a second. The party may still walk through people (only furniture stops a walk).
- Hangars and the party's ships (2026-10-15). Ships were drawn outside a building from the room's plan whether or not they were in, and inside from who was in; ships with a pilot are away most of the time, so hangars looked full from the street and empty within. Now both views draw what is there and an empty berth is a painted berth with the ship's name and "away on a voyage" (a tap says so). A ship stays in its own berth most of the time (`Voyage.homeStay`, 4 to 9 days, then the other ports for 12 to 36 hours: before it was equal shares); the party's ships stand in the berths of the port they are at (`withPartyShips`: a berth left empty by a ship bought or taken from it) and say "Yours".
- Fight depth (2026-10-15; `useCombat.ts`, `PlayCombat.tsx`, `characterCalculation.ts` "Features in a fight", `shared/logic` `statProfile`). A turn has what the Player's Handbook gives it: an action (the Attack action makes as many attacks as Extra Attack allows, one tap each; powers, items and the standard actions are the action too), a bonus action, a reaction, movement. The turn ends by itself when nothing is left that the app can do for the character ("end turn by itself" box; a bonus action the app can resolve, such as Second Wind, holds it open, so tap End turn).
  - Standard actions: Dash (extra movement), Disengage (no opportunity attacks), Dodge (attacks have disadvantage until the next turn), Hide (Stealth against the enemies' passive Wisdom), Help and Guard (for a companion). Moving out of a hostile creature's reach gives it an opportunity attack (its reaction), for every combatant, unless the mover Disengaged. Cover: one thing in the way is half cover, +3 (the book's number; it was +2).
  - Class, archetype, species and feat features are parsed from their text into `CombatFeature`s: when they are used (action, bonus action, reaction, free), their uses before a rest (from the text and the class table's columns), their cost (force, tech or focus points) and what they do where the wording is regular: Extra Attack, Action Surge, a rage (the Rage Damage column; resistances), heal (Second Wind, Lend Aid), standard actions as a bonus action (Cunning Action), a bonus attack after the Attack action or Dodge or a cast power (Martial Arts, Battle Readiness, Combat Tech), extra damage on a hit (Sneak Attack with the column's dice; Force-Empowered Strikes, points chosen before the hit; Ranger's Quarry; Double Strike), reactions that halve or cut damage or add to Armor Class (Uncanny Dodge, Deflect Missiles, Force Shield, Deflection: offered as a prompt when a hit lands), Brutal Critical, Evasion, Reckless Attack, Danger Sense. Anything else with a use is tracked and described, with a button that spends the use and shows its dice to apply by hand ("you apply it"). `npx tsx` over `combatFeatures` lists what each class gets.
  - Uses spent are kept in `playState.featureUses` (keyed by the feature's pool, "Source:Name"; the focus points under `focus`); a short rest gives back those that recharge on one, a long rest all of them. Force Recovery is given at the end of a short rest. Healing features can be used between fights from the rest buttons.
  - Fighting styles and maneuvers are chosen on the character page ("Combat options": how many comes from the Fighting Style features and the class table's Maneuvers Known; saved in `characters.fighterManeuvers` as `{ styles, maneuvers }`). Styles are parsed like features (no disadvantage next to an enemy, the modifier on the off hand, bonus action Dash/Disengage/Help...), plus the minimum roll threshold (the d4..d20 table is derived into lookups.json). Maneuvers use the superiority dice of the class table (or the one die of a Superiority Style) as a pool; the ones that add the die to the attack roll or to damage on a hit (with the saving throw and condition their text names, DC 8 + proficiency + the maneuver ability) are chosen before an attack in the fight panel; Parry reduces damage as a reaction; healing and temporary hit point maneuvers are used as actions. Riposte, Return Fire and the skill maneuvers are tracked only.
  - Weapon properties read: keen (critical range), brutal (extra critical dice), vicious, heavy; light with a second light weapon gives the two-weapon bonus attack (no modifier unless negative, or a style). Not done: reload and ammunition, burst, rapid, auto, penetrating, dire, piercing, thrown, versatile two-hand damage.
  - Enemies: every stat block's attacks, Multiattack ("makes two attacks: one with its bite and one with its claws", "attacks twice with ..."), area actions with a saving throw (cone, cube, sphere; recharge 5-6, uses a day), riders (poison saves, knocked prone), and the traits and reactions that change a fight: pack tactics, regeneration, charge (extra damage and prone), reckless, sneak attack, legendary resistance, evasion, flyby; resistances, vulnerabilities and immunities by damage type (a droid is vulnerable to ion). Parsed into `Occupant.profile` when the world is built; worlds made before have only the one attack. A computer-run creature closes in, Dashes when it still cannot reach, and makes its attacks one by one, with a reaction offered to the character in between. Companions fight by the same code.
  - Initiative uses the stat block's Dexterity modifier; the character's saving throws now include proficiency.
  - Not done: Ready, Search as an action, opportunity attacks by the character (enemies never leave reach), mounted combat, difficult terrain, legendary actions, innate power casting by stat blocks, class features that need a target chosen after the roll (Riposte), Scholar's Critical Analysis (tracked only).
- Downtime (2026-10-15; `downtime.json` derived from Wretched Hives' Entertainment and Downtime chapter by `deriveDowntime`; the engine is the last section of `shared/logic/index.ts`; the Downtime tab of Play, `DowntimePanel` in `pages/GameBoard.tsx`). Every activity's words and tables are the book's: Bounty Hunting, Buying Enhanced Items, Carousing, Crafting, Crime, Espionage, Gambling, Mercenary Contracting, Pit Fighting, Racing, Research, Selling Enhanced Items, Training and Work, with the d100 result tables, the roll modifier table, the complications tables (and the chance of one, read from each activity's text) and the uptime tables where the book has them. A workweek is 7 days of the campaign clock. Needs a settlement (town, city or station for enhanced items) and no enemies about.
  - The procedure (`activitySpec` reads the cost, class tiers, stake and which checks are allowed from the activity's text): pay, make up to three checks (the DCs 5 higher for each check after the first), the best one's roll modifier (+5 at DC 10 to +25 at DC 30) goes to a d100, the row is read (`outcomeOf` reads credits, contacts, blackmail, lore, a title, jail, exhaustion from its words). Meeting no DC after several checks is the book's own failure line ("they are caught and jailed", "they lose their entire stake, and accrue a debt", "they lose all of their bouts and suffer a level of exhaustion", "crash their vehicle, requiring at least 1,000 cr in repairs"). Complications: the chance from the text (10%, +10% for each check after the first, 50% with a shady seller), the table of the social class where there are three; what they cost (a pickpocket's 1d10 x 50 cr) and whether they make a rival or a jail week are read from their words, the rest goes to the log to be played out.
  - What is kept (`playState.downtime`, `DowntimeState`): contacts (named after people of the settlement, at most 1 + the Charisma modifier allied ones), blackmail, lore, blueprints, rare ingredients, trained and expert skills, rivals, trainings under way, titles and a log. House rules, each said where it is used: allied and hostile contacts and rivals in a settlement move how its traders take to the character (attitude +1 to +2 or -1 to -2); a piece of blackmail opens the nearest locked way without a check; a piece of lore finds what is hidden in the room; a rival acts in about a third of the workweeks after it was made (goods stolen up to 15% of the credits, or rumors that cost renown); a week in jail also costs renown; exhaustion gives disadvantage on downtime checks and a long rest takes one level away; titles are only kept and shown.
  - Buying and selling enhanced items: the investigation check (+1 for each five days beyond the first and each 1,000 cr beyond the first 1,000, to +10) gives the rarity found (DC 10 to 30), a named item needs two checks; the d100 with the rarity's modifier gives the seller's price (plain and shady). The item's value is the book's "determine values randomly" (two d100, the higher, times the rarity's modifier, within the rarity's band, half for consumables), seeded by the item's name so that it is always worth the same. Selling: DC 20, days by rarity, 100 cr a day.
  - Crafting: tools by the Item Specific Tools table, raw materials half the value, days = value / 250, the d100 gives how much of the materials it uses (x2 to x0.25). Enhanced items need their blueprint (learned by taking an item apart: DC by rarity, an hour, half the raw materials back) and, from prototype up, a rare ingredient: a defeated creature of a rarity's Challenge Rating leaves one a third of the time (house rule; the book leaves it to an adventure). Standard and premium waive it, as the book suggests.
  - Training: the book's workweeks and costs (language and weapon 10 weeks, 250 cr; tool, skill 5/10/20; saving throw 10/20; a feat 40 weeks), a check each workweek and the d100's advance (half to four workweeks); at the end the language, weapon, tool, skill, saving throw or feat is added; the half-proficiency and expertise tiers are kept and used by downtime checks only (the rest of the game does not know them yet).
  - Not done: Experimenting, Learning Blueprints from found blueprints (there are none in the loot), the uptime versions of carousing, racing and work (their tables are in `downtime.json`; gambling's uptime is "Gambling" under Gear and tools), a party working together on a craft, "Rivals" beyond what complications make, the Faction chapter's own downtime.
- Starship combat (2026-10-15; the engine is the last section of `shared/logic/index.ts` "Starship combat", `ships.json` derived by `deriveShips` from the size texts and the equipment tables of Starships of the Galaxy; `useShipFight` in `useCombat.ts`; `SpaceScene` in `LocalMap.tsx`; `ShipCombatPanel` in `PlayCombat.tsx`; Engage in `GameBoard.tsx`). The party's ship fights the hostile ships (owned by the campaign's enemy) in orbit where it is: the Engage button is on such a ship's card and in the area's ship list.
  - A ship's numbers (`shipSheet`): ability scores 10 + the size's adjustments (Small +2 Dexterity, -2 Constitution ...) + its role's; hull dice and die by size and tier (the book's Hull & Shield Dice table), hull points = the first die's best + Constitution modifier, the rest at the average + the modifier; shields (any ship that carries a shield item): the same dice, points from Strength, capacity and regeneration by Directional, Fortress or Quick-Charge; Armor Class 10 + Dexterity (limited by Deflection or Reinforced armor) + the tier's upgrade, damage reduction 3 or 6 from armor; speed and turning speed from the role. Weapons from the starship equipment: primary (damage, normal and long range), secondary (close and normal range), tertiary and quaternary with the damage of an ammunition (`ships.json` ammunition: torpedoes, missiles, rockets, bombs) and so many pieces as the launcher holds.
  - The fight is on a grid of 50-foot squares (the book's variant), in rounds of ship turns: initiative, a ship's turn is its own; the shields regenerate at the start of it (spending a shield die), a ship at 0 hull makes destruction saves (three successes stable, failures are levels of system damage, six is lost), damage at 0 hull is system damage, damage as great as the maximum destroys. Movement is flying speed: a square 50 ft., a diagonal 75 (house rule: the book alternates 50 and 100), turning 45 degrees costs half the turning speed, no ending on another ship; asteroids are difficult terrain. Actions: Fire (each weapon once a turn; arc: fixed guns fire in the forward 90 degrees, secondary weapons in turrets (house rule); range: long range gives disadvantage; within 50 ft. disadvantage; the helm firing a primary or secondary weapon has disadvantage unless an attack run or a gunner fires), Helm (one a turn: Evade, Fly, Hard Turn, Attack Run after 150 ft. toward the target), Boost engines, shields or weapons (DC 10 Strength, +5 for each boost after the first), Patch (a hull die), Regenerate Shields (a shield die), Direct, Interfere, and jumping away with a hyperdrive. Missiles, torpedoes and bombs call for a Dexterity save against the proficiency-based DC (half on a save; a ship two sizes smaller saves with advantage). Damage types as the book: ion half to the hull and cannot destroy (disables), fire, cold and acid half to shields, necrotic half to both, poison and psychic and sonic nothing; armor takes its reduction off an attack roll's hit (at least 1).
  - Crew: the character takes the helm; each companion who is not a beast is a crew action (a gunner: no helm disadvantage); enemy ships fire every weapon each turn (a crew per gun). The computer's pilot closes to its guns' range facing the target, fires, and evades when its hull is a third. House rules, named on screen: the proficiency bonus applies to attacks (the character counts as a trained pilot; enemy ships use tier + 1, at least 2); the ability score increases of a tier go to Dexterity, Strength, Constitution and Wisdom in turn; no deployments, power dice, ship modifications or quaternary mines drifting between turns.
  - After the fight: the party's ship keeps its hull (`PartyShip.hull`) until refitted at a port (100 credits for each hull die missing times the size's cost modifier, and the size's refitting time: `refitPlan`); a lost ship is gone and the character gets away in a pod with 1 hit point (house rule); enemy ships destroyed are wrecks, disabled ones (`Ship.disabled`) can be taken as prizes, those that jumped are gone; experience is half the hull points of each ship beaten and salvage a tenth of the value of each destroyed (house rule); renown falls with the enemy. New worlds give ships the book's role names.
  - Not done: deployments and their ranks, power dice and couplings, modifications and ventures, fighting more than one party ship, boarding, hyperspace mishaps and interdiction on the way, enemy ships that attack the party's by themselves, a crew of hired NPC officers, point-defense systems of Huge ships (they fire as attacks), cover from other ships, the ionized and slowed conditions from ionizing weapons beyond disabling.
- Life in places (2026-10-14; `Life` in LocalMap.tsx, people from `populateRoom` and `placeContents` in shared/logic). What is shown only; the world keeps everyone's square in the plan, and a fight starts from where they are drawn at that moment (`whereNow`).
  - People are individuals: a room's patrons, shoppers, residents, travellers and so on are each a named person of the planet's peoples (`locals`, roles from `LOCAL_ROLES`; `isLocal` keeps them out of the campaign's NPC list), and one in three rooms has someone with a trade (smuggler, gambler ...). Only named people, staff and hostiles carry a name label; tap anyone for their card.
  - Patrons sit at the tables and booths; now and then one goes up to the bar (two at most at once) and the staff serve them, one calls on someone at another table, or one takes another seat.
  - Cantinas (the cantina's Main bar and a promenade's Cantina; codes `m` and `D`): a stage ("Bandstand", a corner of the room) and a band of three or four, usually of one people, each with an instrument from the equipment data (MusicalInstrument). They play a set of about a minute, then all stop together for a break and mingle with patrons, sit in at a table or go to the bar, then go back up. Seven in ten cantinas have a service droid (T3-Series Utility Droid stat block) that waits on seated customers, comes to the party if they stand about, and wipes each table its people leave.
  - Staff stand behind their counter (`counterSides`: the side away from the room's way in; `isCounter` knows counters, stalls, desks, consoles, workbenches by name) and pace along it. A double tap on a counter (or a tap on the person serving there) walks the character to the near side; whoever serves there comes to the other side, says something, and their card opens. A single tap on a counter says who serves there (after half a second, so that the note does not cover the counter before a second tap).
  - Sentries: the first in a room keeps the post by the way out; the second walks a round of the room's corners, comes back to check in with the one on the post (a word each) and about one time in three they change places. At a building whose entrance room has sentries they are drawn outside when the party is in front of it: one by the door, the other walking a round of the building. A tap on one from the street gives their card (they keep the way in; walk in and the watch begins as before). Hostiles who are questioning or worse (not let pass), or whose building has its alarm up, stop and watch the party.
  - A hostile building's entrance is always held by two at least: the generator prefers a pair the encounter budget allows, else the one it can afford is doubled (a little over the XP budget).
  - A tap on furniture that can be stood on (bench, stage, dais) goes to the floor beneath and walks to that square; other furniture is only named.
  - Speech is a small bubble over the speaker for a few seconds (`Html` from drei, not 3D text: 3D text is laid out late on slow machines). The party walks through people; only furniture is in the way.
  - Not done: people in other rooms of a building moving about while the party is elsewhere, people walking between rooms or buildings, buying from staff (the equipment data has drinks under AlcoholicBeverage), sound.
- Conditions in a fight (2026-10-13): `conditionMechanics(name)` in rules.ts reads what a condition does from the conditions data, not from a table in the code: cannot act ("can't take actions", and "is incapacitated (see the condition)"), advantage or disadvantage on its own attacks and on attacks against it (melee and ranged apart, for Prone), melee hits that are critical, saving throws failed outright or at disadvantage, and "can't attack the charmer". `powerEffect` now also reads the conditions a power imposes ("or be charmed", "is stunned", "falls unconscious", with "While poisoned in this way, the target is paralyzed"), for how long (the phrase beside it, else the power's duration; 10 turns to the minute, 100 = the whole fight), a save repeated at the end of each turn, a hit point pool ("Roll 5d8"), whether it is for the caster (invisibility), whether it ends when its bearer attacks or is hurt, and concentration (a second concentration power ends the first). Sentences that only mention a condition ("if the target is invisible", "can't be knocked prone") are skipped. The fight (`useCombat.ts`: `ActiveCondition`, `attackOdds`, `cannotAct`, `rollD20`) rolls two d20 for advantage or disadvantage, skips the turn of a creature that cannot act, counts conditions down at the end of the creature's own turn, and starts the character with the conditions saved on them (the GM tab can set those). The panel shows each creature's conditions with turns left and one line on what each does. A stat block's attack is melee or ranged as its data says (`attack.ranged`). Not done: conditions from enemies' attacks, Exhaustion levels, Frightened/Grappled/Shocked movement limits (there is no movement in the fight).
- Room plans (2026-10-13, `planBuilding` in shared/logic): every room is a rectangle of 5-foot squares on one grid for its building (`Area.layout`: x, y, width, height, assets). Rooms that open off each other share a wall with the door in it; a room that finds no free wall stands apart and its door is a passage. `ROOM_PLANS` gives size and furniture by what the room is for (bar counter at the far wall, tables with seats, stalls in rows, bunks and consoles along walls, crate stacks, holding cells, a hangar sized for the ship; caves and ruins get boulders and rubble). Furniture that would cut one door off from another is dropped. Doors, the way out, windows (outside walls only, none in vaults and cells) and one secret way are set in walls. `placeContents` gives people, caches and ships a square: staff by their counter or console, patrons by seats, guards by doors, the leader at the far end, beasts in the open, caches against the far walls. `RoomAsset.ref` points at the path, occupant, cache or ship, so what is hidden or gone is not drawn. An older building is planned the first time the party stands in it (Play does this and saves). The plan is drawn only in 3D now (see "The 3D map"); `components/Map/RoomPlan.tsx`, the SVG plan, is no longer shown anywhere (not deleted). Based on five reference maps (Dyson Logos "Twelve Goats Tavern" and "Beneath the Brewery"; Jabba's Palace, Docking Bay 94 and Echo Base skirmish maps): what was taken from them is in the comment above `RoomAsset`.
- Secret ways in: every hostile building has one and a quarter of the others (`Area.secretEntry` on a back room: name, DC 12 + danger). Search (Wisdom, Perception) outside the building or in the room finds it; it is then an exit both ways ("Slip in through the ..."), and whoever comes in by it is not noticed.
- The fight on the grid (2026-10-13, `useCombat.ts`: `Arena`, `reachable`, `coverBetween`, `attackPlan`, `moveTo`, `approach`; `components/Combat/PlayCombat.tsx`). A fight is fought on the room's plan (`skirmishGround` gives open ground with scattered cover where there is no plan). The party starts at the door it came in by, the enemy where the plan has them. Movement: speed in feet (the character's, the stat block's), 5 feet a square, a diagonal counts as one square (Player's Handbook grid rule), round furniture and other creatures, no cutting corners; conditions that stop or halve speed are read from their text. Reach and range: melee needs the weapon's reach (5 ft., 10 with the reach property; a stat block's own reach); ranged attacks use the weapon's normal and long range from its properties ("range 50/200") or the stat block's ("range 100/400 ft."): beyond long range no attack, beyond normal range disadvantage, and disadvantage with an enemy within 5 feet. Powers have their range from the powers data (Touch 5 ft., "60 feet"; looked up by name through `/api/sw5e/powers`, with duration and concentration, because a saved power only has name, level and text). Cover: furniture flagged as cover on the line to the target and within 5 feet of it (a ship's hull anywhere on the line): one is half cover (+2 AC), more is three-quarters (+5). Play: on the 3D map, tap a green square to move, tap an enemy to pick it out; Melee closes in first and strikes if it gets there, an attack or power ends the turn, End turn to only move. Enemies that fight hand to hand close in; those that shoot hold their ground unless out of normal range. Advantage and disadvantage from any number of sources cancel to a straight roll. Not done: opportunity attacks, moving after attacking, difficult terrain, thrown weapons, powers that fill an area, creatures larger than one square, and Dash, Disengage, Dodge, Hide.
- Art (2026-10-13; superseded the same evening by the 3D models, see "The 3D map": of sprites.tsx only `tokenOf`, `floorOf` and `SIDE_COLORS` are still used, its SVG drawings are no longer shown and are not deleted). `components/Map/sprites.tsx` drew everything on the room plan and the fight grid as SVG (no image files): floors by building type (plating, hangar deck, sand, rock, stone, clay), about thirty kinds of furniture by name, tokens for people, troopers, officers, Force users (with a lightsaber), astromechs, battle droids, other droids, beasts, horrors, swarms and Hutts (`tokenOf` picks from the stat block's creature type, name and role), and four ship outlines by role and size (fighter, shuttle, light freighter, corvette). `RoomFloor` in RoomPlan.tsx is shared by the plan and the fight. The 3D Local Map (`components/Map/LocalMap.tsx`) now builds places from plain solids: buildings by type (domed cantina, landing pad with lights, bunker with turrets, market awnings, tents, cave mouth, broken columns, wrecked hull ...), settlements by type (towers, domes, outpost, station ring ...), walls and ground coloured by the planet's biome, planets by biome and stars by class; rooms with a floor plan are drawn wall to wall with their furniture as blocks. Stat block occupants now carry `speed`, `size`, `creature` and their attack's `range`/`long` (worlds made earlier lack them: 30 ft., 5 ft. reach, 80/320 ft. are assumed, and tokens fall back on names and roles).


House rules (not from the books; each is labelled in the game where it appears):
- Passage on someone else's ship: the fuel the trip burns at a Medium ship's fuel price (100 credits a unit); the shuttle between surface and orbit is free. The books have no fare table.
- A dead character can be revived with 1 hit point ("Revive (house rule)"), so a solo campaign can go on.
- A game master's reply cannot reduce hit points below 1 or kill; only a fight can.
- Enemies leave a downed character alone instead of attacking them, since the character is alone.
- One character flies any ship: crew sizes and deployments are not checked.
- Enemy ships can be taken once their guards are beaten.
- A look through a window from the street shows who is in that room, without a check.
- Contraband (`contrabandFrom`): spice and lightweapons are seized wherever there is law, explosives, poisons, slicing and forgery gear, restraining bolts and exotic weapons where it is policed, martial weapons and heavy armor where it is strict. Lawful traders do not sell what the law seizes and will not buy it; in lawless places merchants also sell spice and explosives. Buying contraband says so; nothing yet checks what the party carries.
- Haggling DC: 12, +2 for the black market, -1 per step of the trader's goodwill. Opening hours by building type, and the black market from 18:00 to 04:00.
- Time on the way: walking 80 m a minute, an hour between the surface and orbit or a jump point, a minute through a door or into a building (the books give travel times only for realspace and hyperspace).
- The watch (`ALERT_LEVELS`, `arrivalAlert`, `setAlert`, `watchDC` in shared/logic; `Area.alert`, `Area.allowed`, the building's `alarm`). Hostile creatures notice the party by degrees: unaware, suspicious, questioning, confronting, attacking. Coming in by a secret way: unaware. With "Move quietly" ticked: Dexterity (Stealth) against 10 + their best Wisdom modifier (+5 with the alarm up); made, unaware; missed by less than 5, suspicious; else as if walking in. Walking in: questioning at a building's entrance, confronting further in, attacking where the alarm is up; beasts and horrors do not talk and attack at once. Unaware or suspicious: moving on or taking a cache is a Stealth check and a miss moves the watch up a step; "Ambush" from unaware costs the enemy the first round and raises no alarm. Questioning: Bluff or Persuade (Charisma against 10 + their Wisdom + danger); success and they let the party pass (caches are then off limits), failure and they confront. Confronting: Intimidate at +5, go back the way you came, or fight. Attacking: the fight starts by itself. Any fight but an ambush raises the alarm through the building; a long rest clears alarms. Hostiles with nothing recorded (older saves, enemies the GM tab added) count as confronting, which is how the game behaved before.

- The law's response (`reportCrime`, `responseFor`, `pursuitAt`, `lieLow` in shared/logic; `World.pursuit`). House rule, the books give none. A crime raised in a place sends officers after a delay set by the law there (strict 4 min, policed 10, frontier 30; a heinous crime halves it, a theft or trespass makes it 1.5 times). Officers: 2 on the frontier, 3 to 4 policed, 5 to 7 strict, heavy troopers in strict law or for a heinous crime. The search covers the settlement (`districtOf`) for a time set by the crime (trespass 30 min, theft 60, brawl 120, assault 240, heinous 720) and the law (x0.5 frontier, x1 policed, x2 strict). Lawless space sends no one, unless the place is ruled by a crime syndicate: its enforcers come then. Raised by being seen picking a lock or forcing a door (trespass), taking a cache (theft), a blasted door (heinous), a brawl, and the lawmen turned on a party that was seen (see "The law in person"). Fighting hostile creatures is not a crime (it was until 2026-10-16: every fight sent officers, which made them come for a party defending itself). "Lie low" is a Stealth check against 12 + officers (+2 heavy); failing it is a fine of 100 x crime x law (x2 heavy). Leaving the district ends the search for the party.
- Ships in the round (2026-10-15; "Ships in the round" in shared/logic: `SHIP_FOOTPRINT`, `hangarRange`, `shipDeck`, `boardShip`, `moveShipInterior`, `rampLeadsTo`, `helmExits`; `boardNow`, `flyShip`, `disembarkNow` and the "Aboard" panel in GameBoard). House rule, the books have no deck plans. A ship covers squares by size on a hangar floor (Tiny 6x4, Small 10x6, Medium 16x10, Large 26x14, Huge 40x22, Gargantuan 56x30; was always 6x4) and a hangar is drawn to hold the ships that berth in it with a margin (`hangarRange`; side by side when there are several); worlds before life 8 get their too-small hangars planned again with their district's streets. The party's own ship has one deck (`shipDeck`, by size: Tiny 5x3 to Gargantuan 52x26): the pilot's seat (a station, `ref: "station:helm"`) and navigation, gunnery and co-pilot seats at the bow (east), bunks, a table and a medical bed amidships, cargo and the reactor aft, the boarding ramp at the stern. The deck is a room of a building of type `starship` (ids `ship-<id>`, `deck-<id>`) that is made on the first boarding and stands wherever the ship does (it is a child of the settlement, planet or system the ship is at, and is left out of districts and routines). Board from the ship's card on the map ("Board"); tap the pilot's seat or "Take the helm" for the helm, which lists every take-off, landing, jump or fly-in from where the ship stands with its fuel and time (the exits that need a ship, `helmExits`; a port that is not the planet's gateway still lifts off to orbit); the party stays aboard when the ship flies. The ramp (or "Disembark") steps out into the room it was boarded from while the ship is at a settlement. Not done yet: a viewscreen showing what is outside, starting a ship fight from the gunnery seat, and seats for companions.
- Landing anywhere on a planet (2026-10-17; `exitsOf`, `helmExits` in shared/logic). The helm in orbit lists a landing at every settlement or point of interest of the planet, not only its gateway; a settlement without a port lifts off to orbit the same way (its "leave" exit needs a ship, as a port's does). Not a house rule: the books give no landing rules beyond the ship's own footprint, and the ship's deck is drawn in the settlement like any starport's.
- Flying games (2026-10-17, HOUSE RULES; `flightCourse`, `stepFlight`, `inRing`, `flightPayout` in shared/logic, the "Flying games" section at the end; `components/Map/FlightScene.tsx`; `FlightActivity` and the downtime menu's groups in GameBoard). The downtime menu is in groups: Side quests (bounty hunting, espionage, mercenary contracting, crime), Fighting games (pit fighting), Racing games (racing), Flying games (taxi run, delivery run), Everyday downtime (the rest). Taxi run: a landspeeder (needs one of the party's vehicles) flies a course of five rings over the settlement's ground at a hover of 3 m; delivery run: a ship (needs one of the party's ships) flies six rings through the space of its planet. The drag pad steers (yaw; pitch for a ship, a drag down pitches down), Boost and Brake set the throttle, the chase camera follows; the rings are taken in order (within 7 m or 14 m) before the time runs out (1.8 times the flight at top speed). Pay: per ring (60 cr taxi, 200 cr delivery), a bonus for every ring (150, 600), and a share of the time to spare when every ring is taken. A run takes a day. Flying, extended (2026-10-17; the same model in shared/logic "Flying": `flightCourse` with laps and count, `alongCourse`, `raceRivals`, `raceResult`, `stepDogfight`, `dogfightPayout`, `routeCount`, `pilotMinutes`; `FlightRun` and `DogfightScene` in FlightScene.tsx; `FlightActivity`, `pilot` in `travel`, and the route dialog in GameBoard). The airspeeder is a third craft (70 m/s, climbs, hover 12 m); the ship and the airspeeder pitch, the landspeeder does not. Speeder race (Racing games): two laps of the taxi course against four rivals who keep a pace of 80-100% of top speed (the rivals' finish times are fixed by their pace); placed by the time taken, prizes 1,000 / 500 / 250 / 100 cr. Dogfight (Flying games): a ship against four foes that close on the craft and fire every 3.5 s (30% a hit, 10 hull); the craft fires a bolt every 0.6 s at the nearest foe in its cone and 320 m range (70% a hit; a foe with 2 hull takes two hits); paid 250 cr a foe down and 500 cr for a clear sky. Pilot's route (house rule, travel): a party vehicle's ride can be flown ("Fly it yourself"): the trip is a run of 3 to 8 rings (one per 6 km), and the trip takes a fifth less with every ring taken and a fifth more with none. Not done yet: a route's crash, damage to the craft after a dogfight, companions at the guns in a dogfight, the racing book table in the same 3D race, and the pit arena. The e2e steps "flying games" check the menu groups, a taxi run and a dogfight to their end; the pilot's route is not played through in the browser.
Minigame pages and the fixes of 2026-10-17 (these supersede the rival-pace race and the flash-only shots above). Every minigame (taxi, delivery, route, speeder race, dogfight) opens on its own full-screen page (`MinigamePage`, FlightScene.tsx) with a five-second countdown before anything moves; Leave it forfeits the run (no pay, no day). Races (`raceStart`, `stepRace`, `raceOrder`, `raceResult` in shared/logic): the racers start on a grid two abreast, six metres behind the start line, all facing the first ring; the party and four rivals fly the speeder models (`SpeederModel` from LocalMap). Rivals keep a lane across each ring and a pace of 86-97% of top speed. Racers bump: a scrape at low speed costs nothing, a crash costs one hull and one more for each 20 m/s of the impact; a racer at 0 hull is out. Hazards on the legs of the course: pylons (2 hull, pushed off), slicks (speed capped at a third while on them), mines (3 hull, once). Placing is by finish time, then rings taken; prizes 1,000 / 500 / 250 / 100 cr. Dogfight foes (2026-10-17, `foeAim`, `flyFoe`, `DOGFIGHT`): a foe flies at 34 m/s and turns at 0.7 rad a second (the craft's boost is 80 m/s and it turns at 2), so the craft can get onto a foe. A foe keeps 70-140 m from the craft: it closes beyond 140 m, breaks off inside 70 m and holds a slant across the middle distance so that it does not fly straight into the line of fire. A body within 35 m (the racing contacts' spheres, 5 m each, and a margin) is a -1 signal that pushes the foe off it, so foes steer round the craft and each other; a foe that touches the craft bumps it (both lose 1 hull, as in a race). Foes have 2 or 3 hull and fire every 2.6 s when in range and facing the craft. A simulated player that steers at the nearest foe and fires when it is on the reticule wins four runs in five with 6-9 hull left (`tsx` check; the human's aim will be worse). Dogfight (`stepDogfight`, `targetOf`): the party's ship and the foes are the ship models (`ShipModel`); each shot is a tracer, the party's blue and the foes' red, for 0.18 s; the reticule turns red when a foe is on it (the nearest in the cone and range); foes off screen show a red pip at the screen's edge pointing to them. Not played in the browser: a race's hazards in a full race, the reticule turning red on a foe, and the pips' placement in a crowded fight.
- Speeder race, overhauled (2026-10-18, at the user's request; HOUSE RULES; supersedes what the two paragraphs above say of races: rings, rival pace, 5 m spheres, the old hazards). Shared/logic "Racing": `raceCourse`, `raceStart`, `stepRace`, `raceOrder`, `raceResult`, `groundAt`, `raceDistance`, `speederKind`, `RACE`, `SPEEDER_BODY`; drawn by `RaceGame` in FlightScene.tsx. What was wrong: racers and pylons collided as 5 m spheres round models about 1.4 m wide; a pair that had touched stayed "touching" within 8 m and lost 40% of its speed every frame (two racers side by side crawled, which read as boost being cut); the Boost button let go when the thumb slid off it; frames of up to 0.25 s let a racer pass through things.
  - Course: a closed loop (centripetal Catmull-Rom through 10-12 control points round a centre, one or two pushed out into lobes, two pinched into hairpins), 16 m wide, 1.85-2.25 km a lap, three laps (about two and a half minutes for the winner). A loop is kept only if it never comes within 38 m of itself and bends no tighter than 22 m; one with a shortcut is preferred (18 seeds in 20 have one, 6 have two). The start is on the straightest stretch. Gates (6-8 a lap, posts either side and a bar over the track; the first is the start and finish, chequered) are passed in order through their opening (the line of the gate crossed between the posts); the finish is the start line after the last lap. Ground: three long swells (never steeper than about 1 in 10) and hills rising 8-13 m either side of the course from 8 m off it, so it runs in a valley; one function (`groundAt`) is both the drawn ground and the physics.
  - Shortcuts: where the track swings round a lobe, a trail 9 m wide across its neck that saves about 150 m, branching off at half the angle between the track and the chord; never over the start; gates are moved 90 m past where a trail joins (time to settle onto the track), so both ways pass every gate; a gate counts when the racer's middle passes inside the posts' middles. Each trail has a ramp at its middle and a ravine 24 m long and 5 m deep past the ramp's lip: cleared at speed (boost), dropped into and climbed out of if not. Its edges are lined with trees and boulders.
  - Jumps: two ramps on straights of the main track (12 m long, 2.2 m high, on one half of the track; the other half drives by). A speeder hovers 0.7 m on a spring over the ground (gravity 20 m/s²) and flies off a lip; in the air it steers at a third and has no grip; a landing faster than 11 m/s down costs hull. Time in the air refills boost faster.
  - Speeders: rigid boxes measured from the models (`buildSpeeder`, drawn 1.3 times map size in a race: a landspeeder 3.4 by 1.8 m, a bike 3.6 by 0.5 m, an airspeeder 3.9 by 3 m), with mass (bike 0.4, landspeeder 1.1, airspeeder 1), hull (9 / 14 / 12), top speed (a bike 4% faster) and steering. Velocity is apart from heading: grip stops sliding sideways at 30 m/s² (18 off the track, 6 on oil), so a fast turn slides wide and the slide scrubs speed; steering is slower at speed; slopes speed it up downhill and slow it uphill. Plain driving is automatic to 38 m/s (rivals at their pace of 92-99%); Brake brakes and backs up (8 m/s).
  - Boost: 46 m/s (the old top speed of 40 and 15% more) with twice the push; the meter drains in about 4.5 s held and refills over about 14 s once it has rested 0.8 s; nothing but an empty meter stops it (collisions do not).
  - Contacts: box against box (racers, barriers, crates, statues: separating axes), box against circle (trunks, posts, boulders, pylons), each resolved by impulse with restitution 0.25 (0.3 between racers), friction 0.35 and the turn from where it was struck; a racer one passing high over another (a jump) misses it, and low rocks and shrubs pass under a hover. A face of ground too steep to ride up (a ramp's side or back) is struck as a wall. Hull lost: none below 7 m/s of the blow's head-on speed, one at 7 and one more each 8 m/s over (a 46 m/s head-on into a tree is 5; a graze along a barrier is 0); between racers each loses by the change in its own speed (a bike knocked by a landspeeder loses more). Shrubs and the low boughs of conifers drag (soft); crates and pylons break when met at over 4 m/s (they take a little speed). Mines (3 a course): set off by a racer low over one; those within 8 m are thrown and the one over it loses 3 hull (1 within 4 m). Oil (2 a course, on bends) takes the grip. A racer at 0 hull is out of the race (its wreck stays, heavy).
  - The race steps at 1/60 s however slow the frames (up to half a second a frame is taken at once), so nothing passes through anything at 46 m/s.
  - Rivals: follow their lane of the track ahead of them (a point 10 m plus half a second ahead), go round what is on the track ahead (mines, crates, pylons, slower racers, wrecks), line up on a gate's opening when it is near, brake for a bend they could not take at their speed in the distance they have (24 m/s² across), boost on open straights, take a shortcut by their daring (per rival and lap), boost into the ravine's jump, and back out when stuck.
  - Towed (house rule): a racer stuck (under 2 m/s for 3.5 s while not braking), 45 m off the course for 2 s, or 60 m down the track past a gate it did not go through (not while on a trail), is put back on the track (before that gate), and passes through the others for 1.5 s.
  - End: when the party finishes or is out, or 30 s after the winner (then the party has not finished), or at the time limit. Placing as before (finish time, then gates and distance to the next); prizes unchanged (1,000 / 500 / 250 / 100 cr). The log line says the place, time and hull left.
  - Drawn: the ground mesh (200 by 200 cells, mottled, verges worn to the track's colour, coloured by world: `RACE_LOOKS`), a sky dome and fog, the track as a ribbon with kerbs (red and white on bends), trails, gates (the next one lit yellow), ramps with treads and a lit lip, mines blinking, oil, the props from the site library (below), ground cover (900, or 260 on a lite device), the racers as the speeder models with a pilot each, a shadow under each, a flame while boosting, a hull bar over each rival, sparks and debris where something is struck, a flash for a mine, a chevron over the party's speeder pointing to the next gate. The chase camera sits 11 m behind and 5 m up, widens in portrait and with speed and boost, shakes on a hit. By the start: grandstands with a crowd, banners and lamps along the straight, and on the inside a formal garden from the palace garden pieces (hedges, clipped trees, statues, columns, a fountain).
  - HUD: place, lap, gates passed, km/h, the race clock, a hull bar and a boost bar (`race-hull`, `race-boost`, with `data-value`), the standings, the finish window once the winner is home, and what just happened to the party. The Boost button fills with the meter. The end shows the order with times.
  - The world: Downtime passes the settlement's planet type; `raceScenery(biome)` gives its trees, shrubs, boulders and ground cover (desert: cactus, palms, dry trees; ice: pines; forest: pines, logs, stumps; and so on).
  - Checks: tsx scripts (session scratchpad, not kept) generated 20 courses, ran races with a bot and with an idle party at 0.1 and 0.25 s frames (every rival finishes in 2:15-2:55; no tunnelling at 46 m/s with 0.25 s frames; a head-on hit costs 5 hull, a graze none, a rear-end hurts the bike and not the landspeeder; boost is held through a hit). The e2e step plays a race to its end on the VM (about 11 minutes under software rendering: the race runs at a quarter speed there) and checks the bars and that the meter drains while Boost is held. Test hooks: `localStorage sw5e.autopilot = "1"` hands the party's speeder to the rivals' driving; `window.__race` is the race as it stands.
  - Not done: damage to the party's vehicle after a race; the pilot's route and the taxi run still use the old flight model (they were not asked about).
- The law in person (`lawmenAt`, `lawWatchDC`, `turnLawmen`, `engageOfficers`, `maxSquads` in shared/logic; `lawUnseen`, `callTheLaw` and the squad effect in GameBoard). House rule. Guards, officers and customs officers who are not hostile see what the party does in their sight, in the room and, in the open, on the roofs and catwalks of the district: picking a lock (trespass) and taking a cache (theft) are a Stealth check against 10 + the best Wisdom modifier among them (+5 with the alarm up); forcing a door in front of them is seen outright. Caught, every one of them turns hostile with a stat block (junior officer, trooper in strict law), the alarm goes up, the crime is reported and the fight begins by itself. The officers a search sends are real: at the scene a squad (the search's officers, as "Constables", "Security troopers" or "Syndicate enforcers") stands in the room hostile when the delay is up; entering another place of the district while the search is on is a Stealth check against the lie-low DC, one try a place, and failing it brings a squad. Only places of the law count: in a room where `danger` is above 0 (enemy ground) nobody minds a lock or a cache. At most 2 squads in a search (3 for heavy troopers or a cartel). Lying low still ends the search.
- Sight, leaving and the calm-down (2026-10-16; `SIGHT_SQUARES`, `leaveSight`, `calmedAt`, `inSight`, `blockedExit` in shared/logic; `Area.calmAt`; `leaveBy` in GameBoard). Supersedes the watch's "questioning: they bar the way" rule. Watchers see only 6 squares (about 30 feet; was 18, which put a whole district in sight, so a sentry on a far roof barred every exit). Nothing bars the way: leaving past watchers who can see the party is a Stealth check (`unseen`); a miss moves their watch up a step and the party may still go, unless that puts them on attack, and then the fight begins where they stand. When the party leaves a place the watchers there cannot see (`inSight`), that place's watch calms down after `WATCH_COOLDOWN` (10) minutes and goes back to unaware; coming back into their sight cancels it.
- The search on foot (2026-10-16; `Squad`, `startSquads`, `advanceSearch`, `searchNeighbours`, `squadSightDC` in shared/logic; the search effect in GameBoard). House rule. The squads a search sends (two, or three for heavy troopers or a cartel) start at the scene and walk the settlement's rooms and buildings (through gateways and the street between buildings), a room every `SEARCH_STEP` (2) minutes; a squad's first step is at the scene. A squad questions the people in each room it walks into, and where the party was seen before (`Pursuit.seen`) it learns who the party is (`identified`), then walks straight for the party's last known room (`known`). Where a squad walks in on the party (or on a roof or catwalk in its sight), the party rolls Stealth against `squadSightDC` (10, +2 for heavy troopers; 15 in a sheltered room); a miss means the squad closes in and the fight begins (`engageOfficers`, with the squad's count); a success lets it walk on.
- Criminal reputation (2026-10-16; `HEAT_POINTS`, `heatAt`, `heatLevel`, `addHeat`, `blockadeAt`; `World.heat`; the "Marked" line on the map). Two cooldowns run side by side. The short one is the search (`searchUntil`, as before). The long one is the settlement's heat: each crime adds points (trespass 1, theft and brawl 2, assault 3, heinous 5; only where there is law to report it) and a day takes one away. Level 1 (3 points) is known to the watch, level 2 (6) wanted, level 3 (9) marked: while a search is on at level 3 the officers hold the ways out of the settlement (`blockedExit`: leaving it is refused) and every vehicle or hired ride is blocked (on foot is still open), until the search is called off.
- Comms (2026-10-16; `hasCommlink`, `noteArrival`, `interceptCall`; `Pursuit.reports`; the call panel and the comm jammer in GameBoard). Only someone carrying a commlink calls over distance. A neutral civilian with a commlink who sees the party in a room that the search covers calls security, put through two minutes later (`REPORT_MINUTES`); the search then knows who the party is. A comm jammer switched on (an hour, from the card, as before) jams the district: no call goes through, the party's own calls to allies are blocked too, and no alarm goes up through the building. The party can stop a call on the card of the caller: "Talk them down" (Intimidate against 10 + the caller's Wisdom) or "Silence them", which turns the caller hostile and starts the fight. Friendly people do not call.
- Shelter (2026-10-16; `shelterOwner`, `shelterExits`, `grantShelter`). During a search, a room's owner (bartender, barkeep, innkeeper, resident, owner, landlord, proprietor or keeper, friendly) who is in the room can open a hidden or locked back room of the same building for the party: "Ask ... to open" is a Charisma (Persuade) check against 12 + the law. The way is unlocked and unhidden and the room becomes `sheltered`, where squads only find the party with a Stealth check against 15.
- The status line (the map, under the search): the number of squads on foot, whether they know who the party is, and what they last did (questioning people, a call, a walk-in). The squads are not drawn on the 3D map yet.
- Ways in and out kept open (2026-10-16, life 9; `streetGround`, `wayOutSquare`, `fixStreetWays`, `repairAccess` in shared/logic). Found by auditing the saved worlds: "Side entrance" doors were set on wall squares where furniture or a cache stood (16 of them in three worlds), and a building's way out could open on a pocket between buildings or under an upper floor that overhangs it (a hangar built to fit a ship stands over the concourse's door). Now a way out is only set on a wall square that nothing stands on, that opens on ground joined to the streets, and that leaves every way of the room reachable; the main ways the buildings were planned with are checked the same way and moved to another wall (or another ground-floor room) if they are dead. Worlds made before are mended once when Play opens them (`livenWorld`, `LIFE` 9: furniture, caches and ships moved off ways, dead ways moved or taken out). Checked on three saved worlds and six generated ones with an audit that walks every room's ways and every street door.
- Squads and barricades on the 3D map (2026-10-16; `Squads`, `Barricade` in LocalMap.tsx). The squads of the search for the party, and of the searches for locals (below), are drawn in the district: in front of the door of the building they are in (side by side where there are several), or up on the roof or catwalk they are on; they walk to their next place as the search moves them on (every two minutes of the clock) and go about a little on the spot. While a blockade is on (`blockadeAt`), each signpost for a way out of the district is walled in by a ring of striped barriers two squares out, with two officers behind them: the squares are not walkable, a tap on the sign or a barrier says why, and travellers going out or coming in wait at the barricade, "questioned at the blockade". Vehicles are barred by the exit list, as before.
- Traffic, lifts and incidents (2026-10-16, life 10; `outingVehicle`, `liftsBetween`, `rollIncident`, `advanceIncidents`, `closeIncident`, `incidentReward` in shared/logic). House rules. The day trips people make (`Routine.outing`) go by a vehicle (`outing.by`): speeder, airspeeder (long trips), mount (on worlds with beasts, short trips) or hauler (merchants); more people go (30%, merchants 50% and every 2 to 4 days) and the map draws what carries them. A party that wants to go where someone is going is offered a lift in the route list ("A lift with X, leaves at ..."): it leaves when they do, for a few credits. Once an hour of the clock, for the settlement the party is in, a crime may happen (about 1.3% an hour in a policed town, half that in lawless ones, never while another is open): a local steals, brawls, or takes a parked speeder. The culprit lies low in another room (a speeder thief flees to another settlement of the planet); constables search for them as for the party: squads walk the rooms, question whoever is in them, and a quarter of the time a witness tips them where to go; finding the culprit ends it, and so does the time running out. The culprit stands in their hiding room marked Wanted: the party can find them first and turn them in (the stolen sum, 25 x the law and three times that for a speeder, which goes back to its building). The kiosk's notices list what is open.
- Rides at houses and shops (2026-10-16; `placeVehicles`, `Vehicle.civil`). House rule. About half of the houses, shops, ports and workshops have a speeder parked outside, belonging to a local; a vehicle dealer has several. The party can hotwire one ("Steal it": Dexterity or Intelligence against 12 + the law, a security or slicer's kit if trained): seen by a lawman, or failing, it is theft and the law is called; done quietly, nobody knows. Locals can take one too (above).
- Droid dealers (2026-10-16; `droidStock`, `droidPrice`, `droidOffer`, `droidRepairPrice`, `droidShop` in GameBoard). House rule, the books give no droid prices. The showroom and repair bay of a droid dealer sell the droid stat blocks of the rules data (44 of them) at 6 credits an experience point (150 at least), four to six on the floor a day; droids built for war (security, battle, assassin, tactical, probe, ...) only where the law is frontier or lawless, or from the black market at 1.6 times; nothing dearer than the settlement carries. A bought droid follows the party as a companion. The dealer buys the party's droids back at 40% of the price (less for the hurt), mends one for 5 credits a hit point plus 5 per point of challenge (half an hour), and fits the droid customizations the party carries for the surgeon's fee (100 credits an hour, 8 hours by size), with no check and no kit. The motor upgrade is as before. Customizations themselves are still not sold over a counter (Wretched Hives: a downtime activity).
- Ship crew (2026-10-16; `seatsOf`, `crewOf`, `PartyShip.crew`; the "Stations" list in the Aboard panel). House rule. The seats of a ship are the stations on its deck (helm, co-pilot, navigation, engineering, and one to three gunnery seats by size). Each seat has a menu of the party; a member sits in one seat. In a ship fight the character flies, and each other living party member (not a beast) seated at a station gives the ship one more action (up to four), and each one at a gunnery seat is a gunner, whose shots are not made from the helm; when a companion has the helm, the character at a station counts. A ship whose crew was never assigned is crewed by the first companions in order, gunnery seats first, as before.
- Cameras, panels and kiosks (2026-10-16, life 10; `useKindOf`, `camerasIn`, `addPanels`; the asset card in GameBoard). House rule. Rooms in the plans now get a security camera, an alarm panel, an access panel or an info kiosk by what the room is (offices, halls, command rooms, vaults and cells); older worlds are given them once. A camera records a crime done in front of it in a place with law 2 or more (the law is called, a moment after the act) unless the cameras are looped: "Loop it for an hour" is Intelligence or Dexterity against 12 + the law (a security or slicer's kit if trained). The alarm panel silences or trips the building's alarm; the access panel opens every locked door of the building (Intelligence against 12 + the law; a miss reports trespass); the kiosk lists the wanted, what the search knows, and the settlement's heat. Info kiosks and camera poles also stand in the streets of urban settlements. Rooms are about 28% larger by area (each side one square more, the maximum two), because the districts had a good deal of empty ground; older worlds keep their sizes.
- Passage off world (`passageOffers`, `passageCheckDC`, `stowawayFine`, `SMUGGLER_SHARE`; `passagePanel`, `bookPassage` in GameBoard; life 7 of `livenWorld` adds the people). House rule, the books price no fares. A ticket agent (the "Arrivals concourse", "Port office" or "Airlock" of a port; was the dockmaster) sells a berth to any planet the lanes reach: the fuel the trip burns at a Medium ship's price, plus half an hour to board (an hour more by shuttle from a port that is not the planet's gateway); the party arrives in orbit. A smuggler (each port settlement has one, in the bar) sells it at 60% with a Deception check against 10 + 2 x law to fool the customs scan (none in lawless space); failing it reports trespass and nothing is paid. "Stow away" is free: Stealth against 12 + 2 x law, failing it is a fine (50 x law) and the same report. The exit buttons' passage from orbit stays as it was.
- Heist countdown is 45 minutes (was 25: the stages' own minutes ate it), and opening the lockout in time counts even if the clock ran out during that stage. Heist DCs came down (the old ones were close to impossible at low level): 10 + law for customs, terminal and guards, 12 + law for the lockout (were 12 + 2 x law, 14 + 2 x law).
- Hired rides (`groundRides`, `hasRidesForHire`): any settlement with a type that has traffic gives one, from the settlement or any building in it: 2 credits + 1 per 10 km in a city, town or station (was 5 + 1 per 4 km, and only standing in the street); a village or outpost has a hauler at double the fare and a half hour more to find (was none).
- Taking a ship (`startHeist`, `heistStep`, `takeShip` in shared/logic; `World.heist`). House rule, the books give none. A ship that is not an enemy's can be bought, or stolen through four stages: customs (bribe at 2% of the price times law + 1, or forge a clearance: Deception), the hangar terminal (slice it: Intelligence; this starts a countdown of 25 minutes to take-off), the guards (intimidate or charm: Charisma; or fight: Strength or Dexterity, a lost fight is capture), the lockout (overcome it: Intelligence with a slicer's kit). A failed check raises the alarm and can be retried; four failures in all catch the party. The countdown runs on the clock whatever is done, and the ship lifts off without them when it runs out. Taking the ship is a heinous crime.

- Districts (2026-10-15, life 6; `districtsOf`, `planDistrict`, `DistrictPlan`, `World.districtPlans` in shared/logic; `BuildingScene` in LocalMap.tsx). The user's design: a district is four to six FULL buildings with several ways in, catwalks between them and exterior ladders to the roofs, on one shared 3D map that the party and the people walk across, with the street and the roofs in sight of each other. `districtsOf` puts a settlement's buildings in a line by theme (civic: palace, garrison, archive, command post; market: market, workshop, warehouse, residences, tenements, dealers, medcenter; docks: spaceport, gates, cantina, hideout, transit hub, shipyard) and by place, and cuts it into as few districts as hold it (four to six where the settlement has the buildings; wild sites have none). New optional buildings fill them: tenements, transit hub, droid dealer, vehicle dealer, shipyard. `planDistrict` moves every building's rooms onto one grid (alleys of 3 squares, avenues of 6, a street round the whole), gives each building of three rooms or more a second way in ("Side entrance", a gateway room), a roof (a room of form `roof`, open air, furnished with cover and given sentries: hostile ones where the building is hostile; reached by a ladder up the outside wall, `Roof` is a gateway room of the building so "Climb the ladder to Roof" is an exit of the street), and catwalks (rooms of form `walkway`, joining two roofs at the same height up to 12 squares apart; the validator allows a "Catwalk" path between buildings). Worlds before life 6 get all of it when Play opens them (`livenWorld`). The scene draws every building of the district, the lots they stand on, the roofs with their cover and sentries, the ladders (tap the pad at the foot) and the catwalks on posts; the party walks the street freely and goes in by any way in of any building (`go` carries the squares walked, a minute for fifty); `usePartySpot` is on the district's grid.
- Sites (2026-10-18, life 12; the user's design; `SITES`, `SPOTS`, `makeSatellites`, `districtsOf`, `siteGrounds`, `hangarsOf`, `migrateSites` in shared/logic; `siteAssets.tsx` and `BuildingScene` on the client). The settlement's own map lists buildings (spaceport, cantina, market, palace, warehouse, tenements, junkyard, estates, ...); the local map of each is a site: that building and the buildings that belong to it (tag `site:<anchor id>`, a "satellite"), on one map of streets like a district. Satellites are drawn only on their site's map (`isSatellite`; the settlement view filters them). Cities and towns only; outposts and stations keep the old districts of four to six. Plans are bounded the way the districts were (the largest, a spaceport with seven buildings, about 135 by 115 squares; the rest 40 to 115): `planDistrict` packs its rows at several widths and keeps the squarest.
  - Where things stand (`SPOTS`, a little uneven): the port on the west, the palace on the east, the warehouses between the port and the market (`warehouse` and two more as its site), the cantina on the way to the market, the tenements beside the junkyard (a scrap trade: `junkyard`, `scrap dealer`), the estates (`estates`, `manor house`) beside the palace. Cities get all of them; a town has half the satellites of a city and fewer listed buildings.
  - Spaceport site: the terminal (arrivals concourse, customs, docking bay, control tower, bonded warehouse) with a refuelling depot, a cargo hangar, the mass transit concourse (`transit hub`: concourse, ticket kiosk with a ticket agent, customs and security checkpoint), a public hangar, a private hangar and the shipyard (mechanics' hangar and the upgrade showroom). A town's landing field has a refuelling depot. Hangars are ground-floor with sliding roofs (`GROUND_HANGARS`) and keep free berths for visitors (room code `berths:<n>`, `visitorBerths`, empty ship places in `placeContents`).
  - Landing (house rule): a ship that puts down at a port chooses a hangar (HUD, under the helm: `hangarsOf`, `PartyShip.hangar`, `chooseHangar`); it cannot disembark first, the ramp leads into that hangar's room (`rampLeadsTo`), and a ship can be moved to another hangar for ten minutes. The royal hangar is by invitation (renown 3 with the faction, `hangarOpen`). The fuel attendant (`refuelling depot`; the old Refuel button stays only at ports with no such hangar), the mechanic (mends the hull: `repairPrice`, 10 credits a hull point for a Tiny ship, 20 for a Small, and so on) and the upgrade showroom's outfitter (`outfitterStock`, `fitting`: armor, shield, reactor, power coupling and hyperdrive replace the one of their kind for half of its cost in part exchange, up to four weapons) deal with the ship that stands in their hangar, from their cards (`shipServices`). HOUSE RULES: the books price no repairs or fittings.
  - Cantina site: a larger cantina (dance floor, gambling room, booths, back room, cellar) with a cookshop, a rooming house (guest rooms upstairs), a gambling hall and a pawnshop.
  - Market site: the market hall, six shops in a city and three in a town (general store, armsmith, tech shop, apothecary, outfitter, curio shop), each with a sales floor of walkable furniture that shows its line (`SHOP_WARES`, models in models.tsx; `SHOP_TRADE` sets what the shopkeeper sells), and outdoor stalls along the avenues and before the buildings (`siteGrounds`: up to 12 in a city, 8 in a town), each kept by a stallholder (an `Occupant` with `stall`, kept in the market building, with a routine in the market's hours). By the clock (`packedness`): a stall is open with its wares out, is packed in the forty minutes before closing (crates), and is packed away (canopy rolled) at closing, when the keeper has gone home (`withPresence`). Shops keep the market's hours (`OPENING`) and are locked after them (`afterHoursLock`: DC 12, +3 for each step of law above lawless, to 18; `LOCKED_AT_NIGHT`); the ways in are drawn locked, and a tap on one tells the hours and asks for the lock to be picked (Dexterity or Intelligence, a security kit helps; a trespass the law sees is reported; the door then stays open to the party until morning). HOUSE RULE: the books give shops no hours.
  - Palace site: the palace is large and has floors from the vault (-1) to the towers (3): entrance hall, security checkpoint, petitioners' hall (petitioners), audience chamber (the dignitary: role `dignitary`, code `V`), guard post, kitchens, offices, guest rooms, servants' quarters, the palatial suite (code `F<n>` names a room's floor in a building's plan), a tower at either end (sentries), a vault. Floors are joined by stairs and lifts (never a ladder: `linkLevels`; no roof ladder either: `planDistrict`). Round it: the royal hangar (reserved for the dignitary), a guardhouse and a gatehouse. The district is walled (`SiteGrounds.wall`: three squares in from the edge, round towers, a gate in the west wall and one in the south) and planted: every stretch of free ground inside is a garden (`SiteGrounds.gardens`; hedges, topiary, fountain, beds, statues, benches, lamps, trees and shrubs by `plantGarden`). The wall and the stalls block movement (`fixturesOf` for the walk, `districtArena` for a fight).
  - Scripted exchanges (a few; to be filled out: `SCRIPTS`, `scriptPanel`, `runScript`): the customs officer (declare the cargo, or smooth the inspection for 50 credits), the chamberlain (enter a petition: a slip for the audience chamber until 12 hours on, `World.audiences`), the dignitary (present the petition: renown +1 with the faction; needs the slip or renown 3). All checks and results are house rules, said on the card.
  - Asset library (`components/Map/siteAssets.tsx`, the one new file; the user's rule is to extend, and models.tsx and LocalMap.tsx are already over 3,000 lines): pieces are known by name and tags (`LIBRARY`, `piecesTagged`), each built in code from the Kit of models.tsx (nothing downloaded), baked once and drawn with all their copies in an instanced mesh per finish (`Scatter`); a `Palette` per biome (`paletteOf`) colours them for the world (palms and pale hedges in the desert, white-green conifers on ice, black bark and ember beds on volcanic worlds), and `gltf` on a piece is for using a model from an asset pack once the file is under client/public (loaded, merged, vertex-coloured; `build` is drawn until it has loaded). `/dev/models?section=sites` shows the library by world, the stalls open, packing and packed, the wall and the shop pieces. To add a piece, add it to `LIBRARY`. Wild ground and racing pieces (2026-10-18, made for the speeder race, in the library for any site): `tree_tall`, `tree_pine`, `tree_birch`, `cactus`, `rock`, `boulder`, `rock_small`, `log`, `stump`, ground cover (`grass_tuft`, `wildflowers`, `fern`, `reeds`), and `barrier`, `crate_stack`, `pylon`, `grandstand`, `banner`. A piece that stands in the way gives `hit` (circle or box, height, a soft radius that drags, whether it breaks; at a scale of 1, drawn round the same parts as its build), so the race strikes what is seen; `Placed.h` sets a piece on the ground's height. `/dev/models?section=sites` shows them in a temperate and a desert palette.
  - Load on the device (the map is drawn on the player's device already; the server only sends the world): what costs frames there is draw calls and triangles, so the new ground is instanced, and the detail follows the device (`useQuality`: "lite" on a phone or a device with few cores or little memory, lowered when the render-scale monitor has taken the scale down to 0.75, and fixed by the "Detail" button on the map strip: Auto, Lite, Full; lite plants fewer trees and no flowers or urns). No download from the home page: nothing is added that a phone would have to fetch, and an installable page (Phase 4) would only help the load time, not the frames.
  - Old worlds (`migrateSites`, life 12, run by `livenWorld` when Play opens a campaign): in each built city or town the roofs, catwalks and second ways in of the old districts are taken down (a party on a roof or catwalk is put back at the start), the listed buildings are set out as `SPOTS` has them, what a city is missing is added, a transit hub or shipyard it had is taken into the spaceport's site, each site gets its satellites, the cantina and palace get the rooms the plans now have (what is there stays), and the districts are planned again.
- Streets, hangars and encounters (2026-10-19; commit series on `revive` after a9a7fd1). House rules and setting; the books give none of it.
  - Heights: a floor of a building is `LEVEL_H` = 2.8 squares (it was 2): walls, roofs, stairs, pedestals, the floors above and the district fight's heights all follow. A room with a mezzanine (`layout.tall`, a gallery at half a floor) is two floors high: the renderer had ignored the flag, so figures on the gallery (floor at 1.4 now) had their heads through the roof. Seen from the gallery the host is still cut down (`lookedFrom`). A ship in a hangar under a sliding roof is kept under it (`ShipModel`'s `ceiling`). Curtain walls and the 0.8 cut-away walls of an opened room are as they were.
  - Hangars (shared "Ships coming and going": `voyageTimes`, `visitorAt`, `bayStage`, `bayBerth`, `bayDoor`; `BayBerth` and `HangarRoof` in LocalMap.tsx; `withPresence` also returns `based`, the ships that belong to each room). A berth's ship is at home or away by the clock: a voyage's ship by its own schedule, and the berths kept for visitors (`code:berths:<n>`) by a cycle of their own (16 to 48 hours, 5 to 16 of them with a ship in, a different ship each time; seeded by room and berth, nothing is stored). A ship that comes in descends from above the roof over `BAY_MOVE` (6) minutes and one that leaves rises out; the roof doors slide back `BAY_DOOR` (3) minutes before and shut as many after, and are shut at every other time (4% of the time in the play-through's scan). The map reads the minute between the clock's ticks (`minuteNow`). A visitor is not for sale or taking (its card says so). Not done: the party's own ship landing or taking off is not drawn this way (a state change, as before); nobody is drawn aboard a ship.
  - Street scenery (`ambientBlockers`, `ambientCover`, `ambientBackdrop` in siteAssets.tsx; the `street` memo in `BuildingScene`): the speeder races' trees, boulders, shrubs, ferns, rocks, grass, flowers and reeds, by the world's biome. Only trees and boulders block (130 at most, kept four squares apart, two squares clear of every way in, signpost and thing that stands about, and off the streets between buildings, a palace's grounds and its gardens), and they do not depend on the picture quality; the low cover (150 to 420 pieces) and a belt of trees beyond the ground (80 to 220, only to be seen) do. The old one-model-each trees and rocks are gone from `DECOR`.
  - Encounters are people (`Encounter.phase`; shared "The people in a district"; `Wanderer`, `AlleyWatch`, `AlleyBlock`, `Delivery` in LocalMap.tsx; `answerEncounter`, `ambush`, `deliverEncounter`, `startGangFight` in GameBoard). A person in need, a fixer or a master (`rollEncounter`, 12% an hour, only while the party is on the street, `ENCOUNTER_GAP` = 4 hours after the last one ended; it was 40% an hour, anywhere) comes in from a way out of the district and walks up to the party (`route`, the same walking the party does); only then is anything asked (a short panel, no dice). One in need follows the party (`warp`: one left behind catches up) to the building they must reach (a place of the district, named in the panel); going in at its way in, or standing by it, with them close behind pays 50 credits a level and +1 renown. The fixer's job and the master's lessons are as before.
  - The gang in the alley: in a lawless place (`lawAt` 0) walking two squares into an alley of the district's plan (`alleysOf`, 8 squares long at least) may bring a gang (`ambushIn`, 45% for that alley that hour, then 4 hours apart). It starts at both ends of the alley between the buildings (`gangStarts`), closes in and stands round the party (`gangSpots`); every open square round the alley is barred (the blockade's own `barred` squares and `Barricade`-style crates, `AlleyBlock`), doors included while they hold the party (`gangHolds`), and a walk out answers "The gang bars the way out of the alley." The panel (once the leader has come up) offers "Fight the gang" or "Pay the toll" (a tenth of the credits).
