The gang is from `makeGang` (`POST /api/world/gang`): a boss for the party's level (Wookiee Berserker, Trandoshan Warrior, Trandoshan Hunter) and two to four Tusken Raiders as street toughs. The fight is on the district's own map in the alley (`districtArena`); when the leader falls the rest run (`breakGang` in useCombat.ts: `Combatant.gang`, `leader`, `fled`, hit points 0, not counted among the slain, drawn no more), which ends the fight as a victory. A win pays the boss's purse (25 credits a level, no crime: the party was set on) and the experience of those struck down; left for dead, the gang takes its toll; leaving the fight leaves the gang there.
  - Test switches: `localStorage["sw5e.noEncounters"] = "1"` stops anyone coming up to the party and any gang (the play-through sets it, "0" turns it off for the steps about them); `window.__bay(roomId, minute)`, `window.__street()` and `window.__street.ambushHours(alley, day)`, `window.__folk` are read by the new steps.
  - Tested (test copy on 5001): e2e "hangars: the roof doors are shut ...", "streets: someone in need walks up ...", "streets: a gang in a lawless alley ..." (and a look at the mezzanine from the gallery and from the street, `/tmp` shots). A bug found on the way: two siblings with one React key (the person and its delivery watch) made the person mount again every render and pile up on the map.
  - Fixed with it: an upper floor that was opened (or a roof) was a slab the size of the room below it, standing in its top and sides; the block below now ends where the slab begins (`sunk` in `BuildingScene`).
  - Tested (2026-10-18, test copy on port 5001, commit 9af3f38): e2e "sites: ...", "sites: by day the market's stalls ...", "ports: the ship puts down in the hangar of its choice ..." (and the ship step now chooses a hangar). Desktop 85 of 86 (the one failure, the 3D fight step, is the render stall: it passed in both phone runs); phone 83 of 86, failing droid, captive-and-hire and beast-and-stable, the same three that fail on the code from before the sites work (80 of 83), so they are not from it. A first phone run also failed the travel and quests steps in one world: policed law and a camera at the cantina entrance made taking the start cache a reported theft, and search squads came (old behaviour). Not done: stallholders' own talk beyond the trade card; the palace's petitioners queue; croupiers and cooks have no scripts; the ticket kiosk is the old passage panel; garrisons, medcenters and workshops have no satellites; the shops' wares are furniture, not items you can take; no cargo is carried or inspected.
- Fights in the open (`districtArena`, `levelsArena`, `watchersAt`, `exposedRooms`; `Arena.platforms`, `links`, `district` and `Combatant.z` in useCombat.ts). House rules; the books give no elevation. Height is in levels of 10 feet; two squares make a level, and the greatest of horizontal and vertical squares is the distance. A ladder or stair costs 10 feet a level, either way. Buildings are blocks with a height that a shot cannot pass; whoever stands on a roof shoots over its edge squares (the squares further in stop a shot to or from the street). Cover is what stands at the target's own height within 5 feet of it. In the street, on a roof or on a catwalk every hostile on the district's roofs and catwalks is a watcher (`watchersAt`: arrival alert, the watch's DC, who bars the way, who joins a fight), and a fight there is fought on the district's own map by the same scene (`DuelPieces`: lit squares at their height, fighters on theirs). A room with a gallery fights as one place (the gallery a raised floor of half a level, the stairs the way up); a hostile building keeps a sentry on its gallery.
- Pits (`furnishLevels`, `trapsIn`, `Path.climb`, `Exit.climb`): some rooms (3 in 10 in hostile or wild places, 1 in 12 elsewhere) have a loose flagstone over a pit (a room of form `pit` below, level -1). Passive Perception notices it from two squares (DC 12 + the room's danger) and Search finds it; stepping on it drops the character a level (1d6, never the last hit point outside a fight) into the pit, a minute spent. The pit has a way out up to the room: Athletics or Dexterity against DC 12, tried again as often as it takes, a minute for each try.
- The master (`pathToTeach`, `teachingFor`, `rollEncounter` with kind `master`). Once the party has renown of 3 or more with the settlement's faction, a master may be waiting in a district (once for each settlement) who teaches the path the character lacks: the Force to one who has tech powers, tech to one who has the Force, and to one who has neither whichever suits their Force alignment and skills (`leaningOf`). The lessons are an at-will power and a power of the first level, of the character's alignment where the path has one (the master's two days of teaching and 200 credits a level), and a pool of power points to cast them from: the character's level plus the casting ability's modifier, at least two, from the same pool a caster's are. Renown is earned by helping people, jobs for fixers and the quests of the settlement's faction. The other encounters of a district are someone in trouble (Wisdom, Medicine, DC 12), a gang that means to rob the party (talk or fight, a brawl reported to the law) and a fixer with a job (a package to take to another building of the district).

- Moving things with the Force (2026-10-18, asked by the user: "the force push/pull power to be able to actually move map assets"; `powerMotion`, `motionFeet`, `motionSummary` in rules.ts; `bulkOf`, `shoveAsset`, `moveRoomAssets` in shared/logic "Moving things with the Force"; `objectReach`, `shoveCreature`, `strike`, `fall`, `sweepObjects` and `castPower(index, choice)` in useCombat.ts; the chooser in PlayCombat; `forceMoves` in GameBoard). What a power moves and how far is read from its text: Force Push/Pull (by size, 20/10/5 ft for Tiny/Small/Medium, more at 5th, 11th and 17th level, Strength save, 1d4 kinetic growing with level when it strikes something, the one moved saving with Dexterity), Force Throw (up to Large, 90/60/30/10 ft, 2d8 force to both when it strikes), Telekinesis (a creature up to Huge 30 ft, then restrained; an object up to 2,500 lb 30 ft), Force Propel (15 lb, 60 ft, 3d8 kinetic with a Dexterity save for half for the one struck), and the pushes and pulls that come with a failed save or a hit (Force Repulse and Force Current push 5 ft, Overload 10 ft, Telekinetic Wave 5 ft, Wire Line, Wire Tow and Grasping Vine pull), with the small objects an area power sweeps aside (Repulse: Small; Current: Medium). HOUSE RULES, the books give furniture no size or weight: size from the footprint and the kind of piece (one square Medium, or Small for a footlocker, parts bin, cart or low table; two to four squares Large; more, or three in a line, Huge; nine Gargantuan), weight from size (Small 40 lb, Medium 150, Large 600, Huge 3,000, Gargantuan 12,000; stone four times), and built-in pieces do not move at all (counters, the bandstand, booths, bunks, consoles and terminals, tanks, cells, pillars, masts, panels, cameras, kiosks, seats on a deck, the boarding ramp, workbenches; a building's block on a district's map). A moved piece goes a square at a time along the line (a diagonal step counts as one and cannot squeeze past a corner), stops before a wall, another piece or anyone standing in the way (striking them), drops off the edge of a raised floor, and never stops where it would cut the floor or block a way in or out (`floorFaults` may not go up). Out of a fight: a tap on a piece opens its card (size and weight, and for each power the character knows that can move it, Push and Pull, with its cost and reach); it moves away from the character or towards them, stopping short of people, is saved in the room, and glides there on the map. In a fight: a tap on a loose piece picks it out (a blue outline); a moving power's button opens a chooser: push or pull the piece, hurl it at the enemy picked out, or push or pull that enemy; a creature is moved after its save, strikes walls, pieces and others, and goes over the edge of a roof or gantry (1d6 per 10 feet and prone, Player's Handbook "Falling"). What a fight moved stays in the room (`moveRoomAssets` when the fight ends); on a district's streets only the crates set out as cover can be moved (they are drawn now; what stands on the roofs comes from the world and stays put) and they are set out afresh next fight. Not done: lifting creatures upward with Telekinesis, Crush and Choke's bonus-action moves, objects striking what is below when they fall, the area powers' pushes on more than the one target the fight engine resolves.

- Water worlds, swimming and water craft (2026-10-30, house rules; see "Caves, ceilings, vaults, round buildings, water worlds" below): every city, town and outpost of an ocean world stands on the sea (gantries and catwalks, open water round them) or, three in five of those that are not the world's way in, under it (domes on the sea floor; out of doors is the hazard underwater); off the decking a creature swims at half pace unless it has a swimming speed (`swimPace`); the Gungan bongo, a submersible and a hydrospeeder are sold on ocean (and swamp) worlds at a landspeeder's or speeder bike's price.
- Over the water (2026-10-30, house rules; see "Sea crossings and water craft" below): every route between two places of an ocean world is open sea: nobody walks it, rides a beast over it or drives a speeder of the land across it (its repulsors are made for the ground); a water craft goes, the ride for hire is a water taxi (the hired ride's fare and time), and only a lift that flies (an airspeeder) is offered. A water craft goes on no road, except a swamp world's, which it takes by the channels. A trip by water craft is driven as legs of open water, buoyed, with no roadblock; the ambush still comes, on hydrospeeders and bongos. In a place on the water the party's craft is moored by the decking and taken out at three times the walking pace, out to 14 squares past the place's ground; a submersible's crew breathes its air (no hazard underwater at its helm), and a party that comes into a place under the sea in one arrives at its helm; under the sea only a submersible is taken out (a craft of the surface waits on the surface over the place).

### Gathering and materials (2026-10-16, all house rules)
SW5e has no harvesting or mining rules; it has the Scavenging, Bioanalysis and Archaeologist kits, Jeweler's and Tinker's implements, and the Crafting downtime (raw materials worth half the item's value). The game fills the gap (`shared/logic`, "Gathering and materials"):
- Caves, ruins and wrecks hold **resource nodes** (ore, plants, crystals, salvage) as `LootCache.node`, placed by the room codes `N:ore|plant|crystal|salvage` and drawn on the 3D map as a vein, patch, cluster or heap. Worlds made before them get them from `livenWorld` (life 11). Tier 0-3 = Standard to Advanced; DC 10/14/18/22 (the book's blueprint DCs).
- A harvest is a check (10 minutes) with the family's kit (`FAMILIES`); without a kit only tier 0, at +5. Each 5 over the DC and a trained kit add a unit. A node has 2-4 harvests, a natural 1 spoils one, it recovers 3 days after it ran dry.
- Materials are stacks in `playState.materials`. The Crafting activity pays raw materials with them at their value (the dearest unit that fits first) and the rest in credits. **Refine** turns 4 units into one refined unit worth 1.5x the four, with a kit and a check at the tier's DC.
- **Salvage (2026-10-16):** beasts and horrors that fall leave a carcass (a fifth family, hides and parts: `makeRemains`, a node that is worked once and does not come back) in the room, placed on the floor by `withCacheAsset`; a powered-down droid has a "Strip for parts" button (salvage family, the droid goes); a destroyed enemy ship also gives parts worth a twentieth of its value (salvage and ore) beside the credits. The tier follows the Challenge Rating like the book's rare ingredients (`tierOfCr`).
- **What a thing can be made of:** each tool works its own families (`familiesForTool`: Armstech's ore and parts, Biochemist's plants and hides, Jeweler's crystal and ore, Tinker's anything); an enhanced item by its type (`familiesForEnhanced`). Only those families count toward the raw materials; the rest is paid in credits.
- **Who rolls and with which skill (2026-10-16):** every ability check and tool check (`abilityCheck`, `toolRoll`, and Crafting's) opens `components/Play/CheckDialog.tsx`: the player picks the party member (the character, or a companion with a stat block: its bonus is read from the stat block's skills and modifiers) and one of the skills that bear on the check (by ability, or the tool's own), with the bonus shown for each; a trained tool adds its proficiency (no stacking with the skill's) and, with a trained skill, gives advantage (the existing house rule). "Not now" calls the check off without consequence. A box in the dialog turns it off for good (the character then rolls with the best skill; local storage key `sw5e.autoChecks`); the play-through test sets it. Downtime activities other than Crafting still roll with the character's own `checkFor`.
- Not done: species/background gathering perks (Defel, Scavenger...), hazards, gathering in settlement districts, and a browser (e2e) step for harvesting and for the dialog.

- Low fuel and the tanker (house rules, 2026-10-24; `isLowFuel`, `LIMP_FACTOR`, `tankerCall` in shared/logic, the helm's LOW FUEL banner): a ship at 0 fuel limps: it flies any trip its crew could make at all, at 3 times the time, and burns nothing. From the helm a tanker can be called: 4 hours, it sells 2 units of fuel at the size's price, and its call-out is 2 more units at that price. The fuel attendant of a refuelling depot sells fuel in the trade panel to a ship put down in the hangar.

### Jumping, climbing, falling and gravity (2026-10-26; the user: "a jumping mechanic and gravity ... jet packs, climbing, grapple hooks ... falling damage (depends on armour, and falling speed, jet packs need a burst before hitting the ground)")
The books' rules (Player's Handbook: long jump = Strength score in feet after 10 ft. on foot, half standing; high jump 3 + Strength modifier, half standing, reaching up the jump plus 1½ times the jumper's height; climbing costs double without a climbing speed; a fall 1d6 kinetic per 10 ft., at most 20d6, prone unless unhurt) are in `jumpReach`, `fallPlan`, `fallOutcome` (shared/logic, after "Climbing the outside of a building"). House rules, said on screen where they apply:
- **Gravity** (`gravityOf`): standard on most worlds, 0.9 or 1.1 by the world, 0.7 on barren worlds, 1.5 out of doors on a gas giant's platforms (where the gravity hazard is; indoors the plating keeps it standard); aboard a ship or with a gravity generator running, standard. Jumps go as far divided by the pull; a fall hurts as for its height times the pull; falling speed is 500 ft. a round times the pull. A fight in a pull other than standard says so in its log.
- **Armour**: heavy armour lands harder, d8s instead of d6s.
- **Jetpacks**: flight or a repulsor pack running sets the creature down unhurt; a jetpack or rocket boots carried but not running can be fired in a burst before the ground (in a fight its reaction): Dexterity (Acrobatics), DC 10 + 1 per 10 ft. of the fall (times the pull) past 30; success, no damage; failure, half.
- **A jump down on purpose**: Dexterity (Acrobatics) DC 10 takes one die off.
- **Height for reach**: Tiny 1½ ft., Small 3, Medium 6, Large 12, Huge 24, Gargantuan 48.
- **In a fight** (`reachable` and `walk` in useCombat; the lit squares are green for walking, blue for a jump, climb or flight, orange where the move ends in a fall): a flyer goes to any height beside it at 10 ft. a level; a wall beside the creature is climbed to the floor over it (a roof's edge, a catwalk) at double cost when it is within the reach of a high jump, at the feet with a climbing speed (friction grips), or higher with a grappling hook's line (the hook thrown with the action; the line is the hook's plus the cables carried); the same ways down; a long jump crosses a gap between floors of one height (the run-up: 10 ft. moved this turn before it); anything else off an edge is a jump down that ends the move. Creatures run by the computer never jump down more than a level.
- **Out of a fight**: a balcony or an upper window is left with a jump when nothing else will do ("Jump down (N ft.) to ..."); a failed climb up a hook's line by 5 or more slips from halfway; the pit trap's fall follows the same rules. As before, a fall out of a fight never takes the last hit point.
- Tests: `tests/items/jumping.test.ts` (7), `tests/items/climbing.test.ts` (jump down).

### Jumping, hooks, jetpacks and climbing outside a fight; every roof (2026-10-27; the user: "needs to ALSO be applied OUTSIDE of fight scenes. ALL roofs should be accessible via these methods ... add a simple animation for climbing up/down ladders, the 'magic-ly appearing on a roof' is a little jarring")
House rules (shared/logic "Climbing the outside of a building", `climbWay`, `outsideClimbs`, `verticalMove`):
- **Which roofs**: every roof room of the building the party stands in front of, with or without a ladder (before, only balconies had gear ways; roofs had their ladders). Up: flight (a jetpack or rocket boots running) and friction grips need no check; a **running leap** when the character's jump reaches the edge (`jumpReach` high jump + 1½ times their height, with a run-up, in the world's pull; one storey is 10 ft.) is a Strength (Athletics) check, DC 10, missed by 5 or more a fall from half the height, else back down a minute later; a grappling hook's line (50 ft. and the cables carried) the same check as before. Down: the same gear, a repulsor pack's slow drop, or a jump (a fall: `fallPlan`). The ladder stays.
- **Seen**: `verticalMove` (a ladder, a roof's way up or down, a balcony only by gear) makes GameBoard `leaveBy` ask the map (`useTransit`) to walk the party to the foot of the way (the ladder's, or for gear the nearest edge with open ground under it) and carry the figure: a ladder or grips hand over hand, a line with a rope to a hook on the edge, flight with a flame, a leap (spring, hang, haul), a jump down (fall). The path is `interiors/transit.ts` (pure, tested); the party stands where it ends (`usePartySpot`) and is held there until the area changes. About 1.4 s a storey for a ladder (0.9 for flight); a build with VITE_TEST_MENU=1 slows it with localStorage sw5e.transitSlow.
- Not done: no way onto a palace roof (the palace has no roof room: its towers are manned from inside); jumping across to a neighbouring roof; windows above the ground floor by gear; the companions do not climb with the party (they appear with it).

### Games at the table, jobs from people, bets on the pit (2026-10-27; the user's long list: gambling tables with real games, pit-fight betting, downtime only in the test version, mission-giver NPCs)
House rules, all pure and tested (tests/games, tests/world/contracts.test.ts):
- **Pazaak** (`shared/logic/pazaak.ts`): the user's pasted rules: a 40-card main deck, 4 side cards each from a chosen 10, 20 wins, first to three sets. **Sabacc** (`sabacc.ts`): the classic 76-card deck, draw/stand/swap with a Sleeper's procedure, Pure Sabacc +-23, at most three raises a hand. **Dejarik** (`dejarik.ts`): the No Name Publishing copyleft rules (see CREDITS.md), the ratings read from the creatures' descriptions. The AIs are `sabaccAi` and friends.
- **Sabacc table on the map**: tap the table, the party walks to a free stool and sits; Life fills the other stools with patrons and a croupier deals (`useTable`, `lifeLink`); a stake panel in GameBoard buys chips in at 20 times the stake and cashes out on leaving. Pazaak and Dejarik are played with a person who has the set ("Play it properly"); the old five-check game stays as "Quick game". Card art is our own SVG (`components/Games/cards.tsx`).
- **Jobs** (`shared/logic/contracts.ts`): bounties, robberies, data jobs, den-clearing and gang jobs from bartenders, officers, bounty hunters, smugglers and informants; the giver's tier opens at 1/3/6 jobs done for them. Accepting places the target and guards, the locked cache or the gang in the world (`Occupant.contract`); a bounty target is found by "Ask around" checks (known first only to the system, the world or the town by tier); done is read from the world, never flagged; Intimidation to take a target alive pays a quarter more.
  - **Few give work** (2026-11-02; the user: "too many NPCs offer side quests ... we need to reduce the number of NPCs that offer quests"; HOUSE RULE, `giversIn`, `workFrom`, `GIVERS_PER` in contracts.ts): of everyone in a settlement whose role could give work, only 4 do in a city, 2 in a town and 1 anywhere else (the user's numbers; a generated city had 75 who could). The same people every visit (a hash of settlement and person, nothing stored), one of each sort before a second of any (a bar, the law, the shady, a bounty hunter). The rest still talk and trade.
- **Downtime**: the Downtime tab exists only in `VITE_TEST_MENU=1` builds. In the production build the activities are reached through the people who give them (`jobsOf`: a pit boss's pit, a dockmaster's races, ...) in the Trade tab.
- **Pit bets** (`pitbets.ts`, `components/Games/PitBets.tsx`): three bouts a day between creatures of near challenge rating; the odds come from 240 fights played out, less a 5% house cut (never below 1.05 to 1); one bet a bout a day.
- Not done: Chance cubes, Kirgatz and Chatta-ragul as games; a mercenary "defend" job; the speeder travel overhaul (a point-to-point road with biome shifts, roadblocks, gunner/driver swap, scenery collisions): travel by pilot still flies the old ring run; the party does not sit at tables in the eye views; the other tables still have standing patrons.

### Rarity, smaller stock, master artificers, rare rewards (2026-11-02; the user: "Stallholders/shop vendors offer too many items, these should be limited (as relevant to their solar system), we also need a rarity applied to items, weapons, armour, etc. (the most rare items should be available through mission rewards, and NPCs who can apply rare upgrades (etc.) should be few and far between, and unlocked through high reputation with these characters")
All HOUSE RULES (the user chose the numbers on 2026-11-02); shared/logic "Rarity of the goods" and "Master artificers", tests in tests/items/vendors.test.ts and tests/world/contracts.test.ts.
- **Rarity** (`gearRarity`, `RARITY_NAMES`): enhanced items keep the books' rarity; plain gear, which the books give none, is Standard under 1,000 cr, Premium from 1,000, Prototype from 3,000, a lightweapon a step rarer. Shown, coloured, on trade lines (`RARITY_TONE` in GameBoard), in the inventory and on job offers.
- **What a place deals in** (`tradeTop`): Standard in an outpost or village, Premium in a town, station or city, Prototype in a city of the Core worlds; the black market a step more (Prototype at most). It caps a trader's plain goods, a technician's modifications, a surgeon's augmentations and a droid dealer's customizations (before: up to Advanced in a Core city).
- **Smaller stock of the system's goods** (`stockOf`, `systemCarries`): 2 to 4 lines a stall, 4 to 7 a shop or counter (was 5 to 14; modifications and droid parts 4 to 7, was 5 to 9). Each star system carries its own share of the plain goods, the same every visit: 55%, +25% for what its world needs (DEMAND), +15% in the Core worlds, -15% on the Outer Rim and beyond, -10% a rarity step; goods named for its own world always.
- **Rare rewards** (contracts.ts `REWARD_RARITY`, `rewardItem`): a regional job pays a Prototype item on top of the credits, a far one Advanced, a notorious one Legendary (a weapon, armour, shield, modification, augmentation, focus or gear of that rarity, by the job's id). A job reported also raises the giver's regard (`CHANGE.helped`, +25; it was defined but never given).
- **Master artificers** (`artificerIn`, `artificerStock`, `ARTIFICER_REGARD`; GameBoard `modsPanel`, `artificerChassis`): about one city in four (a hash of the city) has one, the first technician of its workshop (else tech shop, armsmith, droid dealer, shipyard, outfitter). They sell modifications and chassis above the shops: Prototype to anyone, Advanced once friendly (regard 40, reachable by trade), Legendary once devoted (80, needs deeds: jobs), never Artifact; a quarter dearer. They work a carried chassis into an item without a check (eight hours, twice a modification's fee). A master artificer is always one of the city's few job-givers (espionage), so their regard can be earned.

### The arena district, and people who talk to one another (2026-11-02; the user: "We need NPCs that talk to one another ... Pit fighting and betting should take place in a physical arena (its own district), with a very large, round, amphitheatre style building, with a viewing platform (populate this with NPCs with cheering animations), and a room with betting kiosk (and relevant NPCs), and a room for signing up to the pit fights (and relevant NPCs)")
- **The arena** (shared/logic: `SETTLEMENTS` "arena" in every city, "arena?" in a town; `SITES.arena` = [], a district of its own; `SPOTS.arena`; `BUILDINGS.arena`; tests in tests/world/layouts.test.ts). Three rooms: the **Arena floor** (the way in; always round or oval, `linkRounds` forces it and the planner centres the ways into it; 24 squares across, three storeys under a dome in the eye views, `vaultOf`; sand, `floorOf`), the **Betting hall** (a betting kiosk, an odds board, two bookmakers, punters) and the **Fighters' sign-up** (the sign-up desk, the fight roster, the pit boss, pit fighters), both off the floor through corridors (with the gate as a room of its own the corridor to the floor was hemmed in by the two rooms placed first, and the floor was left with no way in).
- **The stands** (`standsOf`, `standRow`, `STAND_ROWS` 3, `STAND_STEP` 0.45, `isStand`): every square of the floor's outer ring is a "Viewing stand" (it blocks: nobody walks there), three rows rising to the wall, broken by an aisle at each way in; notches where the floor would be pinched are filled, and any pocket the middle cannot reach is stands too (the access and layout audits pass). They are drawn as one instanced tier piece a square (siteAssets `arena_tier_<row>`), not a model each. The fighting pit (a disc of ochre clay with torch posts, walked on) is in the very middle.
- **The crowd** (room code `A`, 14 to 20 `spectator`s placed on stand squares by `placeContents`): on the map they are Life's `spectator` brain (LocalMap): drawn on top of their tier, they face the pit, cheer in bursts (limbs.ts `standActPose` "cheer": both arms thrown up and pumping, a bounce), the whole crowd roars together every 10 to 22 seconds with a shout or two, and neighbours talk.
- **The pit and its bets are the arena's** (contracts.ts `jobsOf`): the pit boss at the sign-up desk offers "fighting in the pit" (the Pit arena run, PitArena.tsx), a bookmaker "bets on the pit" (PitBets); bartenders, croupiers and gamblers no longer offer either (they keep games of chance and a night out). A pit bout is fought on open sand (`launchBout`: `open`, floor "arena") with the stands and a cheering crowd round it (LocalMap `PitCrowd`, `FightScene crowd`).
- **Older worlds** (life 15, `addArenas` in `livenWorld`): every built city, and one town in two (by a hash), gets its arena, and the settlement's districts are planned again.
- **Talking to one another** (relations.ts `smallTalk`, `talkPlace`, HOUSE RULE flavour; LocalMap Life `converse`): two people of the room who meet take turns at two to four lines of small talk by place (the arena: bouts and bets; a cantina: drink and rumour; a market: prices; guards: their watch; else the day's news), facing each other, the speaker's hand gesturing (limbs.ts "talk", laid over the arms only, `overlayAct`; also seated). It starts when a patron visits another or a musician mingles on a break, between people at one table, two of the staff behind one counter (a pair of bookmakers), and neighbours on the stands; walking off ends it.
- Checked in the browser (dev 5099, campaign 626, test database): the life-15 migration gave Theed and Kaadara arenas; the floor seen in the shoulder view and from above (stands, crowd cheering on the tiers, pit, torch posts, dome), conversation bubbles between spectators and their shouts, the bookmaker's bets, the pit boss's "fighting in the pit", a bout opened on the sand with the cheering crowd round it. NOT watched: conversations in a cantina or market (the browser pane was hidden by then, and a hidden pane draws no frames); `npm run e2e`.
- Not done: nobody fights in the pit unless the party does.

### The arena, second design: tiers over the floor, pens under it (2026-11-02; the user: "Tiers should be mezzanine levels ... the pit boss's room should contain a variety of cages/pens to hold fighting beasts, and be entirely separate from the spectator entrance. The arena fighting floor should ONLY be accessed from the pit boss room ... 3-4 levels of spectator tiers in big cities, 1-2 in smaller settlements ... [the pit boss's room] BELOW (basement level) the fighting arena with 2 separate (switchback) ramps leading UP to opposite ends of the arena")
- **Replaces the stands** (`standsOf`, `isStand`, `standRow`, `STAND_ROWS`, `STAND_STEP` are gone). `planBuilding` hands an arena to `planArena` (shared/logic, "The arena"), which lays the whole building out itself. `BUILDINGS.arena` rooms: Betting hall, Arena floor, Fighters' pens (was "Fighters' sign-up"), Fighters' gate; the Spectator tiers are added by `arenaTiers` (city 3, 4 in the rich regions; town 1 or 2; remembered in a `tiers:N` tag). `arenaSize`: floor radius 7-8, tiers 4 squares deep, a city arena 40-48 squares across.
- **Tiers** are rings on one footprint, a storey each (`level` 1..n), the open middle `layout.well` growing outward tier by tier so the floor is in sight over the rail of the tier below (the test checks the sight line with eye 0.57 and rail 0.36 storeys). The crowd (`spectator`s) stands at the rail, the two rows nearest the middle. Spectators come in by the betting hall and go up a stair tower (a switchback a storey) tier by tier; they never reach the floor or the pens.
- **The floor** is round at the ground with the pit in its middle; its only ways in are two switchback **ramps** (`ramp: true`, drawn as slopes) up from the pens, west and east. The **pens** are one storey down (`level` -1) under the floor and the south half: the sign-up desk and pit boss, fighters, a roster, weapon rack, feed trough and 3-4 **Beast pen** cages (models.tsx; LocalMap draws a beast of `PEN_BEASTS` in each). The **gate** is the fighters' street entrance on the far side from the hall, with a flight down to the pens. No path joins the hall's side and the fighters' side.
- **Rendering** (LocalMap `isArenaBowl`): the floor and its tiers are one bowl, seen together from above.
- **Older worlds** (life 16, `rebuildArena` in `livenWorld`): the hall, floor and pens (old sign-up) keep their rooms and people; the old floor's crowd goes to tier 1; the gate, tiers, stairs and ramps are made new.
- Tests (tests/world/layouts.test.ts, "the arena (2026-11-02 ...)"): tiers, sight lines, round floor, pit in the middle, the two ramps, pens under the floor, reachability from the hall vs the gate, and the life-15 rebuild.
- Checked in the browser (dev 5099, campaign 625, test database): Rakessos Arena (3 tiers) from above: round floor with pit, rails with the crowd on the tiers, east and west ramps; the pens (after a few seconds' load): cages with beasts, pit boss and fighters, two ramps, the stairs. No console errors. Sulolon Arena (2 tiers) floor from above also fine. NOT checked: walking up the ramps and stairs in play; a pit bout opened on the new floor; the crowd cheering; a phone; `npm run e2e`.

### No type errors, no build warnings (2026-11-02; the user: "We have many legacy errors ... I would prefer NOT to have warnings or errors (even silent or no-impact)")
- `npx tsc --noEmit` went from 363 errors to 0, and now covers `tests/` and `tools/*.ts` as well (tsconfig). `vite build` prints nothing (no eval, dynamic-import or chunk-size warnings, no old browserslist note); the browser console on Home, character creation (every tab), the character page, campaigns, the map, Play and the GM tab shows no warnings (the React Router v7 flags are set in main.tsx).
- Fixed in used code: the zustand middlewares (`withHistory`, `withValidation`: typed as pass-through, which cleared 70 errors in the character store; the unused `createStore`/`withDefaultMiddleware` helpers went), the character form pages, `useCharacterForm` (missing `useMemo`; the performance stub now has `measureOperation`/`measureAsyncOperation`), `useDice` (called an audio function that does not exist: now `playSound("hit")`), the LLM `campaignGenerator` (`require` in an ES module), the unified Character schema (bonds, ideals, flaws, personality traits, power prerequisite, which the database already had), `Stock.tsx` (a haze-finished model part would have crashed), `routes.ts` (the "missing column" fallbacks, which could not work), `@types/howler`.
- Deleted at the user's choice (nothing imported them; git history keeps them): about 45 unused files with errors, among them the old CharacterCreator and its pieces, `lib/api/sw5eApi.ts`, `lib/core`, `useCharacterStore`, `persistence.ts`, `combatmanagers.ts`, `server/middleware/auth.ts`, `migrate-with-verification.ts`. About 50 more unused files with no errors (shadcn `ui/*` parts and small helpers) are still there.
- New: `shared/data/text.ts` (`summarize`, so that the client no longer pulls in the dataset loaders).

### The pit's weekend card, bets by ticket, staff at their posts, underwater bubbles (2026-11-02; the user: "We need a pit boss NPC serving (signing up fighters) at the desk in the pit boss's room. We need NPCs manning the betting kiosks and refreshment stalls. we also need NPC fights in the arena, these should be at regular intervals and accurately reflected in the betting options (trade tab with betting kiosk NPCs). Pit fights should only be fought on weekends (day 6 & 7, and their multiples). Results of pit fights between high level combatants should be reflected in NPC conversations for 2-3 days after the bout (NOT all NPC chat should reference pit fights ...) upcoming bouts should be advertised at info terminals and be discussed between a few NPCs on the friday prior. Underwater settlements/districts should NOT cause players to drown when WITHIN the bubble of buildings. Players should not begin their quests in underwater locations UNLESS their species is aquatic. Party members should be drawn swimming if the environment requires it.")
- **Staff at their posts** (`placeContents`, shared/logic): the arena's stallholders stood behind the bookmakers' kiosks (a stall against a wall has no room behind it, so they fell back to any counter's back); now a stallholder or bookmaker takes a counter of their own trade that no one of it stands at yet, from behind it or, where it has no behind, from its side (`ownCounters`, `besideOwn`); the pit boss takes the "Sign-up desk". Worlds from before (life 17, `livenWorld`) have the betting hall and pens placed again.
- **The calendar and the card** (shared/logic/pitbets.ts "The weekend card"; HOUSE RULES, the books keep no calendar of the pit): days run in weeks of seven, day 1 a Monday (`weekdayOf`, `isBoutDay`: days 6, 7, 13, 14, ...; "their multiples" read as every week's sixth and seventh). Five bouts a fight day at 12, 14, 16, 18, 20 (`BOUT_HOURS`), each fought in half an hour (`BOUT_LENGTH`), its result known at the end. `cardFor(arena, day, monsters)` is pure and fixed by the arena and the day (cached); each arena has a stable of eight named fighters with stat blocks of their own for good (`stableOf`: its own pit fighters, then ring names, from CR 1 to 12 times a size factor of its tiers), and its beasts are anonymous (a stat block of the rules). Pairings are tried until neither side is under 15% (`chanceOf`, as for any bet), odds less the house's twentieth; the result is a fight played out with the bout's own dice (`fight`). Officers, rulers and the like of the stat blocks are not gladiators (`gladiatorsOf`).
- **Bets are tickets** (PitBets.tsx, GameBoard `jobsPanel`; the old three-bouts-a-day bet is gone): the bookmaker's panel (Trade tab, "Ask ... about bets on the pit") lists today's and the next fight day's card with the odds; a ticket is bought before the bout (10 credits or more, stake taken at once), kept in `playState.pitTickets` (a week at most), and collected at a kiosk once the bout is over: the stake times the odds it was bought at if its side won; a lost one is torn up. The pit boss's sign-up for the party's own fights (`openPit`) is for fight days only.
- **The bouts are fought on the floor** (LocalMap `PitBout`; both the opened floor and the floor seen from a tier): while the clock is within a bout's half hour the two fighters are in the pit, in from the ramps, trading blows, the loser going down at the end; who wins is the card's (the same as the bets are paid by). The clock store `useTalkWorld.matches` holds today's matches for the settlement (GameBoard, every half hour of the clock).
- **Talk of the pit** (`pitNews`, `pitGossip`, `pitLines`; LocalMap `pitTalk` at both talk sites): only bouts between two of challenge rating 4 or more are talked of, one per day (the headline, by challenge rating); a talk is of it by its day: 3 in 10 the evening of the bout, 1 in 5 the day after, 1 in 10, 1 in 20, then none (Monday 0.2, Tuesday 0.1, Wednesday 0.05 for a Sunday bout); in the arena itself three times as often; on the Friday before, 1 in 8 for each day's headline bout. The model is told the news (`news`), the stock lines are used when it is not running.
- **Advertised at info terminals** (GameBoard, the kiosk card of `useKindOf` "kiosk": street info kiosks, notice boards and now the arena's odds board): the settlement's arenas' card for this and the next fight day, with results for the bouts that are over.
- **No drowning inside a bubble** (shared/logic `hazardsAt`, LocalMap `swimsAt`, siteAssets `UnderSea`): a place under the sea is one bubble of air round its buildings, streets and all (a translucent dome over the whole ground; kelp, fish and rising bubbles are outside it); the party walks it and swims only where the water is (off the decking of a place on the sea, at the helm of a craft). The "sea round X" hazard is gone; wrecks and ruins of the sea floor keep `underwater`.
- **A job or quest under the sea is offered to all, with a warning** (`submergedAt`, `breathesWater`, `worldQuests(world, earnings, aquatic)`, `Quest.warning`, contracts `needsGear`, `GEAR_WARNING`, `Contract.warning`, `accept(..., aquatic)`; /api/world/generate takes `options.species`; 2026-11-02, the user: such work "SHOULD be available for non-aquatic species, BUT MUST contain a warning that sub-aquatic gear is required"): the sea floor's wrecks and ruins stay on offer, but unless the species breathes air and water (13 of them) the quest (Quests tab, the new-campaign screen) or the job (on the offer before it is taken, and on the job taken) says in red that sub-aquatic gear is required. This replaces the first reading, which left such sites out. Campaigns made before keep their quests as they were (no warning on them).
- **Drawn swimming**: followers on foot (not beasts, not droids) take the swimming pose when the party swims (LocalMap `Follower`), co-op peers' swimming is shared (`partyHeight.s` 3 and the water height), the grid fight on open water draws everyone on foot swimming (`swimmerIn`), and so do the party's allies in the real-time fight (Shooter `swimAt`).
- Tests: tests/games/pitbets.test.ts (calendar, card, tickets, news ratios, staff at posts), tests/items/hazards.test.ts (the bubble, aquatic species), tests/world/water.test.ts and tests/world/contracts.test.ts (the gear warning).
- Checked in the browser (dev on `sw5e_test`, campaign 625): Saturday 16:16, the 16:00 bout's two fighters in the pit; a bet bought at the bookmaker, the clock waited out, the ticket shown as won and 99 credits collected (50 at 1.98:1); the odds board's card with results matching the ticket; the talk bubbles in the arena naming the 18:00 bout's winner; Sulolon (a town under the sea): the dome over the ground and the party walking on the sea floor, 30/30 hit points for 27 minutes of the clock; Rakessos (on the sea): walking into the water starts the swimming pose and a sea beast comes. NOT checked: the swimming pose of followers and fight figures close up (the pane was too small to tell); the Friday-before talk and a Monday's talk; the weekday refusal of the pit boss's sign-up; the staff of an arena in a world made after this change (the unit test covers one); a phone; `npm run e2e`.

### The model makes their talk, people in the street talk, beasts wait apart, palaces in 60% of cities (2026-11-02; the user: "Let's up the roll chance for a palace to 60%, also extend the NPC conversations to people passing in the street, and ensure that these conversations are driven by the Gemma4 (via ollama) llm. Also NPC beasts stand on the exact square at the entrance of the buildings their riders enter, beasts should not loiter on the same square, and should not stand on the square that shares the building entry")
- **Palaces were never lost**: a city's palace was a 50% roll (`"palace?"`); the arena is an extra entry, and the life-15 migration only adds (tests/world/layouts.test.ts now checks every building, district and district plan is kept). The roll is now 60% (`OPTIONAL_ODDS` beside `SETTLEMENTS`; 60 seeds: 21 of 31 cities, 18 at 50%). Only new worlds change: a built city keeps what it has.
- **The model writes what two people say** (server/dialogue.ts `POST /api/chatter` `{a, b, scene, turns}` -> NDJSON `{line: {who, text}}` as each is made, then `{done}`): each line is one request to Gemma 4 E2B with the same style guide as the party's dialogue (so the primed prefix holds: about 3.5 s a line here), the speaker's card with the other person as the one talking to them and the other's last line as what they said, "one short sentence, 15 words at most", 40 tokens; `chatterLine` keeps a sentence or two, 18 words at most, no name or narration. **The party comes first**: one talk at a time in all (a second gets 429), and a talk stops between lines when a reply to a player is waiting. The scene (relations.ts `chatScene`) is where they are (room, building, settlement, planet or district), the hour and what people there talk of.
- **On the page** (client/src/lib/voice.ts `chatter`): one talk at a time on the page too; while it is going nobody else stops to talk. Lines show as they come (a bubble a reading pace long, wrapped); two people wait 25 s for a line, then part. **Only when Ollama or the model is not running** (`/api/dialogue/status`, checked once a minute) are the stock lines of `smallTalk` said instead (HOUSE RULE: flavour, nothing rolls).
- **Street talk** (LocalMap `Strollers`): each walker on foot is now one of the district's people (by name, species, trade and look; topped up with people of the settlement's other districts when few are about at that hour; nobody walks twice, so walkers past them are nobody in particular and do not talk). Two named walkers within a square and a half of each other may stop (a check twice a second, an even chance), face each other and talk; their own clock is held while they stand, so they walk on from where they stopped, and neither stops again for 30 to 70 s. `window.__strolls()` lists the walkers, their ends and doors, who is talking and what is said (for the play-through test).
- **Beasts by a door** (LocalMap `hitchBy`): a rider's beast went between the two squares before the doors, so it stood on the very square of the way in, and two beasts at one door stood on the same square. Now each beast has a hitch of its own by each door: two or three squares out and to the side, never the square before a door or the line straight out from it, never another beast's hitch; the rider walks the few squares to the door and back (up to 5).
- Checked in the browser (dev 5099, test database, campaign 626, Ollama running gemma4:e2b): in the Iron Gambit cantina two patrons talked with model lines ("The synth-ale is hitting just right today. What's got you looking so serious, Pria?"); in Theed Market's street two walkers stopped and talked four model lines; the market's two mounted walkers wait at hitches two squares from their doors, none on a door square or shared. The lane frees within about 4 s when the page walks away from a talk (a script). Not checked: a phone; the fallback with Ollama stopped (only its code path); `npm run e2e`.
- Not done: the people in the `world.moving` crowd and street encounters do not talk; on a slow model, rooms with many pairs (the arena's stands) wait their turn. (Spoken aloud and with their own relationships since: the next section.)

### Their own voices, natures and bonds (2026-11-02; the user: "Yes NPCs should speak aloud in their own voices, they should also have their own reputation/relationships (use our existing ENRage/nemesis system), which impact their dialogue content")
- "ENRage" is not a name in the code; the system meant is the nemesis one (shared/logic/nemesis.ts: personalities and their tempers, hate tiers, notoriety, news) with regard and families (relations.ts). Everything here is built from those, a labelled HOUSE RULE, flavour only (nothing rolls on it), worked out from the world when asked (older worlds have it too; nothing new is saved).
- **A nature** (relations.ts `natureOf`): one of the nemesis personalities (hothead, coward, proud, cold, greedy, loyal, drifter); a nemesis keeps their own, anyone else by their id. Civilians are described in everyday words (`NATURE_WORDS`), a nemesis by their temper's blurb.
- **A bond between two people** (`bondOf`, -100 to 100, read like regard and hate): kin by their family tie ("your son, and you are close"; 15% of families at odds over an inheritance, a marriage or a debt); anyone else by a shared past drawn from the pair, pulled by both natures (`LEAN`: the loyal warm to people, hotheads and the proud less so, two hotheads or two proud ones clash, a drifter cares little either way). 60 and up a close friend, 30 a friend (with a reason: an old friend, the same shift, the same street...), 10 someone they get on with; below, a grudge by the nemesis tiers (`tierOf`: resentful, vengeful, murderous) with a reason (owes credits, cheated at sabacc, talked to the law about a cousin...).
- **What they know of the party** (`partyViewOf`, `townTalk`): their own regard for it; a relative who is the party's nemesis ("Your cousin Vex Vos is vengeful against Gear") or whom it killed; the party's notoriety (`NOTORIETY` names) and the latest nemesis news of that settlement (`rumorsFor`).
- **Into the dialogue** (server/dialogue.ts `cardText`): the speaker's nature and view go in their notes (`talkNotes`), the bond as "WHAT <NAME> IS TO YOU" (named: with "they" the model took the grievance for the party's), and the town's talk as "TALK IN TOWN (gossip; bring it up only if it fits)". NPC talk: the bond is also in the opening moment, and the gossip goes to one talk in three (`gossip` in LocalMap: given every time, the model talked of nothing else). The party's own talk with a person (TalkPanel) now has their nature, a nemesis relative and the town's talk too (`news`), the regard as before. Measured: siblings talk of their lives ("Quiet is good for business, sister"); a vengeful hothead and a greedy Rodian snap at each other ("You always play the greedy one").
- **Spoken aloud** (voice.ts `speak` with `ambient`, LocalMap `voiceLine`): each line in the speaker's own voice (Piper's 904 speakers, by the speaker's id, as in "Voices"), as loud as they are near (full within 3 squares, nothing past `HEAR_SQUARES` 12), and only with the Voice option on. Background talk never plays over a reply to the party (not started while one plays; a reply stops it) and a new line stops the last; it waits for the browser to allow sound (a first tap on the page) rather than hang. The next line waits until this one is heard (at most a slow reading of it). The party's standing reaches the map through `useTalkWorld` (LocalMap; GameBoard sets it).
- Also fixed: a street walker's person was chosen again whenever the world changed (the clock), so a walker could turn into someone else mid-talk; they are chosen once for the district now.
- Checked: tests/world/relations.test.ts (natures, kin and stranger bonds both ways, the grudge tiers and reasons, the party view, the town's talk); the chatter route against the live model (siblings, a grudge, with and without gossip); in the browser (dev 5099, test database, campaign 626, Piper): street talk carrying notes and bonds; in Theed Arena's stands two talks whose four lines were each sent to /api/speech in the speaker's voice. Not heard (the browser pane's sound does not reach me); not checked on a phone; `npm run e2e` not run.

### The trip: speeder travel as a road driven in legs (2026-10-27; the user: "speeder travel overhaul": point-to-point routes, biome shifts, chases and roadblocks, gunner and driver, scenery to collide with)
House rules (`shared/logic/roadtrip.ts`; the road itself is `raceCourse(seed, scenery, 1, { block })` in index.ts, the play is `kind: "road"` of `MinigamePage` in FlightScene.tsx and `trip` / `tripLegDone` in GameBoard.tsx):
- **What it replaces**: "Fly it yourself" (the party's own speeder on a route) was a run of rings. It is now a trip of **2 to 5 legs** (the route's kilometres by seven), each an **open road** from a start gate to a last gate (the loop of the race's generator cut where the last gate stands; no lap back to the start, no mines or pylons, lamps and a banner at the ends). The rides that do not pilot (the auto ride, a hired ride) are unchanged, with the old ambush roll before the trip.
- **Ground changes** (`planTrip`, `BIOME_LINKS`): both ends are the planet's own ground; the legs between wander to what lies beside it (a forest world: temperate, jungle, swamp; a desert's: barren, temperate; ...), and the leg before the last always joins the planet's own. Each leg is its own course with its own seed, trees, sky, gorge, hill or river (the biome's `crossings`). The countdown says what the road runs into (`metText`).
- **What is met** (rolled once for the trip, seeded by the minute and the destination): the road ambush of the old rule (`roadAmbush`), on one of the later legs: the riders are after the party for that leg, which it runs (the party drives or takes the gun; Fire shoots back) or stands and fights (the fight at the roadside, which ends the trip at its end, as before). A **roadblock** on one leg before the last: a checkpoint of the law where `lawAt` is 2 or more (a fee of 10 + 15 x law, DC 11 + 2 x law, +4 when wanted), a gang's toll gate where there is little law (a fee of 40 + 10 x level, DC 12 + level/2). Likelier with less law and when the party is wanted (+0.3).
- **At the roadblock**: crates across the road (a speeder fast enough breaks through them, at a hull cost), barriers to either side and the guards' tent. A party that comes to rest short of it is **stopped**: the race waits and the page offers Pay (not when the law knows the party), Persuasion or Deception (a d20 + the character's bonus against the DC; a failure raises the fee by half and calls off more talk), or Ram it. Paid or talked through: the crates are cleared and the leg goes on. Rammed (stopped and then run at, or simply driven through): the roadblock's riders are after the party on the next leg (a patrol or a gang, `blockPursuit`).
- **Seats**: with a crewmate (the first companion on their feet that is not a beast, `crewmate`: droids drive at 0.97 of a plain pace, others 0.92) the page has Swap seats. The crewmate drives (the autopilot's line at their pace, careful of shortcuts, and it stops short of a roadblock) while the party is at the gun: the pad then **aims** the blaster round the whole circle (up: ahead, down: behind) and Fire shoots with a steadier hand (+25%). Or the party drives and the crewmate's gun fires on the nearest rider in reach (every 1.3 s, 30% at point blank, less true than the party's own aim). Without a crewmate the party drives and fires as before.
- **Time and hull**: the trip takes the ride's minutes, a fifth less for a clean quick drive and up to half as much again for a slow leg or one not finished (`driveMinutes`, par = the road's length at two thirds of the plain speed). The hull is carried from leg to leg. A speeder wrecked on a road with no riders on it is towed on, patched to 4 hull (a wreck costs time, not the trip); with riders after it, the fight at the roadside.
- **Found on the way, and what was done**: the scenery of a race course (trees, boulders, logs, barriers) was **never solid**: the course's lookup of what stands by it was built before the props were placed and never rebuilt, so the "collide with the shape of the model" above never happened. Roads and chases are now laid solid (`raceCourse(..., solid)`: crates, barriers, trees, boulders and logs are struck; a graze costs nothing, a hard hit costs hull; the party on the autopilot takes no damage on a plain road). **Races are left as they were tuned** (not solid): in a bot race over the real shapes a solid wood wrecked 11 of 60 racers and cost the party 7 hull, because the rivals steer round what is on the track but not the trees beside it. Making races solid is one flag (`solid = true` in the race's `raceCourse` call, FlightScene.tsx) plus teaching `rivalInput` to keep to the road.
- **Fix (2026-10-28, the user: travel "automatically transported"; it should open the speeder minigame)**: the trip only opened for the separate "Fly it yourself" ride; the party's plain speeder ride, and the default ride of a map tap or the main exit button, went straight to arrival. `travel` now opens the trip for any unblocked ride with a party vehicle over a route with kilometres (`tripLegDone` re-enters with `cleared`, so it arrives), and the "Fly it yourself" duplicates are gone. Hired rides, lifts, transit and mounts are still instant. Not browser-tested yet.
- Test switches (the test build): localStorage `sw5e.ambush = 1` (every trip ambushed), `sw5e.roadblock = 1` (every trip has a roadblock), `sw5e.autopilot = 1` (the party driven by the autopilot). `/dev/dogfight?game=road&seed=3&biome=forest&block[=toll]&mate&chase` plays one leg.
- Tests: `tests/world/roadtrip.test.ts` (10). Not done: the auto and hired rides still have no legs; a leg's riders are the old stat-block ambushers (no gunner seat for them); no vehicles for the party's companions to ride; the speeder's hull does not stay damaged after the trip.

### Real-time jump, hook and jetpack on the map (2026-10-27; the user: "the entire game is a real-time movement system, ONLY fights are turn based: we need a REAL-TIME aim & fire mechanic for jetpack flight and grapple hook shots PLUS a jump button, space bar, mouse click is activate, phone controls need separate buttons for jump & activate")
`client/src/components/Map/interiors/air.ts` (pure parts and the store, tests `tests/interiors/air.test.ts`), `AirButtons.tsx`, and the `air` block of `BuildingScene` in LocalMap.tsx (`airStep`, `beginJump`, `fireHook`, `fireBurst`, `ignite`, `land`); Play gives the store the gear (`airGear` in GameBoard.tsx) and answers the map's `climb`, `power` and `fall` actions.
- **Keys and buttons**: **Space** jumps (held with a pack, it lights it and climbs), **F** fires the hook, **Left Shift** fires the pack's burst; **E / Enter / a click** activate (as before); R runs, V changes the view. On a phone: Jump (held = thrust), Hook and Burst buttons (the last two only with the gear) beside the stick and Use button in the eye views, and in the same corner over the overhead view. Not read in a dialog or a text box.
- **Jump** (house rule on the Handbook's numbers, `jumpOf` over `jumpReach`): the feet rise the high jump's height (3 + Strength modifier ft., half from a standstill, over the world's pull) in 0.7 s, carried along the way the stick or walk was going by the long jump's distance; what stands low (furniture, benches, crates: `hoppable`) is hopped over when the jump clears a counter's height (about 2 ft. 3 in.: a weak jumper cannot), a wall never. At the wall of the building the party stands before, with a roof one storey up that a leap reaches, the jump is the leap onto it (the same way as the exit button).
- **Hook** (`fireHook`): fired from the hand along the crosshair (eye views), the mouse (overhead, once it has moved) or the way the figure faces, at most its long range (the wrist hook 30/60 ft.; the plain grappling hook is thrown, 30/50). It bites a wall or the ground, not a person (at a person it is a weapon, drawn in a fight). Where it bit by the front building and there is a way up for its line (60 ft. cable) it is the climb of that way (the Athletics check and the animated climb as before); elsewhere the line **reels the party** along it, 15 squares a second, over low furniture, to the last open square before a wall. A cooldown of 1.2 s. The line is drawn from the hand to the hook.
- **Jetpack and rocket boots** (`ignite`, `fireBurst`, `flightOf`): Space held (or Burst) lights the pack if it is carried and not running (a power cell's minutes, as the Pack tab's Switch on does) and climbs at the pack's rate; released, it sinks. W A S D and the stick fly over low furniture at the pack's pace (its flying speed over the walking speed). **Burst** is the aim and fire: a rush of 0.55 s along the crosshair in three dimensions (look up to climb), or along the mouse's line over the ground from above, every 1.6 s. Under a roof the pack stops at the ceiling; out of doors it goes to 4 + feet/6 squares. Beside the front building with a way up by flight, at the roof's height, it is the way up (the animated flight). The pack is put out again after the party has stood 2.5 s (what charge is left is kept: the charge runs only while it is lit), and if it runs dry in the air the party falls from there, rolled as any fall (`fallOutside`).
- **Fixed on the way**: the Wrist-Mounted Grappling Hook (an enhanced item of category AdventuringGear) was not read as a hook at all: the old test looked for names starting "Grappling hook" among the Utility items. `gearUse` now reads any grappling hook or harpoon (cable from "N foot cable", the range from "range of 30/60"), so its 60 ft. line counts for climbs, in a fight (`hookFeet`) and for the shot.
- **Flying onto roofs, and the user's second round (2026-10-28)**: the pack was stopped by a building's wall however high it flew, because a building's squares (`inRoom`) were never passable in the air. Now a square under a building is flown over once the party is higher than what stands on it (`topAt`: a storey of block for each level, a balustrade's 0.6 on a roof or balcony): `overRoof`. Down over a roof or balcony room of the street at its height, the party **alights** on it (`alight` action: any roof of the district, not only the front building's; Play stands the party at the nearest free square of the roof and goes there as a walk to it would, with the trespass sight check). From a roof or balcony, flying off its edge is the way down to the street (`climb`, `down`). The `climb fly` that was the only way before is gone.
- **Crosshair at 174 ft.**: the closed blocks of a building seen from the street had no hit-box (only interior walls were `AIM.solids`), so the crosshair and the hook went through them to whatever lay beyond. Each closed room block now has an unseen `AimBox` of its size, and the box that a raised room stands on is a solid too.
- **The flat wall**: the plain block under a raised room (`lift`) was drawn in the wall's plain colour (x 0.7), not in the room's wall set; it showed wherever nothing stood below it (the blue-grey slab in the middle of the user's street). It takes the wall set now.
- **The reticle's ring** round a target is gone (the tick marks stay).
- **Ground**: an urban world had no ground texture at all (`groundTexture` returned null for it: a flat #1c222b plane, black at night). It has the worn concrete of the floors as ground (copied to `textures/ground/concrete_floor_worn_02`), with its normal map and parallax like every other biome. The night is a little less dark (ambient 0.42 against 0.32, the moon's light 0.24 against 0.14).
- **Sun and moon**: they were set from the clock rounded to five minutes, so they jumped; `WorldSky` takes a `now()` (the hour with the minute's fraction) and moves the suns and moons each frame, and the sun's light too. A moon is drawn 2.6 times larger and about twice as bright, with a stronger earthshine on its dark side.
- **Roofs that are not rooms (2026-10-28, the user: the party fell through them and stuck)**: the top of a building's block (a flat roof, a dome, a hangar's roof: any that has no roof room of its own) had no floor, so a descent through it ended inside the block. Now there is a surface under every square (`surfaceAt`: the block's top, else the ground) and a jump, a flight, a fall or a hook's reel that comes down on it **stands on it** (the `perch` mode): the height follows the roof, W A S D or the stick walk over squares of about that height (a block that is higher is a wall), Space jumps and a held Space lights a pack from there; pushed over the edge, or onto a lower square, the party **falls** to the street (a fall from 10 ft. or more is rolled as any fall). A party found standing inside a block on the ground is put out to the nearest open square of the street. Script `perch.mjs` (scratchpad) came down on a plain block, walked and jumped on it and off its edge.
- Test hooks: `window.__aimInfo()` (the solids and hit-boxes), `window.__airAt(x, y, altitude)` (put the party in the air), `window.__bigMeshes()` (what the big meshes are drawn with). Script `roof.mjs` in the session scratchpad flew over a roof and alighted, and off its edge to the street.

- Checked in a browser: the standing and running jumps, the hook reeling in a room and climbing the Cantina from the street (first person and overhead), the pack's climb, burst and flight to a roof, the leap at a one-storey wall; e2e step "real-time moves" (47 s). Not done: companions do not jump or fly with the party (they follow along the ground); the hook has no use on people (a weapon in a fight); no sound; no hook line drawn after the climb begins (the climb animation has its own).

### Air battles (2026-10-26; the user: "extend the dogfighting mechanic to 'in-atmosphere' flying and air battles (defend settlement against bombing raids, conduct bombing raids against settlements, protect/destroy ground vehicles)")
All house rules (the books give no real-time fight). Four scenarios join the Dogfight downtime activity, flown in the air of the planet the party is on (`airStart` in dogfight.ts; the steps in dogfightSim.ts; the scene in ArenaScene `AirBackdrop`/`GroundPiece`; `/dev/dogfight?scenario=defend&biome=desert&g=1` to look at one):
- **Defend the settlement**: 7 buildings; 3+ bombers (4 bombs each) and 2 fighters come in. Bombers pick a building each, fly their run at 140 m, release when their bombs would land on it (one a pass), go on out and come round again. Won when no bomber has a bomb left; lost when half the buildings are down. Pays 250 for each building kept.
- **Bomb a settlement**: 8 buildings, 4 marked as targets; 3 flak turrets (lasers reaching 420 m, missiles) and 2 fighters. Won when the targets are down. A ship with no bombs is lent a bomb deployer (4 proton bombs).
- **Convoy escort / strike**: 3 haulers (and a flak speeder in the enemy's) drive a road at 14 m/s; escort is won when every hauler left is through, lost when all are destroyed; strike is won when the haulers are destroyed, lost when the rest get away.
- The ground is y = 0: a craft that strikes it is pushed up and takes damage by how fast it came down (more than 6 m/s); foes keep above 45 m; the ceiling is 1,300 m. Bombs fall at 9.8 m/s² times the world's pull (`gravityOf`); the HUD shows where a bomb let go now would land (BOMB), friendly pieces in green, targets as TARGET. Buildings 50 hull, flak 30, vehicles 40 (+6 a tier); they pay 300, 150, 250. Ruins stay on the ground. The foe is the world's antagonist.
- Tests: `tests/ships/airfight.test.ts` (8).

### On the road: ambushes and chases (2026-10-26; the user: "procedural attacks against hunted players (empire (depending on alignment), pirates, enemy factions) while travelling on speeders between planet settlement locations - extend the existing speeder race mechanic to speeder travel")
All house rules (the books give no encounters on the road), in shared/logic "On the road" (`roadAmbush`, `roadAmbushers`, `chaseStart`, `chaseResult`, `CHASE`):
- **Who and how often**: each trip in the party's own speeder along a road (a path with km) rolls once, seeded by the trip and the minute: 6% + 5% for each step of law below strict (lawless roads 21%), + 12% when the authority hunts the party for its side, + 30% when the party is wanted (a search is on). The authority's patrol comes when the party is wanted, or hunted for its side: an Imperial authority (Galactic Empire, First Order) hunts the Light-sided, the Republic the Dark-sided (the character's Force alignment, else Good/Evil); pirates (a swoop gang) work lawless and frontier roads; otherwise the world's antagonist sends riders.
- **The riders**: a patrol is scout troopers (2-4 by level) led by a shore trooper (a mounted trooper from 5th level, a captain from 9th); anyone else is a gang (makeGang). Their leader down, the rest ride off.
- **Run for it**: the speeder race's course and driving (`MinigamePage` kind `chase`), one lap to safety, the riders 70 m behind and a little faster (pace 1.1). A rider in reach (55 m) with the party ahead of it fires every 2.4 s (27% at point blank, less with distance, 1 hull); the party's Fire (button or Space) shoots ahead (75 m, 50%, 2 hull), or back at a rider behind at a third of that. Through the lap: escaped; all riders down: beaten off; the speeder wrecked (or "Leave it"): caught, and those still riding fight.
- **Stop and fight** (or caught): the trip is finished and the fight is on open ground with rocks for cover (`roadFight` in GameBoard); won, the leader's purse is the party's (`gangPurse`) and the riders' experience.
- Test switch (test builds only): `localStorage sw5e.ambush = "1"` ambushes every trip. Tests: `tests/items/roadchase.test.ts` (4). Looked at with a scripted run (ambush dialog, chase, fight at the roadside).

### The rest of the weapon properties (2026-10-26, from the "not yet" list)
Read from the property text (`useCombat` `saveStrike`, `makeAttack`, `beginTurn`; `carriedWeapons`; buttons in PlayCombat `combat-modes`):
- **Bright, Biting, Corruption**: "Strike for a save" makes the target save (Dex, Con, Wis) instead of the attack roll, DC the lower of 8 + the attack bonus and the number; advantage on the attack is disadvantage on the save and back. Failed: the weapon's dice and modifier (house reading: not what features add to a hit), and bright burns again at the start of its turn for the governing modifier, biting puts its next attack before the end of its next turn at disadvantage, corruption stops it regaining hit points (a stat block's regeneration) until the striker's next turn. Biting's disintegration at 0 hit points is not played (a foe at 0 is out anyway).
- **Specialized (bright)**: the property's number is the DC alone (house reading of "bypasses the base and cap"); other specialized properties (shocking, disintegrate) are not played.
- **Penetrating N**: "Penetrate" spends N rounds a shot; cover is a step less (three-quarters to half, half to none), and a creature within 15 ft. behind the target on the line of the shot takes the weapon's damage if the roll would hit it.
- **Switch**: a button cycles the weapon's alternate damage (its dice and each of its types) and back.
- **Smart (15/+2)**: the weapon's own Dexterity score and proficiency bonus in place of the wielder's.
- **Modal**: each named mode is carried as a weapon of its own numbers and proficiency (house reading: switching is the free object interaction).
- **Luminous**: a luminous blade in hand gives disadvantage on hiding in a fight (its light in the dark was already played).
- **Interlocking**: no difference in the fight (separated, the two halves strike as the double weapon's second blow does); not played. Still not done: Respiration Facilitator (the fight's saves do not know the power), the 40 text-only adventuring gear (Mag-lock Boots and Boots of Bounding would fit the jumping and gravity rules next), set bonuses.
- Tests: `tests/items/weaponprops.test.ts` (6); looked at in a browser fight.

### Ships leaving and coming into a hangar (2026-10-26; the user: "Ships ... currently just disappear when they get above the building - this is WRONG - Ships should pitch up and fly out of sight")
`BayBerth` in LocalMap: a ship rises straight through the open roof doors for the first third of its move, then pitches its bow up and climbs away along its heading, faster and faster, out of sight (260 squares); one coming in comes down the same way from astern, nose down, levelling over the bay (`bayStage` gives `leaving`).

### Skies, horizons, globes and the land across a course (2026-10-26; the user: "refactor planets surfaces so distant views from settlements show relevant geographical features hills, mountains, volcanoes, also add clouds. geographical features should be visible and encountered when travelling between locations and on speeder races (down into gorges, over hills, across bridges over water/lava etc. ...) also add sun/s, moon/s, and celestial bodies to the skies. geographical features should show on the planet surface at relevant scale when players are in orbit above a world, also players are currently shown "standing" in space ... Players ships should be drawn - also the larger ships do not match their assets style and shape in the dogfighting mode")
A look, not rules: nothing is stored and nothing changes play except the race's crossings (house rules, below). Pure part in shared/logic "Skies and landforms" (`skyLook`, `worldLook`, `landformsOf`, `biomeSky`); drawn by `components/Map/scenery.tsx` (one new file: the street, the settlement, the globe, the race, the chase and the air battles all use it, and LocalMap.tsx and models.tsx are over 3,000 lines).
- **Sky** (`WorldSky`): a dome kept round the camera (shader: zenith to horizon by the hour, the sun's glow wider in haze, the far ground's haze under the horizon), the sun or suns (the system's star class gives colour and size; a system in five has a second, smaller sun), the planet's moons (0-3, a gas giant 4; the same from every place on the planet) and the system's other planets (small discs; a gas giant the world circles hangs large, one in three, ringed or not), all lit from the sun's side so a moon shows its phase and only its lit side shows by day; stars at night; clouds by the biome's cover (a desert nearly clear, a swamp overcast, ash on a volcanic world) drifting; the scene's fog in the horizon's haze so the ground fades into the far land. The map's light still comes from the same sun arc (`Sky` in LocalMap).
- **Horizon** (`landformsOf`): a low band of the biome's land all round (dunes, hills, ridges, a city's towers, a sea, a deck of cloud on a gas giant) and 6-11 landforms of the biome's kinds in front: snow-capped mountains, hills, volcanoes (active ones glow and smoke; always on a volcanic world, now and then a dormant one in jungle, ocean, temperate and barren), mesas, dunes, ridges, karst spires, cliffs and sea stacks, skylines with lit windows at night. Seeded by the settlement, so every building of a settlement shares its horizon; hazed by distance. The street (`BuildingScene`) and the settlement view show it; aboard a ship or on a station in space there is none. Under an open sky the walkable ground goes on as `FarGround` (siteAssets: the same photograph and tint, plainly lit, drawn first under everything, 1000 squares in the street, 1400 in the settlement view) into the fog. Cost on the device (headless Chromium, software rendering, the street, warm runs): about 30% of the frames (11.6 fps against 16.9 with the sky off); the dome is coloured per vertex (no full-screen shader) and the clouds are few, for that reason.
- **Globe from orbit** (`PlanetGlobe`, `globeOf`): the planet view's sphere is an icosphere (detail 64, 40 on a lite device) raised by the land's height and coloured by it, by biome recipe: seas and coasts, mountain ranges on ridged noise, canyons, volcanoes with craters (a volcanic world's lowlands are lava, glowing), craters on barren worlds, ice caps round a tilted axis (an ice world all ice), a city world's grey grids, a gas giant's bands. The land is flattened under the places on it, the roads follow the ground, and the travellers between places (speeders, mounts, haulers) ride over it (`Walker.hover`). Clouds drift over it; volcanoes glow and smoke.
- **The party in orbit or at a jump point** (`PartyCraft` in LocalMap): drawn as its own ship when one of its ships is there (`starships` on the map's world, from the campaign), else as a passage shuttle; it bobs and turns slowly with its engines lit. Figures are only drawn on the ground (the outskirts of a settlement).
- **Space and air fights**: the planet behind a space dogfight is the world itself (`PlanetBall`, the biome of the party's planet: `DogfightSpec.planet`), with a moon; the air battles have the world's sky and horizon (`AirBackdrop`).
- **Dogfight big ships** (shared/logic/dogfight.ts `hullSpec`, `hullSpan`, `CORVETTE`, `WEDGE`; `capitalGeometry.ts`): Large, Huge and Gargantuan ships now have the map model's shape (models.tsx buildShip), stretched to the class's measures: the Empire's (First Order, Sith) a wedge with a smaller upper tier and its tower astern (the shield generators are the tower's two domes), everyone else's a corvette with a hammerhead bow, a slim neck with the red stripe and a block of eight engines astern. The old frigate, cutter, carrier and citadel blocks are gone. The hull is the same volume the rules use: the wedge a convex solid, the corvette three boxes (`HullVolume` "compound"; `hullRay`, `hullPoint`), so a shot beside the neck passes it; turrets stand on the deck where it is (`hullSpan`: low on the neck, high on the blocks). The liveries are the map's colours and calmer (`busy`: mostly plain plates with rivets, no hazard stripes on the Republic's), with the engine block and stripe painted as vertex colours (`hullMaterial({ vertexColors })`); `worldUV` keeps a part's own normals so round hulls are lit round.
- **The land across a course** (shared/logic `layCrossings`, `CROSSING`, `RaceFeature`, `RaceRiver`, `RaceBridge`, `raceWater`; HOUSE RULES): the speeder race and the chase on the road meet the world's `crossings` (`biomeSky`): a **gorge** (desert, ice, forest, jungle, temperate, volcanic, barren: the track drops 9 m over 70 m into a slot 250 m long, walls from 4 to 20 m off the track's edge, struck like any face too steep to ride up), a **hill** (12 m, 110 m across, centred on the track: a speeder goes light over the crest), and a **river** (ice, forest, jungle, swamp, ocean, temperate, a city's canal) or **lava** (volcanic): a winding channel 20 m wide with 7 m banks across the whole ground, its surface 2.6 m under the land, which a speeder hovers over (off the way, so at the rough speed); a bridge wherever the track or a trail crosses it (the deck is the land's height out to 2.4 m past the way's edge, railed with barriers; under it a slab and piers). Lava burns a speeder down in it (off a bridge, low over it): one hull point each half second. Kept clear: the start, the gates (30 m), the shortcuts' ends and their jumps, each other; the jumps and the hazards keep off them, and trees, rocks and ground cover are not set in a channel. A gas giant's platforms have none. Courses that met them in bot races: rivals finish in 2:34-2:48 as before.
- Test hooks: `/map?campaign=<id>&here=<area>&view=<area>` (the atlas puts the party and the view anywhere); `/dev/dogfight?game=race|chase&biome=<b>&seed=<n>` (a course of a world); `window.__raceTrack` and `window.__raceWarp = s` (put the party's speeder s metres round the lap, gates before it passed). Tests: `tests/world/skies.test.ts` (4), `tests/ships/dogfight.test.ts` (hull volumes: the wedge and the corvette).
- Not done: rivers and landforms on the street map itself (the street's ground is flat); the horizon is not placed from the globe's own mountains (both come from the biome, seeded apart); the pilot's route and the taxi run still fly the old flight model over flat ground.

## Interiors overhaul (2026-10-19; `components/Map/interiors/`, tests in `tests/interiors/`)
The finding that shaped it: only the room that is open (`opened` in `BuildingScene`) is drawn with its furniture; the others are blocks. So a rich room costs one room, and a separate interior page is not needed for speed (it would be for a closer look; not built).
- Pure modules (tested in Node, 3 s, no browser): `wealth.ts` (six wealths from the building and room, `LOOKS`, `skinOf`: a `Kit.skin` that turns the browns of the furniture into the room's woods, steels into its metal, golds into its trim and other soft colours into its cloth; lights, glass and blacks are left), `atmosphere.ts` (`moodOf`: ambient, key light, up to four coloured lights with flicker, dust, a shaft, mist, by room kind (cave, ruin, detention, medical, reactor, hangar, tavern, palace, shop, barracks, slum), tinted by the world; `budgetOf` cuts it for "lite"), `weapons.ts` (217 weapons of the rules data to 18 kinds; `hungWeapon` picks a Blaster Kit model by name, `buildWeapon` builds every kind in code at "hero" or "low" detail, the low one under 120 triangles), `stock.ts` (`holdsOf`, `stockLayout`: what a weapon rack, display shelf, display case or armory shelving holds, by the kind of place, seeded), `cave.ts` (`rockRun`: a row of rocks for a wall, a belt about 1.3 squares thick that never reaches into the room; stalagmite, column, crystals, pool; `caveFeatures` along the edge squares), `footprint.ts` (`radiiOf`, `standing`: which squares of a round, oval or chamfered room stand; `waysStand`).
- Drawn (`Atmosphere.tsx`, `Stock.tsx`, `CaveRoom.tsx`, `assets.ts`): `RoomShell` takes `mood` {buildingType, roomName, biome} (given for the open room unless it is open air or a stair, lift or gallery); it lights the room, draws visible lamps (tallow, bulb, globe, lantern by wealth), motes, a shaft, mist (full only), passes the wealth skin to `Furniture`, draws `Stock` on the racks, and for a `cave` building draws `RockWall` runs for its walls and `CaveDressing` for its rim. `Sky` takes `indoors` (halves the daylight ambient and cuts the sun to 40%). The stock is drawn as one merged mesh; the hero models within 11 squares of the camera, the low ones beyond.
