- Assets: `tools/convert-assets.mjs` (`npm run convert:assets`) bakes the texture colours of a downloaded CC0 pack into vertex colours, quantises, welds, and with `LOD=0.12` writes a `.lod.glb` beside each (the blasters 470 to about 100 triangles). Packs under `client/public/assets/`: `kenney-blaster` (blasters, clips, grenades, crates; steel ramp, `tone=gunmetal`) and `kenney-space` (barrels, machines, desks, rocks, crystals, weapons; converted, not yet used in a scene), 1 MB for both; `CREDITS.md` lists them with their licences and the research read. The pack's sources are not kept (downloaded again from kenney.nl into a scratch directory); node 18 here, so glTF Transform is pinned to 3.10.1 (4.x needs import attributes).
- Tests: `npm test` (all, about 3 s alone), `test:weapons`, `test:atmosphere`, `test:caves`, `test:assets` (they check every pack has its licence and is in CREDITS.md, every catalogued model exists in both detail levels with the length the catalogue says, no textures, the 10 MB budget). Browser: `/dev/models?section=interiors` (racks by place, six wealths, caves in three worlds, the mood of ten rooms) and `e2e/interiors.mjs` (`npx vite --port 5173 --host 127.0.0.1`, then `BASE=http://127.0.0.1:5173 node e2e/interiors.mjs racks|wealth|caves|moods`; a view takes about two minutes in software WebGL; the vite dev server logs an old `Alert`/`alert` case clash from its scan, harmless). Do not `pkill -f` a pattern that is in your own command line.
- Found on the way: the Blaster Kit's models carry node transforms (a scope and a magazine moved into place), so their length is not that of the raw mesh (blaster-e is 1.39, not 1.64) and their origins are not centred; the loader centres them. Quantised files must be read out to floats before a matrix is applied to them.
- 2026-10-19 (doors and stacked floors; asked by the user, supersedes the upper-floor rule of the line below): the doors were wrong because (a) a passage to a room already placed was never checked for a shared wall, and fell back to a door in the outside wall (14 such fallbacks in seed 34 with `linkUpperFloors`, 3 without); (b) `linkUpperFloors` added loop passages that could not be laid out beside their rooms. Now: a door is only put on a wall two rooms share on a floor both stand on (`sharedWall`); a passage that cannot be is dropped; a room with no room to touch is set against another placed room, and its passage joins that one (`spotsBeside`). `linkUpperFloors` is removed. Upper floors are STACKED: `linkFloors` gives a ground room with a door to a ground partner (the partner is the room below) the `stack:<partner>` tag; the upper room takes the partner's footprint one level up, and each room gets a 1x2 stairs asset at the same square (`Stairs up/down`, `blocks`). Name-given upper rooms are stacked on their partner too, when the levels differ by one. A stacked room is only placed from its partner (`BFS`), so its footprint is the partner's. Measured in seeds 34, 1-5: no same-floor overlaps, no isolated rooms, 2 to 8 stacked rooms per world; rooms still stair-linked (no partner) are the rest. Tests: `tests/interiors/doors.test.ts` (physical: door on the shared wall, door back across it, no floor-joining door except a stairway), `floors.test.ts` (stacked or reached). Known gap: the district's catwalk and roof doors join another building's room and are laid out by the district, not checked here. The map was not re-checked in a browser for this change; the test server is redeployed with a new world (seed 34 for new campaigns); the campaign created at 19:53 (id 703) was made by the earlier build and keeps its world.
- 2026-10-19 (packs in rooms, upper floors; asked by the user): (1) `interiors/packs.ts` picks a room's pack by its name: palace guest rooms, bedchambers, palatial suites, audience and dignitary rooms take `station` (Space Station Kit in the Imperial palette, plus a sofa and a low table from the Furniture Kit converted the same way), servants' quarters, apartments, tenement and residence rooms take `furniture` (Furniture Kit, frontier palette). `Furniture` draws a piece from the room's pack when the pack has it and the code's piece otherwise, and `RoomShell` passes the pack for the opened room. Not yet: packs for other rooms, and the wealth skin does not apply to pack pieces. (2) Upper floors: `linkFloors` gives a building of five rooms or more one or two ground rooms an upper floor (code F1, not the way in or a store/bay), before `linkLevels` joins them with stairs or a lift; `linkUpperFloors` then gives every upper-floor room that has one way a second (a short corridor to another room on its floor, or stairs down to a room below). In the test world, upper-floor rooms with one way went from 64 of 94 to 0, and big buildings now have more than one floor (`tests/interiors/floors.test.ts`). (3) Not done: the open ground around buildings. `planDistrict` packs buildings in rows, each row as tall as its tallest building, so short buildings leave empty ground; a skyline packer would close it, and is the next step if asked.
- 2026-10-19 (round rooms, corridors, packs; asked by the user): `linkRounds` (shared/logic) marks one room of a round kind of building `round` (60%) and splits each door joining it to a room on its floor with a corridor, as stairways are split in `linkLevels`; the planner makes it round where only corridors meet its standing squares (`meetsStanding`). Round rooms in new worlds went from 2 in six worlds to 10 in twelve. The test copy (`deploy/test-copy.sh`, `SW5E_SHOWCASE_SEED=34`, `server/showcase.ts`) now gives every new campaign that one world (a city with three round rooms, a cave system and ruins); new campaigns only, the older ones are unchanged. Packs: `tools/convert-assets.mjs` takes `tone=gunmetal|imperial|frontier` (`TONES`, one shared ramp function). Kenney Space Station Kit added as `kenney-station` (tone=imperial, 97 pieces, 0.4 MB) and four pieces toned frontier as `kenney-station-frontier`; `/dev/models?section=packs` shows them beside the space kit and a steel blaster. Not done: the one-pack-per-location rule is not in code (furniture is still drawn in code, and only blasters and crates come from packs); no scene places `kenney-station` yet; the paid GameDev.tv kit is not bought (no payment from us); no foreign-site source turned up in two rounds of search (Poly Pizza mixes licences per model, Molten Maps and Jamzuz are CC0 or unclear and not yet downloaded).
- 2026-10-19 (round rooms, first slice): `shared/logic/footprint.ts` (moved from `client/`, so the planner can use it) gives the curve; `layout.shape` ('round' or 'oval') is set by `planBuilding` on a room that stands clear of the others (its ring of squares is empty on its floor), in a round kind of building (`ROUND_BUILDINGS`: guard posts, cantina, cookshop, pawnshop, curio shop, scrap dealer, huts and the like), 70% of the time, at least five squares a side, and only where the middle of each side stands. `standsAt` makes cut squares not floor in `freeSquares`, `findSpot` and `placeContents`; doors go only on standing wall squares; `LocalMap` draws the planned curve (`radiiOf`, `insideRadii` not capped). Tests: `tests/interiors/round.test.ts`. Only new worlds get them (`LIFE` not bumped). Browser: `/dev/models?section=rounds` (`e2e/interiors.mjs rounds`) shows three rooms furnished by `furnishRoom` on their curve, and the doors and furniture stay on it; a real planned round room in a world is not yet seen in play; about 2 rooms in six worlds, so the 70% and the clear-ring rule are to tune.
- Not done yet: round and oval buildings beyond that first slice (more shaped rooms: the ring rule keeps most rooms rectangular in multi-room buildings; whole round buildings); held weapons in `buildHumanoid` still use their own pieces, not `buildWeapon`; the Space Kit's models (desks, consoles, generators, barrels) are converted but no piece uses them; clutter by wealth (`look.clutter`); a viewscreen and bloom.
- 2026-10-19 (night, asked by the user: wire in the Space Kit and the station walls, recolour assets per area, kitbash new models; test copy only, nothing shipped to the `sw5e` service). Recolour: the converted packs now keep their own colours on disk (`kenney-station`, `kenney-furniture`, `kenney-space` re-converted neutral; `kenney-station-frontier` is gone) and `interiors/tones.ts` turns them at load time (`useModels(urls, anchor, tone)`, one retoned copy per tone and file): greys follow a dark-middle-light ramp by lightness, saturated colours become the palette's accent. Palettes are in `shared/art/tones.json` (gunmetal, imperial, frontier, industrial, military, medical, criminal, hull, ruin), also read by `tools/convert-assets.mjs` (`tone=`; `tests/interiors/tones.test.ts` checks the tool and the client turn colours the same). `packs.ts` is now seven styles (station/imperial, furniture/frontier with its ramp drawn from the world's wall colour, industrial, military, medical, hull, criminal): `packOf(roomName, buildingType)` picks by room name then building; a style has a palette, its furniture by `ROOM_PLANS` name, and whether its rooms get the station kit's walls (all but furniture and criminal). Walls: `interiors/Walls.tsx` replaces only the solid `Box` runs of a straight wall with tiles of `wall`/`wall-window` (one mesh per room, stretched to fill, 0.8 high like the box), the plain boxes standing until the models load; doorways, windows and rounded corners are unchanged. `PackPiece` now faces away from the wall (`facing`), turns to the footprint's long side, caps its height (`maxHeightOf`), repeats small pieces (two consoles on a 2x1 desk) or stretches counters (`fitPiece`, `stretches`). Rounded corners of round and oval rooms are bent tiles too (`cornerGeometries`/`bend` in `Walls.tsx`: a tile to about each square of the quarter ring, its length laid along the arc and its depth across the ring, so tiles meet exactly on the curve and on the straight runs; the plain `CornerWall` stands until the kit loads, in the palette's middle colour). Space Kit machines serve the industrial, military, medical and hull rooms (console, generator, barrels, dish, chimney, pipe, chair), and its crystals and rocks are the edge dressing of caves (`CaveRoom.tsx` `KitRocks`, in the cave's own stone and crystal colours; the stalagmites, columns and pools stay in code). Kitbash: `tools/kitbash.mjs` takes converted models apart (islands of a mesh, optional `clip` by height) and builds new ones from pieces and solids, recipes in `tools/kitbash-recipes.mjs`, output `kenney-kitbash` (22 models, 212 KB, hero and low detail, CC0 licence copied, credited): armour light, medium and heavy on stands (astronaut body, arms, legs with plates), a physical shield and a shield generator, kit case, roll and bag, tool roll, medpac and trauma case, prosthesis arm, leg, hand and eye, and melee weapons and a launcher (dagger, blade, axe, mace, pike, electrostaff, launcher; `hungWeapon` now returns them with a `dir`). `stock.ts` has new holders: `armour` (an armour stand shows armour by the kind of place: palace heavy, garrison medium, outfitter light), `kits` (shelves of tech shops, outfitters, workshops, general stores), `medical` (cabinets and shelving of medcentres and apothecaries, on the cabinet top), `prosthetics` (display cases of tech shops, droid dealers, medcentres) and `tools` (workbenches); a piece that carries stock is built `bare` (no generic wares on the shelf, no dummy on the stand). Kit models take the room's palette, or their own by kind (`DEFAULT_TONE`: armour gunmetal, kits industrial, medical white and teal). `/dev/models?section=packs` shows every palette on the same pieces, a room for each style and the kitbash models in four palettes; `?section=interiors` has the shop stock row; `e2e/interiors.mjs` has views `tones rooms kitbash shops` (`CAM=`/`LOOK=` override). `tools/inspect-glb.mjs` prints a model's size and materials. The original kits are not kept: download them again from kenney.nl to rebuild (`npm run convert:assets`, `node tools/kitbash.mjs`). No Quaternius or other packs (no more downloaded); weapons for bows, whips, sabers and shields are still code-built; prostheses for ear, throat, facial and torso, the droid-customization items and the armour the rules data names one by one have no model (one suit stands for a class); stock on `Armor stand` is chosen by the kind of place, not by what the shop sells; the kit furniture is in the test copy only until the user plays it there.
- 2026-10-19 (night, asked by the user: over-the-shoulder and first-person views with an accurate reticle, walking animation, the models on characters, and a list of what has no model). **Views** (`interiors/aim.ts`, `interiors/ViewRig.tsx`; "View:" button in the map bar on foot, key V, remembered in localStorage `sw5e.view`): `ViewRig` replaces the overhead `Rig` and its OrbitControls on foot (`BuildingScene`) and in a fight (`FightScene`, from the party character's square). Drag to look (a drag under 8 px stays a tap), W A S D or the arrows or the on-screen stick to walk, left and right arrows turn, E, Space, Enter or the Use button act. Walking asks the map's own walk for a step (`stepToward`: eight directions, a diagonal only past two open squares, blocked ones slid along a wall), so traps, secret ways, arrivals and `spot.stand` still happen. Walls rise to 2.4 in these views (`RoomShell fullWalls`), name labels shrink to a third (`useView.eyes`), the character is hidden from the eyes and turns to the camera over the shoulder, and the shoulder camera is pulled in by a wall behind it. **The crosshair** is at the middle of the canvas (a DOM root appended to the canvas's parent, so it is centred on the canvas, not the window): each tenth of a second `aimAt` casts from the camera through NDC (0, 0) against the registered targets (the `Hit` meshes, now carrying `label`, `onTap` and in a fight `plan`) and solids (walls, curved corners), and against the floor; a wall nearer than a target hides it; over the shoulder a second ray from the shoulder to the aimed point marks the shot blocked and where, and a ring marks where it lands. In a fight the target's `plan` is `attackPlan` (reach, range, cover, advantage), so it says what the roll will say, in the rules' own feet; Use picks the target out as a tap does and the game decides. Tests: `tests/interiors/aim.test.ts` raycasts in node (a known target is the one picked, a wall between camera and target hides it, a wall between the shoulder and the target but not the camera blocks the shot). Not done: the flying and space scenes and the speeder race keep their own cameras; the district (street) fight has no view of its own; no gamepad. **Walking** (`interiors/limbs.ts`): a person is baked with each vertex marked with its limb and pivot (`Kit.limb`, `Kit.animated`), and their materials (clones that share one compiled program) swing legs and hanging arms about the pivots in the vertex shader, so a walker is still one draw call per finish; the stride comes from how far the figure itself moves each frame (`useStride`), so the party, followers, strollers and fighters all walk without any movement code being touched; droids, beasts and war machines bob and rock (`Gait`). Shadows stay in the rest pose. Gallery `?section=walk`. **Gear on people** (`interiors/gear.ts`, `buildHumanoid`'s `attach`): `wantsOf` says which models a figure has: an NPC's weapon from the pose its outfit gives (pistol, rifle, vibroblade, vibroaxe, vibropike, electrostaff) and kits by work (medic: medpac, mechanic: tool roll, merchant: bag, scout: roll); the player's from what the character chose to wield and wear (`gearOf` in characterCalculation, from `equippedNow` and the rules data's classes): the weapon (and its pose), a shield (physical on the arm, a generator on the forearm), armour weight as pauldrons and plates (light a vest, medium and heavy armour), kits, tools and medicine on the hips and back, a prosthetic arm or leg over a metal limb. The models are loaded centred and in their palette (`DEFAULT_TONE`), baked into the figure on the limb they are on (so a hanging pistol swings with its arm), and the code's own weapon stands until they are in. Gallery `?section=gear&loadout=heavy|light|medic|cyber`. Not done: the held weapon for sabers, bows, whips and thrown weapons (still code); eye, ear, throat, facial and torso prostheses; named armour suits look alike within a weight.
- Dogfight: shield, mouse, pilot's seat, choice of ship (2026-10-25, asked by the user; the arena itself is `shared/logic/dogfight.ts`, `dogfightSim.ts`, `tunnel.ts` and `components/Map/dogfight/`, tested in `tests/ships/dogfight.test.ts` with the bot in `dogfightBot.ts`; `/dev/dogfight` plays it without a campaign: size, role, tier, seed, scenario, owner=the Empire for the Imperial looks, view=cockpit, noshield). **House rules, all said on the countdown screen where they apply:** a ship with no shield fitted is lent a deflector for the sortie (`lentShield`: three quarters of its hull points, regenerating a die of its hull a round, like the lent cannon; `ARENA.lentShield`); the party's shield recharges slowly (`ARENA.partyWait` 5 s without a hit, then `ARENA.partyRegen` 0.3 of the sheet's rate: about 35 s from empty for a lent one, a minute for a fitted one; a foe's shield waits 3 s and takes the whole rate, and the party's capital ship now regenerates too, it used to be left out). The HUD shows hull and shield side by side, the shield pulsing while it recharges. **The active weapon** (`weaponSlots`, `Dogfight.slot`; input `trigger` and `cycle`): the nose cannon, each ordnance launcher, then the bombs; the trigger fires the active one, the cycle moves to the next that has something to fire (it passes an empty launcher). The keys and buttons still fire each kind on their own (Space, M, B; the Fire, Missile and Bomb buttons). **Controls** (`DogfightGame.tsx`): with a mouse the cursor is a stick that is the whole screen (the middle is straight on, 40% of the shorter side is full turn, a small dead zone, `shaped` from `interiors/stick.ts`) and a ring shows where it is beside the nose sight; the left button fires the active weapon, the right button cycles it (no context menu); V switches mouse steering on and off (on by default where there is a fine pointer, remembered in `sw5e.dogfight.mouse`), C the view (`sw5e.dogfight.view`), E the next weapon, Q the next launcher; over the buttons the mouse does nothing. Keys, pad and mouse add up. **First-person view** (`Cockpit.tsx`): the eye is where the model's pilot sits (`eyeOf`: under the fighter's canopy, in the Imperial ball, behind the shuttles' screens, out in the freighter's tube to starboard, on a capital ship's tower), the party's own model is not drawn, the field of view is 75 degrees (65 in the chase view). The cockpit is a scene of its own drawn after the world with the depth cleared (`CockpitLayer`, which takes over the drawing at priority 1), in the eye's own space: a wall with the window cut out of it, struts, a tilted dash with a screen drawn ten times a second (hull, shield, speed, throttle, the active weapon and its ammunition, the target, warnings, the clock), a stick, yoke or levers that lean with the steering. One look for each exterior model of `buildShip` (`cockpitStyle`): X-wing fighter (strut frame, wing roots and the four cannon tips in view), Imperial fighter (a round window with spokes in a ball), shuttle (wide screen, red-edged wings), Imperial shuttle (boxy and heavy, folded wings), freighter (pilot's tube, the hull's mandibles to port, dice on a cord, the hull's tone), corvette bridge (long slit, the neck and hammerhead bow beyond), Imperial warship tower (the wedge deck sloping away). Looked at in a browser on `/dev/dogfight` for all seven; not played in the station tunnel's first-person view (it uses the same `placeEye`), and a portrait phone gets the cockpit squeezed to 40% across (the dash shows, the frame is cropped). **Which ship:** the downtime menu's dogfight lists every ship the party owns (`partyShips` in `DowntimeProps`; one that is at a yard or wrecked is greyed, with why) and the pilot picks one; with one ship nothing is asked. Not done: the chosen ship's hull is not worn down by the fight (the sim ignores `ship.hull`, as before), the cockpits have no glass or reflections, and the delivery run still flies the first ship.
- Dogfight, second round (2026-10-25, asked by the user; supersedes the controls above where they differ). **Upgrades:** the ship the pilot picks is `shipSheet(ship, rows, proficiency)` of the campaign's ship, so its modifications, enhanced armor, shield and weapons, equipment and tier are in the sheet; the arena now uses all that the sheet carries that a fight with no crew can: speed, turning, AC, damage reduction, resistances, saves, power backups (as before), and, which were dropped before, a weapon's damage bonus (`Gun.plus`; the old code multiplied it by 0 and used the attack bonus as the damage bonus), its attack bonus (`Gun.bonus`), damage dice of 1 and 2 rolled again (Gauss Rounds; `rolled(expr, roll, again)`), flares (Flare Pods: a failed Dexterity save against a warhead is rolled again, `Dogfight.flares`, a use a tier and at least one) and feedback shields (`strikeBack`: a foe that hits the craft with a cannon or turret takes the shield's dice). Not in the arena (there is no crew): crew actions (`specials`), the conditions of a critical hit, no disadvantage at the helm. The picker's line and the countdown screen list the upgrades fitted (`sortieNotes`); a ship with no shield is still lent one. **Flight:** A and D roll the craft (`input.roll`, `Vessel.roll`, `ARENA.roll`: a real roll: a turning speed that eases to what the keys ask (`ARENA.rollEase`, 0.12 s), so that on letting go the roll dies away in about a quarter of a second, some 15 degrees on, and the craft stays where it is turned (the user, 2026-10-25: release stops the rolling, a slight decay is fine); there is no auto-levelling; the HUD has a roll indicator and the cockpit screen says ROLL); the mouse (on by default now, with or without a fine pointer; the saved choice still wins) and the arrow keys turn the nose in the craft's own frame, so that once it is rolled they turn the heading by both yaw and pitch (`flyParty`, tested). `Vessel.bank` is the lean into a yaw, for show: its sign was wrong (a yaw to the left leaned the craft to the right) and is fixed (into the yaw, the way a foe's lean was already). The chase camera rolls with the craft (camera up is the craft's up), the tunnel is flown level. **Throttle:** a setting that holds (`Dogfight.throttle`, starts at `ARENA.cruise`): W and S move it by `ARENA.throttleRate` of its range a second while held, a notch of the wheel (up for more) by `ARENA.dialStep` (`input.dial`, a count of notches); the speed follows it and stays; the HUD shows the bar and the m/s, the cockpit screen a bar with a tick for the speed. The touch buttons are Faster and Slower (the same input, held). In the tunnel W is still boost. **Test tools** (a build made with `VITE_TEST_MENU=1`, which is the 5002 test build and never the build played on, as the game's test menu): a button "Test: unlimited hull and weapons" (`TEST_TOOLS` in `DogfightGame.tsx`; `input.god`, `Dogfight.god`, `unlimited` gives back hull, shield and ammunition every step; a badge on the HUD says it is on). Nothing needs commenting out for production, the flag keeps it out of the build; the constant and the button are marked if it is to be removed by hand. **Turrets** (batteries and point defense of capital ships and the station): each standing one that is in sight within 1,260 m is marked on the screen (an orange circle; the 16 nearest), and a part the hull stands in front of is neither marked nor a target (`partVisible`, a ray against the hull; `aimFrom` uses it); Y or the Part button makes the next turret, generator or hangar bay of the locked ship (or of the ship the reticule is on) the target (`subTarget`, `Dogfight.lockPart`, `subTargets`: nearest first in bands of 40 m and then by number, because a pair of turrets either side of the craft are as near as each other and ordered by distance alone they swapped places every step), after the last the ship itself; the chosen part is marked as the target with its health, the reticule takes it from a wider cone (`ARENA.subAssist`), warheads home on it, and the target card says TARGET. A turret shot out is silenced for good (`stepParts` skips a dead part; the log says how many are left) and the model shows it: a muzzle glow just before a turret fires, the head blown off and tumbling away, a charred mount with a broken barrel burning where it stood (`CapitalShip.tsx`). Looked at in a browser on `/dev/dogfight?scenario=battleship` (`VITE_TEST_MENU=1 npx vite`). Tested in `tests/ships/dogfight.test.ts` (upgrades, roll and lean, throttle, test tools, turrets).
- Dogfight black screen (2026-10-25, reported by the user: after a crash the arena was black with the HUD showing and did not recover). **Cause:** React Three Fiber runs every canvas of the page from ONE frame loop and a canvas that throws in its frame aborts that frame for the canvases after it, every frame. The map's `Motes` (`interiors/Atmosphere.tsx`, the dust in a room) kept its `<points>` when a room's mote count changed, so a buffer made for one size was given an array of another ("THREE.WebGLAttributes: The size of the buffer attribute's array buffer does not match the original size", hundreds a second), and the arena, drawn after the map, never drew or stepped (its frame count and sim clock stood at 0) until the errors stopped. It also made the whole page slow (the e2e dogfight step took 158 s, now 52 s). **Fixes:** `Motes` is keyed by its count; the arena is drawn from a loop of its own (`OwnLoop` in `DogfightGame.tsx`: the canvas is `frameloop="never"` and advanced from `requestAnimationFrame`, each frame guarded), so that nothing else's fault stops it; a lost or restored WebGL context, a second of failed frames, or a throw in the scene's render tree builds the canvas again (`ContextWatch`, `SceneGuard`, `rebuild`: the fight is in `sim`, not in the canvas; at most 4 rebuilds, then it says so and asks the pilot to leave and come back); the cockpit pass no longer clears before the world is drawn (a failing frame left a black screen, now it leaves the last frame); the shield-flash shell's shader had never compiled (`instanceColor` was declared by us and by three: now `#ifdef USE_INSTANCING_COLOR`). The e2e dogfight step checks that the sim clock and the frame count move. Not changed: the speeder race and the delivery run still use the shared loop, so another canvas that throws would still stop them.
- Dogfight, third round (2026-10-26, reported by the user: the Medium ship's controls felt weird, were dead in some directions and rolled with the mouse; the mouse stopped steering when the cursor left the screen; the guns fired along the nose, not at the cursor). **Causes and fixes:** (1) The lean into a yaw (`ARENA.lean`, `Vessel.bank`: up to 0.5 rad on the model, half of it on the chase camera) is gone: only A and D roll the craft, never the mouse or the pad. (2) The craft was turned by adding to the heading's angles with the pitch held at +-1.2 rad, so steeply up a yaw spun the view about the nose and hardly moved it sideways, and the nose stopped at the limit. Now `turned()` (shared/logic/dogfight.ts) turns it about its own axes (yaw about its up, pitch about its starboard, nothing about the nose) and reads the angles back from the new axes; the party's pitch has no limit (a pull goes over the top and the craft is then upside down, roll 180 degrees; the HUD's roll is the angle against the horizon, so it changes a little when the nose swings round a tilted craft); foes keep the limit. (3) The steering went off while the pointer was over a HUD button (the bottom corners), over the page's title bar and when it left the arena. Now the cursor is a point held inside the arena and the steering never goes off for where the pointer is (a pointer out of the window holds its last place at the edge); a click while the mouse steers captures the mouse (pointer lock: the cursor moves by the mouse's movement and cannot leave the screen; Esc lets go; a click on a button under the cursor, the ones marked `data-tap`, presses it; it is let go when the fight ends). Fullscreen: a button and the F key take the whole page (title bar, countdown and end screen: `shell` of `DogfightGame`); no button where the browser has no fullscreen (iPhone). **Aim (house rule, the books have none):** the guns fire at the cursor. The scene turns the cursor's place into a ray from the camera (`input.aim`); `aimFrom` picks what is under it (a pointer is exact: `ARENA.cursorAssist` 0.05 rad besides the target's size, against the reticule's 0.3); a laser is drawn from the muzzle to that point (a miss goes on along the ray) and a missile homes on it. The guns turn to any place on the screen: there is no firing arc. The Aim button (X) swaps to the reticule (the nose): with the cursor it is a small dim ring marking the nose, with the reticule it is the sight; it is drawn where the nose points on the screen and no longer in the middle of the view (the chase camera is above the craft, so the middle was about 10 degrees off). With no mouse (a phone), in the station's tunnel and while the pointer is over the buttons the guns are on the reticule. Tests: `tests/ships` 36 of 36 (new: the nose turns at the stick's rate steeply up and goes over the top; the guns follow the cursor); every point above checked in a browser (Medium freighter on `/dev/dogfight`, chase and cockpit views, mouse and touch; the scripts are in the scratchpad, not the repo) and the `e2e/play.mjs` dogfight step passes.
- 2026-10-20 (asked by the user: stacked buildings, stairs, caves, sewers, ceilings, ship interiors, the stick; the order of work is in HANDOFF.md). **Audit:** `auditBuildings(world)` (shared/logic) reads every building's laid-out rooms and reports floors that are cut or pinched (`floorFaults`, which now counts stairs as reached from beside them: they used to be unreachable, so a room with stairs got no blocking furniture), ways on cut squares or under furniture, doors without doors back, stairs without stairs back, stacked rooms off their partner's footprint or without stairs, galleries whose stairs miss them, caves with plain or too-wide floors, rooms no path leads to. `tests/world/layouts.test.ts` runs it over 23 worlds. **Generator fixes:** tall rooms are not stacked; stairwells are laid out after the doors (`stairwellSpot`: 2x3 or 3x2 switchbacks against a wall, else 1x2; `galleryStairs`) on clear standing squares leaving the floor whole; doors, exits and secret ways keep off occupied squares and cut squares; ships, caches and people keep off every way; a displaced room is anchored only to a room on its floor; rooms stranded by a dropped passage are joined by a door or a bridging corridor (`joinStranded`); a room is never stacked that another is stacked on; three-floor towers (`addTower`, up to four on city planets). The 3D `StairsAsset` draws a stairwell of two flights as a switchback. **Ship decks** (`shipDeck`, one fixed plan per size, hand-authored, not random) had a shut-in corner on Tiny and Small decks and pinched corners on the rest; the stations now stand against the bow wall and `tests/world/decks.test.ts` walks every size; the party arrives beside the boarding ramp, and a deck is laid out afresh on each boarding so older saved worlds are mended. **Caves** (`shared/logic/caves.ts`): a cave room keeps its rectangle on the building's grid but its floor is a `mask` (`layout.mask`, read by `standsAt`): a winding tunnel, a mouth or a chamber chosen by the room's name, passages 2 to 6 squares thick (`thickness`: the largest block of floor holding a square; a chamber may be wider), one piece, no corner-only touches, holding every way; `RoomShell` draws the floor squares (`MaskFloor`), rock along the edges (`CaveWalls`), rim formations, and from outside a mound with an open mouth (`CaveMound`). Cut squares (round rooms, caves) are no longer walkable (`open` asks `standsAt`). **City planets:** an urban planet's wild site is a sewer system (never a cave): built passages (straight walls, `sewer` and `sewer access` buildings, channels 3 to 5 squares wide, junctions, pump chambers and a cistern), its districts' way in is a speeder platform (not a gate; `speeder platform` building, an open ground) and a second platform, more rooms are stacked (towers up to four floors), and `kindLabel` calls a settlement there a district (GameBoard). `tests/world/city-planets.test.ts`. **Eye views:** a ceiling over roofed rooms (rock over caves, a deck head in a ship, plain elsewhere; none over open air), tall rock walls in caves, and the view starts looking into the room. **Stick:** one size and curve for the walking stick, the race and the dogfight (`interiors/stick.ts`: 176 px pad, a dead zone and an expo curve, `paceOf`), a Run/Walk button (key R; walking is 30% slower than the old pace of 7 squares a second, which is Run) in the eye views. **e2e:** the floors step no longer duplicates the audit; the fight step finds squares through the probe (`me+dx,dy`); `ONLY=` slices on a plain copy (`deploy/test-plain.sh`, port 5002) run the map, trade and ship steps; the speeder race is opt-in (`RACE=1`).
- 2026-10-20 (asked by the user: fix the accent): the accent no longer turns a whole bunk, table or sofa one colour. `tones.ts` `toned` reads saturation as a band, not a switch: below 0.5 a colour is a material and follows the ramp, from 0.75 up (hazard stripes, lamps) it is the accent, between the two are mixed (`accentShare`, a smoothstep), and a coloured material sits lower in the ramp than a grey as light as it (wood does not turn to paper). `holdOf(geometry)` takes the area share the accent covers and, above 35%, scales it down (floor 0.7), so a mostly coloured model (sofa, barrel) keeps part of its ramp. A barracks bunk is now a khaki frame with an ochre blanket; stripes on a grey body (reactor, consoles) are as before. `tools/tones.mjs` has the same turn (`hold` 1) for baked packs; `tests/interiors/tones.test.ts` checks both. Look: `e2e/interiors.mjs tones rooms` on the Vite server (`CAM=16,9,22 LOOK=16,0,15` is the barracks).

## Item models (2026-10-20; `npm run build:items`, `npm run coverage:models`, `npm run audit:items`)
Every item of the rules data has a model: 507 equipment items (286 of their own, 221 on a shared design by kind) and all 1,918 enhanced items (228 distinct models; ship equipment is not carried). What the game does with each item is in **`ITEM_TODO.md`**, generated by `tools/item-audit.ts` from the game's own functions and tables, with the gaps by group written in that tool.
- **Making them:** headless Blender 4.0.2 (installed 2026-10-20) with the user-level skill `blender-sw5e` (`~/.claude/skills/blender-sw5e/`: conventions, `scripts/sw5e_bpy.py`, batch converter, LOD maker). Work and sources live outside the repo in `~/asset-sources/` (MANIFEST.md, SELECTION.md, `builders/*.py`, `tools/`, `staged/`). CC0 sources: Kenney's Survival, Food and Pirate Kits, Quaternius' LowPoly Medieval Weapons; most models are generated from primitives. Animation-ready: moving parts stay named (blade, string, limbs...), pivots at hinges, sockets `grip`, `off_hand`, `tip`, `muzzle`, `mount`.
- **Wiring:** `tools/build-item-assets.mjs <staged-dir>` converts the staged glb into 14 packs `client/public/assets/sw5e-*` (hero and `.lod.glb`, `License.txt`) and writes `shared/art/itemModels.json` (the catalogue: pack, triangles, box, sockets, parts with pivots, `meta.slot/hand/tone/...`) and `shared/art/itemIndex.json` (item name to model, plus blade shape/scale, grade, chassis). Each vertex carries `_MARK` = [finish, part]: finish 0 sheen, 1 matte, 2 glow, 3 cloth. `assets.ts` reads it (`mark`), `splitByFinish` gives the Kit one part per finish (`Kit.addModel`), `retone` leaves glow alone, a model whose `meta.tone` is `none` (grenade types, suits, blades, protocols: 173 of them) is never put in a room palette (`KitLook` `plain`). `interiors/items.ts` is the catalogue API and `composeSaber` (a hilt without its blade plus one of 15 blade shapes at the hinge); `interiors/anchors.ts` says where things are worn on every species (hips, back, belt, shoulder; bulk, hunch, wings); `gear.ts` maps item to model (`coverageOf`), and figures wear weapons, shields and worn gear (`wantsOf`, slots hipR, hipL, back, belt, shoulder, wrist). Shelves: `stock.ts` holders for goods, devices, droid parts, clothing, instruments, vials, the bar, ammunition and named armour; `Stock.tsx` draws finishes. Dev gallery: `/dev/models?section=items&pack=sw5e-sabers` and `?section=worn&loadout=worn|hunter` (species rows); screenshots with `e2e/interiors.mjs worn|hunter|items` on a Vite dev server.
- **Budget:** the asset budget was raised from 10 to 24 MB (`tests/interiors/assets.test.ts`); the 14 packs are 7.2 MB, the largest 1.4 MB. Models load lazily by what a scene shows, so no e2e timeout was changed; the catalogue and index are about 35 KB gzipped in the bundle. `npm test` takes more than ten minutes on this one core because of `tests/world/layouts.test.ts` (23 generated worlds): use `npm run test:interiors` (about 50 s) for the models.
- **Licence decision to confirm:** the packs' `License.txt` offers the models as CC0 (original shapes plus pieces of CC0 packs); the project owner may change that for the original parts.
- **Not done (see ITEM_TODO.md):** head, neck, finger, feet, hands and torso slots on figures (need per-species fitting: horns, lekku, snouts), blade colour from the character's alignment, the `grip` socket instead of the guessed fraction in `inHand`, animation clips, droid modules on droid figures, mods on weapons, an inventory item viewer (`ItemModel` in `interiors/ItemModel.tsx` draws any catalogue model for it).

## HUD tabs, compass, stick, facing, test menu (2026-10-21, user's list)
- The HUD (`pages/GameBoard.tsx`) is in tabs under a compact header (name, HP, credits, status, encounter, dead, level and points): Here (helm, heist, location, exits, people), Party, Trade, Quests, Inventory, Downtime, GM Interface (and Test on a test copy). The tab buttons are `role="tab"` with those names (the e2e scripts click them). Picking out someone who deals with the party (`dealingNow`) opens Trade by itself; their stock, what they buy, and every other dealing (ship fitting, parts, scripts, mods, passage, hire, play) are there, not in the map card. The card keeps a name line and a "Trade tab" button (the full list returns to the card only when the HUD is hidden). `e2e/play.mjs` and `works.mjs` read dealer panels from `map-card` or `hud-trade`; not re-run since.
- Compass (`Map/Compass.tsx`, `interiors/compass.ts`): N is the top of every room's plan (the y = 0 edge, scene -z); it turns with the camera in every view (a ref, no state). A piece "against the north wall" faces south.
- Stick (`interiors/stick.ts`, `ViewRig.tsx`, `LocalMap.tsx stepToward`): the pad was swallowing into the map (touches on the overlay bubbled to the canvas's event source and arrived as a tap on the map, aimed from the pad's corner, which sent the party off and kept the stick out while a tap's walk was queued); the overlay now stops pointer and click events at its root. `stickWalk` (pure) keeps only the square being entered, so a turn of the stick shows at once and the stick takes over from a tap's walk. The curve is nearly straight (EXPO 0.15, full at 90% of the pad, was 0.6), walking is 0.85 of running, the pad is 158 px (a tenth less; the dogfight/race pad shares `STICK`).
- Furniture the code draws (shelves, racks, counters, cabinets) had its back to -z whatever wall it stood on, so those on the south and east walls faced the wall. `codeTurn` (packs.ts) turns them half a turn; `Stock` takes the same `face`.
- The Blaster Kit's 18 blasters (+ lods) came with barrels to -z (the game holds and hangs everything barrel to +z); turned in the files with `tools/turn-models.mjs` (run once per file); `tests/interiors/assets.test.ts` pins it.
- Test menu (`Play/TestMenu.tsx`): only in a build made with `VITE_TEST_MENU=1` (`deploy/test-plain.sh`, the 5002 copy on `sw5e_test`): heal, revive, credits, XP for the next level, give items by category tabs (Weapons, Weapon upgrades, Armour, Armour upgrades, Misc gear, Ships, Ship upgrades, Speeders, Speeder upgrades, Droids, Droid upgrades, Prosthetics/cybernetics, Beasts; each a filterable dropdown of everything of that kind in the rules data: equipment and enhanced items are carried by name, a ship is fitted by its size's role table (`roleShip`) and stands at the berth, ship upgrades go to the hold as ship parts, a speeder is parked, a droid or beast joins the party within the limit of four; the books have no speeder upgrades, so that list is empty), time (noon, +1 h, +8 h), go to any planet, settlement, building or room. The live 5000 build never has it. 5002 is on the tailnet at https://sw5e.tailb1e5d7.ts.net:10000 (`tailscale serve --bg --https=10000 http://127.0.0.1:5002`).

## Dice on the table (2026-10-21, the user's pasted physics roller)
- The physics roller (first pasted as `client/public/dice/roller.html`, three r128 and cannon.js from cdnjs) is `ui/dice.tsx` `diceTable()` since 2026-10-31 (Babylon, cannon-es; see "Babylon draws the 3D map"). `components/ui/DiceRoller.tsx` is the overlay: mounted once in `App.tsx`, it covers the screen whenever a roll is asked for, and holds the settled faces for 2 s before the logic reads them. The old inline roller on Home is gone.
- `useDice.ts` `rollOnTable(label, dice)` queues a request and resolves with each die's face. `lib/sw5e/dice.ts` has the mapping: a d3 is thrown as a d6 (halved, rounded up), a d100 as two d10s (tens, then units; 00 and 0 is 100).
- The player's rolls on the table: ability checks (`performCheck` in GameBoard: the d20s, two with advantage or disadvantage, and the substance's die; this includes a companion's check when the player picks the companion to roll); haggling, forcing a door, gentling a beast, play for the room (and its tool's die), breaking chains, the cold-night save; in combat, the attack roll, the weapon's damage dice (read from the equipped weapon), the saving throws, the death save, a power's attack roll, damage and healing dice.
- `onTable` in `useCombat.ts` wraps the attack, the end of turn, the enemy-turn step, reactions, casting and features. It runs the action; when the action needs a die not yet thrown (`NeedDice`), the store is restored and the action runs again from the start, so the run that is kept used the thrown faces. The runs must take the same path: `Math.random` is recorded on the first run and replayed on the next (`runOnce` in `dice.ts`), so an enemy's choice or a prepared die cannot change between runs. One `onTable` at a time: a call made while the overlay is up is ignored. Enemy dice stay instant.
- A check or a damage roll with a threshold or regulated armour throws its dice in one request (rerolls included), so one overlay per roll, not per die.
- The page locks after its result until the next roll; its 8 s fallback does not report while the player is holding the dice. The overlay sits at z-80, above the minigame screens (z-70).
- Not on the table yet (still random): `stealthRoll` (lying low and sneaking: five sync callers), the hazard saves (`deadlyAt`), the gamble, the forcecasting d100 (`findCrystal`), the other GameBoard rolls (Hit Dice and healing in rests and items, `checkFor`, the augment fitting), the substance and tool boosts inside combat, the other combat dice (boons, maneuvers, the enemies'), and the ship fights (`combatmanagers.ts`).
- An automated browser (`navigator.webdriver`) gets its faces at once (`useDice.ts`), so the e2e play-through does not wait on the overlay. Players always throw.
- Tests: `tests/dice/tableRolls.test.ts` (the mapping and the replay engine). The page was run headless with a host frame and posted the faces back; the overlay in the app has not been clicked through in a browser yet.

## Ground textures and no grids (2026-10-21; `components/Map/siteAssets.tsx`: `groundTexture`, `GroundSurface`; textures in `client/public/textures/ground/`)
- No grid lines on the 3D map (user, 2026-10-21): the squares of the rooms, the squares of the outdoor ground, the settlement's grid in `WorldLayer` (the view a new campaign opens on), the speeder flight's grid (`FlightScene`), and the coordinate grid of `LocalAreaMap` are removed. The starship fight's 50-foot grid (`SpaceScene`) stays: it is space, not a place.
- The ground round a building is a Poly Haven texture (CC0, 1K; credits in the folder's README): bare earth (`dirt_floor`, recoloured to the world's ground colour) for most worlds, `dense_sand` for desert, `dark_rock` for volcanic, `brown_mud_03` (sludge) for a sewer system. Urban (Coruscant) keeps the plain ground: no texture, no parallax (the user's rule).
- The settlement view (`WorldLayer`) uses the same textured ground, 600 units across; a planet's globe and a system's view are not ground.
- Hybrid: a Phong material with the normal map, and the diffuse and normal samples shifted toward the viewer by the height map (single-step offset parallax, `PARALLAX` 0.05, one repeat = 8 squares). Lambert takes no normal map, hence Phong with black specular.
- Deployed to 5002 at 63788ad + the WorldLayer/FlightScene fix (`sh deploy/test-plain.sh`); the served chunk has the shader and no grid colours. Not seen in a browser yet (no e2e was run on purpose; a hard reload may be needed to drop the old page). Check on 5002 for a black ground (a shader compile error blanks the plane), a swimming ground (lower `PARALLAX`) and the brightness of the recoloured bare earth (tint keeps the biome hue at full strength times `EARTH_LIGHT`; the first version, the biome colour times 2.5, came out black).
- Not done: the room walls.

## Floors, hull wear and panels (2026-10-21, asked by the user: the interior and rooftop floors were black)
- Room and rooftop floors (`siteAssets.tsx`: `floorLook`, `FloorSurface`; sets in `client/public/textures/floor/`, fetched by `tools/fetch-polyhaven.mjs`): a textured plane (shape of the room with its rounded corners) laid 0.004 over the flat slab in `RoomShell`, inside a Suspense (the slab shows until the maps load), lit by its normal map and shifted by its height map as the ground is (`groundShader`). By floor kind (`sprites.tsx` `floorOf`): plating `metal_plate` tinted steel-blue and lifted (the photograph is dark), hangar `hangar_concrete_floor`, stone `marble_tiles` (palace, archive) or `stone_tiles` (ruins), clay `dirty_carpet` (cantina, residences) or `terracotta_floor_tiles` (market, gates), sand `dense_sand`, rock `dark_rock`, and `concrete_floor_worn_02` for any roof. Cave floors (the `mask` ones) stay flat. Seen on 5002 in a cantina, garrison armory, palace, shipyard hangar and a roof; the plating was lifted after that (not seen again).
- Hull wear on ships and speeders (`models.tsx`: `Model`'s `wear`, `hullMaterials`, `boxUV`; `interiors/wear.ts`: `hullWear`, `speederWear`; textures in `client/public/textures/hull/`, made by `tools/build-hull-textures.mjs` from the Poly Haven sets in `textures/wear/`): the baked kit geometry has no UVs, so a hull model is baked with box-projected ones (per triangle, `grain` for small models) and its painted finishes (matte, sheen) are drawn with Phong materials that carry a panel normal map (plates, grooves, rivets, hatches, vents) and a shader patch laying grime, rust, scratches and chips (`wear.png`, one fetch, masks ranked so a level covers about that share) and scorch (`damage.png`) by two levels in a uniform. Age and damage are three steps each (`Wear`): 9 material pairs, one shader program. Age is a look from the name (the Empire's ships clean, freighters old hands, wrecks worst); damage: wrecked, disabled or hull 0 is 2, a ship carrying a `hull` score (only the party's after a fight) is 1. A speeder is aged by its model and name. Glass, glow and haze are untouched.
- Not done: parallax on hulls (a height map `panels_height.png` is made but not used: the offset is only right on flat faces); wear on buildings; max hull is in the fight's ship sheet (`shipSheet`), not on the world's `Ship`, so partial damage is one step, not by ratio.
- Damage step 1 is wrong after a fight: `GameBoard` writes `hull` back after every fight (even when shields took it all), and `hullWear` reads any `hull` as damage, so the party's ship looks scorched until repaired (GameBoard counts `hull === hullMax` as whole). Fix where `shipSheet` is at hand (pass a damaged flag), not yet done. The `SpaceScene` ships are not given `hull`.
- Ships are stretched to their footprint, so `ShipModel` passes a `grain` (the stretch, in steps 1 to 9) and the UVs are baked denser by it; seen only in the gallery (scale 1) and on dark Imperial hulls in hangars, not on a big freighter in a hangar.
- The plating floor is `floor/deck_plate`, a steel version of `metal_plate` made by the hull script (a tint cannot remove the photograph's brown).
- e2e: the step "3D map: no 2D plan; a tap on the floor walks the character there" fails the same way (the character does not move) on the build before the floors (7074487) and after, run as a slice (`ONLY="fighter matches|3D map: no 2D plan|FALLBACK"`); it was not caused by the floors. Not investigated.
- Cost (5002, the hangar probe `e2e/probe.mjs`, 1280x900): heading at 11 s, worst frame gap 1.0 s. No A/B against flat floors was run.

## Play-through test (e2e/)
- `npm run e2e` drives the whole game through the real UI and deletes what it created: the rules data endpoints, 25 generated worlds checked against the rules data, two characters made by the real rules (tools, languages, the feat's ability and skill, a species' hit points and unarmed strike), a campaign with a world and its quests, world edits, then in Play a fight (with death saves and the revive rule if it goes badly), loot, travel, a short and a long rest, the quests tab, paid passage, a level gained on the character page, and a consular casting the powers chosen in creation. It needs the running service: `npm run build`, then `sudo systemctl restart sw5e`. Do not build while it runs.
- The 3D map is tested in a campaign of its own (seed 7): walking on a tap, the way out, a signpost, a secret way found and used from the street, the views by double tap, a move in a fight, and the cantina's main bar (people one by one, a band on a stage, someone moves within 20 seconds, a double tap on the bar brings the bartender's card). Adding generator codes changes what a seed builds, so this campaign's rooms change with them. The map keeps `window.__map3d.at(key)` for this (`Probe` in GalaxyMap.tsx): page coordinates of `me`, `sq:x,y`, `area:<id>`, `sign:<area id>`, `exit:<building id>`, `secret:<room id>`, so the test taps where the thing is drawn. Headless Chromium draws a frame in a second or so; on a phone it is smooth.
- Gear, companions and transport (2026-10-15) are tested in campaigns of their own, each built through the API with a hand-made room (a disabled droid, a captive, a bounty hunter, a gambler; two troopers and a bolted droid; a dealer, a stable keeper and a wild beast; a base with a speeder bike and a wreck; a hangar whose ship is away): the Use buttons and a medpac, a tool panel, a substance's high; stabilize, mend and bolt a droid; free a captive and hire a bounty hunter (party kept after a reload); five rounds of gambling; a fight with an ally, a grenade through the combat panel's Items; buying a speeder and a mount, gentling a beast; taking and mending speeders and driving a route; the empty berth.
- Moving things with the Force (2026-10-18): two steps, each on a campaign built through the API (seed 3, the start room emptied and a crate set three squares in from its way in; a level 5 Togruta Consular with Force Push/Pull and Force Throw): a tap on the crate opens its card and Push moves it (saved in the room); then with a trooper across the room, a tap picks the crate out in the fight, Force Throw's chooser hurls it at the trooper (the log says how far it flew and the damage), and after Leave the crate is saved where it landed. The Consular's own fight step answers the chooser if its first power moves things.
- `/dev/models` shows every model for a look in a browser (see "The 3D map"); a screenshot script against it is a few lines of Playwright (`window.__gallery.info()` gives draw calls and triangles).
- Options (env): `VIEW=phone` for a 412x915 touch viewport, `FORCE=0` to skip the second (Consular) character, `KEEP=1` to keep the rows, `OUT=<dir>` for the screenshots (default `e2e/out`, gitignored), `BASE=<url>`, `SHARPNESS` (the 3D map's render scale, default 0.75), `NAV_TIMEOUT` (default 90 s).
- Failures are diagnosed (2026-10-18): while a run goes, `OUT/sys.log` gets a line every 5 s (load, CPU pressure, busiest processes, the server's answer time); each failed step adds an entry to `OUT/failures.log` (the server, whether the page's main thread answers, long tasks, requests still open). `e2e/probe.mjs` loads one 3D scene and profiles it; `e2e/probe-home.mjs` times deletes on the home page.
- The 2026-10-18 failures were a render stall, not the server: this VM has one core and no GPU, so a 3D scene's first frame is drawn in software and blocks the page for up to ~30 s (a hangar with a ship at 1280x900 took 31 s; the server answered in 10-90 ms throughout). A reload or goto then hits the default timeout. Hence the longer navigation timeout, and the map's render scale is now remembered in `localStorage` (`sw5e.sharpness`).
- `node e2e/shots.mjs` saves a Home screenshot and a mid-combat screenshot.
- Playwright is pinned to 1.58.0 because newer releases need Node 20. The browser is installed once per machine with `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium` (already done here, in `~/.cache/ms-playwright`). It renders WebGL in software, so the 3D map works without a GPU.
- About 13 minutes per run on this VM (10 at phone size without the second character). Pages with long lists are slow under software rendering (2 to 5 seconds a click in the powers step), which is the test machine, not the app. `TIMEOUT=25000` gives slow steps room.
- Quicker checks of one area, made during Phase 2f, were throwaway scripts (a caster made through the API, then Play; levelling 1 to 4; a feat at 4th level; the maps). If one is wanted again, write it against `e2e/lib.mjs`.

## Figures: textures, fur and gaits (2026-10-26, the user's list)
- Textures and normal maps (`tools/build-figure-textures.mjs`, made from noise here, CC0): `client/public/textures/figure/` skin, face, fabric (a twill), leather, plate (dents and scratches), scales and fur, each a pale detail map the vertex colour is multiplied by, a normal map and a height map; `fur/strands.png` for the shells. New finishes in `models.tsx` (skin, face, fabric, leather, plate, scales, hair, fur; `MATERIALS`, grains in `surfaces.tsx` `SURFACE_GRAIN`, normal strength per finish). A builder dresses its colours (`Kit.dress`: a person's skin or fur or scales, the clothes, leather, armour; a beast's hide by `hideOf`) and a droid's sheen becomes plate (`Kit.restyle`). Faces have eye whites, irises and pupils, brows, lids, a nose with a bridge and nostrils, lips and plain ears where the species has none of its own.
- Fur: the fur finish is drawn again in shells (`FurShells`, `furShellMaterial`: 12 instanced shells, 6 on a touch device), each pushed out along the surface and drooping a little, keeping only the strand map's hairs that reach that high, darker at the roots. Furry species (Wookiee, Ewok ...) and furry beasts (bantha, wampa, tauntaun, hounds) have it.
- Gaits (`interiors/limbs.ts`): each limb turns by a quaternion (`uQ`, vec4 x 13: body, legs, arms, hind and middle legs, tail, roll, wings) in the vertex shader. `Mover` in models.tsx draws every figure with its gait: walk (people, battle, protocol and assassin droids, brutes, runners), quad (beasts in diagonal pairs, tail swaying; spider droids), hexa (bugs in threes), wings (fliers beat their wings, faster flying on), roll (a BB unit: the ball rolls by the ground covered, about the axis square to its way, kept in the world so it rolls true round a turn, the head level on top), tripod (a droideka's three legs one after another), glide (astromechs, mouse, service and medical droids, slugs, serpents: no bob, a slight lean) and hover (probe droids float up and down and nose into the way they go). Nothing bounces any more (the old `Gait` bob).
- Droideka (the book's Ball Form: 15 ft. attack form, 60 ft. ball form): `Droideka` rolls up into its wheel when it goes faster than a careful step and unfolds onto its legs once it has stood still a quarter of a second (under half a second either way); in a fight it stays unfolded (`Figure fighting`), and steps at 1.2 squares a second; as a follower it shuffles on its legs for a short step (0.9 squares a second) and rolls for a longer one.
- Fighters walk between squares (`Stepper` in LocalMap, 4 squares a second) instead of being put there, so their legs move in a fight too.
- The gallery's walk section (`/dev/models?section=walk`) has a BB unit, a droideka, a battle droid, a probe, a bantha, a tauntaun, an acklay and a mynock pacing; `e2e/movers.mjs` shoots it and close looks.

## Failures and missing rules hunted (2026-10-26, second round; the user: "Investigate all failures and fix them, also hunt for failure modes that have not been identified yet")
- Dev mode loaded `main.tsx`'s imports twice: `server/vite.ts` added a random `?v=` to main.tsx, which Vite carried into main's own imports, so the pages loaded later used a second query client (a level-up never showed on the character page in dev). The cache-buster is gone. Production builds were not affected.
- Travel days are lived as they were travelled (`liveDays` in GameBoard): `passDays` judged every day of a journey by the place it ended at, so a five-day passage that ended outside a settlement, with no water carried, killed the character of thirst. House rule: a paying passenger (a fare, a ticket, a smuggler's passage, a hired ride) is fed and watered aboard; a stowaway lives on their own rations and the ship's water; the party's own ship is a ship's day; days on the roads are days in the wild.
- Work (downtime) "supports a lifestyle for the week": that week's lifestyle is no longer charged on top (`lived` in `commitDowntime`).
- Exhaustion's levels (the rules data's table) now apply: 1 disadvantage on ability checks (`performCheck`; downtime checks had it), 2 speed halved, 3 disadvantage on attack rolls and saving throws, 5 speed 0 (`speedFactor`, `attackPlan`, `savingThrow` in useCombat), 4 the hit point maximum halved (`hpCap` in GameBoard: rests, healing, the HUD, the fight).
- Grappling and shoving (Player's Handbook): Grapple, Shove (prone or 5 feet away), Escape and Let go in the fight (`grapple` action, `contest`: Athletics against Athletics or Acrobatics; one attack of the Attack action; a creature at most one size larger, within reach). House rule: the book's dragging is not played; a grapple ends when the two are out of reach (the grappler walking off lets go). Stat blocks whose hit grapples ("grappled (escape DC 16)", 26 of them; restrained too where said) now grapple (`StatAttack.grapple`), and Escape is made against the DC.
- Damage taken while dying is a failed death save (two on a critical hit), and damage of at least the hit point maximum kills (`dyingDamage`).
- Weapon properties now played: versatile (the two-handed die with no shield and no second weapon), thrown and returning (a melee weapon thrown at its range, as many times as there are of it carried; a returning one comes back; the last one thrown leaves melee unarmed; house rule: what was thrown is picked up after the fight), mighty (Strength or Dexterity), dire and piercing (the player's toggle trades to-hit for damage), defensive (the turn's first attack and damage for as much AC until the next turn), auto (only burst or rapid; a single penetrating shot is played as a plain shot). Not yet: penetrating, switch, modal, smart, specialized, interlocking, biting, bright, corruption, luminous's light.
- Substances: a high "for the next minute" ran out in the minute that taking it passes (since graded spices got parsed durations). Back to the house rule: out of a fight the high waits for the next check, at least ten minutes.
- Enhanced items read from their text now (`parseItemEffects`, `itemSenses` in withItemEffects): darkvision (goggles, visor, Mandalorian helmet) against darkness; advantage or disadvantage on Stealth (echo belt, dampening boots, cybernetic legs) into hiding; a decoy shadow (Defel mimicker: attacks against at disadvantage until hurt); weapon damage reduced by the proficiency bonus (Jensaarai headgear; house reading: all weapons, the fight does not know enhanced ones); water at will (atmospheric decanting vessel). Set bonuses ("while wearing and attuned to this cloak and Jensaarai Armor") are not read. Still text only: 40 adventuring gear rows (class charge items, storing rings, boots of bounding ...).
- The map: a person's tap box wins over furniture under the same pointer (a bench in front of a disabled droid took its taps), the Here tab's names pick people out as a tap does (in a crowded room someone walking past takes a tap), and a fight is drawn beside the combat panel on wide screens (`BesidePanel`: taps on the character's squares landed on the panel).
- Tests: `tests/items/fightrules.test.ts` (10). e2e: steps written before the HUD tabs open the Party tab; the dealer, vein and minigame steps match the current wording and the day's lifestyle; `tapFor` retries a tap and falls back to the Here tab's names; `inView` aims the camera with the probe's `look` when zooming cannot show a square; `e2e/lib.mjs` samples memory with tasklist on Windows.

## Walls, doors and windows on the wall line (2026-10-26, the user's list with screenshots)
- The user: "ONLY the area of the wall WITHIN the window frame should be transparent"; "Corridors for round/oval buildings should meet the curved wall with a matching radius, and doors/windows in curved walls should also match the radius of the wall"; "Doors are rectangular when their frames have a curved top ... door textures should be relevant to the texture pack used by the settlement".
- Found: a window took its whole square out of the wall from the floor up (only a lintel stood over it), so the wall was open beside and under the frame; an arched doorway's square was open over the arch; a round room's doors and windows stood on the straight line of its bounding square while its curved wall ran on through them; the corridor to a round room stopped at that line, short of the curve; every leaf was a plain board of planks.
- The wall line (`outline`, `outlineAt`, `outlineOf`, `wallStretches`, `insetAt` in shared/logic/footprint.ts, tests in `tests/interiors/footprint.test.ts`): a room's outline as one path (straight sides, a quarter circle at each rounded corner). Every door, way out and window stands on it across from its square (`wayPlace` in LocalMap), turned along it; the wall is the path less a square's width at each opening (straight runs and partial arcs: `CornerWall` and the kit's bent tiles take `from`/`to`).
- Each opening's square of wall is drawn whole but for the frame's outer outline (`openingOutline` in models.tsx, `holeOf`/`piercedWall` in interiors/Walls.tsx, `PiercedWall` in LocalMap; tests `tests/interiors/walls.test.ts`): beside and under a window and over both; a window taller than the low overhead wall leaves the wall beside and under it. On a curve the piece, the frame (`Opening`, now `BentModel`) and the leaf are bent onto it (`bendAlong`/`bendOf`). The same placement is used on a round room's closed block seen from outside.
- A room set against a round room (`meetsOf`, `MeetFill` in LocalMap): its floor and closed block are filled out to the curve, its side walls carried on to meet it, its own wall along that stretch left out (the round room's curve is the wall there) and its door there stands on the curve where the round room's does.
- Doors (`DoorLeaf`): the leaf is the shape of its frame's opening (arched, rounded or square) and of what the room's walls are (`DOOR_LOOKS` by wall set: planks for plaster, sun-bleached planks for sandstone, plate for concrete, shutter for corrugated iron, rusted panels for brick and old hulls); hangars, gates, vaults, cells and airlocks keep the painted shutter.
- e2e/bases.mjs (10 steps) now also checks the rider, Dismount, the hovercart and the tent's card. A full play-through on 2026-10-26 (dev server, FORCE=0) passed 62 of 75; see HANDOFF.md for the failing steps (none in the code this round touched once the dealer step's names were updated).
- Looked at with `e2e/walls.mjs` (seed 7's cantina: its oval Entrance and the corridor to the main bar; close looks via `__map3d.look(from, to)`, added to the map's probe in GalaxyMap.tsx; it holds the camera until a reload).

## Spoken dialogue (2026-10-28, the user: Gemma 4 e2b through Ollama for NPC <-> player and player <-> party member talk; prefix caching, 1 GB iGPU + CPU + 32 GB RAM; 1-3 paragraphs; every prompt a fresh session)
This is a local model on the user's own machine, not an API-based GM (Phase 5's "ask first" does not apply); the manual GM Interface loop stays.
- Where: `server/dialogue.ts` (`POST /api/dialogue` streams NDJSON `{delta}` then `{done, text}`; `GET /api/dialogue/status`), `components/Play/TalkPanel.tsx` on the card of a person (replaces "say what you do on the GM Interface tab") and of a party member (not beasts). Env: `OLLAMA_URL` (default 127.0.0.1:11434), `OLLAMA_MODEL` (default `gemma4:e2b`). If Ollama or the model is missing the panel says so and the GM tab is the way.
- **Fresh session**: every request is `[SYSTEM, one CARD]`: never a transcript. The card is the scene (place, planet, time, law), who is speaking (name, species, role, disposition or "in the party"), who the player is, the speaker's own last reply (one, trimmed to 300 characters, kept in the page: the only memory, so a follow-up like "what do you mean?" has something to mean), and the player's line. This recap is a small step beyond "fresh"; drop `recap` in TalkPanel to have none.
- **Length**: the prompt asks for 1-3 short paragraphs, 120 words; `num_predict` 240 is the cap; the server stops the model at a 4th paragraph, removes markdown and cuts a capped reply at its last whole sentence. `think: false` (the model thinks by default, which would add many seconds).
- **Prefix caching, measured here** (Ollama 'llamacpp' runner, gemma4:e2b, ~10 tok/s out, ~75 tok/s prompt): a prompt is reused only up to a start the runner has seen as the END of an earlier prompt (the model has sliding-window attention), so a shared system prompt alone gave no saving (full 6 s prompt every time, even with 400 shared tokens). Sending the system prompt alone once (`prime`) makes it such a start: every later request then evaluated only its card (about 1.3 s instead of 6 s; first token about 1.1 s). So: SYSTEM is constant (changing a word re-primes), `num_ctx` is fixed at 2048 (changing it reloads the model), priming runs at server start and again when a reply's prompt rate shows a full evaluation (> 7 ms/token), `keep_alive` is 2 h. Replies are one at a time (a queue).
- Measured replies: 70-95 words, 11-13 s whole, words streaming from ~1 s. Generation speed is the limit, not the prompt; a smaller `MAX_TOKENS` is the lever if it is too slow.
- House rule: what is said is colour. It rolls nothing and changes nothing in the game (Persuade/Deceive/Intimidate stay the buttons for that); the model is told not to decide outcomes, invent items, prices or quests, or speak for the player. It does not know the speaker's real inventory or prices: only what the card says.
- Not done: the model is not given the world's quests, rumours or the speaker's wares (a card line for them would be the next step); no conversation memory beyond the last reply; hostile enemies in a fight do not talk; no voice. Not browser-tested: the endpoint was tested with a script (`dlg-test.ts` in the scratchpad), the panel only typechecks and builds.

### Voices (2026-10-30; the user: "see if the model can generate audio speech responses (relegating text to optional subtitles) ... ONLY produces relevant speech ... voices to be unique, and persist to the character ... emotional tones ... non verbal sounds ... septabee")
- **Gemma 4 E2B cannot speak.** Its model card: text, image and audio IN (a ~300M audio encoder for speech recognition and speech-to-text translation, clips up to 30 s), text OUT only (2.3B effective / 5.1B with per-layer embeddings, 35 layers, 512-token sliding window, 128K context). Ollama returns text either way. So the text is spoken by a speech engine on this PC.
- **Speech only**: the system prompt now asks for the spoken words alone, with sound cues in square brackets from a fixed list (`CUES`: chuckle laugh sigh breath groan cough wheeze hmm gasp growl beep whistle static), stutters and trailing off for the nervous; `speechOnly` (server/dialogue.ts) strips what still slips through: *asterisks*, (brackets), other [bracketed] text (a variant such as [sighs heavily] becomes [sigh]), narrating sentences ("Hessa glances over.", "R5-D8 beeps twice."; a sentence with I, me, you or we in it is kept as speech), text outside quotation marks and the marks themselves, and the speaker's own name at the start. 90 words at most now. Checked on three live replies (merchant, astromech, hostile Trandoshan): speech only.
- **The engine** (server/speech.ts, `GET /api/speech/status`, `POST /api/speech` -> WAV, cached in `.cache/speech`): Windows' OneCore voices (George, Hazel, Susan on this PC, all British English), driven by one PowerShell process kept running (WinRT SpeechSynthesizer, SSML rate and pitch): 0.5 s for a first line, ~80 ms warm. Optional **Piper** (`PIPER_EXE`, `PIPER_MODEL`; NOT installed: needs the user's go-ahead to download; a multi-speaker model such as en_US-libritts_r-medium gives 904 different speakers and American accents). No engine: the browser's own speech, without effects.
- **The voice of each speaker** (client/src/lib/voice.ts `voiceOf`): drawn from the speaker's id and name (so the same on every screen and every visit, nothing stored), species, role, kind and a gender where the game has one (party characters' appearance; people met have none, so theirs is by the seed). Engine voice; pitch by playback rate with the speech rate compensated (only the pitch moves); EQ; gravel. Droids: astromechs, BB units, gonks and mice speak in bleeps (subtitles say what they mean); protocol droids polished metal; B1s nasal and ring-modulated; others deep and ringed. Wookiees growl; Jawas and Ewoks chatter (`gibberish`, the same sounds for the same words); Hutts and large species low and rough; small species high. Troopers, Mandalorians, bounty hunters and pilots through a helmet; inquisitors, Kel Dor and Gand through a mask, breathing between sentences; mercenaries, pirates and smugglers rough; nobles, officers and Jedi polished. `comms` on TalkPanel puts a voice on a comlink (nothing passes it yet).
- **Mood** (`toneOf`): nervous, weary, amused, hostile, excited bend pace, pitch, loudness and gravel for the reply.
- **Cues**: from a SeptaBee-made bank when `client/public/audio/voice/manifest.json` lists one (empty now), else made in code (noise through bands for breath, sigh, gasp, wheeze, cough, static; a buzz for groan, growl, hmm; tones for beeps and whistles); chuckle, laugh and hmm are spoken by the voice itself for people.
- **The panel**: Voice and Subtitles checkboxes (kept in this browser); with subtitles off the panel says "X speaks..."; cues show as "(sighs)". Stop speaking button.
- **SeptaBee**: a GUI-only modular DAW (no command line, binary .bee projects; hosts VST3 and LV2). Claude cannot drive it: the user builds and exports, Claude wires the files in. How, and what the game wants: `.claude/skills/septabee/SKILL.md`.
- Checked: tests/world/voice.test.ts (4); in the browser (dev 5099), `speak` played a merchant, an astromech, a B1, a trooper over comms and a Wookiee to the end with no errors (not heard: the browser pane has no speakers I can hear). Not checked: the panel in play, a phone, co-op (each screen speaks its own replies).
- **The harness** (2026-10-30; the user: "design and build a harness for septabee ... suggestions for ways to make the program more accessible for you"): SeptaBee has no command-line flags, MIDI in, OSC, socket or scripting (strings of septabee.exe and septabee-sounds.exe), so the harness is around it: `tools/soundlab.mjs` renders sounds from JSON recipes (21 presets, one or more per cue; IRs from filter chains), inspects WAVs and imports a SeptaBee WAV export as a take of a cue or an IR (mono, 48 kHz, trimmed, -3 dBFS, manifest entry). Tests: tests/world/soundlab.test.ts (4). The presets are drafts, not yet heard or installed: the game still makes its cues in code until `bank` or `import` is run. Suggestions for SeptaBee's author (render command, text project format, control port, text modules, MIDI in, accessibility tree) are in the skill.
- **Piper, 2026-10-30**: the user put Piper at ../rhasspy-piper-38917ff, which was the **source** (no piper.exe; its only model, etc/test_voice.onnx, is the project's rough 16 kHz test voice), and said yes to a download: Piper's Windows release 2023.11.14-2 (22 MB, github.com/rhasspy/piper) is unzipped in `release/` and the voice model en_US-libritts_r-medium (79 MB, huggingface.co/rhasspy/piper-voices; 904 speakers, American English, 22.05 kHz) is in `voices/`, both inside that folder (outside the repo). `findPiper` (server/speech.ts) now finds Piper by itself (PIPER_EXE/PIPER_MODEL, PIPER_DIR, or a folder beside the project with piper in its name; piper.exe and a real model with its .onnx.json; the test voice is skipped) and `/api/speech/status` has a `piper` note saying what is missing. Piper is now the engine (`/api/speech/status`: piper, 904 speakers): each character gets one speaker of the 904 by the same seed as before, with the page's pitch, effects, moods and cues over it. Piper loads its model in 2.5 s each start, so `server/speech.ts` keeps one process per pace (length scale in steps of 0.1, three at most, let go after 3 idle minutes; `--json-input`), falling back to one process a line: first line 2.6 s, then about 0.5 s, and cached lines at once. Checked: three lines through the route; in the browser `speak()` for a merchant and a trooper (helmet) with no errors; not heard. The Windows voices still speak if Piper is moved away. `tools/soundlab.mjs look` draws a waveform and spectrogram PNG, so Claude can see a sound it cannot hear.
- Not done: gender for people met (would need a field in world generation); accents beyond British English (Piper would bring them); a comms call in the game to put `comms` on; the party member's own voice (the player types).

## People and regard, families, comlinks and the microphone (2026-10-30; the user: "assign gender to NPC's and have this inform name generation too ... NPC's with the same last name MUST share a race, and become family members, interactions with familial NPC's pass on reputation data to other family members (at a reduced rate - rate depends on the strength of the family tie) ... levels of friendliness/unfriendliness - as players improve their friendliness with NPC's we unlock more details about their character sheet ... players and party members to EACH have a comms item ... the users mobile device microphone as an option to speak vocally to NPC's and distant party members (fallback to keyboard)")
- **What was there**: `attitudeOf` (a trader's view of the party, -4 to 4, from faction renown, species, dealings), `Standing` { renown by faction, dealings by trader } in `campaign.factionInfluence`, the Nemesis records (own names, no species or kin), a `hasCommlink` check for NPCs, a `call` gear kind (an allied contact's tip), and TalkPanel with an unused `comms` prop. No gender, no families, no per-person regard, no sheet reveal.
- **Gender and names** (`Occupant.gender` male / female / other, `GIVEN_NAMES` by gender in shared/logic/index.ts; `personFor` / `personIn`): a civilian is made male or female (4% other), takes a given name from that list and a family name. Worlds made before have no gender: `genderOf` (relations.ts) reads it from the given name, else the id. Droids are "other". The gender reaches the dialogue card ("(she/her)") and the voice (`gender` on the TalkPanel speaker, so a woman is no longer given a man's register).
- **Families (house rule)**: `World.families[surname] = { name, species, members: [{ id, given, to?, kin? }] }`. A new person is, 35% of the time, of a family of THEIR species already in the world (a family name is never shared by two species; names run out, then a surname is made), related to one member as sibling / child / spouse / parent / cousin (a spouse or a parent only where the other has none). The tie between two people is the strongest chain of links, each worth `KIN_TIE` (spouse .6, parent/child .7, sibling .6, cousin .35; a chain is the product). Old worlds have no families (`kinOf` is empty). Captives and the nemeses made while playing are named the same way (a nemesis's record id is its member id, so its family is in `World.families` too, and `NemesisRecord` has species, gender, family).
- **Regard (house rule)**: `Standing.regard[occupantId]`, -100 to 100, in `factionInfluence`. Gains stop at a cap by kind: talk 25 (a reply: +1), trade 45 (a deal: +1), deeds 100 (hiring +8, freeing a captive +40); falls: attacked -50 (a named person fought while hostile, halved), killed -80. Levels: one per 20 points, the words of `ATTITUDES` (hostile, cold, wary, cool, neutral, warm, friendly, generous, devoted). `attitudeOf` takes the person's level as one more term (their prices move by it). `reputation(world, standing, id, amount, cap)` moves the person's regard and each relative's by amount x tie (rounded; under a point is not heard), and says who heard. Killing a named person in a fight tells their family (toast "will hear of it"); killing a nemesis does too, and a relative who is a nemesis takes the grudge on (`hatePerKin` 40 x tie, "will not forget"). Contract completion and theft do not move regard yet.
- **The sheet (house rule)**: `sheetFor(world, occupant, regard)`: level 0 shows species, gender, role; 1 (warm) their family, home and what they carry; 2 (friendly) hit points, armor class, speed, size; 3 (generous) attack, saves, challenge; 4 (devoted) other attacks, traits, reactions, resistances. The rest show as "??? (friendly)" and the panel says what the next level would show. `PersonSheet` (TalkPanel.tsx) is under the talk box of a person picked on the map.
- **Comlinks (house rule, labelled on the Comms tab)**: `shared/logic/comms.ts` `commsOf`, `needsComms`, `canCall`. Everyone in the party carries one: GameBoard issues "Commlink" to a character or companion without (a droid has one built in, a beast cannot carry one), with a toast.
