A call needs a commlink at each end and the same planet (the book's range is 30 miles, less in dense places; between worlds needs a ship's transceiver, not played). The **Comms** HUD tab (`CommsPanel.tsx`) lists the people the party is warm to (regard 20 or more) who carry a commlink, and the other players of a co-op game; a contact is talked to with TalkPanel in comms mode (their own voice, the comlink's sound); a co-op member is sent a typed or spoken message (`say` over the co-op socket; server/coop.ts relays it; the receiver sees a toast and hears it in the sender's character's voice over the comlink).
- **The microphone**: `POST /api/stt` (server/dialogue.ts) sends a short 16 kHz mono WAV (base64) to the same Gemma 4 E2B that writes the replies (Ollama takes audio in through the message's `images`; checked: a Piper-spoken sentence came back word for word; about 1.2 s warm, 17 s on a cold model) and returns the words. `recordSpeech`, `wavBase64`, `transcribe` (client/src/lib/voice.ts) record until 1.3 s of quiet, and `useMic` (TalkPanel.tsx) is the Speak button, in the talk box and on a co-op message: tap, speak, the words are sent. **Nothing leaves the PC** (no browser speech service). A browser lets a page use a microphone only on https or localhost: a phone at http://192.168... cannot (the button is disabled and says so; the Tailscale Serve https address works), and a refused permission leaves the keyboard.
- **Checked**: tests/world/relations.test.ts (6: gender, kin ties, regard caps and spreading, the sheet tiers, a generated world's people and families, nemesis families and kin vengeance), tests/items/comms.test.ts (calls), tests/world/voice.test.ts (WAV encoding, transcripts). In the browser (dev server on the TEST database): the Comms tab, a commlink issued and saved, a contact at regard 45 listed as "friendly" and called (a reply came back), the Speak button's refusal message when no microphone is allowed. The microphone itself (a real recording) and the co-op message between two clients were NOT tried; the STT route was tried with a Piper recording.
- **Not done**: the world in an existing campaign has no genders or families (only new worlds do); a person met is not told apart by species-flavoured names; regard from contracts, theft, gifts and bribes; kin of a nemesis who are civilians do not turn hostile (their regard only falls); a family tree view; the NPC's own commlink calling the party; the ship's transceiver between worlds; a reply sometimes talks ABOUT the player in the third person ("Gear, the fighter, sounds eager"): the narration filter does not catch a sentence that starts with the player's name.

## The law in person, cells, shops by day and night, errands (2026-10-30; the user: "Captives need to be restrained/confined in physical cells that need to be broken into/unlocked. We need more realistic NPC movements to and from relevant areas (their homes, jobs, traders, palace invitations (as relevant), going to the garrison to report thefts/crimes. Also security response times (we should have security physically despatched from the garrison, unless they are on patrol and are closer), We also have troopers in some locations that fail to respond to hacking attempts, theft from locked loot boxes etc. We need some physicalised trade inventory within shops that can be stolen (immediate security dispatch during the day, alarms and cameras that need to be disabled during the night while the shop is closed and locked")
- **What was wrong** (found by reading, then fixed): the camera loop, the access panel and the alarm panel asked nobody (no watch check at all); `unlock` and `takeLoot` asked the law only where `here.danger === 0`, so troopers in a room the generator rates dangerous (a garrison's detention block, a hideout) stood by; the lawmen were read from the SAVED world, not from where the clock has them; and a crime sent officers who started AT the scene after a table delay (4, 10, 30 minutes by law), not from anywhere. Picking a shop's lock reported trespass whether or not anything saw it.
- **Watching (the fix)**: `slipped(what, crime)` in GameBoard: the enemy's watchers first (`unseen`: Stealth against their Perception, a miss raises the alert), then the law's own and any camera (`lawUnseen`), whatever the danger of the place. It guards: picking a lock, taking from a cache, every panel (loop the camera, open the doors, silence/disarm the alarm), cells, shop stock, the shop door. `lawUnseen` and `callTheLaw` use the world as the clock has it (`withPresence`), and `pullPatrols` brings a patrol standing there off its rounds into the room before it is turned on the party. `isLawman` also takes troopers, constables, sentries, sentinels, watchmen and security roles (never droids, never the enemy's hostile troops).
- **Dispatch (house rule, `reportCrime(world, area, crime, now, via)`; the books give no police)**: the settlement's own garrison (`lawBase`: a garrison, else a command post, guardhouse, gatehouse or gate; one held by the enemy, `BUILDINGS[type].hostile === "faction"` of the antagonist's side, is not the law) musters a duty squad after the law's **turnout** (`responseFor`: strict 1, policed 3, frontier 8 minutes; theft and trespass x1.5, a heinous crime x0.5; the cartel 5), and it **runs to the scene a room at a time** (`RUN_STEP` 1 minute a room, `townPath`: rooms, doors and the street between a district's buildings). `Pursuit.arrivesAt` is when the first squad gets there; `Squad.to` is the room it runs for, `Squad.from` the building it left. Squads are on the map from the minute they muster (in front of the garrison door, then on the way, "(answering the call)"), they are walked in on like any squad (the Stealth roll, `advanceSearch`), and at the scene they search the district as before. With no garrison, or no way there on foot, they come to the scene after the old table delay.
- **Patrols (house rule)**: 80% of a garrison's lawmen (not the gate, the detention block, the armory or the command room) walk the settlement's buildings by day (`Routine.patrol`, `addPatrolsAndVisits`): 07:30 to 10:30 at one building, to 13:00 at another, an hour and a half at the barracks, then a third until 18:30 (the stops are contiguous, so they are seen walking between). `nearestPatrol` finds the one nearest the scene by the ways on foot; if it would be there before the duty squad has turned out, it goes ahead as a first responder (1-2 officers; the rest come from the garrison) and is off its beat until the search is called off (`Errand` kind `dispatched`, `to: null`).
- **How the law is told (`ReportVia`; house rule)**: a lawman on the spot, an alarm and a shopkeeper's panic button reach the garrison at once; a camera takes a minute (a clerk watches the feed); someone with a commlink takes two; **a witness without one walks to the garrison** (`WALK_STEP` 2 minutes a room, five minutes at the desk), a real person who leaves the scene and shows up in the garrison (`World.errands`, `errandAt`, `withPresence`), and the officers go out when they arrive. A crime nobody saw is not reported (`witness` with nobody about). `Pursuit.via` and `from` are shown on the map's status ("sent from Theed Garrison (the shopkeeper hits the panic button)").
- **Calls and rounds (house rule; `Routine.visits`)**: some residents, shoppers, patrons and locals are called to the palace's petitioners' hall (12%; every 3 to 6 days, 10:00 to 12:30; only where there is a palace) and some residents, shoppers and locals go round a shop of a trader they deal with (40%; every 2 or 3 days, 16:00 to 17:00). They walk there (15 minutes, as every move between places), stay, and walk home (`whereAt`). People already had homes, jobs, markets and pubs by their routines; the new ones are these and the errands.
- **Cells (house rule; "Cells" in shared logic)**: those held are in a holding cell or slave pen, a barred locked piece of the room's furniture (`RoomAsset.cell {dc, open}`; shut at DC 15 when it says nothing; `cellOf` shares a room's captives out round its cells by id and `placeContents` puts each inside its own). The hideout's holding cells and the camp's pens have cells now (they were given a store's crates before): `fitBuilding`, the last step of `planBuilding`, replaces such a room's furniture with cells and a restraint rack and places the people inside; it and the shop alarm panel use dice of their own, so a seed generates the world it did before (a first version drew from the plan and shifted the dice: seed 34's gambling hall then had a room no corridor reached, a latent planner fault now hidden again; tests/world/layouts.test.ts is the check). To reach a captive: pick the cell's lock (security kit or slicer's kit), break the door down (Strength, DC 5 over, noisy: the guards come), or open every cell from the guard console (Intelligence, 12 + the law). One held in the cell alone (`restraint "cell"`) is free when it opens; shackles and collars are dealt with as before once it is open. An open cell is drawn with its door swung back and no field across the bars. Rooms made before cells keep freeing as they did, except detention blocks that already had cell furniture.
- **Shops by day and night (house rule; "Shops by day and by night")**: the stock of a shop is on its sales floor, dealt out from the trader's stock for the day (`stockOf`, `shelfStock`: the same every time; a tap on a shelf, rack, case or bin lists it, with Buy and Take it) and the 3D shelves show the models of what is left. Cases, weapon racks and cabinets are locked (DC 15). **By day** the keeper watches the floor: Take it is Sleight of Hand against their passive Perception (10 + Wisdom); seen, they hit the panic button and the law is on its way AT ONCE (`via shopkeeper`), their regard falls 20; unseen, the loss is found when they count the stock ten minutes before closing and reported then (`World.discoveries`, `noteTheft`, `advanceDiscoveries`: a call, or the keeper walking to the garrison). A taken item is gone from the day's stock (`Ledger.stolen`). **By night** (shut, door locked) in a place with any law the shop is **armed** (`shopArmed`): a sensor on the door and another on the stock. Picking the lock now first rolls to disable the door sensor (Intelligence or Dexterity against 12 + the law, a kit helps; a miss rings the alarm through to the garrison, the lock can still be tried); inside, taking anything while armed rings it at once; the shop's alarm panel (the sales floor has one now) disables both until the morning's opening (`Area.disarmedUntil`). The camera is the existing one: it records what is done in front of it (law 2 and over) unless looped. A theft at night is found when the keeper opens.
- **Old campaigns** (`LIFE` 14, `livenWorld`): patrols and visits are added to every built settlement, shops get an alarm panel; no new cells in rooms already built (their captives keep the old way unless the room has cell furniture).
- **Checked**: tests/world/security.test.ts (8: the dispatch and the run, how the law is told and the witness's walk, the patrol going first and its beat, the lawmen roles, cells, the stock and its theft, the armed shop and the counting, the visits). In the browser (dev 5099 on `sw5e_test`, campaign 498): the cell card (pick, break, console, open the cell and a captive standing in it, an open cell drawn without its field), the shelf card of a shop with the real stock (Bucket x1, Buy, Take it), a lawman in a detention block seeing the console work (Stealth failed: turned on the party, reported), and a theft reported from the keeper's panic button: the squads left the garrison gatehouse after the turnout, ran to the shop and found the party there. The dice (rolled in the throttled pane) were NOT waited out: the cell lock, Sleight of Hand and door sensor results were not seen end to end in the browser, only in tests and by reading.
- **Not done**: the e2e run (`npm run e2e`; the service has to be built first) was not made; the settlements' own crimes (`rollIncident`) still send their squads as they did; there are no security tags on stock (taking an item out past the door); shelves show no vehicle or droid lines; a witness who is stopped on the way (talked down, silenced, killed) does not stop the report (`interceptCall` is only for the search's calls); no sheet or dialogue knows a person was called to the palace; a patrol is never seen to come back to its beat after a search (it is simply back at the minute the search is called off).

## Babylon draws the 3D map (2026-10-31; the user: "using Babylon js to overhaul our graphics, shadows and lighting", every 3D scene, a full move; branch `babylon`, merged into main on 2026-10-31 at their word)
- **How (decided with the user)**: React Three Fiber still builds each scene as a three.js scene graph and runs the frame loop, the cameras and the taps (its raycasting on the three objects), so the scenes, picking and the e2e hooks (`__map3d`, `__gallery`, `__arena`) are as they were. `components/Map/babylon.ts` is the renderer R3F is given (`<Canvas gl={babylonGl}>`): every frame it mirrors the three scene into a Babylon scene and Babylon draws it. three.js stays for building geometry, for raycasting and for the tests; it never draws. (The first plan, React components of our own in place of R3F and every scene ported to them, was dropped for this with the user's agreement: the same look for far less code changed.)
- **The mirror**: the Babylon scene is right-handed, so three's coordinates and rotations carry over; its meshes must be told that three's triangles are counter-clockwise from the front (`sideOrientation` 1), else Babylon draws their backs. World matrices are copied as they are when they change (`freezeWorldMatrix`), and the camera's view and projection matrices likewise (`MirrorCamera`), so what is drawn is exactly where three's raycasting finds it. Geometry is shared by every mesh using it, with its vertex colours and the extra attributes the shaders read (limb, pivot, mark), and goes when three disposes it. Materials become physically based ones: Lambert → roughness 0.95; Phong → roughness 1 − shininess/140 and a little metal from its specular (none when see-through); Standard as it is; Basic, sprites, points and shader materials → unlit; maps, see-through, sides, depth, additive blending and `toneMapped: false` are kept. Instanced meshes → thin instances; points are sized as three would; lines (drei's fat lines a pixel wide for now); sprites face the camera; drei's Text is drawn on a card. Light strengths: both engines put 1/π in a direct light's diffuse, but three divides its ambient and hemisphere lights by π too and Babylon's hemisphere does not, so those are × 1/π; an ambient light is a hemisphere whose ground is its sky.
- **Shadows and lights**: the sun's shadows are cascaded (`CascadedShadowGenerator`, PCF) and follow the camera, in place of three's fixed box (±30, ±60); a lamp that casts gets cube shadows where the tier allows. Tiers (`useQuality`, which siteAssets.tsx hands to `setRenderTier`): lite 2 cascades of 1024, no lamp shadows, 4 lights a material; full 3 of 1024, 1 lamp, 6 lights; full on a desktop 4 of 2048, 2 lamps, 8 lights. Each mesh is lit by the lights nearest it (`requireLightSorting`). ACES tone mapping and the exposure as three's.
- **Points** have a shader of their own in the bridge (three sizes points in its vertex shader, which Babylon's materials do not): soft round sprites, or the material's map, a world size shrinking with distance as three's (drei's Stars about 90 pixels a unit away). Without it the dogfight's space dust was a wall of white squares.
- **Every canvas** is given the bridge: GameBoard's map (and its fights and space), PitArena, `/map` (GalacticMapView) and its old viewers (GalacticMap3D, SystemMap, LocalAreaMap), FlightScene's three, the dogfight (its own loop calls R3F's `advance`, which renders through the bridge), the sheet's dice, the unused PlanetaryExploration. Seen in the browser: the gallery, the game's fight in a shop (floor textures, reachable squares, the aim line, figures; `__map3d.at("me")` still projects), the dogfight lab (ship, dust, foes firing), `/map` of the same campaign.
- **Shader patches**: what three did in `onBeforeCompile` is told to the bridge by whoever makes the material, `shaderPatch(material, patch)` (a WeakMap beside it: `userData` is cloned as JSON; `limbMaterial` hands its base's patch to its copy). Fur shells (the instance is the shell, `gl_InstanceID`), hull wear (grime, rust, scratches, scorch) and sea waves are material plugins (`CodePlugin`, with the mesh's own texture coordinates passed on: Babylon passes its own only for a map without repeats); the limbs are `LimbPlugin` (`limbMaterial` sets `userData.limbParents`). Parallax (the textured walls, floors, ground, rock and bark, the dogfight hulls) is Babylon's own: the height map goes into the normal map's alpha once a pair (`packed`), marched through (parallax occlusion) on a desktop's full tier, a plain offset elsewhere. Three's own shader materials (the planets, the capital ships' shields, the shield flashes, drei's Stars, the galaxy) run their own GLSL in a Babylon ShaderMaterial, given what three declares (`threeShader`: matrices, attributes, instancing; three's colour-space chunk written out, its other chunks left out) and their uniforms copied each frame.
- **Limb shadows**: a moving figure's materials carry a `ShadowDepthWrapper`, so its shadow is drawn with its own vertex shader and falls as it stands (it was the rest pose).
- **Matching three's look**: three's ACES brightens by 1/0.6 before its curve and Babylon's does not, so the exposure is divided by 0.6 (everything was darker before); Babylon 9's PBR defaults to an Oren-Nayar diffuse that darkens rough surfaces, so materials use Lambert's as three does; Babylon reads a map's alpha only when told it has one (`hasAlpha` for see-through and cut-out maps: the dice's number cards were opaque black).
- **The cost of shadows**: Babylon draws every caster into every cascade. Each cascade now draws only the casters across it (its four side planes; what stands between it and the sun still casts), the far cascades leave out casters under 1/150 of their width, and a lamp's cube shadow only what is within its reach; thin instances get bounds over all their instances (camera culling too). The sites gallery went from 2,608 draw calls to 1,194; three drew the same page at the same 15 fps in the browser pane. An engine that cannot draw cascades (WebGL 1) gets one map over the casters.
- **The look** (full tier, a canvas drawn whole; not an overlay such as the cockpit): the backdrop (rendering group 0: what three draws first, the sky, its suns and planets, the far land) is caught from the camera in a small cube (`ReflectionProbe`, 128 on a desktop, 64 on a phone, every 30 frames) that the surfaces reflect, as bright as the sun is; its ambient part is zeroed, since three's ambient and hemisphere lights give that, so only the shine is added (metal and glossy hulls look like metal now). Bloom (`DefaultRenderingPipeline`, image processing off: the materials tone-map themselves) round what is drawn near white (threshold 0.98: lower, the white decking hazed over by day), MSAA 4 on a desktop, FXAA on a phone. Contact shadows (`SSAO2RenderingPipeline`, on Babylon's prepass, half size) on a desktop. Lite has none of it.
- **Street lamps after dark** (`models.tsx` `useLamps`/`LampLight`): the sky (LocalMap `Sky`) lights them from dusk (daylight under 0.3) to dawn; a street's lamp (`Decor`) and a garden's (`siteAssets.tsx` scatter pieces tagged "light") are then each a warm point light under the bulb (14, reach 10 m, decay 2), marked `userData.cluster`. The bridge gathers those into one `ClusteredLightContainer` (WebGL 2 with float blending; elsewhere they stay lights of their own), so a street of lamps costs about one light. Seen on Ulatx's decks at 22:16 (12 lamps).
- **The dice table** (was `public/dice/roller.html`, three r128 and cannon.js from cdnjs in an iframe, posting messages): `ui/dice.tsx` `diceTable()` is the same table, dice, throw, settling and reading, with three's geometry drawn by the bridge and cannon-es; `ui/DiceRoller.tsx` loads it on the first roll (so the pages that never roll do not load the 3D code) and calls it directly. The baize is lit (it takes the dice's shadows; three's ShadowMaterial has no Babylon match). No more runtime CDN.
  - **Its frame** (2026-11-03, the user: the dice roller ran at a very low frame rate). Measured on the HD 530 with GPU timer queries: the table took 19 ms on the GPU at 615×615 points and 2 pixels to the point (bloom, the reflection cube and MSAA in a pass of its own: 9 ms of it; the pixel count most of the rest), and the map under it went on drawing in full (19 ms more in a small pane, far more full screen), though the table hides it. Now the table has none of the full look (`ren.look = false`; the canvas's own antialiasing) and at most 1.5 pixels to the point: 6.4 ms. While it is shown, `COVER.by` (Map/babylon.ts) keeps every other renderer from drawing (their frame hooks still run; they keep their last picture), and between rolls its loop neither steps the dice nor draws (0.1 ms). The dice catch up a slow frame in up to 8 physics steps (was 4, with frames capped at 50 ms), so they fly at their own speed down to 15 frames a second instead of slowing below 30.
- **Checked**: tests/interiors/babylon.test.ts (8, on Babylon's NullEngine: a mesh's matrix, vertices, colours and front faces; the material mapping; the limb plugin; thin instances; light strengths; the patches as plugins and a copy keeping its base's; a three shader material's code, uniforms and blending; the sun's shadow and its casters). In the browser (dev 5099, side by side with the user's three.js server on 5000): the gallery (ships with hull wear, the sea's waves, Wookiee fur, parallax rock and bark, figures; brightness now as three's), the dice table (a d4 to a d20 thrown, settled, read and handed back), the game's street on a sea world by day and at night with the probe, bloom, contact shadows and clustered lamps.

- **Assets gone after the settlement view** (2026-10-31, the user: "When switching from the settlement view back to the local map all of the assets disappear"): Babylon's `Mesh.dispose` disposes the mesh's geometry with the last mesh using it (mesh.pure.js: `releaseForMesh(this, true)`), but the bridge keeps a cached geometry for each three geometry, and the game builds its shapes once and hands them to every scene. Leaving a scene for another (the street's `WorldLayer` for a building's `BuildingScene`) disposed the Babylon copy; the next scene found it in the cache and drew it empty (87 of 225 meshes with 0 vertices: figures, pillars, signs; found through `window.__babylon`, which is how to look for it). `drop` now lets go of the geometry first (`releaseForMesh(mesh, false)`), `geometry()` makes again one found disposed, `sync` frees a geometry unused for 1800 frames (`IDLE_GEOMETRY`) and `meshNode` re-applies a mesh whose geometry changed. Test: `tests/interiors/babylon.test.ts` ("a shape kept by the game is drawn again…", fails on the old code). Seen after: three round trips settlement and party, 0 empty meshes. It needs a rebuild: the shortcut builds.
- **Walking, balustrades, night, moons, mounts, warm-up** (2026-11-01, the user's list with two screenshots of a gantry from inside and out):
  - *Stride*: the legs went a whole cycle for each square (3.2 a second on a walk). `advance` (interiors/limbs.ts) now cycles `CYCLES_PER_SQUARE` (0.3) a square, capped at `MAX_CYCLES` 2.2: about one stride a second on a walk, 0.6 crouched, two on a run. It is for every figure that strides, not only the party.
  - *Prone*: `beginJump` (LocalMap) does nothing while prone (a leap at a wall included).
  - *Keys*: the stick's and the keys' walk is marked `driven` (stick.ts `stickWalk`); letting go of the last key (or the stick, or a blur) calls `step(0, 0, 0)` at once from the key handler (ViewRig), and `stepToward` ends a driven walk where the party stands. It used to finish the square it was heading for, and the map's own frame ran once more before the rig's. A tap's walk is not driven and goes on to its end. Checked in the browser: the position at the key's release is the position half a second later.
  - *Balustrades*: one `Balustrade` (LocalMap): a top rail, a lower rail and posts, pale steel, 1.0 high (`BALUSTRADE_HEIGHT`). The outside of a walkway, balcony and gallery (`rail`) and the inside of a gallery's overlooking sides and a walkway's long sides (the sides with no way in: `rails` in RoomShell) are the same piece. Inside they were the building's wall texture, at a rail's height (gallery) or a full wall's (walkway). The ways at a walkway's ends keep their wall. Not looked at in the browser (the test campaign has no walkway): look at a gantry on an urban world or a station.
  - *Ground parallax*: `PARALLAX` in siteAssets 0.065 → 0.045 (it swam). Interior floors (`FLOOR_PARALLAX`) are as they were.
  - *Night*: the ambient and the moon's key light at night are half what they were under a full moon, and less under the other phases (`night` in `Sky`: 0.5 × (0.35 + 0.65 × `moonlight`), the half indoors, where the moons do not count); the day's lights are unchanged; the street lamps are unchanged (LampLights).
  - *Moons* (shared/logic `moonPeriod`, `moonElongation`, `moonLit`, `moonlight`): each moon goes through its phases in a period of its own, 8 to 24 days (by its drift), from a start of its own (its azimuth), by the clock's days (they stood in the same place each day, so were never new). A moon stands on the sun's arc turned on by its elongation (full: opposite the sun, rising at dusk; new: with it), in WorldSky (`moonDir`, its `clock` prop), and the sun lights it as before, so its phase is on its face. `moonlight` is each moon's lit share by its height, weighted by its size. Test: tests/world/skies.test.ts. House rule: the periods and starts are invented, not the books'.
  - *Riders*: about one in four of a district's strolling folk (`strolls`, 5 squares or more between their doors) rides a beast (`pickMount`), and has an errand at each door (`MountedStroll`, `errandAt` in limbs.ts): rides to it, gets down at the beast's side, walks to the door and is out of sight for 7 s, comes out, loads a package on the beast, gets up and rides on to the other door. Nobody rides inside: the street folk are not drawn inside a building; the people of a room are the room's. Test in limbs.test.ts. Not looked at in the browser.
  - *Room changes*: the district's rooms are warmed while the party is in the street (an effect in BuildingScene, from 1.5 s on, one job every 90 ms): the floor and roof maps of each building (drei's texture cache), the wall set of each room (`wallMaterial`), the kit's walls and the furniture files of each room's pack in its tone (`loadModel`). That is the loading, the decoding and the tinting; what it does not do is compile the shaders or put the maps on the graphics card, which still happens when a room is first drawn. If a first look at a room still hitches, that is next (Babylon `whenReadyAsync` on a hidden copy).
  - *Shortcuts and the wi-fi* (the user, later the same day): both desktop shortcuts always listen on the local network (HOST=0.0.0.0, firewall rule for the local subnet, once, needs an administrator window), so friends join by the join code over the wi-fi; `-Lan` is accepted and ignored. Still no login: only on a network you trust.
- **Wall textures, resolution, Options** (2026-11-01, the user's screenshot of a wall with two textures fighting; and "very low resolution"):
  - *One wall set a building*: the walls of a room took their pack (so their texture and kit) from the room's name first, so two rooms sharing a wall (a servants' hall beside an audience chamber) drew it in two textures, sandstone against concrete, in one plane. Walls now come from `wallPackOf(buildingType)` (interiors/packs.ts), one for the whole building, everywhere (`RoomShell`, the cutaway rooms, doors, dome roofs, the warm-up); the room's own pack is only for its furniture (`pack`/`packLook` in RoomShell, `wallPack`/`wallLook` for the walls). Test: packs.test.ts.
  - *The pattern in the building's frame*: `boxUV` takes an origin; the plain wall's box (`WallGeometry`, with its `at`) and the kit's walls (`PackWalls`) lay the pattern out in the building's coordinates (the `WallAnchor` round each room of the cutaway), so two faces left in one plane draw the same pixels. Pierced pieces round doors and windows, corners and `MeetFill` still use their own frame. Test: walls.test.ts. Not looked at in a palace (the test campaign is a cave): look at a palace or a big station cutaway for any fighting that is left.
  - *The low resolution was the Auto scale*: the render scale steps down 0.25 at a time while frames are under 30 a second, to a floor of 50%, and is remembered (`sw5e.sharpness`); on the dev machine (Intel HD 530) it had settled at 50%, with the full-tier effects on. Options (the button in the map strip, replacing Detail, Mode, Full screen and Hide HUD, which moved into it; their test ids are kept) has Resolution (Auto, 50 to 200%, Native), Detail, Effects (shadows, ambient occlusion, edge smoothing, glow: `EFFECTS`/`setEffects` in babylon.ts, kept in `sw5e.effects`, applied through `retier`) and Display. A fixed resolution is not changed by the frame-rate monitor.
  - *Where the frame goes* (measured 2026-11-01 on the dev server, the spaceport street of the test campaign: 2430 three objects, 1235 Babylon meshes, about 800 materials, 210 calls, 107 k triangles; Intel HD 530, render 704×720). Frames were driven by hand from the console (`mirror.sync`, then `scene.render`, then a `readPixels` to wait out the GPU), because the browser pane, hidden, makes no frames of its own; that is also why the earlier "13 fps at 50% and at 100%" was wrong. Before the fixes, by night on full: CPU about 21 ms a frame (matrices 1, the mirror's sync 11 to 12, Babylon's culling and calls 6 to 9, the game's 153 `useFrame` hooks 0.6 together), GPU 26 to 34 ms. The GPU is the pixels: half the resolution took it from 17 to 9 ms with the effects off, so at full screen it is several times this. Of it: post effects (SSAO 3, bloom 2.5, 4× MSAA under 1) and their fixed passes about 4.6 even with nothing drawn; parallax occlusion 5; the sky probe 1.5; the lights a few; the geometry with no lighting at all about 6 (layers that fill the screen: the sky dome, the sea, the ground, which is two meshes of 343 k and 183 k vertices). The sun's shadows (forced on there; a district casts none) on the desktop tier, 4 cascades of 2048 at high filtering over 901 casters: GPU +27 ms, CPU +9, +550 calls; 2 of 1024 at low: GPU +4, CPU +5.6. Babylon draws solid things grouped by material, not nearest first (its default `PainterSortCompare`); nearest first saved about 20% of the GPU in a try, but two meshes in one plane would then swap which one shows as the camera moves (the fighting walls above), so it is not done.
  - *Fixed* (babylon.ts): a light was told `setEnabled` every frame, and Babylon then walks every mesh of the scene for it (18 lights × 1235 meshes, 1.4 ms): now only when it changes. The sync made strings and arrays for every mesh and material every frame (the geometry's and the maps' signatures, the material lists, the emissive colour, the walk's stack): now compared in place (`sameGeometry`, `sameMaps`). Sync 11 to 12 → 3 ms. An integrated chip (an Intel GPU, not Arc: `INTEGRATED`, from `getGlInfo().renderer`) gets a phone's full look on "auto": 3 cascades of 1024 at medium, one lamp's shadow, 6 lights a material, no SSAO, no parallax occlusion (plain parallax stays); MSAA and bloom stay. By day with the street's rule (no sun shadows): GPU 17 ms, CPU about 9 (was about 26 and 21). With sun shadows (as in a building): 25 against 31 on the old setting. Options still turns any effect up. Test: babylon.test.ts ("each frame copies only what changed").
  - *Next, if frames are still short*: the resolution (each step down is the biggest single saving left: the GPU is pixel-bound); fewer full-screen layers and a lighter far ground (the 343 k vertex mesh); a depth pre-pass or a stable nearest-first order; one Babylon material for three materials that are alike (800 materials for 1235 meshes: each call binds its own); shadow maps not redrawn when nothing moved; the first look at the full tier compiles shaders for about 20 s on this machine (the scene stays black).
- **Room changes, the district's loading screen, nearest first, looks alike** (2026-11-01, the user: "are we able to use more than one thread", a loading screen when entering a district that does not come back between rooms, nearest first with the flicker answered, merge the materials that look alike). Measured on dev 5099 with the test menu's "Go there now" and frames driven by hand (R3F `advance`), in the Rakessos palace:
  - *Why changing rooms stalled*: walking into the audience chamber compiled 87 shaders, frames of 1.5 s, 355 and 277 ms; back out and in again, 549 ms. Three causes. Babylon compiles a material for the lights that reach it, and each room brought its own (an ambient, a key light, lamps, one casting shadows), in a different order each time, so every material in sight was compiled again. Babylon frees a shader with its last material, so going back compiled it again. And a moving figure's shadow shader (`ShadowDepthWrapper`, for the limbs) is named with a random token for each figure and each shadow, so its cache never matched: 257 of the 300 shaders were these, 8 of them different.
  - *Fixed* (babylon.ts): one rig of lights made once (`makeRig`): the sky (every ambient and hemisphere light summed: exact), 3 sun slots (the one casting shadows first; its shadow generator kept, idle when nothing casts, so the street with no sun shadows and a room with them share shaders), the tier's shadow-lamp slots (the nearest lamps that cast; idle: dark), and every other point and spot light in the clustered light, from a pool (`pool`: making a light goes through every mesh twice). A light with no falloff, or a device that cannot cluster, keeps a light of its own as before. `Effect.PersistentMode` keeps every compiled shader for the session. The engine names a source-made shader by its source (`sourceName` in the renderer's constructor). Maps: the pixels of each image are on the card once (`gpuOf`, by three's `Source`, which a clone shares; an image file's pixels are not uploaded again when a clone's `needsUpdate` moves the version: the floors did that on every visit), the packed normal and height maps likewise; a label's card is kept by what it says (`cardOf`). The lamp's shadow (6 faces, 540 calls a frame in a palace room) skips what is under 8 cm and is drawn every other frame (`LAMP_REFRESH`). Result: 52 shaders for the whole walk, none on going back; changing rooms 60 to 160 ms at worst (React making the room, Babylon readying its meshes).
  - *The loading screen* (LocalMap BuildingScene, `useWarmup`, GameBoard `DistrictLoading`, test id `district-loading`): the first time a district is entered this session, one room of each kind (its building's kind, its pack, its form, above or below ground: 30 kinds in the spaceport's district) is opened in turn behind the screen, the renderer drawing everything in view or not (`preparing`, Babylon `skipFrustumClipping`), until all it drew is ready (`prepared`) or five seconds pass. 7.6 s for the spaceport's district on the HD 530. Then rooms never entered compile 0 or 1 shader. Not on a software renderer (the play-through test's browser). The city has several districts, each shows it once.
  - *Nearest first* (Mirror `nearFirst`): solid surfaces by bands of distance (two to each doubling) that a surface leaves only a quarter band past their edge, and by material and mesh within a band. Two surfaces in one plane lie side by side, nearly always share a band, and swap at most once as the camera crosses, never back and forth; walls in one plane draw the same pixels anyway (one wall set a building, its pattern in the building's frame) and decals have their polygon offset. GPU in the street 22.4 → 19.2 ms. Not looked at moving (no frames in the hidden pane): watch for a surface that pops as the camera passes.
  - *Looks alike* (Mirror `material`, `lookKey`): three materials that show the same (type, colours, maps, blending, sides...) share one Babylon material; one whose colour or opacity changes a third time gets its own (a flicker), as do those with a plugin (fur, wear, waves, parallax, limbs). In the street, 1162 meshes now draw with 190 materials (50 looks, 118 of their own: figures, patched or changing; 22 labels and shaders) where they had about 790. Babylon's CPU per call barely moved (7.5 ms for 224 calls): its cost is per draw, not per material.
  - *More than one thread*: no gain for the frame rate. The GPU is the limit (19 ms against 11 ms of CPU in the street at 704×720), and the browser already draws in its own GPU process and compiles shaders on driver threads (`KHR_parallel_shader_compile`, which Babylon uses). WebGL cannot draw from two threads into one canvas; drawing from a worker (OffscreenCanvas) would need the three scene, which React builds on the main thread, sent across every frame. What a worker could take is one-off work that hitches: world generation, path finding, packing the normal and height maps (now once a session). WebGPU (Babylon has it) would cut the CPU per call, not the GPU's work.
- **Trees, rocks and plants missing inside the walkable ground** (2026-11-01, the user: "some 3d assets are not being drawn (rocks I think)", then "all rocks and trees missing from within the confines of the local 3d map, they are drawn OUTSIDE of the traversable area", "generate a new campaign so you are not restricted to the cave"; a new campaign, 626 on Naboo, a forest world, in the test database):
  - *Cause* (an old bug of the Babylon renderer, 2026-10-31, in the build on 5000 too): Babylon keeps a thin-instanced mesh's instances on its GEOMETRY's vertex buffers (`world0..3`, `instanceColor`), and the mirror shared one Babylon geometry between every mesh using the same three geometry. The game draws one piece of the asset library in several places (`LocalMap` BuildingScene: `Scatter` of the open ground's blockers, of the cover, of the belt beyond: the same `site:<piece>:<v>:<biome>` model, so one three geometry and three instanced meshes). The one set last took the instances for all of them, so the belt outside drew the trees, rocks and plants of the ground inside as well, and the ground inside drew none. 31 of the 83 instanced meshes in Theed's street shared a geometry. Found by drawing each mesh alone, close to an instance of it (a mesh of 22 trees drew 0 of 22, its twin 22 of 22; setting its buffer again made it draw), with markers at the data's positions.
  - *Fix* (babylon.ts `own`, `giveBack`, `fill`): an instanced mesh has a copy of the shape of its own, taken from the cache entry's `spare` list (or made) and given back, stripped of its instance buffers, when the mesh goes; plain meshes still share one. Test: babylon.test.ts ("two instanced meshes of one shape keep their own instances"). Checked in the browser on dev 5099 (campaign 626): no geometry is shared by two instanced meshes, and every tree, rock and plant tried (52 instances of 18 meshes) is drawn at its place; overhead, the yards between the buildings have their trees again.
  - *Not the logic*: where the trees and rocks stand is `ambientBlockers` (siteAssets.tsx) on the squares left by the rooms, ways, doors and signs (LocalMap `clear`); unchanged since 2026-10-19. A district of streets and lots keeps most of its ground clear by that rule: the trees stand in what is left. If the inside is still thinner than wanted, that rule is the place (`GROWTH`, `BLOCKING_CAP` 130, the margin of `clear`), not the drawing.
- **Not done yet**: fat lines with their width; the phone check by the user (lite and full).

## Ammunition on the fallen and Nemeses (2026-10-29; the user: "NPC corpses drop ammo along with their weapons" and "borrow HEAVILY from scripts/NemesisMod.dll ... a 'Nemesis' style system")
- **Ammo drops (house rule)**: `ammoDrops` in `shared/logic/gear.ts`, called by `afterFight` (new optional 3rd argument `weaponOf`, which GameBoard passes from `factsOf`): each fallen creature leaves, beside its weapons, 1-2 spare power cells or slug cartridges, 3-8 arrows/bolts/darts, and a launcher round half the time (a power generator is not spare). Seeded by the creature's id, so the same creature leaves the same. Droids leave none (they are powered down, not looted). `PLAIN_ROUND` moved from GameBoard to gear.ts. Tests: `tests/combat/ammodrops.test.ts` (4).
- **What `scripts/NemesisMod.dll` is**: a compiled .NET mod for Red Dead Redemption (ScriptHookRDR). There is NO source in the folder; only its structure (types, fields, tuning-constant names) was read, by reflection-only loading of a copy (the originals are marked as downloaded from the internet and Windows refuses to load them; they were left untouched). So this is a Star Wars design on the same model, not a port, and no code was copied. Its pieces that carry over: a record per enemy with hate (tiers resentful / vengeful / murderous) and fear; personalities with temper profiles (hothead, coward, proud, cold, greedy, loyal, drifter); intent (hunts / won't hunt / provoked only); notoriety scaling ambushes; scars; history; grudge origin; mercy (spare / finish / interrogate); leader seats filled when a boss falls; news. NOT carried over (the mod has them, this does not): the Mind (facets, values, needs, ties, goals), life events and backstories, camps, supply caches and treasuries, wars between gangs, the newspaper's articles, telegrams, kids and pets.
- **Where**: `shared/logic/nemesis.ts` (pure, JSON state in `World.nemeses`, so it is saved with the campaign), `components/Play/NemesisPanel.tsx` (the "Enemies" HUD tab; a red dot on the tab when someone lies at the party's mercy), GameBoard (`sayNemesis`, `showMercy`, the daily `tickNemeses` effect, the settle block in the fight-end effect, the hunt in `ambush`). Combatants gained `struck` (damage the party dealt, by type) and `lastHitBy` (who last struck the character) in `useCombat.ts` `tally`. `nemesisAmbush` / `Encounter.nemesis` in shared/logic: a "robbery" encounter led by the nemesis, whose only option is "Face them" (no toll).
- **House rules** (the books have none): a fresh foe becomes a nemesis if it is a person (not a beast, horror or droid) and (it fled: a leader always, others 70%) or (it brought the party down) or (a beaten leader lives on 35%, anyone else 10%). A record has its own id (`nem-N`): combat ids like `trooper-0` are reused between fights. Hate and fear gains are scaled by the personality's temper; fear gains by the party's notoriety. A hunt needs a hate tier (35 vengeful, 70 murderous; 12 resentful), nerve (fear < 80; below 55, or while fiercer than 70, they come; "provoked only" only when murderous), rest over and a pause since the last hunt (36 / 18 / 8 hours by tier); the chance each alley is 4 / 12 / 30% x notoriety multiplier (1 to 1.8) x aggression / 50, and under the law's eye half of the alleys. Rank 0-5 (Recruit to Warlord): +20% hit points per rank, +1 attack per two ranks, +1 AC per three; the crew is 1 + rank/2 (max 3) of their kind. Scars come from the damage type that did most (a heavy beating can take a hand, eye, arm or leg: title "Hand-Taker", "One-Eye", "Hobble"; the kit has prosthesis-* models to draw it with, NOT yet drawn). Executing may fail (they "cheat death" 25% + 5% a rank, twice at most) and then they return days later scarred; sparing a proud one deepens the grudge, others cool and a greedy / fearful / drifting one may turn informant (no use made of that yet); questioning frightens them and may mark two others as "known". A fight's win by the party raises notoriety by 0.25 a kill, a nemesis killed by 8; it decays a point a day. The leader's chair: when the top-ranked of a side dies their next takes it (+1 rank).
- **Tests**: `tests/combat/nemesis.test.ts` (13), including that a reused combat id is not taken for a nemesis. `npm test` 423/423; a vite build to the scratchpad succeeds. NOT browser-tested: the panel, the toasts, the alley hunt and the mercy buttons only typecheck and build. To see a hunt quickly in a test build: finish a fight where an enemy flees, set `hate` high on the record in the campaign's galaxyMap, and walk into an alley in a lawless district.
- **Not done**: nemeses are met only in alleys (a street meeting, a visit to the party's base, a ship boarding would follow); an informant does nothing yet; "known" nemeses are not shown on the map; the shooter mode (see Next) will need a real-time version of the same settle step; the co-op host decides mercy.

## Modes and the real-time shooter (2026-10-29; the user: "2 major modes ... selected after generating the campaign: tabletop RPG (dice, turn-based) and real time (first/third person) shooter: no dice, no turns, thrown grenades, blaster fire, animated lightsabre combat; KOTOR and Battlefront 2" and "we already have real time movement, jumping, jetpacks, grapple hooks and a targeting reticle: extend the existing traversal logic to incorporate shooter gameplay; top down view should NOT be available in shooter mode")
- **The mode** is `campaign.specialRules.mode` (`"tabletop"` | `"shooter"`; the column existed, now declared in `insertCampaignSchema` so the server keeps it; no migration). `modeOf` in `shared/logic/shooter.ts`. GameBoard asks once the campaign has a world and no mode (dialog `mode-dialog`, also for campaigns made before), and the **Mode** button in the map strip asks again. A campaign with no mode plays as the tabletop until it is chosen.
- **No top-down view**: `useView` (interiors/aim.ts) has `shooter`; `viewsFor`/`coerceView` take "overhead" out of the cycle (V and the button) and turn a stored or asked-for overhead view into over-the-shoulder; the stored tabletop preference is not overwritten. The overhead rig is therefore never used in a shooter campaign, in or out of a fight (`tests/combat/shooterview.test.ts`).
- **How a fight runs**: every fight begun on the walkable map (`fight()`'s three cases and the alley gang's) goes through `startFight` in GameBoard. On the tabletop it is `initiateCombat` as before. In the shooter mode it also makes the combatants (so the end of the fight is settled by the SAME code: loot, XP, rounds spent, companions, nemeses, renown), then sets `useCombat.realtime` (keeps `inCombat` true so every "not while fighting" guard still holds; the turn-based scene and `PlayCombat` are hidden; LocalMap's `duel` is off so the map stays the walking map) and starts a session in `useShoot` (interiors/shoot.ts). `interiors/Shooter.tsx` (`ShooterLayer`, rendered by LocalMap's BuildingScene beside `ViewRig`) runs `shared/logic/shooter.ts` each frame against the map's own collision, floor heights, aim (`aimNow`) and muzzle (`handNow`), draws the foes with the shared `Figure`, bolts, grenades, blasts, the blaster viewmodel and the animated saber, and at the end calls `useCombat.finishRealtime` (outcome victory, stable or left; fled foes hp 0 + `fled`; `struck`, `lastHitBy`, `ammo`, `spentAmmo` filled in). Grenades thrown are taken out of the pack by GameBoard (`useShoot.last`). Walking out of the place, or a scene that never starts (5 s watchdog, with a message) ends it as "left"; an unmounted layer ends it as "left" too (never stuck "in combat").
- **Controls** (interiors/shoot.ts, ViewRig.tsx, ShooterHud.tsx): mouse look by pointer lock (the first click in a fight captures it; Esc releases), left button fires (held), right button guards with a saber, **G** grenade, **R** reload (R is the run toggle outside a fight), **Q** swaps gun and blade, **B** guard and **X** fire on a keyboard; W A S D walk, Space jump, F hook, Left Shift burst, E/Enter use, all as before. During a fight a click is never a tap on the map. A phone has Fire/Swing, Grenade, Reload, Gun/Saber and Guard buttons beside the stick (a touch still drags to look). The HUD: hit points, saber focus, hostiles, gun and magazine and pack, grenades, hit markers (white, gold for a head, red for a kill), a red flash when hurt, gold when a bolt is turned.
- **House rules (all in shared/logic/shooter.ts `SHOOT`; no dice)**: damage is the printed dice at their average (+ bonus); an automatic weapon (Auto/rapid/burst) fires 5 a second for 45% damage each, others 1 shot per 0.33 / 0.5 / 0.85 / 1.4 s by damage; a head is x1.5. A shot is a bolt (24 squares a second) that hits what it passes through; a miss is spread (3% at rest, more moving, flying and with each shot, wider past the weapon's range). A wall stops a bolt; furniture only below 0.9 squares (so a counter is cover). Armor Class takes 2% off a hit per point over 10, to 40%. A magazine is the weapon's reload number; a power cell/generator refills it whole and uses one cell, other rounds go one for one; the pack and the rounds used are those of the turn-based kit (`ammoKit`), so the end of the fight takes them from the pack. Enemies see 16 squares (half as far if unaware: the ambush), react in 0.5-1.1 s, take aim for 0.35 s (a red glint: the telegraph), then fire a volley (the stat block's multiattack, 1-3), their aim off by an angle that grows with armor and distance and shrinks with their attack bonus; ranged ones keep about half their range and strafe, melee ones run in, wind up 0.5 s and strike. A gang's leader falling breaks the gang (they run for 3.5 s and are gone: `fled`); a non-brave enemy runs at 22% hit points. A noise (a shot, a burst) wakes foes within 18 / 24 squares. Grenades are read from their rules text (radius, dice at the average, or "blinded"): thrown at 12 squares/s with a lob, they bounce (0.45) and burst after 1.6 s; full damage within 40% of the radius, half at the edge, none behind a wall; **they hurt the thrower and companions too**; a flash blinds for 5 s (shots go wild). The saber (a blade named saber/lightfoil/shoto): a swing is 0.45 s, lands at 0.2 s on everything within 1.9 squares and 66 degrees to either side, a chain inside a second adds 15% a step (three-step chain: left, right, overhead); held up (**guard**) it turns bolts from the front within 80 degrees back at the crosshair, and the reflected bolt bends toward the nearest foe (Battlefront-style), at 4 focus a bolt and 7 a blow (20 focus, 5 a second back; at 0 the guard breaks for 1.2 s). Saber-wielding enemies turn aside 30-35% of what comes at them. **Being brought to 0 hit points ends the fight as "left for dead"** (outcome `stable`: the party wakes later with 1 hit point, as the turn-based fight does after three successful death saves); there are no death saves in real time. **This is a decision the user has not made: ask whether a real-time defeat can ever be death.**
- **Tests**: `tests/combat/shooter.test.ts` (19: bolts, walls, headshots, spread, magazines, enemy sight/aim/telegraph/surprise, armor, gang break, fleeing, grenades, flash, the saber's arc, chain and guard, melee, defeat, placement, replay), `shooterview.test.ts` (2). Real browser (a server on the TEST database: `PORT=5099 DATABASE_URL=.../sw5e_test VITE_TEST_MENU=1 npx tsx server/index.ts`): `e2e/shooter.mjs` (mode saved and honoured, no overhead, a fight: 36 shots with two auto-reloads from the pack, a grenade, the saber, the guard, the end: 50 XP, 2 cells and 1 grenade taken from the pack, the load saved) and `e2e/mode-tabletop.mjs` (the dialog asks, the tabletop fight is still turn by turn). Test hooks: `window.__shooter` (the live sim), `window.__look(yaw, pitch)`.
- **Not done**: Force powers, class features, maneuvers and the character's other turn-based options are not available in a real-time fight (the saber and the gun are; the powers list is ignored); companions fight with their stat block's attack (droid and beast followers as such are not special); enemies steer round walls but have no path-finding (one can get stuck behind a wall); no crouch, prone or sneaking in a fight, no cover mechanic beyond bolts stopping at furniture; bolts have no sound or impact effect; the foe's death is a body falling over (no animation); enemy sabers have no swing animation (the blow is felt, not seen); the party's own figure does not animate the swing in over-the-shoulder view (the blade does; the body does not); a fight against many foes is not tuned; enemies placed in a wall are brought out to the nearest open square, so sentries in rooms above the street come down. **Road ambushes and the pit arena are still turn-based** (they are not scenes on the walkable map). Co-op (up to four players) is not begun: a shooter fight is for one party member's view.

## Shooter: the stuck defeat, walls, powers and the rest of the "not done" list (2026-10-29, later; the user: "I died and respawned with an enemy in the room, the enemy is not attacking, nor taking damage from my blaster fire or lightsabre swings, and the doors are non responsive so I'm stuck. Also in a fire fight some of the enemies got stuck in the wall, after following me into a different room")
- **The stuck defeat (reproduced in `e2e/shooter-defeat.mjs`, CROWD=1 = no safe room to wake in)**: left for dead with no safe room (`safeHarbour` found none and `cameFrom` was null), the party woke in the same room with the hostile's watch still at "attacking". Three tabletop rules then locked the shooter: (1) `autoFought` ("They attack!") had already fired for that room and was never re-armed, so no fight began and the blaster and saber, which only exist inside a fight, did nothing; (2) `leaveBy` barred every door but the way back while the watch was "weapons out" or worse and returned in silence; (3) the Fight button was the only way to start one. Fixes (GameBoard): **left for dead puts the watch of the fight's areas down to "suspicious"** (house rule, both modes: hours have passed and they lost interest; they will notice the party again); **`autoFought` is re-armed when a fight ends**; **in the shooter mode no door is barred** (a fight run from ends as "left") and **a hostile whose weapons are out ("confronting" or worse) starts the fight by itself**, once the map has the party standing in the place (`partyStoodAt`; before that the 5 s watchdog would kill a fight begun at page load); **X (the fire key) with hostiles here and no fight begins one** (you open fire first); in the tabletop a barred door now says so (toast) instead of doing nothing.
- **Walls**: (1) `walk()` in shooter.ts reported a step taken when a body pushed straight into a wall (the free axis "fits" by zero), so `steer` never tried another angle: foes stood pressed to walls. It now counts a step only if it went a fair part of the way. (2) `unstick()`: every half second a foe not standing on ground it fits is set down on the nearest square it fits (a map that changed under it; a start in a wall). (3) `route()` (BFS over the map's walkable squares, eight ways, no corner cutting) and `chase()`: a foe with no clear straight walk to its target goes round by the way found (looked for again every 0.6 s), so one behind a wall comes through the door. (4) **Companions** (`Follower` in LocalMap.tsx) glided in a straight line to their goal from where they stood in the LAST room, so they appeared outside the new room and cut through its wall; they are now keyed by place (they appear beside the party in the new room), walk the A* route, and are set down at their goal if ever found in a wall. `e2e/followers.mjs` (two companions follow the party through a door: 2.2 squares from it at every sample). The hook `window.__followers` shows where each is drawn.
- **More of the "not done" list, done**: foes **take turns** (at most `SHOOT.maxShooters` = 3 of a side aim or fire at once; a gang is a fight, not a wall of bolts); **crouching** (C, a Crouch button; a smaller mark 0.8, spread x0.6, the camera and figure go down: `stance` in interiors/aim.ts); **powers on keys 1-4** (`castPower`, `RtPower`; `client/src/lib/sw5e/realtimePowers.ts` reads them from each power's text with `powerEffect`/`powerMotion`/`castingStats`: damage at the dice's average, healing, stun/charm (holds a foe), fear (a timid foe runs, a brave one is shaken), blind, push and pull with the blow's damage if a wall stops it; of the 465 powers 130 damage, 26 stun, 9 heal, 4 fear, 3 blind, 4 push offered, the rest are buffs or left to the table; they cost their points, which are saved with the character, and have a 0.9 s pause; at-will powers are free); **sounds** (interiors/fx.ts, synthesized, "sw5e.mute"=1 mutes); **impact sparks** where a bolt strikes a wall or a body; **the fall** of a foe is eased over a third of a second; **foes' sabers** are drawn and swung when they strike; **the party's own figure leans into a saber swing** in the over-the-shoulder view.
- **Tests**: `tests/combat/shooter.test.ts` 27 (new: round a wall through a door, `route`, `unstick`, the crowd limit, crouching, powers x3), `tests/combat/realtimepowers.test.ts` 2 (against the real powers data), `npm test` 467 pass + 3 skipped (the test-database ones). Browser (dev server on the TEST database): `e2e/shooter.mjs` (passes after this work), `e2e/shooter-extras.mjs` (Burst, Heal, Push, crouch, a foe in a wall freed), `e2e/shooter-defeat.mjs` (CROWD=1: wakes at "suspicious", the door works), `e2e/followers.mjs`. NOTE for test scripts: after Vite has hot-updated a module, `import("/src/...")` from a script gets a SECOND copy of it (a fresh store): restart the dev server before a run.
- **Still not done**: class features and maneuvers in real time; companions fight with their stat block's attack (no droid or beast specials) and do not cast; enemy Force users do not cast; no prone or sneaking, and cover is only furniture a bolt cannot pass (and crouching); a powers' areas beyond "within N feet of the target" are not drawn; **road ambushes and the pit arena are still turn-based** (not scenes on the walkable map); **no co-op fight** (a fight is one member's: two members in a room are in two fights); frightened non-brave foes leave the fight for good (they do not come back).

## Shooter: no tabletop outside the fight (2026-11-02; the user: "ensure we do not have any bleed from the tabletop game into the FPS game (I have been getting a prompt to roll when landing from a jetpack, this is WRONG)")
- **The cause of the jetpack prompt**: the map's clock runs a minute each real second, and a jetpack lasts "1 minute per power cell" (its text), so a cell was one SECOND of flight; the pack went out in the air, the party fell, and `fallOutside` asked for the tabletop's "burst of the jetpack before the ground" (Dexterity (Acrobatics)) in the check dialog and threw the d20 on the dice table. The same dialog and dice table came up for every other check (about 30 kinds: climbs, locks, persuasion, tools, downtime ...).
- **Fixes (shooter mode only; the tabletop is as it was)**. (1) `askCheck` asks nothing and `performCheck` makes a **passive check** (`passiveD20` in shared/logic/shooter.ts: 10 + the bonus, +/-5 for advantage/disadvantage); the toast says "passive". (2) `useDice.passive` (set by GameBoard while Play is open in the shooter): `rollOnTable` throws nothing and answers each die's middle face (`passiveFace`: d20 10, d6 3), so the direct throws (forcing a door, haggling, the night's cold, performing, chains, gentling a beast) show no dice either. (3) The character's own silent rolls (Stealth, substance saves, finding a crystal, downtime checks) are passive too (`ownD20`, `checkFor`); the world's own rolls (a surgeon's, a droid's save, loot and outcome tables, gambling games) stay random. Downtime results are told without their d100 (`toldRoll`) and its button reads "See what comes of it". (4) **A fall**: no burst check (the player lights the pack with Space in the air, new, or fires a burst with Left Shift); what is not avoided is the dice's average (`passiveDamage`). (5) **Flight gear burns in real time** (`burnRate` 60 for flight gear: a minute of charge is a minute of flight) and **fits a carried power cell by itself** when lit empty (`switchOn`), as the guns reload from the pack. (6) **Sea beasts and road ambushes** are fought in real time where the party stands on the walkable map (`fightAroundParty`; the riders wait for the party to stand at the trip's end: `roadPending`), not on the turn-based grid.
- **Checked**: `tests/combat/nodice.test.ts` (4); `npm test` 555 pass + 3 skipped; tsc 0, build clean. Browser (dev server on the TEST database, campaign 625 in the shooter mode): lit empty, the pack fitted a cell ("Jetpack on with a fresh power cell") and flew for as long as Space was held; switched off at 16 ft, the party fell with no dialog and no dice table and took 3 (the d6's average). Not checked in a browser: Space in mid-fall, a sea beast or road ambush in real time.
- **No turns either (2026-11-02, the user: "There should be ZERO turn based mechanics in the shooter mode. Also fix the RPG mode jetpack implementation so it works with the game clock")**:
  - **The pit arena**: `openPit` walks the party out onto the arena floor of the building (`saveCampaign({ currentLocation })`, back to where it signed up on leaving: `pitFrom`); `launchBout` queues the bout (`pitPending`) and `startPitBout` fights it in real time round the party (`fightAroundParty(enemies, false)`: the character alone, as the tabletop's pit) once the party stands there. `PitArena realtime` is then only a banner over the map (no FightScene canvas, no PlayCombat). The end is the pit's: `pitBout` makes the fight-end effect settle ammunition and grenades but skip experience, "left for dead" and the move away, and the hit points writer skip the end (the pit carries a beaten party out at 1). Grenades used come out of `pitBase` too, so the next bout's banking does not give them back.
  - **The opponent on the pit's card is the one fought** (both modes; a bug found here): `opponentFor` draws at random and was called at every render, so the card named one creature and the bout was against another; now drawn once a bout (`opponentOf`).
  - **Engage** (`engage`): the dogfight (`MinigamePage`, kind "dogfight", scenario skirmish) against the hostile ships in orbit themselves (`StartOptions.foes`, each vessel's `ref` is the world's ship id; a capital ship among them gets the carrier fight's time limit), the party's ship starting at its own hull (`ship.hull`). Its end goes through the same `settleShipFight` as the book's fight: a ship not left in the sky is a wreck (salvage, parts, experience), a cleared sky is a win, shot down is a lost ship, broken off or out of time is "fled" (a unit of fuel). The payout line of the downtime dogfight is not shown for it.
  - **The practice fight** (campaign without a world) is not offered in the shooter.
  - **The jetpack and the clock (both modes)**: `burnRate` is gone. Flight gear switched on (`flyingGear` -> `flightOn`) makes the clock tick a minute every 60 real seconds (`slow` in the clock's tick), so the book's "1 minute per power cell" is a real minute and the clock says the same; the clock also runs while it burns in a real-time fight (it stops in a fight otherwise, and the pack never ran down there).
  - **Checked**: tests/ships/dogfight.test.ts (the Engage skirmish: those ships only, by ref, the hull as it was, a capital's time); `npm test` 556 pass + 3 skipped; tsc 0; vite build clean. Browser (test DB, campaign 625, shooter, Rakessos Arena on day 6): the pit took the party onto the floor, the bout ran in the shooter (no turn panel), a loss carried it out at 1 hit point, a win paid 200 cr and an item at full hit points with no "Victory" toast, Leave put it back in the pens; the card's opponent was the one fought. The pack on: the clock moved 0 minutes in 10 s; off: 11 in 11 s. NOT checked in a browser: Engage's dogfight (needs a ship and hostile ships in orbit), the tabletop's pack (the same code path).

## Items without logic: the audit (2026-11-02; the user: "There are also still game items that currently have no logic to support their intended function. Please conduct a deep audit and report")
- **Method**: every item of equipment.json that is not a weapon, armour or ammunition (214) was run through the game's own classifiers (`gearUse` / itemFacts `use`, `accessoryOf`, `suitOf`, `airOf`, `foodOf`, `containerOf`, `lightOf`, `generatorOf`, `grenadeOf`, tools.json), then each use kind was followed to its panel (`gearPanel`, `usePanel`) and its fight action (`combatItems`); the 1,918 enhanced items were counted by whether `parseItemEffects` reads anything from their text.
- **Plain gear, tabletop**: all 214 have a use and nearly all do what their text says. Gaps: the **Tracker utility vest**'s "light items carried without counting toward encumbrance" is shown but `loadOf` ignores it; the **Rocketpack**'s built-in rocket launcher is text only (no attack in either fight); the **Focus generator** (plain) shows its text and does nothing (a focus is not required or used to cast); `cook` and `spanner` are use kinds nothing returns (the mess kit and hydrospanner are tools instead); prostheses only act through the surgeon (right, but their card says nothing).
- **The shooter's real-time fight** has only guns, blades, grenades (by name: "Grenade, ...") and powers. Not usable there: medpacs and stims (no healing in a fight), traumakits and emergency batteries, antidotes, substances, poison on a blade, **mines**, the remote detonator, **charges and the thermal detonator** (not named "grenade", so `grenadeOf` skips them), the portable teleporter, the fire extinguisher, the calibrators/collimators' bonus and shock gloves (not traced into the shooter's blade; to check), the rocketpack's launcher, a tripod or bipod.
- **Enhanced items** (as audited; see "Gear in the shooter" below for what was done): 776 of 1,918 read no effect from their text (no bonus, use, timed effect or property): item modifications 575 of 1,025 (clothing patterns "proficiency in X increases by one step" 194, armour 77, vibroweapon 75, blaster 64, lightweapon 53, wristpad 43, focus generator 37, augment 32), droid customizations 43 of 48, foci 18 of 18 ("+N to force/tech attack rolls and save DCs through this focus"), adventuring gear 64 of 156 (charges recharged on a long rest, worn-slot items), weapons 36 of 58 and armour/shields 18 of 30 (mostly chassis, whose effect is their mod slots), cybernetic enhancements 13 of 46, enhanced ship armour, shields and weapons 9 of 9 (their bonus is read elsewhere, by the yard: to check). Consumables: all 528 read. Most of these wordings are regular (a step of proficiency, a weapon's strength/dexterity number reduced by a step, "+N to the save DC of powers that require X", "+N to force attack rolls"): they can be parsed in `parseItemEffects` as CLAUDE.md asks, not hard-coded by name.

## Gear in the shooter, and foes that use theirs (2026-11-02; the user: "ensure EVERY item has an in-game function relevant to the item's intended use case ... if we need to generate NPC behaviours to make item functions relevant, then do so (utilise/extend the nemesis as required). We should deepen the games functionality as a shooter as much as possible to reflect the depth of the RPG game")
All HOUSE RULES (the books have no real time); the numbers are `SHOOT` in shared/logic/shooter.ts.
- **The item belt** (`FightGear`, `GearAct`, `applyGear`; built by GameBoard `fightGear` from each item's own rules, `gearUse` / itemFacts, as `combatItems` reads them for the tabletop). Z uses the item shown, T shows the next (phone: the item button and ›); G still throws the first grenade. A use takes the hand 0.8 s (no shot meanwhile). Medpac: the hurt companion in front, else the party, the average of the Hit Die roll + Constitution (an enhanced one its dice); repair kit: a droid companion; traumakit / emergency battery: a fallen companion (or droid) in front back up at 1; antitoxkit: clears poison, or guards against it for the fight; a substance: its temporary hit points again every six seconds and a steadier aim for a minute (its high and low and the addiction save run after the fight, `takeSubstance`); stimpacs, adrenals, barriers, catalyzers (timed enhanced items): temporary hit points, AC, damage at once; a collimator or calibrator: its damage on the matching blade; poison: the next blow that lands (and poisons); grenades and the thermal detonator (by `gearUse`, so no longer only those named "Grenade"); a mine: set at the feet, arms in 1.5 s, goes off for the first of the other side within its trigger; a charge: set facing the look, the book's six-second timer, a cone; the remote detonator: pairs the next two set (they then go off only to it, within its 30 ft.) and is not used up; the portable teleporter: to free ground in sight along the aim within its reach (LocalMap `ShooterCtx.place`); the fire extinguisher: a 15 ft. cone that puts out burning ground and bodies and blinds the foes in it once each; binders / a restraining bolt: a foe (Small or Medium / a droid) in reach that is beaten down (a quarter of its hit points, stunned, blinded or running) is taken alive (out of the fight, settled as beaten: at the party's mercy); the comm jammer: on, no foe's call for help gets out; a rocketpack: fires the rockets carried (they burst). Kits lose one use per use (`charges`), the rest one item; mines and charges left on the ground are picked up again.
- **The rules in the fight** (both sides): resistances, vulnerabilities and immunities by damage type (the stat block's, a sealed suit's), condition immunities, armour's absorb, temporary hit points first, a stat block's regeneration every six seconds; bursts bring their conditions as seconds (`blastOf`: stunned/shocked/paralysed a stagger, prone a short one, frightened the unbrave run, poisoned a worse aim (x1.5) and a slower swing, blinded a white view); ground set ablaze or filled with gas for a minute hurts each second (`Field`, damage added up and dealt in whole points: `seep`); burning goes on 1.5 s after the fire; a stat block's attack that poisons (or stuns...) does so on a hit (`rider`); a round that explodes (a rocket's, a missile's ammunition rider) bursts where it stops; the grenade launcher fires the grenades carried, bursting on impact; a bipod or tripod: crouched and still, half the spread and kick; shock gloves' and a calibrator's extra dice on the blade.
- **Foes use gear** (`FoeKit`, GameBoard `foeKit`): the area actions of their stat block (a sphere with a range is lobbed to land at the party's feet, one without is round the creature, a cone or line is sprayed at once, fire sets the party burning; as many times as its uses or recharge allow); soldierly people carry a fragmentation grenade (30%) and a medpac (35%, a leader always), drawn from who they are; a hurt one kneels 1.2 s over its medpac at 40% of its hit points; a poisoned or burning one with an antitoxkit or extinguisher uses it. A foe's grenade near the party flashes "GRENADE!".
- **Calls for backup** (`callForHelp`, `stepBackup`): a leader or one of the watch, soldiers, officers or guards calls once the fight has gone 10 s or half its side is down; one or two more of the weakest of them come in 12 s later from out of sight 12 to 20 squares off (they give no loot or experience: they are not on the map's lists). The party's comm jammer, switched on first, stops the call.
- **Nemeses learn** (`learnKit` in shared/logic/nemesis.ts, `NemesisRecord.kit`, a "learns" event): a survivor burned brings a fire extinguisher, poisoned or burned with acid an antitoxkit, blown up the same kind of explosive the party used on them (a mine or charge becomes its grenade), beaten down or made to run a medpac (two at most); six things at most. A nemesis of rank 2 or more carries a medpac more. Fed from the shooter's belt and the tabletop log (`partyUsed`).
- **Checked**: tests/combat/shootergear.test.ts (13: bursts read, heal/revive/repair, antidote and poisoned aim, mine/charge/detonator, binders, teleporter, fire and extinguisher, resist/vulnerable/immune/temp/absorb, foe lob/spray/medpac, backup and jam, bite rider and blade poison, nemesis learning); `npm test` 568 + 3 skipped; tsc 0. Browser (test DB, campaign 625, a pit bout): the belt showed and cycled; frag and incendiary grenades and the thermal detonator thrown, the mine set and paired, the detonator set it off, the extinguisher, jammer and antitoxkit used, the charge set; after the bout the pack had lost the grenades, the detonator-blown mine, two extinguisher uses and one antitoxkit use, kept the jammer and detonator and got the charge left on the ground back. NOT checked in a browser: foes throwing, spraying, healing or calling (no soldierly fight was staged; the unit tests cover them), the teleporter, binders on a living foe, a phone. Found on the way: a level-3 character standing still loses a pit bout to an adolescent dewback in about 5 s (melee foes hit every 1.2 s with no roll: the existing balance, not changed).

## Enhanced items' wordings read, and the tabletop's gaps (2026-11-02, the same request)
- **Read from the text** (`parseItemEffects`, new `ItemEffects` fields; `itemFactsOf`): proficiency raised in a named skill, saving throw or tool, by steps or to a tier ("increases by one step", "You gain grand mastery in Stealth"; Wretched Hives' six tiers: `PROFICIENCY_TIERS`, `tierBonus` trained half, proficient 1x, expertise and up 2x, `tierAdvantage` mastery and up; `raisedTier`); force and tech powers' melee and ranged attack bonuses and penalties and save DC bonuses by the ability saved with (focus generator and wristpad modifications, a focus's "+N to the force attack rolls for force powers you cast through this"); a Sith robe's "+N to damage rolls with weapons and force powers"; resistances ("resistance to ion damage", "resistant to all unenhanced damage"); a weapon modification's "loses the X property" and "dexterity number is reduced by one step"; an augment's plain "score increases by 1" with its "One ability score ... decreases by 1"; a clothing pattern's "gains the insulated 1 armor property". Item cards say all of these (`describeEffects`). Unread enhanced items: 714 -> 325; what is left is class features' charges (Cape of the Orator, Gauntlets of the Warrior ...), droid protocols and parts, powers cast from an item, rings of force storing, "these tools" (the GM's choice), weapons whose effect is their plain weapon (nets, bolas, grenade launchers), and one-off riders (on a critical hit, once per turn ...).
- **Applied** (`withItemEffects`: `itemProficiency`, `itemPowers`, `itemForceDamage`, `itemResist`): the check dialog's rows and downtime checks (`checkFor`) use the tier (mastery and up roll with advantage; passive +5 in the shooter); saving throws in a fight use a raised save tier; each power's attack and DC in the fight get the focus's bonus (melee if its reach is 5 ft. or less), force powers the robe's damage; resistances join a sealed suit's. Weapon mods take away and lower properties in `carriedWeapons` (`reshaped`). **Armour, shield and clothing modifications' properties now count in `wornTraits`** (they were never passed: an armour mod's "absorbing", a pattern's "insulated"), and their lost properties (Balanced Shielding: no longer imbalanced) are removed. House simplification: high and grand mastery's rerolls are not played (advantage only).
- **The tabletop's gaps**: the Rocketpack's launcher fires the rockets carried in a fight (Items: one option a rocket kind, its range 100/400, the rocket's burst or burning ground as an explosive, used up); the plain Focus generator's card says what it is for (the chassis of focus modifications; what is fitted and whether it is attuned); the Tracker utility vest WAS applied (the audit was wrong) but by name and carried: now read from its text and only when worn ("the wearer").
- **Checked**: tests/items/itemwordings.test.ts (4: tiers, powers, robe/resist/props/augment, the unread count at most 330), tests/combat/shootergear.test.ts (the incendiary rocket's burst and fire). NOT checked in a browser: a clothing pattern on a check, a focus mod on a power, the rocketpack option.

## Monsters, foe wind-ups, melee pace (2026-11-02, e9c1f59; the user: "Monsters should not use grenades, weapons, or items. Foes throwing, spraying, healing or calling for backup need relevant animations ... we also need to balance melee damage")
All HOUSE RULES. `monstrous` (beast, plant, aberration, undead, elemental, monstrosity, ooze, swarms; wildlife, horror roles): natural attacks only (spat or flung globs and spines, named for the stat block's attack, coloured by damage type, bursting on impact), no grenades, medpacs, calls or learned kit; a monster nemesis comes back resistant to the party's worst damage type (two at most, if it took a quarter of its hit points: `NemesisRecord.adapted`). A foe's throw (0.5 s), spray or breath (0.55 s), call (1.4 s) and medpac (1.2 s) wind up first (`SHOOT.act`, limbs.ts poses, a monster rears and lunges: `creatureLunge`); a stagger of 0.4 s or more breaks it off. Melee: a stat block's attacks a round come as one blow every 4 s shared between them (never faster than 1.2 s), rolled as a chance against the AC (`hitChance`), after a 0.5 s wind-up the party can step out of.

## The rest of the items and the powers, in the shooter (2026-11-03; the user: the 325 unread items "+ any force/tech powers that we do not yet have logic for ... if these make reference to game mechanics that we do not yet have implemented, extend the game logic ... Focus on the shooter implementation for now")
All HOUSE RULES where the books' turns, rounds and dice do not exist in real time; numbers in `SHOOT`.
- **Items read** (`fightFactsOf` -> `ItemEffects.fight` / `FightFacts`; `withItemEffects.itemFight` by source, "Weapon: Mod" for a fitted modification): unread 325 -> 68 (left: class-feature charge items, class augments, tools, vocoders, droid interface protocols, Siege Weapon, Biometric Safety: the RPG's, later). The GM's choices the text leaves open are taken by the item's name (`hashOf`): an Ostrine crystal's damage type, Adapted Armor's resistance (`chosenResistance`), a ring of storing's powers (GameBoard `storedPowers`, refilled at a long rest).
- **On the weapon** (`Rider`, `partyStrike`): extra dice (Damind, Quantum, Serrated, Ostrine), an opener (Flashy, Sparkles: the first hit on each foe), types whose resistance is ignored (Ion Amplifying Core, Monomolecular), a foe's saber turns its bolts half as often (Maser Core), a slow (Corellian Crippler), a quickening (Celerity, Aratech), once a rest a chain to the next foe (Rylith, Ventilation), a cone (Electrifying), a shove (Staggering); the third blow of a saber chain is the shooter's critical (Burning Cell: the foe left open); an edge's doubled die on advantage (Nagai: 1 in 20 against a foe unaware, staggered or blind). Range (Precision Chamber), a quick reload (Expanded Magazine, Power Supply Port: x0.4), a scope (no long-range penalty), less kick (Recoil Dampener: 8% a step), an enhanced launcher's dice (a grenade's die taken as half its damage).
