A bayonet or shotgun axe when there is no blade, a hilt blaster when there is no gun, an arm's unarmed strikes (Shock Arm, Iridonian), reach (Magnetic Forearm), +1 melee (High-Grade Arm).
- **Defences**: evasion (Fortitude/Reflex/Will enhancers and protocols: half from bursts, cones, breath), Enhanced Endurance (once a long rest to 1 hit point), no criticals (Matrix Armor), Ballistic Polymer (kinetic and energy cut by half the proficiency bonus while temporary hit points last), Bioenhancement Matrix (its hit points a level as temporary hit points at the start of a fight: the max hit points are not changed), a hood's resistance for the first 6 s (Cowl), a respirator's poison resistance. **Foes now crit**: a natural 20 (the lowest twentieth of the roll; 5% of bolts on the party) lands x1.7.
- **The belt** (GearAct): nets and bolas (`snare`: a Large or smaller foe held until it frees itself: each 6 s its bonus against the DC, the average of that, at most 18 s; picked up after), harpoons and wrist hooks (fired: a smaller foe reeled in, the party to a bigger one or to a wall; also the map's hook), Overload Shield (foes within 5 ft. shoved and knocked down, 2 a short rest), Shield Amplifier (+2 AC round the party for 6 s), the Baragwin stealth field (on/off), Heavy Suit and mag-lock boots (anchored: no push, half pace), a hidden compartment blaster (one shot a short rest), Venomous Oscillator (its poison, once a long rest), a droid's medic and mechanic parts (restore, stabilize, cure: each once a rest), a jammer in an implant, a detachable eye (sees foes round it for a minute), a flashlight (a spotlight; foes see the party from 40% farther), Boots of Bounding (a 10 ft. leap a charge: half the fighter level), the powers items cast (Viir Agathys: voltaic shielding, 3 charges; Active Camouflage: infiltrate at will; Analysis Protocol, Sentry, Infiltration Package: once a long rest; Slipstream Kickers). What is spent for a rest is counted in `featureUses` ("item:" long, "item~" short; a short rest clears "item~"), not taken from the pack.
- **Through a focus or wristpad** (`Unit.channel`): damage per point spent (Bansche, Forged Ferricite x0.5 to x2.5), temporary hit points per point on a tech heal (Sienar), force range +10 ft. (Duplexed), radius +5 ft. (Phobium), push +5 ft. (T-Cycle), cold tech damage slows (MerenData), a d12 kept after a tech power takes hold (Czerka Elite), +1 to a tech shield's AC (Bansche Purifier), Byrothsis cells (weapon dice on a Force-thrown saber), Telgorn (a force imbue made lightning +1d8 for a point). Subroutines spend points on their own, a proficiency bonus of times a long rest: distant (double reach when nothing is in reach), twinned (a second target), quickened (no pause after), improved (x1.15), heightened (conditions x1.5), extended (a buff twice as long); careful, seeking, lingering, pinpoint and refocused have nothing to do here.
- **Movement and senses**: integrated jetboosters, repulsor lifts and a helisaber fly as a jetpack does (the map's flight); leg reinforcements double the long jump; emergency landing braces halve falls. Truesight (Microphasic), the zoom (Y or the Zoom button: x4 Hawkeye, x3 macrobinoculars, x2 a scope; a reticle with the range in feet), hearing (an ear augmentation: shots out of sight within 14 squares flash an arc the way they came), a sense power (foes within its reach marked through the walls). Darkvision items were already read ("gain darkvision" now too).
- **Powers** (`realtimePowers` `readMore`, from the texts, none by name; 332 of 465 play, the 133 left are exploration and utility: the RPG's): 18 known powers, nine a page on keys 1-9, 0 turns the page. New kinds (`castPower`): imbue (a weapon cantrip's next hit: its dice, growing by level), buff (temporary hit points, AC, the Dodge action or a blur as AC +4, resistances, immunity, haste, damage back at melee attackers, invisibility that an attack ends, damage on every weapon hit, duplicates at the book's d20 chances, a d4 on attacks as 20% off the spread, a reaction readied for the next hit, holding at 1 hit point, fearless, healing a second, a saber spun round the body), blink (a teleport or leap to the aim), sense, mark (a hex's dice on each hit; a shatterpoint: +20% and no saber block), slow (a slowed level: a third of the pace; AC lowered: +5% damage a point), restrain, mind (a foe unaware stays so; an aware one charmed, at most 12 s; a blow wakes it), zone (ground that hurts, slows, poisons or hides: those in smoke or darkness aim as the blind, foes see the party in it at 35%), decoy (a hologram foes go for first, 20 s), kill and stun below a number of hit points, revive, cure, disarm (a foe's gun pulled away: it fights with its fists), turn (droids fight for the party a while), throw (a saber thrown and back), dash (across the floor and a blow). Damage split over projectiles (Barrage, Homing Rockets, Project). A duration of an hour or more lasts the fight.
- **The party unseen** (a stealth field, invisibility): foes do not see it beyond 1.8 squares; a shot, a blow, a hostile power or a throw gives it away for a second (and ends invisibility that a hostile act ends); the foes go to where it was last seen.
- **Seen**: a ring at a foe's feet by its state (held, netted, slowed, charmed, unaware, marked, turned, disarmed), a net round one held, the word over it; holograms; domes of smoke and darkness; a harpoon's cable; the party's shield bubble and duplicates (third person); its light; the HUD's buffs with seconds left, "Unseen", "Anchored", foes sensed. The party's pace in a fight follows its haste or slow (`stance.pace`).
- **Checked**: tests/combat/shooteritems.test.ts (10), tests/combat 75 pass; tsc 0. NOT checked in a browser.

## Contraband in the shooter (2026-11-03; the user: "purchase/sale of slaves and other illicit items / mechanics to hide contraband on ships / stop and search / fly casual / smuggler outfit / hidden pockets / weak minded force powers")
All HOUSE RULES (the books give the law no procedure). **Stop and search** (`STOP`, `stopBegin`, `stopTick`; GameBoard `stopPanel`): in the shooter mode a customs post, a checkpoint or landing at a policed port is not a roll: an officer flags the party down and scans it for 8 s. Its suspicion starts at 20 + 12 a piece of contraband carried openly + 8 a level of heat + 10 for heavy armour + 5 a law level + 3 a point the officer's Insight (the law's search bonus) beats the party's Deception, - 15 for a smuggler's outfit (clandestine clothes or stitching), - 10 for a droid that passes as living (Humanoid Replicant). Standing still lowers it 5 a second (flying casual), walking raises it 6, running 30. Under 25 when the scan ends: waved through, nothing seized. Otherwise `lawSearch` with the searcher's passive Investigation, 10 + the law's bonus, -5 under 40, +5 at 60 or more. Before it ends: a mind power that works on a mind not yet hostile (Mind Trick, Affect Mind; Coerce Mind) lets the party go if its save DC beats the officer's passive Wisdom save (10 + the law's bonus: the weak-minded), else the search is thorough; a disguise (a power that changes how the party looks, or a disguise kit: -25 once); a bribe (25 cr x (1 + law) x (1 + heat): the passive Persuasion against 10 + twice the law, +5 when wary; a failed one is pocketed and +30); run (trespass: the search is on) or fight (assault). The tabletop keeps its roll. **Hiding**: clandestine stitching's pockets in clothes worn (`hidingPlaces` third argument), a collapsible suit is not seized for what it is, a hidden compartment blaster is never in the pack. **Ships** (`play.hold`): aboard, items can be stowed in the hold, or hidden in a Hidden Storage suite's compartments; landing where it is policed the berthed ships' holds are searched with the party: the open hold's contraband seized, the hidden storage's only if the searcher's Investigation - 5 (the suite's disadvantage) reaches DC 20. **The slave trade** (`slavePrice` 300 + 200 a CR, a quarter at least; `slaveMarket`): a black-market dealer where a crime syndicate rules deals in the captives of the settlement's pens and cells: their freedom bought (they go free, regard +40), bought as a servant (a hireling companion for no wage; regard -40), or a companion sold into the pens for half; buying or selling a person is a heinous crime where any law holds.

## Co-op (2026-10-29; the user: "co-op mode so I can play with my friends on my local wi-fi (maximum 4). Players walk the local settlement individually (districts and buildings alone or as a party) but the party moves between settlements/planets/systems as a group: one opens a 'room', the others press Join, then they choose ship(s) and roles, or speeder(s) and who pilots and who guns")
- **Shape**: one WebSocket (`/ws/coop`, same port as the game, so a phone on the wi-fi or Tailscale Serve needs nothing more; `ws` was already a dependency). The server (`server/coop.ts`) owns a SESSION per campaign: members (up to `MAX_PLAYERS` 4, one character each, joined by a four-letter code), where each member stands, the LIVE copy of the campaign, and the travel room. It is a relay and referee, not a second game engine: Play stays a client that plays its own character. (The earlier plan, "server-authoritative", is therefore only true of the session, the places and the room; the world is still edited by the clients, as patches.)
- **The campaign is shared as patches** (`shared/logic/coop.ts`: `diffJson`, `applyOps`, `changesBetween`, `applyChanges`; `tests/world/coop.test.ts`, 13). `saveCampaign` and `saveClock` in GameBoard call `saveShared` first (`client/src/lib/coop.ts`): in a co-op game the change is diffed against the last version everyone agreed on (`base`), sent as a few path operations, applied by the server to its live copy (written to the database 400 ms later, only the changed fields), and applied to every other client's query cache, so Play redraws as for a change of its own. Two players' changes to different things both land; the same array item or field is last-writer-wins. An op whose parent was removed is skipped. The clock is shared and never runs backward (`timeElapsed.total` takes the later). While a session is open `GET /api/campaigns/:id` answers with the live copy and a REST save (the campaign page) is merged into it (`mergeLive`).
- **Each member has a place of their own**: `currentLocation` is not shared (`PERSONAL`); the session keeps each member's `areaId`. GameBoard's `hereId` comes from `coopPlace`, the character from `coopCharacter` (a guest plays their own character on the host's campaign), and every `saveCampaign({ currentLocation })` becomes a `move`. The host's place is also written to the campaign (`persistHost`), so a single-player game resumes where the host was. A new member starts where the host stands. Members see each other as figures on the 3D map (`CoopPeers` in LocalMap, position shared by `sharePosition` from the map's `usePartySpot`, 6 times a second at most); a member in another room or building is not drawn. "Go to them" in the Co-op dialog walks to a member in the same settlement (house rule: five minutes).
- **Leaving the settlement is the party's** (`isGroupExit`: a way that needs a ship, or a road of kilometres; a street, door or stair is each member's own). `travel()` (ground, passage, ships by the galaxy map) and `flyShip()` (the helm: take-off, landing, jumps) in GameBoard open a ROOM when another member is connected, instead of going: `openRoom` -> `proposeTravel`. The room (`TravelRoom`, pure rules in shared/logic/coop.ts, state machine in server/coop.ts, panel `TravelRoomPanel` in components/Play/CoopUI.tsx): **gather** (everyone connected gets a Join button; the proposer is joined; anyone can call it off) -> **crew** (each member picks a craft and a role; a craft is one of the party's speeders parked here, one of its ships berthed here, or a way of their own: on foot, a hired ride, the transit line, a passage on a liner; ships and speeders that cannot be used show why; changing a crew puts its members back to not ready; the issues are listed: a craft needs one pilot, no more than its seats, ship stations once each (pilot, co-pilot, navigator, engineer; up to three gunners); everyone ready and no issues -> go) -> **underway** (each unit is a craft with crew led by its pilot, or a member alone). Each pilot's own Play runs the trip with the existing code (`travel(exit, ride)` with `forceShip` for the ship chosen, or `flyShip`); a unit arrives when its leader arrives (the leader's `move` to the destination, or `arrived` for a ship), and the rest of its crew arrive with them; the room closes when every unit has. Anyone can "Call it off" while it is under way (those who have arrived stay there). A passenger's screen is covered by "On the way to ..." until their craft arrives; a pilot sees a small strip.
- **Ships are flown from the helm with the crew aboard**: a ship's deck is a place (`deckIdOf`); at the launch the server moves the crew onto the deck if it stands (else the pilot's client makes it with `boardNow` and the crew follows when that `move` arrives), the ship flies (the deck moves with it, so the crew stays on the deck: `room.aboard`), and `arrived` ends it. A ship sets out only with the ships berthed at the same berth as the one the proposer stands aboard.
- **House rules (labelled on screen)**: seats of a ship by size (`shipSeats`: Tiny 1, Small 2, bigger the whole party); a speeder's seats are the party's, its companions ride along; one pilot per craft; a member alone pays their own fare; walking to a member costs five minutes.
- **What is NOT shared yet (honest list; the gunner and passenger of a SPEEDER are real since 2026-10-30, see the next section)**: the trip itself is one pilot's minigame on their screen; a gunner or passenger is only ridden along (their role is recorded and shown, but they do not aim a gun or fly in the road minigame, and a ship's co-pilot, navigator and engineer do not yet add actions to a starship fight; `crewOf` still reads the old seat list). Fights are each member's own (a fight against what stands in a room is that player's, the world change is shared as patches; two members fighting in one room are not in one fight; the real-time shooter is the same). Two members in one room at once see each other as figures but cannot trade or talk through the game. No names above the heads beyond the figure's label; no text chat. A member who loses the connection keeps their seat for ten minutes of a quiet session and rejoins as the same character with the code (kept in the tab's sessionStorage, so a reload rejoins by itself). A journey whose pilot never arrives (the trip ended in a fight at the roadside, or travel stopped on a toast) has to be called off by hand.
- **Network**: the server listens on this PC only unless HOST is set. `deploy/windows-start.ps1 -Mode prod|test -Lan` sets `HOST=0.0.0.0`, opens the port in Windows Firewall on PRIVATE networks (once; needs an administrator window, otherwise it says what to add) and prints the address friends open (`http://<this PC's wi-fi address>:<port>/coop`). The game has no login: anyone on that network can open it. The Co-op dialog tells the host when the server is loopback-only. Over Tailscale the friends need no setting (the tailnet address of the Serve proxy works; a Tailscale Serve HTTPS address uses `wss`).
- **Pages and files**: `server/coop.ts` (sessions, `/api/coop/info|open|code/:code`), `shared/logic/coop.ts`, `client/src/lib/coop.ts` (the store `useCoop` and the socket), `client/src/components/Play/CoopUI.tsx` (`CoopButton`, `CoopDialog`, `CoopLink`, `TravelRoomPanel`), `client/src/pages/CoopJoin.tsx` (route `/coop`, the Home page links to it), GameBoard (`coopHere`, `craftsFor`, `openRoom`, the launch effect, `goToMember`), LocalMap (`CoopPeers`). Tests: `tests/world/coop.test.ts` (13, pure) and `tests/server/coop.test.ts` (a real WebSocket against a TEST database only: `DATABASE_URL=.../sw5e_test node --import tsx --test --test-force-exit tests/server/coop.test.ts`; skipped otherwise). Browser: `e2e/coop.mjs` (two contexts: host, join by code, room, speeder with pilot and gunner, the trip, arrival) against a server on the test database.

## Co-op, the first real play (2026-10-30; the user played it on two devices over Tailscale: "the NPC follows BOTH players, only visible on the individual's screen", "a beast follower does not show on the other player's screen", "the speeder's hit box is not active for the 2nd player", "the gunner got a menu for the whole journey, no gunner position was applied, and when they chose cancel they stayed behind while the pilot arrived", "a roadblock with no NPCs guarding it: there should be NPCs that fire at the speeder as it crashes through, and sometimes give chase on speeders from the roadside")
- **The wi-fi address**: the Co-op button does not turn the wi-fi on (nothing in the page can: it is the server's listening address). The server must be started with `-Lan` (HOST=0.0.0.0; `deploy/windows-start.ps1 -Mode test -Lan`), plus a firewall rule (now "local subnet only, any profile": the home wi-fi is often labelled Public). The dialog now says so when the server is loopback-only and lists each address in its own labelled box (the two used to run together into one broken link when copied). A phone on the same wi-fi still got ERR_TOO_MANY_RETRIES on `http://192.168.0.156:5002` while the Tailscale address worked (Tailscale showed a DIRECT path over the same wi-fi): the cause is NOT found (server listens on 0.0.0.0, rule present, no other firewall product, no subnet routes); to try: the phone's Tailscale off, then the same URL; the router's client isolation. Tailscale works and is a direct LAN path, so it is the way for now.
- **Lag and the jump**: positions go out from a timer of the link (12 a second, the last place never dropped), and carry a height (`partyHeight` in interiors/air.ts: a jump, a flight or a reel is seen by the others; `h` on the `pos` message and `Peer`).
- **Followers**: a player's companions and beasts are the CHARACTER's (`companionData`), so each screen only had its own. The member now tells the others who follows them (`pets` message, `CoopMember.pets`, `cleanPets`; GameBoard `sharePets`), and `PeerPets` in LocalMap draws them walking behind that member (e2e/coop-followers.mjs). The stranger of an ENCOUNTER (`world.encounter`, shared) walks behind ONE member of those in the place (the host if there, else the first by id: `leader` in LocalMap; every screen picks the same), and only that member's screen takes the "met" step.
- **The speeder on the street** blocks the squares it covers (three along its length: `rigSquares`), not the one it is set on. (The "2nd player only" part was not reproduced: both clients build the same street from the shared world; the one-square block was the visible gap.)
- **The gunner is a real seat on a speeder**: `components/Map/FlightScene.tsx` `RoadRide` (the crew's page) and `lib/roadBus.ts`. The pilot's `MinigamePage` runs the leg and streams the race (15 a second, with the events between) through the server (`road` message, relayed to the crew of the pilot's unit only); the gunner's page runs the same course from the leg's spec and draws the streamed race (carried on between snapshots by each craft's speed), aims with the pad (A and D) and fires (Fire, Space), and sends `gun` (aim, trigger) to the pilot, whose party gun then fires as the gunner says (`input.aim`/`fire`; the AI crewmate is off while a human gunner is aboard). A passenger sees the same road with no gun. A second seat and a pintle gun are LENT to every speeder kind (`buildGunner`, `buildGun`, `GUNNER_AT`; the gun swings with the aim on both screens). Riders have no "call off" any more: only a pilot can call a journey under way off (server `cancel` ignores anyone else; a pilot who leaves the trip calls it off for everyone). **Not done**: ships' co-pilot/navigator/engineer/gunner in a starship fight (the dogfight and ship combat are still the pilot's), a gunner's own seat on a ship.
- **Roadblock guards** (`shared/logic/index.ts`: `RoadBlockSite`, `blockGuards`; `roadStart` takes `block`): four guards stand at a block (two by the speeders kept at the roadside before the barrier, two beyond it; drawn with `buildGuard`), and fire at a speeder that crashes through (rams it, or comes at it faster than 12 m/s within 45 m): a shot every ~1.3 s each, 30% at point blank, 1 hull a hit (house rule, the same numbers as a chase rider's). Whether a block's guards ride after the party is seeded by the course (55% of blocks, `chase`): the two speeders stand idle (`Racer.idle`: not driven, not struck, no hull bar, no rider drawn) until the party runs the block, then the guards mount, the race becomes a chase (`race.chase`, `race.block.mounted`) and they ride after the party in the same leg. The old "riders on the next leg" still happens when the block was run and its guards did not ride in the leg itself. Tests: tests/world/roadtrip.test.ts (12), e2e/roadblock.mjs, e2e/roadblock-look.mjs.
- **Checks**: `npm test` 469 pass / 3 skipped; tests/server/coop.test.ts 3/3 on the test db (gun, road, a rider's cancel ignored, pets); e2e/coop.mjs (the gunner's page, the stream reaches it, both arrive), e2e/coop-followers.mjs, e2e/followers.mjs, e2e/roadblock.mjs (seed 2: guards mount, 2 riders after the party). Not checked: two real phones (the gunner's pad), the encounter stranger across two screens (the rule is simple; no browser run), sound.

## Jointed people, weapon holds, saber forms, recoil and spread, new textures (2026-10-30; the user: "the lightsabre just waves with no character arm or body movement at all - this is WRONG! We need a variety of beautiful relevant animations, and proper character positions for prone/crouch/walk/run/jump movements, with relevant arm and body positions for carrying pistols/rifles/carbines/etc, plus recoil animations and spread depending on the weapon's fire power and type ... a range of lightsabre animations for the variety of lightsabre forms ... extend and enhance the textures/normal-maps for character faces/skin/clothing/armour/humanoid droids + prosthetics and cybernetics")
- A skeleton, not more models: `interiors/limbs.ts` LIMB has six more joints (chest at the small of the back, forearms at the elbows, shins at the knees, the head on its neck, the right hand's weapon at the wrist) and PARENT says what hangs from what; the vertex shader turns a vertex about its own joint and then about each joint above it (pivots of the parents in a `uP` uniform; `Kit.limb` records each limb's pivot and `Kit.bake` keeps them on the geometry). Beasts and droids are unchanged (their limbs have no parents set). `buildHumanoid` builds legs as thigh + shin, arms as upper arm + forearm, the held weapon on the wrist; a long gun is now built shouldered and is lowered by the arms to the low ready.
- `humanPose` (limbs.ts) poses a person from its stride and a `Motion` the scene writes (models.tsx `MotionContext`): walk and run (longer stride, knee bend, lean, elbow pump), crouch (knees bent, body lowered by the real leg geometry), prone (tipped onto the front, chest up, gun forward), jump (legs tucked), what is held (`Hold`: nothing, carried, pistol two-handed at shoulder height when ready, rifle at the low ready or shouldered with a bladed stance and the head turned to the sights, saber, blade, staff), the kick of a shot, a flinch when hit, a parry. Strikes are keyframed (overhead, sweeps, diagonals, rising cuts, thrusts, flicks, a spinning cut, a leap).
- Lightsaber styles: every form in `lightsaberForms.json` maps to a movement style (`FORM_STYLE`, how it looks, not what it does): Shii-Cho, Makashi (one-handed, side on), Soresu, Ataru (high guard, leaps and spins), Shien (blade over the shoulder), Djem So, Niman, Juyo/Vaapad (low, fast), Jar'Kai, Trakata, Sokan; each with its guard and three strikes taken in turn. The character's style is the first form named in their sheet (GameBoard passes the forms through `usePartyLook`), else one picked by who they are.
- Where it plays: the party on the map (posture from Crouch/Prone, the air from jumps: `partyMotion` in interiors/aim.ts), co-op peers (their shared posture), turn-based fights (each figure ready; the combat log says who attacks and who is hit: `useFightMotions` in LocalMap), the shooter (each unit's shots, swings, hits; the party's shots, swings, parries and aim pitch; the figure holds the drawn weapon, `wield`). The floating saber meshes of the shooter are gone except the first-person one. Sabers in a hand are drawn at a real length (gear.ts: the saber models are 0.28 long; x2.8 in a hand).
- House rule, recoil and spread (shared/logic/shooter.ts `gunKick`): each gun has a kick (hold-out 0.15, pistol 0.3, carbine 0.4, rifle 0.55, heavy/repeater 0.8, sniper 0.85, more for a heavier shot) and a steadiness (sniper 0.35, rifle 0.65, carbine 0.85, pistol 1, heavy 1.3, shotgun 1.9). A shot opens the party's cone by 0.5 + 1.25 x kick of the old step; the cone is multiplied by the steadiness. The view climbs with each shot (`recoil` in aim.ts, less crouched); the figure's arms and gun kick by the same number.
- Textures (tools/build-figure-textures.mjs): skin has fine crossing lines and freckles over the pores; faces finer lines and a blush; new sets `droid` (panels, seams, rivets, vents, worn edges: droids and prosthetic limbs), `cyber` (circuit traces and pads: a prosthesis's joints and works), `weave` (coarse homespun: robes, cloaks, rags), `mesh` (ribbed and quilted: armour undersuits, flight and sealed suits). New finishes in models.tsx (`droid`, `cyber`, `weave`, `mesh`).
- Checked: tests/interiors/limbs.test.ts (jointed build, crouch/prone/air, run vs walk, two-handed grips stay together, strikes, styles, kick and spread); the gallery `?section=moves` (rows by what is held, columns by what is done, a row of the saber styles; e2e/moves.mjs shoots it); e2e/shooter.mjs and e2e/fight-view.mjs still play. Not checked on a phone; the shooter seen over the shoulder was not screenshotted mid-swing.

## Caves, ceilings, vaults, round buildings, water worlds (2026-10-30; the user: "there are gaps between the stones that make up cave walls so we can see the surroundings - this is WRONG! The entry to caves (a standard door) looks all wrong ... it should have a proper organic looking entrance. Between the rooms of the cave ... a staircase - this is WRONG we should have a more natural sloping floor (the first room should gently slope a long way down (so the rooms ceilings cannot be seen above ground) ... ceiling and roof textures ... are too low (when using over the shoulder view, the ceiling obscures the view when the player looks down) ... Palaces should have high vaulted ceilings - double or even triple height ... a greater number of circular/oval buildings ... water planets (like Kamino) ... gantry ways surrounding buildings and catwalks between these gantries ... mainly circular buildings ... subaquatic districts (ie. the gungan city on Naboo) we need swimming mechanics, water dwelling beasts, water and subaquatic transports")
- Cave rock (`interiors/CaveRoom.tsx` `caveShellGeometry`, `CaveWalls`): one sheet over the room and a margin of two squares, level with the floor on it (rubble at its foot rounding the squares' corners), rising from the floor's edge to over the roof with a noisy lip and face (value noise, the stone texture, vertex colours dark at the foot), and in the eye views a rock roof (`CAVE_ROOF` 3.5, lower towards the walls, knobbly) that the walls rise through: nothing to see through. The ways open a throat out through the margin. In `AIM.solids` (camera and shots). The rooms round the one stood in are drawn as their floor and rock (`CaveBeyond` in LocalMap), so an opening shows the cave going on.
- Underground caves (shared/logic `levelsOf`): a cave's mouth is on the surface, its tunnels and dens one floor down, a deep chamber two; `linkLevels` puts a "Sloping passage down to ..." between floors (a stairs room, 11 to 14 squares long for a floor, so about one in four), drawn as a ramp of rock (`RampFloor`, the floor height is `climbAt` as before), never steps. From outside only the mouth shows: a hill of rock (`CaveMound`, one sheet) with a rock arch (`RockArch`, dark inside) on the way out; inside, the way out is the same arch with daylight through it and the ways between rooms are openings in the rock (no frames or leaves: `Doorway` `rock`). A cave's secret way in is no longer put in its underground rooms. Older worlds keep their planned caves until a building is planned again.
- Heights (LocalMap): a storey 3.4 (was 2.8), a room's walls in the eye views 3.0 (were 2.4). Over the shoulder the camera keeps a hand under the roof (`roof.above` in aim.ts, set by the room drawn with a ceiling; `poseOf` lowers it).
- Vaults (`vaultOf`, `Vault` in LocalMap): in the eye views a palace's audience chamber or throne room is three storeys high, its halls and its rooms of 70 squares or more two, a great house's hall and an archive's reading hall two; under a barrel vault on ribs (gilt in a palace), a dome over a round room. Only the view from inside is raised.
- Round rooms (`linkRounds`, `ROUND_BUILDINGS`): many more kinds of building (medcenter, archive, market and shops but the droid dealer, whose showroom needs its counter, residences, estates, manor house, palace, rooming house, gambling hall, command post, promenade, habitat ring, operations center, transit hub...), 85% of them get a round room (two in a building of five rooms or more), every building of an ocean world up to three; a room to be made round is at least six squares a side. Corridors to them meet the curve as before.
- Water worlds (shared/logic "Water worlds": `waterOf`, `deckSquares`, `GANTRY`, `swimPace`; map: `Sea`, `Decks`, `UnderSea`, `FishSchools` in siteAssets.tsx, `SeaBeasts` in LocalMap). On the sea: a sea of waves (moved in the vertex shader of a Phong material), decking on piles with a rail along the water, footings under the buildings; off the decking the party swims (`SWIM_Y`, posture 3, half pace). Under the sea: sand floor, domes of air over the buildings, kelp, bubbles, shafts of light, the face of the sea far above, the sea's colour as fog (`Sky` `under`); out of doors the party swims (`UNDER_SWIM_Y`) and `hazardsAt` gives underwater. Fish schools in both; an opee sea killer (and under the sea a sando aqua monster) from the rules data circle beyond the buildings.
- Swimming (interiors/limbs.ts): posture 3 (`Eased.swim`): moving, a front crawl, flat in the water (the root's `tip`); still, treading water. Gallery `/dev/models?section=moves&act=swim` (and `act=tread`).
- Water craft (`VEHICLE_MAKES`, `WATER_LOOKS`, `SUBMERSIBLE_LOOKS`; models in models.tsx `buildMake`): Gungan bongo, Aquadon submersible, Hydrospeeder.
- Galleries: `/dev/models?section=caves` (`&roof=1` puts the roof on the chamber), `?section=water`. The first load of a gallery page takes 15-20 s on the dev server; the headless e2e screenshot of these sections timed out (use the built-in browser).
- Going under the sea: as for any deadly place, the party is not let travel to a place under the sea without protection (gills, an underwater respirator, a diving or water suit: gear.ts `exposure`, GameBoard `deadlyAt`); a submersible ride does not count yet.
- Not done: encounter beasts are not placed in the water; other players (co-op) and followers are not drawn swimming; a species' own swimming speed is not read yet (everyone swims at half pace). A cave not walked through in play (gallery and tests only). (The sea crossing and the water craft in a place: next section.)

## Sea crossings and water craft (2026-10-30; the user: "Yes, do those next", the crossing of an ocean world by boat and riding the water craft)
- Rides (shared/logic "Over the water": `waterwayOf`, `groundRides` `waterway`, `isWaterCraft`, `isSubmersible`): see the Play rules. A tap on an exit over the sea with nothing free to go by takes the water taxi (`defaultRide`). Lifts over the sea are only those in an airspeeder.
- The crossing (`planTrip` `water`, roadtrip.ts; the course `RaceScenery` `sea` in `raceCourse`, scenery from siteAssets `raceScenery(biome, true)`): a leg is flat water (`landAt` 0), no shortcuts, jumps or crossings, buoys (siteAssets `buoy`) along both edges every 32 m and on the bends, sea stacks (`sea_stack`) and boulders standing out of the sea, floating cargo and oil as on a road. Drawn as `Sea` (murky on a swamp world) instead of the ground and the road; the party's craft is its make (the leg's `look`), riders after it ride hydrospeeders (a bike's body) or bongos. Titled "Crossing to ...". Lab: `/dev/dogfight?game=road&water&look=bongo&biome=ocean` (`&chase` for riders).
- In a place on the water (LocalMap `boats`, `afloatRef`, `wetAt`, `moor`; GameBoard `afloat` in playState, the craft's panel "Take the helm", the map action `moor`; PartyVehicle `berth`): the craft floats where it was left or off the first building's way in; at its helm the party sits in it and taps the water to steer (also past the ground, out to `BOAT_REACH`); a tap on the decking or a way in brings it alongside, moors it there (saved) and the party steps ashore; going in through a way in at the helm moors it where the party left the water. Under the sea only a submersible is drawn and taken out. Leaving the place lets go of the helm; going indoors does not (the map moors the craft first). Followers swim after it. Trees and rocks are no longer put on the water or the decking of a place on the sea or under it (the ambient pieces, cover and backdrop are left out).
- Test hook: `window.__street.water()` (the ground, the craft at the helm, and open/water/ground tests for a square).
- Checked in the browser on the test database's campaign 443 (Kamino): at Monarrin (on the sea) the rides to Catotine and the island ruins, the hydrospeeder given by the test menu moored by the decking, taken out, steered past the ground, moored and left; the crossing to Catotine by hydrospeeder, five legs under autopilot with an ambush on the last run from ("Ambush on the water!"); at Catotine (under the sea) the hydrospeeder not drawn, an Aquadon submersible taken out (no breath warning at its helm), steered over the sea floor and moored by going in at the gate. Not seen: a phone, co-op.
- Not done: the craft does not carry the followers (they swim after it); a co-op peer at the helm is drawn walking; the town's overview map (the settlement view of its buildings) is still drawn on sand on an ocean world; a fight with an ambush at sea ("Stop and fight", or caught) is fought on the roadside ground; the party sits on top of a submersible, not in it.
- (Decided 2026-10-30, see the next section) Seen, for the user to decide: arriving under the sea other than in a submersible (by a water taxi, a hydrospeeder) puts the party in the water holding its breath (1 + Constitution modifier minutes), and with the game's clock running the test character went down in the time it took the page to load. Possible house rule: a water taxi or a craft of the surface sets the party down inside the place's first dome.

## Seats, gunners, the convoy, the rest of the water list (2026-10-30, sixth batch; the user: dome drop-off yes, the "not done" list, seats for every speeder and water craft, characters drawn in their seats, turrets and gunners that follow the aim, a convoy minigame with the party in the convoy and bot fighters defending it)
- Play rules (house rules, labelled on screen): **dome** (shared/logic `domeOf`): a party that comes to a place under the sea from another by anything but a submersible is set down in the way in of its first building. **Convoy** (shared/logic "The convoy": `CONVOY`, `convoyStart`, `convoyTick`, `convoyResult`): Downtime > Racing games > Convoy guard; haulers on a road, the party's craft at the back, raiders of the antagonist in three waves, two bot fighters of the convoy's side, each hauler's turret manned; the party drives or takes its gun (crewmate or an RX-series droid pilot drives); pays 150 a hauler through and 60 a raider down. **Sea fight**: an ambush caught at sea is fought knee-deep on a sandbar among rocks (arena floor "sea"). **Sea beast**: swimming 10 s in the open water on the sea (20 s under it) draws the opee sea killer (warned first, a fight in the water if it reaches the party), once a minute at most. **Swimming**: the species' swimming speed (`swimPace`, now reads "a swimming speed of 30 feet"), else half.
- Seats (models.tsx `craftLayout`, `CraftCrew`, `GunMount`; Figure `crew`; limbs.ts `crewPose`): every make has seats to its seat count (a bench behind for more), seated, astride or standing; the gunner sits on the gun's swivel and turns with it, arms raised with the pitch. Guns: T-47 rear gun, cloud car turret, skiff bow gun, troop transport roof turret, a lent pintle for any craft with a gunner. Canopies of the airspeeder, V-35, T-47, Koro-2, cloud car pods, bongo bubbles and the Aquadon's hull were enlarged so the crew sits inside. Gallery `/dev/models?section=crews`.
- Where: the party and the followers who fit sit in the craft at the helm (the rest swim); co-op `pos` carries the craft (`r`) and the peer is drawn in it; the race and road draw real people (party look, companions, scout troopers, pilots) and Racer `gun` / RaceInput `pitch`. The ocean town overview is water. Travel time no longer counts as exposure at the place left (`departed` in GameBoard).
- Checked: tests (convoy.test.ts, water.test.ts additions; full suite 484 pass, the one failure an expectation fixed after); browser on dev 5099 / test db: convoy lab (turret camera, radar, shots down raiders), gallery crews, dome drop-off by water taxi at Catotine, helm at Monarrin with the 3P0 aboard (hook), ocean overview. Not seen: the sea beast attack and the sea fight in play, co-op peers at the helm, a phone.
- Not done: the convoy is one player's (no co-op seats in it); ship gunners in a starship fight. Open: the game clock (a minute a second) makes holding one's breath last a few real seconds; a test-menu respirator protects only once put on.

## Done
Phase 1: package.json `"type": "module"`, nanoid added, Replit plugin removed (vite.config.ts), tailwind.config.ts uses imports, tsconfig jsx react-jsx, species.ts:13 syntax error, unused nextui imports removed from CharacterCreation.tsx, plural route scheme (/characters, /characters/create, /characters/edit/:id, /characters/:id, /campaigns, /campaigns/:id, /campaigns/create, /map, /game/:campaignId) with all links updated, CampaignView uses lib/queryClient apiRequest, server error handler no longer rethrows, /api/sw5e/* removed (tables never existed; data is bundled client-side), insertDebriefSchema from shared/schema.
Phase 2a: server/db.ts uses node-postgres and seeds user 1 (ensureDefaultUser); routes use the insert schemas from shared/schema.ts; PUT/PATCH characters accept partial; DELETE characters added; /api/star-systems removed; client/src/lib/api/toServerCharacter.ts maps the creation form (abilities, hitPoints, powerPoints) to the DB shape (abilityScores, maxHp, maxForcePoints); schema persists feats/force powers/tech powers/personality; dead CharacterCreationForm.tsx deleted; old migrations replaced by one generated from shared/schema.ts.
Phase 2b: CampaignView finds the character in create mode (?characterId= or first); GameBoard loads the campaign's character by characterId (B6); location buttons PATCH campaign.currentLocation; new components/Combat/PlayCombat.tsx (melee/ranged, enemy auto-turns) replaces CombatTracker in Play; player HP PATCHed to the DB when combat changes it; Rest button; campaigns PUT/PATCH partial; CharacterManagement reads the query result; /characters redirects to /; GalaxyMap no longer calls missing fetchStarSystems; react-query staleTime 0; test enemies given armorClass and string ids.
Phase 2c (found by the browser play-through):
- Creation form never submitted: it validated against shared/unifiedSchema.ts, whose required fields (abilityScores, maxHp, startingLocation, ...) the form does not fill. It now has its own small schema and names what is missing.
- The Equipment step crashed the whole page (Radix Tooltip without a provider). Tooltip removed there.
- Clicking Next on the Equipment step submitted the form: React reused the Next button's DOM node as the submit button during the click. The two buttons have keys now. The Continue buttons, the equipment Add buttons and Enter in a text field also submitted the form; fixed.
- Saved stats were NaN or missing. `deriveStats()` in pages/CharacterCreation.tsx computes them from the full class and species data: HP from the hit die and Constitution, species ability bonuses, AC 10 + Dex, speed, languages, saving throws, power points. The Preview step shows them.
- The class picker used the id/name list `CLASSES`, which has no hit die or skills. It now uses `classes` (the seven with data). Skills can be picked (they could not: SKILLS has no ids). Feats: floor(level / 4), so none at level 1.
- Save Draft / Load Draft buttons removed (the endpoints never existed).
- Saving a generated campaign sent PUT /api/campaigns/create and got a 500. It POSTs now and then shows the saved campaign, with Home and Play links.
- Combat: `rollDice` returns an array of dice; the store added it to a number, so damage was text like "052" and every hit killed. Summed now, in the store and on the character sheet. Test Combat is disabled at 0 HP.
- Character page: the HP, Force point and notes fields called a store function that does not exist. They now collect edits that Save Changes sends as a partial update.
- Home: New Campaign and Delete buttons. Server: DELETE /api/campaigns/:id; deleting a character deletes its campaigns.
- App.tsx no longer loads the legacy stores at startup (one call did not exist, the other threw a ZodError on every page).
- Two orphaned test campaigns (ids 1 and 3, their characters already deleted) were removed from the DB.

Phase 2d (rules data) and 2e (world): see the two sections above. Also fixed along the way:
- Creation: species ability score choices (Human picks +2 and two +1, or four +1), class skills from the class's real list, two skills from the background's list, the background's feat, powers known and power levels from the class table, starting wealth by class (Fighter 8d4 x 100, the average), armor counted in AC, the nine alignments.
- Force and tech points come from the class table plus the casting modifier (Consular level x 4, Sentinel x 3, Guardian x 2, Engineer x 2, Scout x 1).
- The character page's Inventory tab shows the equipment carried and can add or remove items.
- Combat: a natural 1 misses; the attack bonus uses the proficiency bonus for the level; damage comes from the weapon carried (unarmed is 1 + Strength modifier; no ranged attack without a ranged weapon).
- The campaign page saves name, description, NPCs, locations and quests (its edits used to go nowhere).

## Findings (from the original static review)
Confirmed and fixed: A1-A4, B5, B6, B7, C11, most of C12.
Still open:
- B8 duplicate/competing code: two character stores (lib/stores/useCharacter.ts, useCharacterStore.ts), two combat stores, lib/data vs lib/sw5e, two campaign generators (lib/sw5e/campaignGenerator.ts is what the Campaign UI uses; lib/llm/campaignGenerator.ts is used by useCampaign.generateCampaign and falls back to an empty campaign), three shared schema files (schema.ts is the real one for the server; unifiedSchema.ts and compatSchema.ts are legacy and used by some client code). The acceptance path no longer depends on them. Delete only after confirming unused.
- B9 about 85 of 205 client files unreachable (not re-measured).
- B10 BasicInfo.tsx imports missing ./LoreTooltip; CharacterCreator.tsx imports missing ./CharacterCreator/SaveControls (both unreachable).
- C13 client calls endpoints the server lacks: /api/characters/validate, /api/campaign/:id/save, /api/campaign/generate, /api/sw5e/*. In unreachable or fallback paths.
- C14 repo root has overlapping one-off scripts (migrate.ts, run-migrations.ts, migrations.ts, verify-schema.ts, shared/scripts/migrate-with-verification.ts, fixSpeciesData.*, bulkFormatSpeedFix.ts, db-fix-script.js, convertSpecies.ts, .replit, replit.nix). They import neon and are unused.
- D15 quests are displayed but nothing advances them. (Combat now starts from the hostile creatures in the area the party is in; Test Combat remains only for campaigns without a world.)
- D16 there is no built-in GM: lib/llm + GMReportManager + ClipboardIntegration implement a manual copy-paste loop (needs HTTPS or localhost for the clipboard).
- E17 phone readiness, seen in screenshots (`~/sw5e-logs/e2e_phone`): the HUD covers about three quarters of a phone screen and the combat panel lies on top of it. Everything can be tapped and the HUD scrolls clear of the button bar, which now fits the screen. Still: h-screen instead of dvh, no PWA manifest, large script chunks on the creation and character pages.

Gaps that remain after 2026-10-15 (companions and transport): companions use their stat block's one best attack and do not level; no companion powers, bonus actions or tactics; riding a mount does not change a fight (no mounted combat); the mount, speeder and companions are not shown on the planet view's globe or in a settlement's streets between buildings; vehicles have no fuel, cargo or combat, and cannot be sold; ground journeys have no encounters or exposure; no 2D anywhere (user's rule), so a vehicle or companion is used by tapping it or from the HUD lists.

Gaps that remain (2026-10-13, after Phase 2f; the list this replaced is closed except for what is repeated here):
- Most features are still only shown. What is automated is listed under "Play rules"; Rage, Sneak Attack, fighting styles, maneuvers, Darkvision and the like change nothing in a fight.
- No multiclassing. Archetypes that grant casting to a class without it (9 of the 137) give no power points. A feat's prerequisite is checked where it is a level, a casting ability, another feat or one ability score; species type, size and either-or ability scores are shown for the player to honour. Creation checks the background feat the same way. One background names a feat ("investigator") that is not in the feat data, so it is not offered.
- Powers: of the 465, 130 resolve by a saving throw, 32 by an attack roll, 11 put a condition on without a roll and 9 heal; the other 283 are cast, paid for and described. A power that imposes a condition on several creatures hits only the first standing enemy. A power whose damage comes later ("At the end of each of its turns ...") deals none in the app.
- Combat is one action a turn: no bonus actions, reactions, movement, range or cover; enemies use their single best attack.
- No starship combat, crew or ship upgrades. `lib/sw5e/combatmanagers.ts` (starship and vehicle combat) is still unused; its d20 rolls are fixed.
- The enemy faction of a new campaign is still picked from the list in `lib/sw5e/campaignGenerator.ts`, which is not rules data.
- Campaigns without a world ("Test Campaign") keep the plain location list, Test Combat and the stock `/map` viewers with their hard-coded places.
- The encounter XP budgets are the 5th edition numbers; the sw5e.com API does not carry Scum and Villainy's encounter guidance. At 1st level alone they only allow very weak foes.
- Monsters carry no habitat in the data, so wildlife is matched to a biome by words in its text; where fewer than four match, any beast is used.
- A character created above 1st level gets that level's hit points and powers but not the Ability Score Improvements of the levels it skipped.
- tsc: several hundred errors, mostly in legacy files (not re-counted). Vite does not typecheck.

## SW5e.com crosscheck (2026-10-12; the content and formula mismatches listed here were fixed on 2026-10-13 by the import, except where "Gaps that remain" says otherwise)
How it was done: sw5e.com is a single-page app over a public API, `https://sw5eapi.azurewebsites.net/api/<name>` (data version 199, unchanged since 2023-05-06). Every dataset was downloaded and compared by script with a JSON dump of `client/src/lib/sw5e/*.ts`. Dataset names: species, class, archetype, background, feat, power, equipment, enhancedItem, monster, maneuvers, feature, fightingStyle, fightingMastery, lightsaberForm, classImprovement, multiclassImprovement, splashclassImprovement, weaponFocus, weaponSupremacy, weaponProperty, armorProperty, skills, conditions, characterAdvancement, referenceTable, starshipBaseSize, starshipDeployment, starshipEquipment, starshipModification, starshipVenture, PlayerHandbookRule, StarshipRule, WretchedHivesRule, VariantRule, ExpandedContent, dataVersion.

Result in one line: the formulas copied from D&D 5e are right; almost all bundled content is invented or mis-statted, and the SW5e-specific rules (power points, powers known, background feat, alignment) are wrong.

Matches the site: ability modifier, proficiency bonus by level, XP table, point buy (27 points, same costs), standard array, HP (full hit die at level 1, average after), hit dice of all seven classes, unarmored AC 10 + Dex, Strength for melee and Dexterity for ranged, critical hits double the dice, carrying capacity Strength x 15, archetype at level 3, 15 of 16 condition names (Shocked has the wrong effects; conditions.ts is not loaded by the app).

Wrong in code the app uses:
- Saving throws: Guardian is Constitution/Charisma (code: Strength/Constitution); Scout is Strength/Dexterity (code: Dexterity/Wisdom).
- Skills (constants.ts SKILLS): "Endurance" does not exist, "Animal Handling" is missing, Nature and Piloting are Intelligence skills (code: Wisdom, Dexterity). Class lists name History, Science, Religion, which are not SW5e skills. Consular picks 2 skills (code 3). No class skill list matches the site exactly.
- Class proficiencies and features: mostly D&D class features under SW5e class names (Ki, Natural Explorer, Favored Enemy, Aura of Protection). Armor and weapon proficiencies differ for every class.
- Power points (deriveStats in pages/CharacterCreation.tsx, calculateMaxPowerPoints in rules.ts): site is Consular level x 4, Sentinel x 3, Guardian x 2, plus Wisdom or Charisma modifier; Engineer level x 2 and Scout level x 1 (from level 2) plus Intelligence modifier. Code gives level + modifier and nothing to Scout.
- Powers known at level 1 (PowersSelection.tsx): site Consular 9, Sentinel 7, Guardian 5, Engineer 6, Scout 0. Code: Consular 2, Sentinel 1 force + 1 tech, Guardian 1, Engineer 2. It gives tech powers to Scholar and Sentinel, which have none on the site.
- Max power level: site full casters reach 9th (1st, 1st, 2nd, 2nd, 3rd ...). Code caps at 5 with ceil(level / 4). calculatePowerSlots is D&D spell slots; SW5e has no slots.
- Species (10 of the site's 141; 5 of the 30 in the Player's Handbook): ability bonuses are wrong for Advozse, Arcona, Human, Twi'lek, Zabrak and Bothan, so deriveStats saves wrong scores for them. Human is +2 to one and +1 to two of the player's choice (or +1 to four), not +1 Strength and +1 Dexterity. Aleena speed is 25 (code 35). 20 of the 23 trait names are invented.
- Backgrounds: every background grants a feat at level 1 and a choice of 2 skills from 4. The code grants no feat (FeatsSelection allows floor(level / 4), so 0 at level 1) and never applies background skills. Ace Pilot, Diplomat, Outsider and the picker's Scholar, Pilot, Merchant are not on the site.
- Feats: 4 of the 13 in feats.ts exist (Alert, Force-Sensitive, Linguist, Tough) and 1 of the 5 the picker shows.
- Powers: 9 of 13 force powers exist by name and only 2 have the right level (Force Storm is 9th level, code 5th). None of the 11 tech powers exists.
- Equipment (equipment.ts, items.ts): 14 of 26 and 13 of 27 names exist; one price in each file matches. Blaster pistol is 175 cr (code 450 and 500), lightsaber 450 cr and 1d6 (code 3000 and 2d8), vibroblade 1d8 (code 1d4). Starting credits are a flat 500; the site uses class starting equipment or a roll such as 8d4 x 100 cr for a Fighter.
- Alignment: site is Light, Dark or Balanced combined with Lawful, Chaotic or Neutral. The code's five options are not SW5e.
- Combat in Play: a natural 1 does not auto-miss; the attack bonus is modifier + 2 at every level; damage is d8 melee and d6 ranged whatever weapon is carried; 0 HP is "defeated" with no death saves; Rest is a free long rest. Test Combat's Stormtrooper (30 HP, AC 13, +3) and Imperial Officer (20 HP, AC 12, +2) are not the site's Trooper (13 HP, AC 15, +5, 1d8+3) and Junior Officer (13 HP, AC 12, +3, 1d6+1), each CR 1/2.

Present on the site, absent or token in code: 137 archetypes (code 13, three real names, invented features), 119 maneuvers (2, neither real), 32 fighting styles and 32 masteries (1 real name), 20 lightsaber forms (0), 46 weapon and 30 armor properties (2), 1918 enhanced items (0), 271 monsters (15 entries, none with the site's numbers), Exhaustion condition, multiclassing. Starships: the site builds a ship from size (Tiny to Gargantuan), tier, hull and shield dice, six crew deployments, 257 modifications, 104 equipment items and 67 ventures; the code has five fixed stat blocks with sizes like "Colossal" that the site does not use.

Site pages with no counterpart: Tools is only a login-gated Character Creator (steps Species, Class, Ability Scores, Description, Equipment; Standard Array, Point Buy or Manual, same three methods as here). Assets is PDFs of the four rulebooks, character, deployment and starship sheets, expanded-content links, a podcast.

Done on 2026-10-13: the data files are generated from the API, expanded content included (the user asked for all of it).

## Decisions
- Package is ESM.
- Storage: Postgres on this VM with node-postgres (user accepted the default).
- Deleted CharacterCreationForm.tsx (dead, corrupted, unreferenced); pages/CharacterCreation.tsx is the creation flow.
- No 2D maps (user, 2026-10-13): what is where is shown on the 3D map and used by tapping it. Lists and buttons in the HUD stay, as another way to do the same things.
- The server binds loopback by default (2c). Phase 3 decides what sits in front of it.
- The class picker offers only classes that have data (2c). Revisit when data for the other three is added.
- Playwright is a dev dependency, pinned (2c).
- Rules data is generated, never hand-written (2d). All ten classes are offered again now that all have data.
- New campaigns always get a generated world; campaigns without one are offered one in Play (changed 2026-10-13; they kept a plain location list before).
- The generator's code lives in the existing shared/logic module and the editor in the existing CampaignManager, at the user's request to extend files rather than add them. The only new files are the import script and the generated data.
- Access is Tailscale-only (user, 2026-10-13); public HTTPS stays a possible later development. No Oracle port is opened for the game.
- Phase 2f added no source files: `lib/sw5e/characterCalculation.ts` (an unused helper with two hard-coded experience tables) was rewritten as the module for features and levelling. New files are `deploy/*` and migration 0001.
- Rules the books leave open are settled by the labelled house rules under "Play rules". Starting equipment follows the starting wealth variant.
